# Changelog

All notable public changes to PiG will be recorded in this file.

## [Unreleased]

## [0.5.2] - 2026-10-22

### Upgrading

- Extensions written in Go, Python or Rust that fell back to a child `pig` process for `createAgentSession({customTools, settingsManager, modelRuntime})` can call the new API. `model` takes a model object, as Pi's option does: resolve a name through `ModelRegistry.Find` first. No existing API changes.
- `HarnessOptions.OnReport` is now `func(err error, info durable.ReportInfo)`, and `durable.TaskRuntime` has `Report(err error, from ...ReportFrom)`: code that sets either must take the new parameter. `harness.Harness` has `RetryTask`.
- A task cut off by a crash 5 times in a row without committing a new state is parked instead of run again; set `HarnessSettings.RestartLimit` to `+Inf` to keep the old behavior. `TaskInspectionState.Reason` can be `restart_limit`.
- If code outside this repository builds `subprocess.CallPayload` with an unkeyed literal, add the two fields (`false` keeps today's behaviour) or switch to keyed fields. `coding.ExtensionHost` gains `SetTimerTurns(*jsloop.Turns)`; an embedder that implements the interface adds a method that does nothing, because `internal/jsloop` is not importable outside the module: it cannot write the parameter type, so such an embedder cannot implement the interface and has no replacement to migrate to. Extensions in every SDK need no change.
- Go API `chord/host`: `Host.Get` and `Ctx.Get` wait until the reference is ready: live, with every state member of its variant. Use `Host.Hold` for the synchronous reference that may still be loading. Before: `ref, _ := host.Get(typ, input, nil)` then `ref.Ready(ctx)`. After: `ref, err := host.Get(typ, input, nil)` for an awaited reference, or `ref, _ := host.Hold(typ, input, nil)` for the old behavior. A failed `Get` releases only its own reference and returns the activation error, `aborted` (`GetOptions.Signal`, or the ctx closing), or `unavailable/ready_timeout` after `HostOptions.ReadyTimeout` (60 s).
- Go API `chord/host`: reading a state member of a reference that is not live yet panics with a `not_ready` ChordError (and `not_in_variant` for a member the variant lacks) where it returned nil. A listener added before the first value gets its snapshot then: the events are `snap`, `ops`, not `snap` with nil, `reset`.
- The Chord link protocol version is 4. A hold implies the state of its variant (`state:false` opts out), state frames are addressed by hold and member (`as: snap | reset | ops | error`, `value`), `sub`, `unsub` and `resync` work per hold, a hold from a client link carries an `origin` the server assigns, and shape frames carry no `codeVersion`. A peer of another version closes the link with `protocol-version`.
- Go API `durable/storage/sqlite`: `Options` is renamed `SqliteStorageOptions`, the name of Pi's type. Before: `sqlite.Open(db, sqlite.Options{WorkingSetBytes: n})`. After: `sqlite.Open(db, sqlite.SqliteStorageOptions{WorkingSetBytes: n})`.
- Go API `durable/storage/sqlite/node`: the connection settings move to `NodeSqliteDatabaseOptions`, and `NodeSqliteStorageOptions` embeds them next to `Prefix` and `WorkingSetBytes`. `OpenNodeSqliteDatabase` takes `NodeSqliteDatabaseOptions`. Before: `OpenNodeSqliteDatabase(path, node.NodeSqliteStorageOptions{BusyTimeoutMs: &ms})`. After: `OpenNodeSqliteDatabase(path, node.NodeSqliteDatabaseOptions{BusyTimeoutMs: &ms})`. A literal of `NodeSqliteStorageOptions` that sets a connection field names the embedded struct (`NodeSqliteStorageOptions{NodeSqliteDatabaseOptions: node.NodeSqliteDatabaseOptions{BusyTimeoutMs: &ms}}`).
- Go API `durable/storage/sqlite/cloudflare`: `DurableObjectSqlCursor` gains `Next() (row, ok, err)`, which returns the next row; a cursor type implements it next to `ToArray`, which then returns the rows not read yet. `OpenDurableObjectSqliteStorage` takes optional `sqlite.SqliteStorageOptions`.
- Go API: `OutputBuffer`, `Progress`, `BoundOutput`, `SanitizeOutput`, `CharacterEnd`, `OutputLimits`, `OutputSlice`, `BoundedOutput` and their helpers move from `durable/harness` to the new package `chord/output`, as Pi 4a5d3729 moves them to `@earendil-works/chord/output`. Before: `harness.NewOutputBuffer(limits)`. After: `output.NewOutputBuffer(limits)`. `PushStringSkipping` and `PushBytesSkipping` take `output.OutputSkip`, which has the fields of `env.ShellOutputSkip`: `output.OutputSkip(skipped)` converts. `Progress`'s clock seam is now exported (`ProgressClock`, `NewProgressWithClock`).
- Go API `ai`: `anthToolResultContent` is internal, but the Anthropic and Bedrock converters now send the images of an error tool result after the tool results of the turn. A caller that read the old note `[image omitted: error tool results cannot contain images]` in an error result sees `(image attached after the tool results)` there and the images in the same user message, after a text block `Images from the error result of tool call <id> (<tool>):`.
- Go API `chord/host`: `CreateHost` needs a principal for the host's own code, as Pi 1.3.0's `createHost({ self })` does, and panics with a `*TypeError` without one. Before: `CreateHost(HostOptions{Name: "server"})`. After: `CreateHost(HostOptions{Name: "server", Self: "host"})`. `HostPrincipal` is gone: PiG no longer makes up principals.
- Go API `chord/host`: `Connect` takes `ConnectOptions` with the principal the parent's own requests act as, and `HostOptions.Upstream` is `&UpstreamOptions{Link: link, ConnectOptions: ConnectOptions{Principal: ...}}`. `Route.Principal` is required. Before: `host.Connect(link, LinkOptions{})`. After: `host.Connect(link, ConnectOptions{Principal: "server"})`.
- Go API `chord/host`: a remote contract says where its objects live and a get no longer names a host. Before: `Remote("t.chat", RemoteDef{...})` and `host.Get(chat, input, &GetOptions{At: &name})`. After: `Remote("t.chat", RemoteDef{..., Host: NewHostFamily("session:", "session")})` and `host.Get(chat, input, nil)`. `GetOptions.At` is gone.
- The Chord link protocol version is 3. A PiG host and a Pi host that speak another version close the link with `protocol-version` and report it through `OnProtocolMismatch`.
- Go SDK: `sdk.AgentToolResult` now marshals as the wire object a tool returns (`content` blocks, `structured_content`, `is_error`) instead of Pi's member names; it was only ever decoded before. Code that marshals it for another purpose should build its own struct.
- Rust SDK: `ExecOptions` has a `signal: Option<ProviderSignal>` field, so a struct literal needs it or `..Default::default()`. Before: `ExecOptions { timeout: Some(250.0), cwd: None }`. After: `ExecOptions { timeout: Some(250.0), ..Default::default() }`. `ProviderSignal` now implements `Debug` and `PartialEq` (two signals are equal when they are the same signal).
- Go SDK `ExecOptions` has a `Signal context.Context` field. Code that builds the struct with named fields is unchanged; an unkeyed literal needs the new field.
- Rust SDK: `pig_sdk::Color` is now `pig_sdk::tui::Color`, so an indexed color is `Color::Indexed(5)` instead of `Color::Indexed { index: 5 }`. `Color::Rgb { r, g, b }`, `Color::Oklch { l, c, h }` and `TextAttributes` are unchanged. The Go SDK's color types are unchanged.
- `ResizedImage` in the Go, Rust and Python SDKs gains `Data`/`data` and `MimeType`/`mime_type`; the Rust struct is no longer `Copy`, and a literal needs `..Default::default()`. `HostInfo` in the host's subprocess package gains `PackageDir` and `Shell`.
- If code outside this repository builds `subprocess.CallPayload` with an unkeyed literal, add a sixth field (`""` keeps today's behaviour) or switch to keyed fields.
- `ai` renames the provider request option `TelemetryContext` to `Telemetry` (Pi `telemetryContext` → `telemetry`, Pi 2ec55d80) on `StreamOptions`, `ClassifierOptions` and `ImagesOptions`, with no alias. Its type is `telemetry.TelemetrySpan`: pass a parent span, not an adapter; nil is the omitted option and uses the no-op span.
- `ai.LazyStream` and `ai.LazyStreamSync` take an optional trailing `LazyStreamObserver` (Pi's `lazyStream(model, setup, observer?)`).
- A Rust extension that calls `ctx.session_name()` receives a `String` instead of a `&str`, and the `session_name` and `model` members of the wire `ReadyMsg` no longer exist (the session name and the model travel in the ready state). A Python or Rust extension that compared `ctx.model` with a model's display name must compare it with the model's id. <!-- fix: 06dc555220 -->
- Rust SDK: a `before_agent_start` handler registers with `Extension::on_before_agent_start` (or `EventSubscriber::on_before_agent_start`) and receives a `BeforeAgentStartEvent` instead of `&mut serde_json::Value`. `Context::current_system_prompt` is removed; the event's `system_prompt()` and `Context::get_system_prompt()` replace it. Before: `ext.on_event_result("before_agent_start", |ctx, data| { data["systemPromptOptions"]["appendSystemPrompt"] = json!("x"); ctx.current_system_prompt(data)?; Ok(None) })`. After: `ext.on_before_agent_start(|ctx, event| { event.edit_system_prompt_options(|options| options["appendSystemPrompt"] = json!("x")); event.system_prompt().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?; Ok(None) })`. `event.payload()` is the host's event without `systemPrompt` and `systemPromptOptions` (`messageType`, `prompt`, `images`, `message`), `event.system_prompt_options()` copies the options, `event.set_system_prompt_options(value)` replaces them, and `event.snapshot()` is the whole event with the prompt and options as they are now. A `before_agent_start` handler still registered through `on_event`, `on_event_result` or `EventSubscriber::on_event` fails each emission with an error that names `on_before_agent_start`. The upgrade rules of `pig extension upgrade` cover Go extensions only, so a Rust extension is rewritten by hand. <!-- fix: 0b6bd6bd45 -->
- Go API `coding/mcpext`: `Host` has a `RegisterTools(definitions []extension.ToolDefinition)` method, which registers a server's tools as one change. A host that has no batch registration can register the definitions one by one. Before: a `Host` implemented `RegisterTool`, `GetAllTools`, `GetActiveTools`, `SetActiveTools` and `GetMcpServers`. After: it also implements `RegisterTools`.
- Go: `ModelRegistry.Find` now returns `(map[string]any, error)`. Receive the error (`model, err := ctx.ModelRegistry().Find(provider, id)`); `nil, nil` still means no model matches, and a failed host call is the error. The SDK upgrade rules name the change (`model-registry-find-returns-error`) but do not rewrite it. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- Rust: `ModelRegistry::find` returns `io::Result<Option<Value>>` instead of `Option<Value>`. Propagate or handle the `Err`; `Ok(None)` means no model matches. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- Python: `ModelRegistry.has_configured_auth` and `is_using_oauth` return `False` for a model with no `provider` instead of raising `KeyError`; drop any `except KeyError` that relied on it. `get_entries()` against a host with no session binding raises instead of returning `None`; do not test its result for `None`. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- If your code declared an `agent.SettleGate` or held an `*agent.SettleSlot` (for example in a tool that settles its own slot through `agent.ToolSettleFromContext`), stop naming the types: keep the value `ToolSettleFromContext` returns in a local variable and call its methods, or drop the call, because a tool that never settles its slot settles it when `Execute` returns. An extension needs no change: extensions in every SDK keep the same order through the wire, not through this type.
- If your code builds an `extension.CallLane` or sets `mcpext.McpToolOptions.Lane`, stop doing so and reserve call order through `ToolDefinition.ReserveCallOrder` with your own `extension.CallOrder` values.
- Extension SDKs (Go, Python, Rust): a dialog returns the JSON value the client answered with, as Pi's does, instead of a string or a bool. Go: `Context.Select`, `Context.SelectWithOptions`, `Context.Input`, `Context.InputWithOptions` and `Context.Editor` return `(any, bool, error)`, and `Context.Confirm` and `Context.ConfirmWithOptions` return `(any, error)`. Before: `choice, ok, err := ctx.Select(t, opts); if choice == "Execute" {...}`. After: `answer, ok, err := ctx.Select(t, opts); choice, _ := answer.(string)`. A cancelled dialog returns `nil` where it returned `""`, and `Confirm` returns `false` when cancelled. Rust: `select`, `input` and `editor` return `(serde_json::Value, bool)` and `confirm` returns `serde_json::Value`; read a string with `value.as_str()`, a boolean with `== json!(true)`; cancelled is `Value::Null`. Python: `select`, `input` and `editor` return `(Any, bool)`, `confirm` returns `Any`; the value is a `str` for a client that follows Pi's protocol, and a cancelled dialog's value is `None` where it was `""`. Node is unchanged. The host and every SDK change together; there is no negotiation.

### Added

- Child agent sessions in the Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs: `ctx.CreateAgentSession(sdk.CreateAgentSessionOptions{...})`, `ctx.create_agent_session(CreateAgentSessionOptions(...))` and `ctx.create_agent_session(CreateAgentSessionOptions { .. })` are Pi's `createAgentSession`. The host builds the child session over the parent's model runtime, so providers the parent registered reach it; `customTools` run in the extension that passed them; in-memory `settings` turn compaction off. The session has `prompt`, `steer`, `follow_up`, `abort`, `bind_extensions`, `state`, `messages`, `subscribe` and `dispose`, and ends with the extension's connection (its run is aborted and it is disposed). Calls block as Pi's Promises resolve: `prompt` returns once the run settled and every listener saw its last event. Node extensions already called Pi's own implementation. <!-- fix: cc584f5b -->
- The conformance rows for child sessions assert Pi's own observation, not PiG's: Node rows run Pi's `createAgentSession` through the sentinel, model-error and abort-mid-tool scenarios, and the Go (isolated, packed, fused), Python and Rust rows assert the same event lists, transcript roles, leading system message and error messages (`test/extension-conformance/testdata/child-session-want.json`). <!-- fix: 304bd443 -->
- `output.OutputBuffer.Push(chunk, skipped)` takes a string or bytes chunk and an optional skipped-output record in one call, as Pi's `OutputBuffer.push` does.
- Durable crash-loop guard (Pi `a30c2a81`): open counts each task that a crash, eviction, or reset cut off (`TaskRecord.Restarts`); a close writes the tasks it stopped back as `pending` without a count, and a new state the task commits starts the count over. At `HarnessSettings.RestartLimit` (default 5; `+Inf` never parks) the task is parked: blocked with reason `restart_limit` in `Inspect` and the task graph, not active in `Wake`, and reported once. `Harness.RetryTask` runs it again. An abort handler cut off that many times ends its task `faulted` with detail `{reason: "restart_limit"}`. Both Durable engines (the reference Harness and the core) implement it.
- `OnReport` receives a second argument, `durable.ReportInfo`, that says what the report is about: `session_failed`, `task_faulted`, `task_parked`, `migration_failed`, `task_outdated`, `task_error`, `extension_error`, `host_error` or `listener_error`, with the task, the extension that installed its definition, the failing host callback, or the conversation. `TaskRuntime.Report` takes an optional `durable.ReportFrom` to report extension or host code a task called.
- `HarnessSettings.AfterFailedRun`: a run that fails with `model_error` or `no_model` keeps the inputs queued behind it (`keep`, the default), starts the next run with them (`continue`), or settles them `unanswered` with reason `run_failed` (`withdraw`).
- The task graph shows why a task cannot run (`TaskGraphNode.Blocked`: `missing_task`, `task_too_old`, `migration_failed`, `restart_limit`) and follows registry changes and failed migrations. A registry change that unblocks a task makes `OnWake` report the Session active.
- Headless MCP (#214): package `coding/mcpext` no longer imports the terminal UI. The `/mcp` manager view and the tool renderers moved to `coding/mcpext/mcptui`, and the `pig` binary wires them through `mcpext.Options.Terminal` (`mcptui.Terminal()`), so its output is unchanged. `mcpext.NewHeadless` is the headless entry point. Package `mcp` links no terminal UI; `coding/mcpext` still links it through `coding/extension` (see `docs/design/extension-api-tui-split.md`). `mcpext.McpManagerView`, `NewMcpManagerView` and `CreateMcpToolRenderers` moved to `mcptui`; `McpMenu.Items` is now `[]McpMenuItem`.
- `ai.ClientIdentity`, as pi-ai's `ClientIdentity`, chooses a request's `User-Agent` (detailed with a version, short, or custom) and provider attribution headers for OpenRouter, NVIDIA NIM and OpenCode. `StreamOptions.Identity`, `ClassifierOptions.Identity`, `ImagesOptions.Identity` and `CreateModelsOptions.Identity` carry it, `ai.MergeClientIdentity` merges identities field by field, and `ai.WithClientIdentity` gives a models object a default identity. Attribution is off by default in `ai` and on by default in the agent loop (`agent.DefaultAgentIdentity`, `AgentOptions.Identity`) and the durable Harness (`HarnessOptions.Identity`).
- Models and the coding Model Runtime add `x-opencode-session` for custom providers pointing at OpenCode, not only for the built-in OpenCode providers.
- `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` is in the catalog for the `minimax` and `minimax-cn` providers. Thinking levels low through max go out as `output_config.effort` with adaptive thinking, and thinking cannot be turned off for this model. New MiniMax models from models.dev now appear without a manual allowlist entry; only the older M2, M2.1, M2.5 and M2.5-highspeed models stay hidden (Pi d2e7d8b7, #11121).
- `HarnessOptions.Telemetry` emits the content-free `harness.DurableTelemetrySchema` (Pi 2ec55d80): public submission admission spans (`pi.durable.submit`), task phase spans (`pi.durable.phase`, including extension tasks), committed entry and submission events, running phase-change checkpoint events, nested-tool replay events, report events and counters, and the `pi.durable.phase.duration` and `pi.durable.tasks.active` metrics. Failed phases record the fixed categories `task_faulted`, `task_failed` and `task_orphaned` with a diagnostic capped at 500 characters; an aborted outcome keeps status ok.
- `durable.TaskOptions.Trace` (a carrier, or `durable.TaskTrace{Root: true}`) and `TaskOptions.Links` choose a task's stored trace carrier and creation-time links; absence inherits the creator's carrier.
- PiG detects the Rex terminal (`TERM_PROGRAM=rex`) and uses OSC 8 hyperlinks and true color there (and, since Pi 396ecd7b, Kitty images; see the later entry). The check runs before the Kitty variables, so a leaked `KITTY_WINDOW_ID` does not turn images on (Pi 0ed1b043, #11131).
- `chord/telemetrysqlite` ports Pi's `@earendil-works/chord/telemetry-sqlite`: `CreateSqliteTelemetry(db, options)` records settled spans with inline events and links and metrics aggregated per series per ten seconds into any `chord/sqlite` database, flushes once a second with retention cleanup, bounds its queue (counting `DroppedSpans` and `DroppedMeasurements`), and serves `telemetry.TelemetryStore` queries over the committed rows.
- `chord/host`: one shared binding per host and key for remote references. References to one object share one hold, one snapshot per member and one frame per change. `HostOptions.Linger` keeps a released binding for a time (cap 4), `GetOptions.State` false gives a method-only reference, and a re-hold after a link loss keeps the last values readable.
- `chord/host`: a local object goes live only with its state, also where it forwards or derives from a remote reference's member; activation and a reload's prepare wait at most `HostOptions.ReadyTimeout` and name what they wait for.
- `chord/host`: `LinkOptions.PendingCap` bounds the state ops that wait for a slow peer on one link (default 4 windows). Beyond it the oldest streams are replaced by their current value, and a relay asks the provider for a reset.
- `durable/storage/sqlite`: `SqliteStorageOptions.Prefix` namespaces every table and index of the SQLite storage, for a database shared with host tables (Pi f7e3cb54). `node.OpenNodeSqliteStorage` and `cloudflare.OpenDurableObjectSqliteStorage` forward it; an invalid prefix (not an identifier, or starting with `sqlite_`) fails before the database is created. The default names are unchanged, and changing the prefix does not migrate existing data.
- `chord/output`: the Go counterpart of `@earendil-works/chord/output`, with `OutputSkip` and `Utf8ByteLength`. `durable.Utf8ByteLength` calls it.
- `ai.SplitErrorToolResultImages` and `ai.SplitErrorToolResult`: split an error tool result into a text-only result and the images to send after the tool results, for APIs that reject images in error results (Pi 5ab7fe74).
- `chord/host`: `Self.State` takes a `StateInit` as the initial value, so a cell's first value comes from its source. `Init` runs after the object's resources are open and before the object goes live, and a reload runs it for a cell it adds. Until then the cell's `Value` fails `not_ready`.
- `chord/host`: contract placement. `NewHostNamed` and `NewHostFamily` give a remote contract its host. `Serve` refuses a type the host is not the place of, a host refuses a hold whose input its own contract places elsewhere (`invalid/placement`), and a relay refuses a hold whose target disagrees with its contract token. The placement is part of the description and the fingerprint, and an `Accepts` adapter never bridges two placements.
- Go, Python and Rust extensions can override a built-in tool and delegate to the original, as Pi's `create<Tool>ToolDefinition` factories let Node extensions do. `sdk.CreateReadToolDefinition(cwd, opts)` (and the write, edit, bash, grep, find, ls and powershell siblings; Python `create_read_tool_definition`, Rust `create_read_tool_definition`) return the tool's definition, which registers as is, and whose `Execute` runs the host's own tool with Pi's options: `operations` and `spawnHook` functions stay in the extension and are called back while the call runs. The `create<Tool>Tool` forms, `createCodingTools`, `createReadOnlyTools`, `createLocalBashOperations` and `createLocalPowerShellOperations` come with them. New host calls `builtinTool.definition`, `builtinTool.execute` and `builtinTool.localExec`. A built-in's result is a value an `Execute` can return or pass to `OnUpdate` as it is. `TestConformance_BuiltinToolDelegationAcrossSDKs` compares every SDK with Pi's own code. <!-- fix: 1b5b705930 -->
- Every extension SDK (Go, Rust, Python and Node) gains `OnThemeChange` and `OnToolsExpandedChange` (a change of the host's replicated theme or tool-expansion state, pushed when it changes), `SetWidgetRenderer` (Go, Rust and Python: a widget laid out at the host's width; Node runs Pi's own factory form), `Bell` (`ui.bell`: BEL between frames in the interactive UI, nothing in print, JSON and RPC modes), `ToolCallIndex`, `ToolInput` and `ToolParams` (the tool call's position in its assistant message and its arguments in `Object.keys` order), `ExtensionSourceInfo` (the loader's provenance for the extension) and `OnLoaded` (handlers that run once the extension is registered and before it serves a request). 
- `pi.exec`'s `options.signal` works in every extension SDK, as in Pi: Go `ExecOptions.Signal`, Rust `ExecOptions.signal`, Python `Context.exec(..., signal=)` and Node `options.signal`. When the signal aborts, the host sends SIGTERM to the command (it kills the process on Windows), and the call returns the output written so far with `killed` set and code 0, as Pi's does. Like Pi, only the command is signalled, not its descendants, and a command that ignores SIGTERM keeps the call waiting. A signal replaces the calling request's cancellation: cancelling the request neither ends the command nor returns from the call. In-process extensions pass the signal in `extension.ExecOptions.Signal`.
- `createAgentSession` of the Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs takes the objects an extension built, as Pi's `CreateAgentSessionOptions` does: `sessionManager`, `settingsManager`, `resourceLoader`, `modelRuntime` (Go `SessionManager`, `SettingsManager`, `ResourceLoader`, `ModelRuntime` fields; Python `session_manager`, `settings_manager`, `resource_loader`, `model_runtime`; Rust the `handle()` of each) and `scopedModels`. The child uses the very objects: the transcript lands in the extension's `SessionManager`, the model and thinking level come from its `SettingsManager` defaults, the system prompt and the extensions of its `DefaultResourceLoader` are the session's, and its `ModelRuntime` serves the models. Naming an object together with the option it replaces (`inMemory`, `settings`, `resources`) is an error. The conformance row `TestSessionGraphChildHandlesAcrossSDKs` runs one script in Go (fused and isolated), Python, Rust and Node and compares every report with the one Pi's own `createAgentSession` produces.
- The Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs can construct Pi's `SessionManager` (`create`, `open`, `continueRecent`, `inMemory`, `forkFrom`, `findById`, `list`, `listAll` and every log method), `SettingsManager` (`create`, `inMemory`, all 134 getters and setters, `flush`, `reload`, `drainErrors`, project trust) and `ProjectTrustStore` with `hasTrustRequiringProjectResources`, as the Node runtime already could. The host owns each object as a handle on the extension's connection (`sessionGraph.*` host calls). A conformance row runs the same script in every SDK and compares it with Pi's own classes run by the Node runtime.
- Extension SDKs (Go `extensions/sdk/tui`, Python `pig_sdk.tui`, Rust `pig_sdk::tui`): Pi's pi-tui helpers `visibleWidth`, `truncateToWidth`, `wrapTextWithAnsi`, `stripTerminalSequences`, `sliceByColumn`/`sliceWithWidth`/`extractSegments`, `getOsc8LinkAtColumn`, `CURSOR_MARKER`, `matchesKey`, `Key`, `parseKey`, `isKeyRelease`, `isKeyRepeat`, `decodeKittyPrintable`, `decodePrintableKey`, `fuzzyMatch`, `fuzzyFilter`, the color helpers (`parseColor`, `rgbColor`, `oklchColor`, `okhslColor`, `colorTo*`, `mixColors`, `foregroundAnsi`, `backgroundAnsi`, `styleText`, `styleTextWithAnsi`), `getCapabilities` and friends, `hyperlink`, `KeybindingsManager`, `TUI_KEYBINDINGS`, `getKeybindings`/`setKeybindings`. Every SDK reproduces vectors generated by running the pinned pi-tui 1.3.0 package's own functions. The SDKs install the host's keybinding table and terminal capabilities as pi-tui's global state, as the Node runtime does, and the Go SDK adds `KeyHint`, `KeyText`, `KeyDisplayText`, `RawKeyHint` and `FormatKeyText`. <!-- fix: 303653f7ac -->
- Extension SDKs (Go, Python, Rust): Pi's coding-agent theme and key-hint helpers `getMarkdownTheme`, `getSelectListTheme`, `getSettingsListTheme`, `getEditorTheme`, `highlightCode`, `initTheme`, `keyHint`, `keyText`, `rawKeyHint` and `truncateToVisualLines`, held to the pinned Pi package's own output by vectors (`test/parity/sdk-vectors/pi-coding-agent/`) and by a conformance row per SDK against the real host (Node runs Pi's own functions in the same rows). The theme helpers read a process-global theme (the host's palette, or the one `initTheme` installed until the host's theme changes); `highlightCode` asks the host's highlight.js port over the new pre-registration `highlight.code` call and `initTheme` the host's theme registry over `theme.load`. `Theme.sourceInfo` is carried in the host palette and read by the three SDKs. <!-- fix: 6d00b4afff -->
- Extension SDKs (Go, Python, Rust, and Node through its own components): the component kit draws pi-tui's and pi-coding-agent's own components as host-rendered view kinds, as Pi renders them (Pi-rendered goldens at widths 30, 72 and 120, held in every SDK's conformance cell): `BranchSummary`, `CompactionSummary`, `SkillInvocation` and `CustomMessage` (coding-agent's message components; a click toggles them as in Pi), `CancellableLoader` and `BorderedLoader` (the focused cancel key aborts and reaches the extension as an `abort` view event), `Input` and `Editor` (the host owns the text and the cursor and runs the component's own key and mouse handling once the view's focus names it; `submit`, `escape` and `change` view events carry the value and the cursor), `ScrollView` and `MouseRegion` (the handler crosses as a description and each event it ran for reaches the extension as a `mouse` view event), and `Autocomplete`, the `CombinedAutocompleteProvider` of an editor. The `ui.view.event` notification gains `submit`, `escape`, `abort` and `mouse` events and a `cursor` on change events.
- Extension SDKs: an `Editor` in a view shows `max(5, floor(rows * 0.3))` lines of the terminal's real height, as Pi's editor does; a view surface reaches the host's terminal height.
- Extension SDKs (Rust, Python): the `EditorBase` the delegated editor component gets is the ledger's cell for Pi's `Editor` and `CustomEditor` queries and setters; `Theme.sourceInfo` is reached by the theme fixtures of every SDK.
- Extension SDKs (Go, Python, Rust): a delegated editor component takes the handlers Pi's `CustomEditor` keeps as public fields on its base: `onEscape`, `onCtrlD`, `onPasteImage`, `onExtensionShortcut`, `actionHandlers` and `onAction`. The host answers the key of an app action the component handles with a hook instead of running the action, and the component runs its handler inside `handleInput`, in the order Pi's `CustomEditor.handleInput` gives them (Escape only while no autocomplete shows, Ctrl+D only on an empty editor), as pinned Pi does for each handler set or not. The base also reaches the members `CustomEditor` inherits from `Editor` (`borderColor`, `disableSubmit`, `focused`, `getPaddingX`, `getAutocompleteMaxVisible`, `setAutocompleteProvider`, and the host's `onSubmit` and `onChange`); a component hears the working status the host shows in its border (`setWorkingStatusIndicator`) and a theme change (`invalidate`, which the Node runtime passes to its `CustomEditor` too).
- `make sdk-surface-drift` now guards the package half of Pi's extension surface. `test/parity/sdk-exports.toml` records, for every runtime export and public class member of `pi-coding-agent`, `pi-tui`, `pi-ai`, `pi-ai/compat` and `pi-agent-core`, what each of the Go, Rust and Python SDKs offers: a native symbol, a component-kit view, a context call, an identity (a type), a numbered divergence, or a `gap:<lane>` whose lane names an owner and the row that closes it. There is no free-text n/a, deferred or pending. A Pi export that appears without a row, a gap whose lane is gone or past its train limit, and a realized cell that no conformance fixture or Pi-generated vector test reaches all fail the check. `make sdk-exports-release` (run by `interface-mapping-strict`, so by `foundation-check`) also fails a gap on an export that five or more of the scanned npm Pi packages import (`test/parity/extension-import-usage.json`, refreshed by `automation/gen/sdk-import-usage.py`). The readable form is `docs/extension-sdk-exports.md`. <!-- fix: 82f59b27 -->
- The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs export Pi's pure pi-ai helpers: `StringEnum`, `getSupportedThinkingLevels`, `clampThinkingLevel`, `calculateCost`, `contentText`, `normalizeContext`, `getCurrentTools`, `getCurrentSystemMessage`, `getCurrentSystemPrompt` and the other transcript helpers, `isContextOverflow`, `isRecoverableLength`, `isRetryableAssistantError`, `retryDelayMs`, `getModelType`, `isModelType`, `hasApi`, `modelsAreEqual` and `envApiKeyAuth`, checked against Pi's own code at the pin. <!-- fix: 6ff1716c0f -->
- The SDKs export `getPackageDir`, `getShellConfig`, `generateDiffString`, `generateUnifiedPatch`, `convertToPng`, `resizeImage`, `copyToClipboard`, `sessionEntryToContextMessages` and `readStoredCredential`; the first six are served by the host (new host calls, and `PIG_EXT_HOST_INFO` carries the package directory and the default shell). <!-- fix: 6ff1716c0f -->
- `createAssistantMessageEventStream` exists in Rust and Python, and in all three SDKs the stream adds `durationMs` to its final message as Pi's does. <!-- fix: 6ff1716c0f -->
- `ai.AITelemetrySchema()` (Pi `AI_TELEMETRY_SCHEMA`, built on first call) and opt-in `pi.ai.request` / `pi.ai.poll` spans around `Models.Stream`, `StreamSimple` and `StreamDeferred` (and their completion helpers): `pi.ai.first_chunk` and one `pi.ai.block` event per completed text, thinking or tool-call block (never per delta, never content), final `stop`, `usage.*`, `cost.total` and `response.id` attributes, and duration, first-chunk, token and USD cost metrics through the parent span's `Metrics()`. A failed request reports status `provider_error` with at most 500 error-message characters; a cancelled one keeps status `ok` with `stop: "aborted"`. Without a parent span nothing is observed and the stream path allocates as before.
- PiG follows Pi 1.3.0+1402973a. The `telemetry` package has Pi's propagation, span links and metrics (Pi 48909d98): `TelemetryPropagation` (`Inject`, `Extract`) over an opaque `TelemetryCarrier`, `SpanOptions.Links` and `TelemetrySpan.AddLink`, `TelemetryMetrics` (counter, up-down counter, histogram) with `NoopTelemetryMetrics` and `NoopTelemetryPropagation`, typed metric schemas through `TelemetrySchemaDefinition.Metrics` and `CreateTypedMetrics`, and an in-memory recorder that records links, propagation and measurements. `telemetrytest` checks adapters for propagation and metrics as Pi's conformance kit does. `TelemetrySpan` gains `AddLink`, so a custom span type must implement it.
- `chord/context` has Pi's explicit `Context.telemetry` (Pi 48909d98). `Telemetry(ctx)` returns the context's telemetry parent and is never nil: `Background()`, `TODO()`, and any context that carries none return `telemetry.NoopTelemetryContext`. `WithTelemetry(parent, telemetry)` derives a context with a telemetry parent and leaves the parent, its cancellation, and its values unchanged. `TraceIn[T](ctx, options, fn)` runs a child span of `Telemetry(ctx)` and passes `fn` the derived context, whose telemetry is that span (`Telemetry(child)`, which `AddLink` and `AddEvent` use); Pi passes one context typed with a narrowed `telemetry` member, which Go cannot express. Concurrent `TraceIn` calls keep independent parents, and there is no ambient current span. These helpers do not instrument Chord hosts, methods, or network frames.
- `durable/harness.HarnessOptions.Telemetry` accepts a host-owned `telemetry.TelemetryContext` that may also implement `telemetry.TelemetryPropagation` and `telemetry.TelemetryMetrics`, found by type assertion where Pi marks them `Partial`. The option reserves the configuration boundary: Durable emits no spans or metrics yet and persists no carriers.
- `make module-size` fails a pull request when the root module zip exceeds the committed byte budget in `automation/release/module-size-budget.txt`, and proves that `go install` of `cmd/pig` works from that zip through a `file://` GOPROXY.
- `extension.SetNoopUITheme` registers the function the shared no-op UI context (`extension.NoopUIContext`) answers `Theme()` from, and the PiG binary registers the active theme, so an in-process extension running with no UI (print, JSON) reads the palette the TUI would, as Pi's no-op UI context does. Before it, `Theme()` answered nil. <!-- fix: 990667a7d9 -->

### Changed

- The Cloudflare Durable host's TypeScript tests type-check and run against Pi's chord, pi-ai and pi-durable from the build of the commit `.upstream/pin` names (the published release when the pin is a tag), not against the npm release of the locked version, so they follow Pi when the pin is a commit after a release. `make durable-core-host-deps` links the pinned build and `check.sh` refuses a tree that does not use it.
- The Cloudflare host's pi-durable facade has `Harness.wake()` and `Harness.retryTask()` as Pi's `Harness` does, and passes the telemetry span in a provider's stream options, between `signal` and `sessionId`, as Pi's generation does.
- CI: the required checks `CI result` and `Security result` now run on `merge_group`, so a GitHub merge queue entry reports them. `automation/ci/ci-tiers.txt` assigns every CI job to a light pull-request run or the full run, and `automation/ci/ci-plan.sh` builds the job matrices from it under unchanged check names. The light run keeps build and vet, `lint-changed`, the drift and contract gates, the sharded unit tests, one parity shard and the Windows and macOS smoke shards; the full run adds the PORT_MAP evidence shards, the other parity shard, SDK conformance and the race, integration and extension gates, `module-size`, the Node extensions and Cloudflare host jobs, and the remaining Windows and macOS shards. The repository variable `PIG_PR_CI_LIGHT` selects the mode: unset keeps every pull request on the full run; `queue` runs the light subset on a pull request and leaves the full run to the merge queue; `label` runs the light subset and makes `CI result` fail with "full run required: add the ci:full label" until the full run has run on the pull request's current head, started by the `ci:full` label, by marking the pull request ready for review, or by dispatching CI on its branch. `.github/AGENTS.md` holds the table with its Actions-minutes estimate. <!-- fix: e43252637e -->
- CI: the secret scan skips `gitleaks-action` on `merge_group`, which the action rejects, and runs TruffleHog over the commits the queue added; the change classifier reads a merge group's diff and fails when it cannot compute one, rather than reading an empty change as docs-only. <!-- fix: e43252637e -->
- `durable/env`: `ListFiles` is a method of `FileSystem` (and so of `ExecutionEnv`), as Pi's optional `listFiles?` is, and the separate `FileLister` interface is gone. An environment without a lister, such as `node.NodeExecutionEnv`, fails `ListFiles` with a `not_supported` FileError, where Pi leaves the method undefined.
- A session without a file (`--no-session`, RPC and print runs) writes the entries it compacted away to an unnamed temporary file instead of keeping their compressed records in memory. The file has no name while it is open and is gone when pig exits. Entries read back unchanged for `/tree`, branching and export. Live memory per exchange of a long session falls from about 1.5 KB to about 0.6 KB.
- A task phase that faults is reported through `OnReport` as `task_faulted`, not only recorded in its outcome. A reconcile pass with work to do, such as the recovery of `OpenHarness`, keeps `Wake` active until it lands.
- `harness.AgentHooks` returns each hook handler table with the name of its extension, `harness.ResolveAgent` and `harness.RenderSections` pass the extension or section to their report callback, and `harness.NewTaskGraphView` takes the scheduler's blocked-reason source.
- The core's ABI gains the `retryTask` api op, and its `report` notice carries an `info` member.
- A finished tool call whose arguments are a valid JSON object is parsed once instead of three times (strict check, repair, strict parse). A 200 KB `write` call gets from the end of its stream to its result about 3.5 ms sooner. Arguments that are not a valid object still go through Pi's repair and partial parse, with the same result and error text.
- Go SDK `chord/host`: `Method` takes its schemas variadically like Pi's `method()` overloads (`Method()`, `Method(args)`, `Method(nil, result)`, `Method(args, result)`), and `PlacementFor` and `PrincipalPattern` are exported for Pi's types of the same names. Existing two-argument calls compile unchanged.
- The wire carries two new optional fields on a host call: `timer` (`CallPayload.Timer`) and `tick` (`CallPayload.Tick`, set with `timer` by the Node runtime for `process.nextTick` callbacks and for timer callbacks that fire while a sibling tool runs). The Node runtime sets it on the calls of a `setTimeout`, `setInterval` or `setImmediate` callback that fires after its request's handler returned, and on the work such a callback starts. The Go, Rust and Python SDKs set it on a call made through a request's context after the request's handler returned. A new host notification `timer_defer` (host to Node runtime, `on` and `seq`) and its answer `timer_defer_ack` (`seq`) carry the command hold. The host and all four runtimes change together with no negotiation, so an SDK built from an older release does not set it and its calls run on arrival, as before. `subprocess.CallPayload` gains the exported fields `Timer` and `Tick`, so an unkeyed `CallPayload{...}` literal needs a fifth value.
- The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs' `resolveTranscriptTools` (`sdk.ResolveTranscriptTools`, `resolve_transcript_tools`) takes pi-ai's `TranscriptToolSupport` (`"none"`, `"additions"` or `"changes"`; Go has `sdk.TranscriptToolSupportNone`, `TranscriptToolSupportAdditions` and `TranscriptToolSupportChanges`, Rust `TranscriptToolSupport`) where it took a boolean, and reports `foldedSystemMessages` (Go `FoldedSystemMessages`, Rust `folded_system_messages`), the number of leading system messages whose tool changes the top-level list already holds, where it reported `anchorsAdditions`. A caller that passed `true` passes `"additions"`, and one that passed `false` passes `"none"`. Carried forward from Pi 1.4.0 (`packages/ai/src/utils/transcript.ts`).
- The coding agent sets its identity from the install-telemetry setting instead of adding attribution headers to every provider request. Direct pi-ai calls from the extension bridge carry no attribution, as under Pi. Attribution reads `PiG` for OpenRouter, NVIDIA and OpenCode alike (the OpenCode client header was `pig`).
- Classifier, llama.cpp, OpenRouter image and pi-messages requests send the identity's `User-Agent`, and Bedrock appends the identity to the AWS SDK user agent.
- A `User-Agent` request header replaces the Claude Code identity of an Anthropic OAuth request, and a `null` request header removes a model header of the OpenRouter image API whatever its letter case, as under Pi. A durable Harness wraps a plain `Models` once, so tools and hooks share one view of it.
- The upstream Pi pin moves from 1.3.0+1402973a to 1.3.0+0ed1b043. The model catalog follows it, including `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash` on `minimax` and `minimax-cn`, and the vendored Node extension runtime carries Pi's `@earendil-works/pi-telemetry` modules that `pi-ai` now imports. Pi's Durable, AI and Chord telemetry commits (2ec55d80, d9025a5f) are deferred to a later milestone: PiG still emits no telemetry spans.
- Durable document definitions require a checkpoint policy, as Pi 5c1736f8 requires `checkpointWhen` on `defineDoc()` and `defineDocFamily()`: `durable.DefineDoc` and `DefineDocFamily` panic when a definition has neither `CheckpointWhen` nor `CheckpointWhenRaw`. Use a function that returns false to keep every change a delta. The nested-call, code mode run and code mode journal documents never checkpoint; the code mode store document writes a base after 64 deltas. The Cloudflare host facade requires `checkpointWhen` too.
- Stored submission records gain an optional `trace` member, and stored task records optional `trace` and `links` members, written only when the telemetry adapter supports propagation. Records written before this change decode without them and no storage migration is needed; a record with the members is read by an earlier PiG as a record with unknown members.
- `session.Hooks.Report` and `SessionImpl.Fail` take the context whose telemetry span the report belongs to, and `harness.AdmitSubmission` takes the admission span and returns an `Admission` (the submission ID and how it was admitted).
- Another model's thinking is dropped from the history sent to a model after a model switch, instead of being sent as plain assistant text. The new model no longer sees the old model's reasoning as part of its replies; text and tool calls are kept. Same-model thinking is unchanged (Pi #11000, #10995). This includes a target that requires `reasoning_content`, which now gets the empty placeholder for such a turn (D126 is retired).
- The `kimi-coding` provider uses OpenAI Chat Completions at `https://api.kimi.com/coding/v1` again (Pi a3f265b1 re-lands b06c1f2c, which 6a1f799b had reverted). `models.json` overrides of the `kimi-coding` base URL must include `/v1`.
- Cloudflare Durable Object SQLite adapters made for the same storage object share one operation queue, so several storages can use one Durable Object database. A transaction holds the queue, a statement runs at once while nothing is queued, and `Get` reads only the first row of the cursor (`DurableObjectSqlCursor.Next`).
- The `kimi-coding` provider uses Anthropic Messages at `https://api.kimi.com/coding` again. Pi reverts its switch to Chat Completions because the model catalog reaches released clients whose provider cannot use it.
- The default GitHub Copilot model is `gpt-6.1-sol`, which replaces `gpt-5.4`, retired by Copilot. Carried forward from Pi e5fe9777.
- Upgrading: the SDK type of `ctx.sessionManager` is now Pi's `ReadonlySessionManager`: Go `sdk.ReadonlySessionManager` (was `sdk.SessionManager`), Rust `ReadonlySessionManager<'a>` (was `SessionManager<'a>`), Python `ReadonlySessionManager` (was `SessionManager`). `SessionManager` is now Pi's class, which an extension constructs. Rename the type where you name it; calls through `ctx.SessionManager()` are unchanged. The `SetupSessionManager.SessionManager` field of the Go SDK is removed with the old type; the upgrade rule `context-session-manager-readonly` names both changes.
- The Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs can construct Pi's `ModelRuntime` (`ModelRuntime.create`, catalog and auth reads, runtime keys, `registerProvider` without functions, virtual models, `refresh`, availability, `stream`/`complete`, `classify`, `generateImages`, `login`, `logout`) and `SettingsManager.fromStorage` (a storage object of the extension, called back on every read and write). Provider objects (`getProviders`, `getProvider`, `registerNativeProvider`) and the deferred calls stay out of the runtime until the owner decides (D78).
- Upgrading: `SessionManager.list` and `listAll` take an `onProgress` argument in the Go (`SessionManagerList(c, cwd, dir, onProgress)`, `SessionManagerListAll(c, dir, onProgress)`; pass nil), Rust (`list(ctx, cwd, dir, on_progress)`, `list_all(ctx, dir, on_progress)`; pass `None`) and Python (`on_progress=None`, optional) SDKs.
- `make divergence-quality` now fails a `docs/extension-api-parity.md` row that calls an extension capability Node-only (`Node only`, `Node runtime only`, `Go/Rust/Python have no ...`); the row says which SDK cells are `gap:<lane>` in `docs/extension-sdk-exports.md` instead. `test/parity/sdk-vectors/` and `automation/gen/generate-sdk-vectors.mjs` fix one format for the Pi-generated vectors the SDK helper ports are tested against, and `go run ./test/parity/cmd/sdksurface -check` validates the files (pin, case source, ledger export).
- The SDK surface scan no longer loses every Rust declaration after a raw string literal such as `r"\\?\"`; `with_file_mutation_queue` was reported missing from the Rust SDK.
- The SDK export ledger now sees each SDK's public subpackages (`extensions/sdk/tui`, `sdk-rs/src/tui`, Python `pig_sdk.tui`): their symbols are named qualified, `native:tui.VisibleWidth`, `native:tui::visible_width`, `native:tui.visible_width`. Rust counts only plain `pub` items that `mod.rs` re-exports.
- The export ledger's reach check counts a generic member name (`set`, `get`, `abort`) called through its class (`Store.set(...)`, `Store::set(...)`), or on a variable when the corpus names the class, so a fixture that exercises `ProjectTrustStore.set` reaches it.
- The wire's host call gains an optional `origin_tool_call_id` (`CallPayload.OriginToolCallID`), which the Node runtime sets with `tick` on the call of a callback that a tool call's handler queued; the host runs the call behind that call's `tool_execution_end`, or at once if the event already passed. The host and the Node runtime change together with no negotiation. The Go, Rust and Python SDKs never set `tick`, so they send none.
- A long session on the Anthropic Messages and OpenAI Completions APIs spends less CPU building each request. The request re-sends the whole conversation, and every message in it was encoded again on every turn; a message is now encoded once and its bytes are written where it goes, found by what it holds (so an edited, replaced or re-converted message is encoded again), and the request body is the same. In an RPC session of one tool call and reply per turn (linux/arm64), the median turn CPU is 3.33 ms at 200 turns against 3.93 ms before (A/A 4.03 ms) and 3.27 ms at 1000 turns against 4.18 ms (A/A 4.17 ms); at 20 turns it is unchanged (2.44 ms against 2.47 ms, inside the run-to-run spread). Peak memory at 1000 turns is 83.1 MiB against 81.2 MiB.
- A request to a model without image input no longer copies every text-only user message and tool result of the history: the conversion keeps them as they are, as it already did for models with image input. Request bodies are unchanged.
- The `pig` binary is 3.7 MB smaller than before (linux/amd64: 70,348,960 to 66,601,120 bytes, the same toolchain and flags; linux/arm64 65,929,376 to 62,193,824, darwin/arm64 67,663,426 to 63,880,962, windows/amd64 72,019,456 to 68,270,592). The embedded Node runtime no longer carries source maps, TypeScript declaration files, the shim vendoring manifest, the documentation screenshots or the Node interactive mode's `clankolas.png` (nothing at run time reads them), which takes its archive from 7,696,169 to 4,581,071 bytes. Extensions, Pi's documentation and examples, and every run-time path are unchanged. <!-- fix: 1177f5f1 -->
- The Go and Rust extension SDK bundles embedded in `pig` no longer include their own test files (the Rust bundle's `src/*tests.rs` modules, which are `#[cfg(test)]`, and the Go `_test.go` files), 630,784 bytes less in the binary. The bundles an extension builds against are otherwise the same. <!-- fix: ade09fc2 -->
- Each turn costs less CPU in a session of a few hundred turns: a goroutine's ID is read from the runtime's goroutine record instead of formatting its stack (the stream and lock checks asked on every request and event), the goroutines a turn starts grow their stack in one step, and a request build no longer deep-copies every tool schema several times. Messages, events, session files and request bodies are unchanged.
- RPC and JSON mode write each event with less CPU and fewer goroutines. The output tail keeps one writer goroutine instead of starting one for every burst of records (200 turns of the arena RPC workload: 8,251 goroutines created before, 2,642 after), and the hot records (`message_start`, `message_update`, `message_end`, `turn_*`, `agent_*`, `tool_execution_*`) are encoded straight into the output line without the reflection walk: encoding a `message_end` takes 3.0 µs and 33 allocations instead of 18.1 µs and 81, a `turn_end` 3.4 µs instead of 21.6 µs. Per-turn CPU of `--mode rpc` in the arena harness (linux/arm64, interleaved runs against the release before this change, with its GC target): 2.01 to 1.59 ms at 20 turns (median of 7, A/A medians 0.02 ms apart) and 3.93 to 3.58 ms at 200 turns (median of 14, A/A medians 0.04 ms apart), with the same peak RSS. The bytes of every record, their order and the output-failure and termination behaviour are unchanged.
- Each turn of a long session costs less CPU: the refresh before every request no longer copies every earlier message of the session (a tenth of CPU at 1000 turns), session entry IDs take their random bytes from a pool instead of one system call per ID, and the kernel release in the `User-Agent` header is read once per process. Messages, events, session files and request bodies are unchanged.
- A turn on the Anthropic Messages API spends less CPU building its request. The request was copied and walked by reflection on every turn, looking for large strings to cache, even in a history that holds none, before it was encoded; the encoder now takes those strings itself without the walk, and the body and the `anthropic-beta` header are the same. In an RPC session of one tool call and reply per turn (linux/arm64), the median turn CPU is 4.39 ms at 200 turns, against 4.73 ms before (20 turns: 2.12 ms against 2.21 ms; 1000 turns: 5.32 ms against 5.46 ms, within run-to-run spread).
- `--mode rpc` sessions collect garbage less often when `GOGC` is not set in the environment, as `-p` and `--mode json` runs already do: the Go GC target is 400 until a collection finds more than 64 MiB live, then the default 100. Measured on a mock-provider session with one tool call per turn (Linux arm64, 4 CPUs): CPU per turn fell from 5.7 to 4.7 ms at 200 turns and from 6.7 to 5.4 ms at 1,000 turns (the run-to-run spread of the same binary is about 0.3 ms), and 32 concurrent sessions of 50 turns fell from about 4.7 to 3.3 ms per turn. Peak memory rose from 52 to 74 MiB at 200 turns, from 57 to 81 MiB at 1,000 turns and from about 38 to 55 MiB per session in the 32-session run. A `GOGC` you set is used as is, interactive sessions are unchanged, and output is unchanged.
- The TinyGo Durable core does less allocation work per turn: it builds no hash map for each record write or for each commit that writes no document, and compares member names by length first. A warm turn executes about 4-5% fewer Wasm instructions and makes about 2% fewer allocations. Behavior is unchanged.
- An idle interactive session holds less memory: when it goes idle after a run, it lets go of the encodings of the conversation that the last requests reused (about 250 KB less live heap at the prompt of a six-prompt session). The next prompt encodes its request as the first one did. Requests are unchanged.
- An interactive `pig` session holds about 3 MB less memory once it has started. The first frame and the start-up loads allocate several times what they keep, and Go's background scavenger returns the rest slowly; the task that registers the remaining syntax grammars now hands that free heap back to the operating system once, after it has queued the repaint, so it never runs on the input loop. On linux/amd64 with a fresh home, the median resident (and proportional) set at the first frame, one second later and after ten idle seconds is 46.9 MB (46.4-47.1, N=16) against 50.1 MB (50.0-50.5, N=16) before; the median time to the first full frame is 24.9 ms (21.4-33.1, N=60) against 24.9 ms (21.2-33.5, N=60), and the median time from a keystroke to its echoed frame is 0.93 ms (0.04-6.21, N=1,580) against 0.91 ms (0.04-4.49, N=1,580). <!-- fix: bdff0a71 -->
- A process no longer starts 16 extra goroutines the first time it identifies a goroutine: the check that reads goroutine IDs from the runtime proves its offset on the goroutines that already ask for an ID. Output is unchanged.
- `--mode rpc` runs on one Go scheduler thread (GOMAXPROCS 1) when the environment does not set `GOMAXPROCS`, instead of two: an rpc turn costs 11-16% less CPU. Parallel tool calls and session loading still run at the full CPU width, and `GOMAXPROCS` still overrides the default. Output is unchanged.
- An interactive `pig` session starts with less memory: the 191 syntax grammars are no longer all decoded and registered at start. Each grammar outside the eager set is decoded the first time code in that language is highlighted, and automatic language detection decodes the rest the first time it runs; highlighted output is the same for every sample of the corpus (checked against the full eager load, including automatic detection over all 650 samples). The grammar load at start allocates 385 times and 2.3 MB instead of 1,135 times and 7.8 MB, and the first frame allocates 5.5 MB instead of 11.1 MB. On linux/amd64 with a fresh home, the median resident (and proportional) set after ten idle seconds is 46.5 MB (46.2-46.7, N=16) against 50.1 MB (50.0-50.5, N=16) before, and the median time to the first full frame is 24.6 ms (20.4-30.1, N=60) against 24.9 ms (21.2-33.5, N=60). The first highlight of a language outside the eager set decodes once (576 allocations, 3.1 MB) and the first automatic detection decodes the rest (1,857 allocations, 6.5 MB), both off the start-up path. <!-- fix: d6f31615 -->
- The Durable harness orders the commits of a job that nothing awaits by goroutine scheduling, where Pi orders them by microtask hops (D181): the job's final state and every settled read are the same, commit sequence numbers can differ, and a read taken the moment an answer settles can still show the job running; await the job or `WaitForIdle` before reading its state.
- A TypeScript extension's session starts with a smaller heap. The extension host's model catalog no longer decodes every built-in model's record into ordered members to learn that the model is unchanged: encoding the 1,609 built-in models allocates 13.1 MB instead of 41.1 MB and keeps 1.2 MB instead of 4.0 MB, and the process holds about 10 MB less at ready (PSS of PiG plus Node with one TypeScript extension, linux/arm64: 173.6 to 163.5 MB with a warm cache and 204.6 to 194.8 MB with a fresh one, against 142.0 MB for Pi with the same extension). The model objects an extension reads are unchanged.
- The Cloudflare Durable host's facade, types and tests follow Pi 1.3.0, as its core already did: tools return `{ output }`, and the facade pins Pi 1.3.0 packages. As in Pi 1.3.0, a tool that still returns the earlier `{ content }` shape produces an empty tool result.
- PiG shows images in the Rex terminal (`TERM_PROGRAM=rex`) through the Kitty graphics protocol. Rex implements the protocol only in part (no Unicode placeholders), but enough for Pi's image output; hyperlinks and true color were already on (Pi 396ecd7b). <!-- fix: fd3dbad86a -->
- `PI_CODEMODE_MAX_WORKERS` sets how many codemode workers may be alive at once across all sessions in the process (default 2). Each worker's script may use up to 256 MB, so raise it when one process runs several sessions (Pi 885a564b, #11184). <!-- fix: e73e536f2c -->
- The bug fix ledger (`docs/bug-ledger.json` and `docs/site/docs/bug-ledger.md`) is listed by entry ID instead of by date and no longer carries a total, and `make bug-ledger` changes only the entries a branch adds or removes, so two pull requests that each add a fix merge without a conflict and a merge of `main` needs no regeneration. `make bug-ledger-refresh` recomputes every entry from the history, as a release does, and `go run ./automation/ci/bugledger -summary` prints the counts the page used to show.

### Removed

- The Cloudflare `User-Agent: pig-coding-agent` attribution, which Pi removed, and `ai.PiUserAgent`, which `ai.FormatUserAgent` replaces.
- Divergence D126 (another model's thinking replayed as `reasoning_content` on targets that require it) is retired: PiG follows Pi 1.3.0 and drops the thinking for every target.
- Divergence D171 (an errored Anthropic tool result carries no images) is retired: PiG follows Pi's converter.
- `agent.SettleGate`, `agent.SettleSlot` and `agent.SettleGate.SetParkedHook` are removed from `github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/agent`. They ordered the continuations of waiters that settle back to back (a parallel tool batch's `tool_execution_end` events, the subprocess host's dialog replies) and moved to the internal package `internal/jsloop` before any release carried them, so there is no public replacement. `agent.ToolSettleFromContext` stays exported and its call pattern is unchanged: it returns the slot of one call of a parallel batch, now of the internal type, so an embedder can call `Settle`, `Run` and `Close` on the result but can no longer name the type.
- `extension.CallLane` is removed from `github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/coding/extension`. It kept the calls of one destination in call order for `ToolDefinition.ReserveCallOrder`; the host now holds that order in an internal package, so there is no public replacement. `extension.CallOrder`, `extension.WithCallOrder` and `ToolDefinition.ReserveCallOrder` are unchanged: an embedder that reserved places with its own `CallOrder` values keeps working. `coding/mcpext.McpToolOptions.Lane` now has the internal lane type, so an embedder can no longer set it; leave it nil, which leaves the calls to that server unordered.

### Fixed

- Durable: opening a store that pi-durable 1.3.0 wrote no longer leaves you unable to go back to it. PiG's Durable writes schema version 2, which no Pi release reads yet, and it upgraded the store in place. `driver.OpenHarness` and `node.OpenNodeSqliteStorage` now copy a store at an older schema version to `<path>.schema-1.bak` before upgrading it; pi-durable 1.3.0 opens that copy. An earlier copy is never replaced, and if the copy cannot be written the open fails and the store is left as it was. The copy is one extra file the size of the store, written once per older store (D188, #245; the commit that made the change calls it D186, its number before the renumber). <!-- fix: e55895a96d, c6cb4f6c37 -->
- A child session's `message_update` event carries the message so far and the assistant event's `partial`, as Pi's `AgentSession.subscribe` hands them to a listener, where RPC mode's trimmed shape left both out. <!-- fix: 304bd443 -->
- A streamed child-session text costs wire bytes linear in its length: `message_update` events travel as a keyframe followed by operations (`set` and `append`) and the SDK hands the listener the full event. A 4096-delta, 98 KB reply cost 406 MB on the connection and 826 MB of host allocation per prompt; it now costs 1.8 MB and 19 MB. <!-- fix: 304bd443 -->
- An extension that a child agent session's resource loader loads now receives the session's events, as in Pi, where `createAgentSession` over a `DefaultResourceLoader` that loads an extension gives it `session_start` (after `bindExtensions`), `input`, `before_agent_start`, `agent_start`, `turn_*`, `message_*`, `context` and `agent_end`. In the Go, Python and Rust SDKs (through the `resources` option or a `resourceLoader` handle) the extension registered its tools and then received nothing: the loader started its extensions under the context of the call that reloaded it, which ends when `createAgentSession` returns, so the host stopped every extension it had just loaded and reported no error. The loader now owns its extensions until its next load or `Close`; the context of a reload still ends the reload itself. <!-- fix: 5f03fa4a4e, d8da9b372a, 78c1d22259 -->
- The contexts of those extensions read the child session: `ctx.model`, `ctx.scopedModels` (the session's `scopedModels`, which the child wire accepted and no extension saw), `ctx.isIdle()`, `ctx.hasUI` (false) and the headless dialogs (`notify`, `select`, `input` and `editor` answer nothing, `confirm` answers false), as the context of a Pi child session has. The loader's extension host has a headless UI bridge for this, and a child session binds the state print mode binds to it. <!-- fix: 5f03fa4a4e, d8da9b372a -->
- `coding.CreateAgentSession` binds the Session it returns to the Extension Host its resource loader started, as it does for a child session an extension creates: the loader's extensions read the Session's model, scopedModels, idle state, tools and session, with no UI. The helpers that bind a Session to subprocess extensions in every mode (`ExtensionHostSessionActions`, `BindSessionExtensionActions`, `BindSessionReadActions`, `BindSessionModelOperations`, `WatchSessionRunSignal`) moved from the CLI package to `coding`, so print, JSON, RPC and interactive mode and a library caller run the same code. <!-- fix: 5ac5e1ad05 -->
- CI: the `Linux / portmap` job (`make portmap-check`) no longer runs out its 60-minute limit. `portmapcheck` builds each evidence package's test binary once instead of once per PORT_MAP row, runs rows that name the same tests of a package once, and keeps a marker row's killed-mutant verdict in its cached record. CI runs the check as three shards (`make portmap-check-N`, `portmapcheck -shard N/3`); every shard computes the same plan and judges its own rows, and the Linux verification job still requires all of them. Each row's requirement is unchanged: its named tests pass, they execute its mapped cited files, and a marker row's mutant makes them fail. <!-- fix: 3ee123c6d5 -->
- Extension tools of one parallel batch reach the host in Pi's order. Pi runs a batch on one thread, so the host calls the tools make in their first tick (`appendEntry`, `ui.notify`, `ui.setStatus`, `setActiveTools`, `sendMessage`, and the rest) apply in the order the extension sent them, whichever tool made each; before, eight parallel tools that each called the host applied their calls in scheduler order, and in a Node extension that also reordered the tools' answers, so `tool_execution_end` and the tool_result hooks came in a different order on every run. <!-- fix: f172d67319 -->
- The `tool_result` handlers of a parallel batch start in the order the tools settled, each running to its first await before the next one starts, and a tool's `onUpdate` reaches the listeners in the order the tools called it, across tools; before, both followed goroutine wake order. <!-- fix: f172d67319 -->
- The Go, Python and Rust SDKs start the handlers of concurrent event and command requests in the order the requests arrive, as they already did for `tool_call`; before, two `tool_result` hooks in flight on one connection could start their handlers out of order (the Go SDK did so in 25 of 3000 probes). <!-- fix: 1886fff37b -->
- An edit or write call of a parallel batch registers in the file mutation queue after the extension tools before it in the batch took the queue, as Pi does: Pi registers a call in the queue when it calls `withFileMutationQueue` and runs each call's first statements in call order. Before, the built-in call registered in the dispatcher's loop, a process hop before the extension's registration arrived, so `[extension tool, write]` applied the write first. The Go, Python, Rust and Node SDKs, isolated, packed and fused, now agree. A native tool that waits on something other than a host call before it takes the queue still registers first, and a write behind a native tool that makes no host call waits for the tool; both are recorded in the parity row. <!-- fix: 536efd5e91 -->
- The Node runtime reports a tool whose execute awaits something that is not a host call, and the host closes the tool's start window after the calls the extension sent before the report, not after the call its handler sends next. <!-- fix: d9ec9560dd -->
- A Node extension tool that awaits the event loop before it takes the file mutation queue registers after the edit or write call behind it, as in Pi; before, the two raced and the extension won every round. The runtime holds the tool's next `fileMutation.enter` until the host reports the writes behind the tool registered. <!-- fix: ee65aead9a -->
- `pig --list-models` now prints only the models the model runtime reports as available, as Pi does. It used to append every bundled model of each provider that had a login or API key, so after `pig update --models` it still listed OpenRouter models that the saved personalized catalog had dropped, such as `openrouter/auto` and the `:batch` variants. <!-- fix: 0b8370a677589e0e2c17b2025c37ab17e718fdce -->
- Fixed CI on `main` after the 0.5.1 ledger trim: the reviewed-pin ledgers (`upstream-v0.84.0.json`, `cli-v0.84.0.json`, `behavior-inputs-v0.84.0.json`, `behavior-input-mapping-v0.84.0.json`) are back, since `interface-delta` reads them as the base of the current delta; the stale testprune ledger entries and bug ledger entries orphaned by the 0.5.0 squash merge are gone. <!-- fix: 0e3411c597 -->
- `/reload` no longer reports `Extension "builtin:codemode" error: This extension ctx is stale after session replacement or reload.` A tool registered while an in-process extension factory runs refreshed the live Session, which ran the `prepareLoadout` hook of the previous load's tool through the API the reload had just retired. Like Pi's loading runtime, whose `refreshTools` is a no-op until the runner binds, a registration during factory load now leaves the Session alone and the reload's own rebuild declares the tools once. <!-- fix: 39e04d0839 -->
- A wake the scheduler took at a change is still delivered to `OnWake` when the Session fails and seals before the goroutine publishing it runs, as Pi's microtask ran first; a failing recovery pass no longer loses the host's active wake.
- `env/daemon`: `listFiles` with `timeoutMs` 0 truncates at once on Windows too; the deadline check no longer misses on the platform's coarse clock.
- `go install github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/cmd/pig@latest` works again. The 0.5.0 Go module was larger than the Go module size limit because it carried parity ledgers for every earlier Pi pin; only the current pin's ledgers ship now, and v0.5.0 is retracted. <!-- fix: b341a82e9d -->
- Prompts submitted after `/scoped-models` refreshed the model catalogs and Ctrl+P switched the model no longer fail with "No model selected". The scope update installed catalog descriptors that carry no backend; it now installs the Session's runtime-bound models, as Pi does. <!-- fix: d81ace2962 -->
- A notification, status or message that an extension queues on `setImmediate` or `setTimeout(0)` from inside a tool now reaches an RPC client after `agent_settled`, as Pi's loop does, instead of before `tool_execution_end`. Pi runs those callbacks when its event loop turns, which a run's chain of microtasks never gives it. The Node runtime ran them as soon as the tool answered, because it is another process, and its host calls arrived mid-run. The host now holds such calls while the RPC run chains microtasks and runs them in arrival order at the next real wait: a model response's I/O, a built-in tool's run, a dialog, a compaction or an auto-retry delay, or the end of `agent_settled`. A `process.nextTick` callback's notification follows the `tool_execution_end` of the tool that queued it, as in Pi, and the callbacks of a tool that returns while a sibling tool of its batch still runs reach the client right after its `tool_execution_end`. <!-- fix: dca5763ac5, 38901751db -->
- A `setTimeout(0)` or `setImmediate` callback that an extension queues while an RPC stdin command runs (a `model_select` handler during `set_model`, say) now reaches the client after the command's response, as in Pi, instead of before it in about one run in ten. Pi runs the command's microtasks and writes its response before its loop runs a timer, so the Node runtime now holds its timer callbacks from the moment the host reads the command until the host has written the response, and the host waits for the runtime's acknowledgement of that hold before it sends the command's first event. A handler that is still awaiting real I/O (a child process, a dialog) is not held back, because Pi's loop turns while it waits and runs the timer. A call that a Go, Rust or Python extension makes through a context whose request returned runs on arrival while another request of the extension is in flight, instead of waiting for a loop turn that waits for that request: a handler that kept an earlier request's context and awaited a call through it used to hang the run. <!-- fix: d6aaf72307, 7a4ddb3733, 8506081bd3, 463879414a, 58563ba284, 5a182154fc -->
- Two parallel `edit` calls through two names of one existing file (a hard link, or a case alias on a case-insensitive filesystem such as the macOS default) no longer lose one of the edits. The built-in file mutation queue made them run at once, so both read the original text and the later write dropped the earlier edit while both calls reported success; it now orders them in call order. Pi does not order hard-link aliases (D190). <!-- fix: b1cea06458 -->
- The Cloudflare host's pi-durable facade sends Pi v1.4.0's client `identity` in every model request's options (last key, after `sessionId`), merged over `DEFAULT_HARNESS_IDENTITY` (provider attribution to pi) with `HarnessOptions.identity` and a request's own `identity`, as Pi's `Harness` does through `withClientIdentity` (Pi `adc5fa9f`, `04a93bad`). The facade exports `DEFAULT_HARNESS_IDENTITY`. <!-- fix: 4ff2bdc6de --> <!-- fix: d8e70b5b2a -->
- The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs' `isContextOverflow` treats an HTTP 413 error as a context overflow whatever the provider or the body says: a failed message that begins with `413` (then a space, a colon or the end) or with `<provider> API error (413):`. Carried forward from Pi 1.4.0 (`packages/ai/src/utils/overflow.ts`). <!-- fix: d7df8b0c4d -->
- The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs' `isRetryableAssistantError` retries on a status code (429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 520, 524) only when it stands alone, not inside a request id or another number, retries on `stream disconnected before completion`, and does not retry a ChatGPT Business workspace usage-limit error (`subscription_sharing_workplace_individual_usage_limit_exceeded`). Carried forward from Pi 6e491cfa, 29057ece and cedefc53. <!-- fix: d7df8b0c4d -->
- CI: `TestNodeCellAddedMembersStayWithinTheirOwnState` no longer fails under load. It compared the RSS of a one-member Node cell with the RSS of a five-member cell against 6 MiB per added member; one member's RSS read 131.8 to 152.2 MiB (6.4 to 27.5 MiB below its own peak) in 19 runs, so the figure ranged 0.8 to 7.2 MiB and failed once. The probe now reads the retained V8 heap plus external memory right after the forced collection, which gives 0.20 to 0.25 MiB per added member in every run, and the budget is 2 MiB. <!-- fix: 19db55583c -->
- CI: a Node extension no longer holds a request the host sends it behind the highlight.js language load. In TUI mode the runtime loads Pi's complete grammar set after the startup events, and `lib/index.js` evaluates its ~190 grammars in one synchronous pass on the thread that serves requests: 125 ms on an idle machine and more than the five-second renderer inactivity lease (D56) on a starved one, so a Markdown transform, render or mouse event sent meanwhile was abandoned while the extension was healthy and the proxy returned its input (`got ""` in `TestMarkdownContextsAcrossSDKs/subprocess-node-packed`). Each grammar module is now required on its own event-loop turn before that pass, which only registers them as in Pi. <!-- fix: 5382ad9af0 -->
- CI: `waitForRenderProxy`, the wait of the render proxy and packed padding tests for the extension's answer, is bounded by the hang budget `testbudget.Wait` instead of a fixed one second; `TestPackedMessageOutputPadAcrossSDKs/go` failed once with "render proxy condition was not met" on a loaded host (not reproduced since: 0 of 108 runs under 150 busy loops, before and after). <!-- fix: 771c29cfd2 -->
- CI: `TestStartupReusesPreTrustExtensionsAcrossEntrypoints` no longer reads an extension process that is still dying as a leak. The RPC entrypoint SIGKILLs its extension processes and exits without waiting, and the test polled `kill(pid, 0)` for five seconds, which also succeeds while the killed process waits for a CPU turn and while it is an unreaped zombie. The test now waits for the process's exit event (pidfd, kqueue `NOTE_EXIT`, process handle). <!-- fix: 8343791091 -->
- CI: the scoped-model selector parity scenarios no longer capture the selector before the catalog refresh settles. Both binaries show "Refreshing model catalogs…" until `refreshModelCatalogs` settles and "Model catalogs refreshed." after it. `13-scoped-models-fuzzy-filter` finished at "> mtw", and `08-scoped-models-selector` and `13-scoped-google-catalog` waited a fixed two seconds, so under load a capture could hold either binary's refreshing line (6 of 25 runs of the first and 21 of 50 of the other two failed on `main` with 250 busy loops); each step now waits for the status on the visible screen. <!-- fix: 895c0830 -->
- CI: a Node extension no longer holds the first request the host sends it behind its bytecode-cache flush. The runtime flushed Node's compile cache when it processed the host's ready message, on the thread that serves requests; the flush serializes every compiled module and took 4.6 s on a starved machine, so a Markdown transform sent right after registration passed the five-second renderer inactivity lease (D56) while the extension was healthy and the proxy returned its input (`got ""` in `TestMarkdownContextsAcrossSDKs/subprocess-node-packed`). The flush now runs when the factory has loaded, before the member registers. <!-- fix: 94f2094e51 -->
- CI: `TestTerminalProgramGetsDefaultSignalsWhenTheDaemonStartedIgnoringThem` no longer fails every run under `make test-fast`. It required every ignored-signal bit of the terminal's program to be clear, but Pi's `pty.rs` (and PiG's daemon, which mirrors it) resets signals 1 through 31 only, and GNU make 4.4.1 starts its recipes with signals 32 and 33 ignored, so the program ended with `SigIgn` 0x80000000 under both daemons. The test asserts signals 1 through 31, the daemon's contract. <!-- fix: abb8c1dc6d -->
- A package manifest glob walks as Node 24.22 and later walk it. Pi runs on that Node, and its `globSync` no longer ends a directory listing at an entry the walk already holds, nor queues the segment after a globstar below a symlink: `a/**/../*.md` lists the root's `.md` files as well, `Sub/**/../*` lists the siblings of `Sub`, and `**/x/**/y` lists every `x/**/y` below the root. <!-- fix: 6c01b755f4 -->
- CI: `make interface-gaps` passes on main again. The interface mapping ledger had not been regenerated for the chord fork replay test that landed with the previous train, so `autobind -check` reported that the ledger is not what the detector derives; `make interface-gaps-update` rewrites the two stale rows. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- CI: the Windows test jobs no longer fail the `pig extension upgrade` notice tests when the temporary directory holds a `~`, as a runner's 8.3 short name (`RUNNER~1`) does. The notice already quotes such a path for the Windows shell (`"C:\Users\RUNNER~1\..."`) and is unchanged; the tests expected the raw path and now expect the quoted word, as the notice test beside them does. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- `pig extension upgrade`: the command a startup notice or the upgrade offer prints now quotes a POSIX path that holds a backslash. Before, the backslash printed bare and the shell read it as an escape, so the command named a different path. A backslash stays plain in a Windows path, where it separates elements. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- An HTTP 413 from any provider is treated as request-size overflow, so the agent compacts and retries instead of showing a raw API error. GitHub Copilot answers a request over its byte-size limit (for example a history of many images) with a 413 and a generic `failed to parse request` body that the token-count patterns never matched (Pi 73f8de80, #11016). <!-- fix: 333229c85a -->
- On Windows, PiG finds PowerShell in its installation directories when it is not on `PATH`: PowerShell 7 under `%ProgramFiles%\PowerShell\7` after `PATH`, and Windows PowerShell under `%SystemRoot%\System32\WindowsPowerShell\v1.0` as the last fallback, on any drive (Pi 61060ddf, #9490). <!-- fix: 4fd80d1cb2 -->
