- On Windows, the legacy WSL `bash.exe` launcher is recognized under the actual Windows directory in `SystemRoot` (`System32` and `Sysnative`), not only under `C:\Windows`, so its command goes over stdin (Pi 61060ddf, #9490). <!-- fix: 4fd80d1cb2 -->
- On Windows, an empty `SystemRoot` takes `C:\Windows` for `taskkill.exe`, as an unset one already did, in the shell tools and the Durable node environment (Pi 61060ddf, #9490). <!-- fix: 4fd80d1cb2 -->
- On Windows, shells that are runnable Microsoft Store aliases are accepted: a configured shell path, `bash.exe` and `pwsh.exe` found by `where`, and the Git Bash locations are checked with an attribute check (`access(F_OK)`) instead of a stat that follows the alias (Pi 28bea006, nodejs/node#36790). Not yet run on a Windows host. <!-- fix: 4fd80d1cb2 -->
- `done()` of an overlay `ui.custom` component hides that component's own overlay instead of the topmost one. With a shell overlay open under a dialog overlay from another extension, the shell's exit no longer removes the dialog or leaves the finished shell on screen (Pi #10999, #10648).
- A call through a remote Chord reference with a `ctx` from another host, or one not made by Chord, fails with `invalid/foreign_ctx` before anything crosses the link, like a call through a local reference.
- Turning thinking off on Claude Haiku 5.5 and Opus 5 sends plain `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` without effort markers, which Anthropic rejects while thinking is disabled.
- The retry classifier treats "The service is temporarily unavailable" and Bedrock's "Service unavailable: ..." as retryable.
- A `Progress` pause longer than Node's timer maximum, or not a number, runs after 1 ms as a Node timer does.
- Anthropic and Claude on Bedrock no longer lose the images of an error tool result: Pi sends them after the tool results of the turn, under a caption naming the tool call, instead of replacing them with a note ([#10794](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10794)). Other Bedrock models keep the images in the result.
- URL parsing follows Node 24.22 and later. Pi runs on that Node, whose URL parser only lowercases an ASCII host: `https://xn--.example` and `ftp:xn--mailto:` are valid, `file://xn--a.b/x` is a UNC path on Windows, and a provider or Radius base such as `file:///C:ab/c` or `file:///u:p@[::1]:9000` no longer keeps its first path segment as a drive letter when a path is resolved against it. <!-- fix: 4636a28415 -->
- A packed Go, Python or Rust cell no longer hangs when a later member's factory calls the host before an earlier member's factory has run. The generated runner started every member on a thread of its own and let them race for the factory turn; the host accepts the members in order and serves a connection only after the members before it, so a later member whose factory subscribed to the event bus held the turn and waited for a host that waited for the earlier member. The runner now starts each member's factory in the cell's order. <!-- fix: d7c7f86a12 -->
- An extension's `ctx.hasPendingMessages()` now asks the Host instead of reading a cached state snapshot, so it reports a steering or follow-up message queued while a handler is awaited, as Pi's live getter does. Extensions that gate delivery on that flag, such as `pi-queue-steer`, no longer report `Pi did not accept the queued message at this delivery boundary` after a message that was delivered. <!-- fix: ea32019f45 -->
- Durable with telemetry off (no telemetry or the no-op span) no longer builds span attributes, events or metric attribute bags, and `ai.Models.Stream` without a parent span captures nothing for the telemetry observer, so both allocate as before the telemetry integration; Durable's reflective restore also caches each struct type's field list. The AI telemetry schema is built on first use instead of at process start, and non-numeric attribute names no longer allocate in `jsstring.ArrayIndex`, which cuts the allocations of a recorded `ai` stream. <!-- fix: 692bdf76eb -->
- The grep tool passes `--no-config` to ripgrep, as Pi does at `8c4e8738`, so a user's `RIPGREP_CONFIG_PATH` file cannot change the tool's contract (literal, glob, limit). <!-- fix: 645bcf8a88 -->
- Recovery of a truncated assistant attempt (retry and overflow/length compaction) finds the tool results that answered the attempt's calls in the agent transcript and omits them with it, so a resumed session no longer persists orphaned tool results that providers reject (Pi `02726086`, #11227). <!-- fix: 645bcf8a88 -->
- Classifier models take images (Pi `7f225f25`): `models.classify()` accepts `images` for TypeSafe's Jev and Cloudflare's Clef models, and provider-level `compat` in `models.json` now also applies to classifier models, with the System One fields `imageInput` and `sendImageOptInHeader`. A model without image input rejects images with a clear error. <!-- fix: a99ac58407 -->
- The catalog tolerates the `free` field Pi v1.4.0's radius data carries (`recursive/flint`), keeping it in the model record as Pi does. <!-- fix: 6d31edae7c -->
- The upstream pin moves to Pi v1.4.0. <!-- fix: 9c48ac7f69 -->
- The durable turn classifier retries on a status code (429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 520, 524) only when it stands alone, not inside a request id or another number, and on `stream disconnected before completion`; a ChatGPT Business workspace usage-limit error is not retried. Carried forward from Pi 6e491cfa, 29057ece and cedefc53. <!-- fix: a1da44daec -->
- `@` file autocomplete finds paths for queries that hold regex metacharacters such as `[` or `(`, for a query with or without a `/` (Pi 612a172e, #10972). <!-- fix: 43d259770e -->
- `/compact <instructions>` applies the instructions to the summary of the start of a turn that compaction splits, and no longer ignores them when the split turn is the only new content since the last compaction (Pi adfc1446, #11058). <!-- fix: 43d259770e -->
- A resumed session and `/tree` navigation activate the extension tools the branch never added or removed, such as tools of an extension installed after the session started, unless they are registered with `defaultActive: false`; a tool the branch removed, also before a compaction, stays inactive. Built-in tools keep following `defaultTools` (Pi bd3c9b82, #11062, #11075). <!-- fix: 43d259770e -->
- Switching to a session whose system message holds a `toolsAdded` or `toolsRemoved` member the loadout restore cannot read fails with the TypeError of Pi's tool-list replay; a system message with an unreadable `content` no longer fails the switch (Pi bd3c9b82). <!-- fix: 43d259770e -->
- Automatic retry no longer treats digits inside a request id or another number (for example `req_011CU503xyz` or `5242880 bytes`) as a retryable HTTP status code: 429, 500, 502, 503, 504, 520 and 524 count only as standalone numbers. Carried forward from Pi 6e491cfa (earendil-works/pi#11057). <!-- fix: dc003a9b7f -->
- Automatic retry now triggers for `stream disconnected before completion` errors that a proxy wrapping the Codex CLI relays. Carried forward from Pi 29057ece (earendil-works/pi#11057). <!-- fix: dc003a9b7f -->
- Sign in with ChatGPT: a ChatGPT Business workspace usage-limit error (`subscription_sharing_workplace_individual_usage_limit_exceeded`) is not retried, and its message tells the user to ask their workspace admin to increase the limit. Carried forward from Pi cedefc53. <!-- fix: dc003a9b7f -->
- RPC mode no longer runs `ctx.ui.setWidget` component factories or emits `setWidget` requests carrying their rendered lines. Like Pi's `rpc-mode.ts`, it forwards string arrays and clears only; print and JSON modes ignore a factory too. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- `Context` and the types that hold one (`EventBus`, `ModelRegistry`, `ReplacedSessionContext`, `SessionManager`, `SetupSessionManager`) in the Go extension SDK are comparable again: the tool-call input a `Context` carries (`ToolInput`, `ToolParams`) is a pointer to the request's arguments instead of a slice, so adding it is no longer an incompatible change. <!-- fix: 4b2f12c842 -->
- `pi.events.emit` in a Go, Rust or Python factory behaves as Pi's does: the first factory-time bus call connects to the host (as `parseFrontmatter` does) and `Run` reuses the connection; a listener the factory subscribes is registered with the host at once and answers the host's dispatches on that connection while the factory waits for a host call, so it hears another extension's emit made while the factory runs, a nested reply to the factory's own emit included, as Pi's shared emitter delivers it; the call returns once the host has delivered the emit to every listener, in registration order (the extensions loaded before this one, then this one's own), and each has finished; a listener that throws or panics is written to stderr as `Event handler error (channel):` and reaches neither the emitter nor the next listener; an `error` emit that nobody hears fails with the emitted error. Before, the emit was queued until after ready, and its failure only reached stderr. A factory with no host (no `PIG_EXT_SOCKET`) gets an error from its first bus call. Go gains `sdk.ServeFactory(conn, factory)` for a fused binary, which gives the factory the pipe as its connection. <!-- fix: afdb030f -->
- The Python SDK test `test_provider_transport_shutdown_does_not_settle_owned_stream[True-*]` no longer fails when the worker thread is still returning at the moment the shutdown finds no thread to wait for: it waits for the worker, as the assertion means "ends", not "has already ended". <!-- fix: de19f796 -->
- A Go extension SDK loop that is served without `Run` (a test on its own connection) no longer waits for a loaded gate that never closes; `TestToolRequestAllocBudget` hung for the whole test timeout. <!-- fix: afdb030f -->
- The Node extension API probe of a failed factory (`TestUpstreamFailedFactoryDisablesCapturedAPI`) names `onLoaded`, which the runtime already rejects after a failed load; the test failed with an uncaught assertion since `onLoaded` was added. <!-- fix: 78fa8e4f -->
- A listener a Go, Rust or Python factory subscribes hears another extension's emit made while the factory runs, including a nested reply to the factory's own emit, as Pi's shared emitter delivers it: the listener is registered with the host when it is subscribed, a factory emit goes only through the host's ordered registry, and the factory's connection answers the host's dispatches while the factory waits for a host call. Before, the listener was registered when `Run` connected and missed such an emit. <!-- fix: 19a0df25 -->
- A command that an extension started with `pi.exec` and an `AbortSignal` kept running after the signal aborted: the signal never reached the host in the Go, Rust and Python SDKs, and the Node runtime sent it as an empty object. In print, JSON and RPC mode the command did not even end with the request, because the host ran it under a context that nothing cancels. <!-- fix: 4984f9db55 -->
- Extension API parity ledger: removed the stale `ctx.ui.custom(options.onHandle)` exception (Go, Rust and Python implement the overlay handle; `TestCustomOverlayHandleSDKsMatch` runs it on every SDK), reworded the keybinding-hint, highlight and `createAgentSession` rows that said "Node runtime only", and added a "Native package-export gaps" table naming the closing lane for every export Node serves and the native SDKs lack. <!-- fix: 1e8ec257e1 -->
- Conformance fixtures now reach the realized SDK export cells the export ledger listed as unreached: the editor component's `insert_text_at_cursor`/`add_to_history` overrides (Go, Rust, Python), the Go `Editor` queries and setters, `DEFAULT_MAX_LINES`/`DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES`, the session manager's `isPersisted`, `usesDefaultSessionDir`, `getChildren` and `buildSessionContext`, Rust's typed `serialize_conversation` and `strip_frontmatter` in Python, each compared with Pi's own answer on the Node runtime or the host's. `pi-tui` `parseColor` (Python) and `styleTextWithAnsi` (Python, Rust) move from `native` to `gap:SdkTuiHelpers`: the Python `parse_color` validates a wire color, not Pi's `parseColor`, and the Rust function is crate-private. <!-- fix: 94e69a91f7 -->
- Releasing a handle of an object a child session was created over (a `ModelRuntime` first of all) no longer closes the object under the session. The host counts the holders of every session graph object, the extension's handle and each child session created over it, and releases the object when the last one lets go. Before, an extension that built a runtime, created a child over it and let the runtime go out of scope had the runtime's model tasks stopped while the child still ran on it. <!-- fix: 8b3e5a68 -->
- Removing a local package reports `start` and `complete` `remove` progress events, as Pi's `remove` does for every source kind. <!-- fix: aa9ff5b76e -->
- `registerProvider` of a model without an `api` fails with Pi's `Set it at model level or provider level.` wording. <!-- fix: 215bbf6c9b -->
- An extension's `SessionManager.appendContextEdit(id, null)` clears the content instead of failing, and `appendCompaction` without `fromHook` writes no `fromHook` member. <!-- fix: aa9ff5b76e -->
- Extension SDKs: `formatKeyText`/`keyDisplayText` upper-cased the first rune of a part, where Pi's `charAt(0).toUpperCase()` leaves a character beyond the Basic Multilingual Plane (half a surrogate pair) as it is. <!-- fix: 6d00b4afff -->
- Extensions: `ctx.ui.setTheme(theme)` with a theme object makes that object the active theme as it is, as Pi's `setThemeInstance` does. PiG re-created the object in the terminal's color depth, so a truecolor theme set on a terminal without truecolor became a 256-color copy (and the other way round). <!-- fix: c9f8961b79 -->
- A PiG TLS client no longer accepts a certificate chain through a compromised eMudhra intermediate or a root Mozilla removed after it; the error names the CA. <!-- fix: 18b1c05f72 -->
- The CI images no longer ship the three removed emSign roots: `ca-certificates` 20261020-r0 in the ci-go lock, 20260909-r3 and a newer Wolfi base (glibc 2.44) in the ci-parity lock, which also takes `c-ares` 1.34.9 and `nodejs-24` 24.22.1. <!-- fix: 18b1c05f72 -->
- Shutting a host down while a child session is live no longer leaves its extension host running. A session an extension created with `createAgentSession` outlives the extension's `dispose()` of it, and a `DefaultResourceLoader` an extension built outlives the extension's handle while a session uses it; the host closed both only when the extension's connection closed, on a goroutine `Shutdown` did not wait for, and never stopped the loader's extension host. `Shutdown` now closes every live child session and releases every object it held before it returns, and a released loader stops the extension host it started. <!-- fix: 54283ceb0f -->
- A notification, status or message that an extension queued with `setImmediate` or `setTimeout` from a tool that returned while a sibling tool in the same batch still awaited reached an RPC client behind the sibling's own output in up to two runs in five, where Pi writes it right after the first tool's `tool_execution_end`. The Node runtime is another process: it wrote the callback's host call a few hundred microseconds after the tool's answer, and the host passed that tool's `tool_execution_end` in about the same time, so a call that arrived after it waited for the next `tool_execution_end` of any tool. The call now says which tool call queued its callback (`origin_tool_call_id`). The host runs it behind that call's `tool_execution_end` when it arrives before the event, and at once when it arrives after, where the runtime says a sibling tool still runs and the loop turns. <!-- fix: 65932e6efb, 4ac21c86c4 -->
- Extensions: in a packed Go, Rust or Python cell, a member that stops or is replaced no longer releases a file mutation turn (`withFileMutationQueue`) that another member's task still holds. The turns of a packed process now belong to the process: they are released by `fileMutation.exit` over any of its connections, or when its last connection closes. <!-- fix: 00e1452222 -->
- Extensions: `withFileMutationQueue` is now one queue for the built-in `edit` and `write` tools and every extension process, as in Pi. Before, each extension process kept its own queue, so a Go, Rust, Python or Node extension tool and a built-in tool on one file, or two extensions, could lose an update. The SDKs ask the host's queue for the file's turn (`fileMutation.enter`, `fileMutation.exit`); the turn is released if the extension's connection closes. <!-- fix: 0275c62f29 -->
- A Durable tool call aborted while the Harness was still resolving the tool to run could stay running for good, with the generation that called it waiting on it. The abort cancelled the call before its tool started, and the native core driver ended the call without telling the core, which settles a cancelled call only on that report. The call now reports the cancelled lookup as a thrown result, as it reports a tool that observes the cancel. It stalled once in about 1,000 CI runs of the abort-with-generation test under the race detector.
- A Python extension's `ctx.session_name` and a Rust extension's `ctx.session_name()` returned an empty string for the life of the process, because nothing ever read the name from the state the host sends. Every SDK now keeps the session name current from each state update, and a Go extension has the same accessor, `Context.SessionName`. <!-- fix: 06dc555220 -->
- `ctx.model` is the model's id in every SDK, as in Pi (`ctx.model.id`). A Python extension's `ctx.model` and a Rust extension's `ctx.model()` returned the model's display name (`Claude Sonnet 4.5`), so a comparison with `claude-sonnet-4-5` or a `provider/model` string built from it failed there and passed in Go. Python and Rust also have `model_provider` and `model_qualified`, as Go has `ModelProvider` and `ModelQualified`. <!-- fix: 06dc555220 -->
- A read of the session name, the session log or the model right after `setLabel`, `setSessionName`, `sendMessage` or `setModel` could return the old value in a Go, Python or Rust extension. The host now sends its state before the result of those calls, and the Go SDK waits for it, so the next read sees the write as it does in Pi. <!-- fix: 06dc555220 -->
- A model reply with many tool calls no longer slows down as calls finish: finished calls are not parsed again on every later stream event.
- An extension that reads `ctx.model`, `pi.getSettings()`, `pi.getSessionName()`, `pi.getThinkingLevel()` or its footer's git branch and provider count from a timer, thread or goroutine between requests now sees a change the host makes itself. A model switch (Ctrl+P, `/model`, cycling, RPC `set_model`), a settings edit, `/name` or RPC `set_session_name`, a resumed session, a branch checkout and a login updated the host but reached an extension only at its next request, so a background reader held the old value; Pi reads the live session. The host now pushes the new state to every connected extension when it changes, off the UI loop, and sends nothing to an extension that already holds it. <!-- fix: 0966844c54 -->
- In a Rust extension's `before_agent_start` handler, `ctx.get_system_prompt()` returns the prompt the handler's own edit of `systemPromptOptions` renders, as Pi's `ctx.getSystemPrompt()` and the Go, Python and Node SDKs do. It returned the prompt rendered before the edit, because the handler held the event exclusively and the context could not read it. The handler now edits the options through the `BeforeAgentStartEvent` the context shares, and a read after an edit asks the host to render the edited options. <!-- fix: 0b6bd6bd45 -->
- RPC mode no longer writes `entry_appended` for a `pi.appendEntry` or `session_info_changed` for a `pi.setSessionName` that an extension's `session_start` handler makes, and `--mode json` no longer writes anything a `session_start` handler produced: its `entry_appended`, `session_info_changed`, and the `message_start` and `message_end` of a `sendMessage`. Pi subscribes its stdout writer after the extensions are bound, so none of these reach a client; the JSON stream now starts at the prompt, after the session header. The `session_start` of a replacement command (`new_session`, `switch_session`, `fork`) that runs after the writer exists is still written, as in Pi. <!-- fix: 0f59853005 -->
- Durable task-graph and view watches no longer send or rebuild the whole graph or view on each commit: a commit costs the same at any number of live tasks or entries.
- Docs: another PiG build that shares the config root no longer removes the documentation bundle a running session's system prompt names. A session leases its bundle until it exits; before, a session that outlived four newer builds' use lost its pages and nothing wrote them again. <!-- fix: 1588d24df6 -->
- RPC mode now writes the members of an `extension_ui_request` that Pi writes empty, as Pi's `JSON.stringify` does: `message: ""` of `notify`, `title: ""` of `setTitle`, `text: ""` of `set_editor_text` (how a client clears its editor), `statusText: ""` of `setStatus` and an empty `options` list of `select` were dropped, so a client could not tell empty from absent. `input` and `editor` no longer write `placeholder: ""` / `prefill: ""` when the extension passed none. In the Go, Python and Rust SDKs `SetStatus(key, "")` is now an empty status text (it used to remove the entry), and `ClearStatus` / `clear_status` is Pi's `setStatus(key, undefined)`; an empty placeholder or prefill is the unset member (Python: `None`). The interactive footer is unchanged. <!-- fix: 1ac1eeebec -->
- MCP: a server's tools, the tools it stopped offering, and the resource tools now reach the session as one change, as Pi registers them in one synchronous turn. A prompt that started while a server was connecting could see only some of its tools.
- On the Cloudflare Durable host, an `AsyncLocalStorage` value set around a call stays visible in the work that call queues (turns, tool effects, timers and commit listeners), as in Pi.
- Starting a second Piglet Binary instance no longer fails on Windows while the first is running. Extraction left the embedded Go, Rust and Python cell binaries rewritten at every start, and Windows refuses to replace the executable of a running process (`Access is denied`); a cell binary that already holds the embedded bytes is now left in place, and only a different one is replaced, with a retry on a transient lock. <!-- fix: 2b5038a6a0 -->
- `pig piglet verify <binary> --source <name|path>` and `pig piglet lineage <binary> --source <name|path>` report `Provenance: matches` for a Binary built from that unchanged source. They compared the Piglet digest the Binary signs, which is the digest of its baked Piglet definition, with the extends graph digest, so every check reported `MISMATCH`. They now bake the source as the build does. A change to a file the build inlines, such as a system prompt file, is now a Piglet digest mismatch. The JSON `provenance` object's `effectiveDigest` field is renamed `pigletDigest`. Binaries built by 0.5.0 and 0.5.1 need no rebuild. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- `pig piglet verify` accepts `--workspace <dir>`, and `pig piglet lineage --workspace` now applies to the provenance check too. A Piglet whose `agentEnv.devContainer` is a `workspace:` path is checked against the workspace its build used instead of failing to resolve from another directory. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- A run that an extension's `sendMessage` with `triggerTurn` starts now carries the session's system prompt. The first run of a session reached the model with no system prompt, as did any later run that a custom message started after the instructions changed. Pi records the run's prompt sections for a custom starting message as for a prompt. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- A Durable Session whose commit was cancelled while it waited for the Session line now closes instead of hanging in `Close`.
- RPC mode writes one `setWidget` request per `ctx.ui.setWidget` call from the Go, Python and Rust SDKs, in call order and before the command's response, as Pi does. A string list with no options went out as an unanswered frame that the host applied on a lane of its own: the last list a command set could be written after the command's response or after a later `notify`, a repeat of the same list was dropped, and a list set after a clear stayed cleared on the client. In RPC mode the SDKs now send the list as the host call every other widget shape uses. <!-- fix: 31e26eac2b -->
- An extension that registers many tools after load (`pi.registerTool`) no longer slows down with each registration. The registration reply and state pushes no longer carry the whole tool catalog, and the Session no longer re-reads every tool on each registration. `getAllTools` and `getActiveTools` return the same results as before.
- In print and JSON mode, a `/skill:name` prompt whose skill file cannot be read now reports the skill's read error (`Extension error (<SKILL.md path>): ENOENT: ...`) before the missing-credentials error, as Pi does. Skill commands and prompt templates are expanded before the selected model's authentication is checked; they were expanded after it, so the skill error never appeared when the provider had no credentials. <!-- fix: 34f3a493d3 -->
- A `context` event with several extension handlers is encoded once per request instead of once per handler, and a handler that leaves the conversation unchanged no longer sends it back.
- Rust SDK: a `context` handler that returns a new list equal to the one it received now counts as a replacement, and an in-place edit marked with `pig_sdk::context_unchanged()` keeps system messages in place, as in Pi and the other SDKs. Return `None` for an unchanged conversation.
- `go install github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/cmd/pig@main` builds again. The root `go.mod` required `extensions/sdk v0.5.0`, which lacks `Result.Drift`, `FileChange.Created` and `Skipped.Suggest` that `pig extension upgrade` uses, so building the module outside the workspace failed, and `make module-size` failed on every pull request. Between releases the root `go.mod` (and `piglets/standard` and `piglets/aot`) now require the SDK at the pseudo-version of the `main` commit that holds the API the root uses; the release replaces it with the tag. `make module-publication` accepts such a pseudo-version, and proves by downloading it into an empty cache, with the committed checksums, that the commit is public. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- Images are now resized with the image profile of the model that answered last under a virtual model selection. Prompt images and tool-result images used the selected virtual model, which declares no image limits, so a routed model's `inputLimits.images.resize` never applied. Pi sizes both with the physical model of the latest routed response, and the selected model otherwise. <!-- fix: 3f517521bc -->
- The system prompt a run forced no longer shows while the bash and custom messages held during that run are flushed at its end. A listener of a flushed custom message that read the system prompt saw the run's forced prompt, where Pi ends the run's prompt options first and reports the base prompt. <!-- fix: 404bc7ca0f -->
- A tool that an extension activates with `setActiveTools` now appears in the system prompt's tool list of the next run that a `triggerTurn` message starts. The request already declared the tool, but the prompt left its snippet out, so the model was not told about it. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- A Durable core session with many live tasks recomputes its next wake without walking every live task on each commit: ending a chain of 20,000 held tasks takes under a second instead of about a minute.
- A `/reload` that arrived while the supervisor was restarting a crashed isolated extension could end the process the reloaded extension runs in, so its next call failed with a reset or closed connection. The restart now runs under the same lock as load and reload: a reload waits for the restart to register, then retains the restarted process; a restart that a reload or load already replaced does nothing. <!-- fix: 4fe779e53e -->
- Registering a large MCP catalog, building a CodeMode sandbox from one, and CodeMode tool discovery were quadratic in the number of tools, so a 100,000-tool server took tens of seconds to register after its last `tools/list` page, `NewSandbox` took 27 seconds, and every `searchTools()` or `describeNamespace()` call rescanned the whole host catalog once per tool. Tool names are now assigned from a per-server count of the sanitized names, the sandbox keeps a name index beside its tool list, and a discovery call reads the host catalog once and resolves each tool's namespace by lookup. Names, collision suffixes, registration order, duplicate-tool errors and the first-entry-wins namespace of a repeated name are unchanged. Discovery also now stops when its context is cancelled and returns the context's error; before, it kept scanning. Pi resolves the same way for the sandbox (a `Map` by name) but scans per tool in the MCP extension and in codemode discovery; PiG's results match Pi's. <!-- fix: 53385a3d93 -->
- An extension that reads `pi.getMcpServers()` from a timer, thread or goroutine and has no `mcp_servers_change` handler now sees a server another extension registers or removes. The host updated the registry but reached an extension only at its next request, so the reader kept the old list; Pi reads the registry live. The host now pushes the new list to every connected extension when the registry changes, off the call that changed it, and sends nothing to an extension that already holds it. <!-- fix: 5630b1c63a -->
- An extension that reads `ctx.ui.theme` from a timer, thread or goroutine now sees the theme the user selects (`/settings`, a theme file edit, the terminal's reported colors). The host replaced the active theme but sent the palette only with the extension's next request; Pi's `ctx.ui.theme` is the global theme, read at every use. The host now pushes the new palette to every connected extension when the theme changes. <!-- fix: 5630b1c63a -->
- A Node extension that registers or unregisters a virtual model (`pi.registerVirtualModel`/`unregisterVirtualModel`) sees the write on the next line, as Pi does: `ctx.modelRegistry.find`, `getAll` and `getAvailable` list the model, a provider of only virtual models is configured and available at once, and an unregistration removes the model. The host also publishes the catalog when it serves either call, so another extension's registry sees the model. <!-- fix: 56fd9ec590 -->
- CI: the nightly `-race` run now covers the packages that own most of PiG's goroutines. `coding`, `coding/cli` (run as four shards), `coding/extension`, the extension hosts (`inproc`, `runtimecell`, `cellpack`), `internal/packagemanager`, `internal/pilock`, `internal/pigsdklock`, `coding/mcpext`, `coding/rpcclient`, the `extensions/sdk` Go module, `internal/codingagent/tools` and the concurrency libraries were in no `-race` job before. `make race-coverage` (part of `check-core` and `ci-drift`) fails when a package that starts goroutines or timers and has tests is in no nightly group of `automation/ci/race-packages.txt` and not exempt with a reason. The first `-race` run of all of them found no data race. <!-- fix: 5d1af780 -->
- A pig session on another machine or container that shares your `~/.pig` (an NFS home, a bind mount) is no longer reported as crashed by the next pig you start. Session markers in `crash-output/` record the host and PID namespace, and a marker from another one is left alone instead of being removed with a false "ended without recording an exit" line. <!-- fix: 63d1bf067f -->
- The bug fix ledger no longer credits a squash commit's tests to a release note that names no fix commit. A release note without a `<!-- fix: <sha> -->` marker had its commits guessed from the history, and the only commit it found for five notes was the 0.5.0 squash, which grew `docs/bug-ledger.json` from about 2.1 MB to 9.8 MB and pushed the root module past its size budget. `make changelog-check` now fails a `### Fixed` bullet without a marker, a marker or fragment name that names a commit `HEAD` does not reach, and `make bug-ledger` refuses, naming the entry, to attribute the tests of a commit that touches more than 500 files. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- Idle and typing frames in a long interactive session no longer redraw from the header down: a frame costs the same at any transcript length. Extension headers and footers still update every frame.
- Markdown with many inline `$` signs renders in linear time, as in Pi.
- A user_bash `operations` object from an extension is no longer released while a command is running through it. The extension's handle is released when the host drops the object, but the running exec did not keep the object reachable, so a garbage collection mid-command could delete the extension's table entry under the command. <!-- fix: 6d3cfc863d52939a998bc61b2183ebac58e5d12f -->
- Extension SDKs: reading the session branch, an entry, a label or the leaf entry right after a new entry is appended no longer costs time proportional to the session length, in the Go, Node, Python and Rust SDKs.
- `go install github.com/MichaelKinsy/PiG/cmd/pig@<version>` downloads a root module of 181 MB instead of 304 MB. Nine data-only directories (parity interface ledgers, pin ledgers, upstream-sync ledgers and large test data) have their own `go.mod`, so Go leaves them out of the root module zip. The files stay in the repository. <!-- fix: 6dfa6bfa3b -->
- Choosing a bundled model no longer builds every model of its provider on each request, and providers in `models.json` with many custom models no longer slow down quadratically. Branch summaries of long sessions are prepared in linear time.
- Two calls of a Go, Python or Rust extension tool in one assistant message that each open a dialog now behave as Pi's one thread makes them behave, as Node extensions already did: the dialogs open in call order (a port's second dialog could open first), and when the user answers them back to back the calls respond, and end, in the order of the answers. The SDKs keep these orders for the calls of one parallel batch, which the host now names on each `tool_call` request (`batch`); calls of other batches, other sessions' calls and tools that make no host call take no part and are never held. A handler that blocks on something other than a host call before its first host call holds the first dialogs of the later calls of its batch, while a call that awaits a host call such as `exec` before its dialog is overtaken by the later calls' dialogs, as in Pi, and a handler whose dialog was answered holds the later-answered handlers' responses until it returns or makes its next host call (recorded as divergence D187: Pi's single thread lets a call that awaits work the SDK cannot see be overtaken). <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- The upstream test inventory records Pi's `shell-path-exists.test.ts` as ported instead of partial: Pi mocks `accessSync` and `spawnSync`, PiG's Windows alias and Unix cases match them case for case, and the native Windows check ran and passed on the Windows runner. The release gate no longer reports it as an open hot-path gap. <!-- fix: 725e104b55 -->
- Fixed a chord host root failing with `superseded: code version N is not retained` although it was started in the current code version: the host picked the current code version and counted the root in it in two separate steps, and a reload's retirement of that code version could fall between them. The root now starts in one step, as upstream's does. <!-- fix: 735c36cdbf -->
- `chord/host`: a host no longer sends on another host's connection while delivering notifications, and a reload that unwinds frees its slot and ends its operation together, so a following reload is not refused as already running.
- Print mode and `--mode json` no longer run an extension command a second time on text an input handler or a prompt template produced. A handler that rewrote the prompt into an extension command's invocation, or a template whose body is that invocation, ran the command and sent nothing to the model. Pi checks for a command on the raw text only and sends the produced text to the model. <!-- fix: 7827d96bf8 -->
- Shutting down a host while a crashed extension waits out its restart backoff no longer leaves a goroutine sleeping for up to 30 seconds. Shutdown ends the wait and waits for it, so embedders and tests see no goroutine after the host is shut down. <!-- fix: 784709c9f8 -->
- Forking a session from a file no longer overwrites a session file that already has the new session's timestamp and id. The fork now fails with `EEXIST: file already exists` and leaves the file untouched, as Pi does, instead of truncating it. <!-- fix: 785bb1bd53 -->
- Extension SDKs: `ModelRegistry.find` reports a host or transport failure instead of "no such model". The Go `Find` returns `(model, error)` and the Rust `find` returns `io::Result<Option<_>>`; Python already raised. A host with no `getModel` or `sessionRead` binding answers an error, so a list-typed session read is never a null. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- Python SDK: `has_configured_auth` and `is_using_oauth` answer `False` for a model with no provider, as Pi and the Go, Rust and Node SDKs do, instead of raising `KeyError`. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- Go SDK: `Context.CancellationReason` returns the reason the host gave when it cancelled the request, as the Python and Rust SDKs do. <!-- fix: 7abdf5b391 -->
- A compiled-in extension factory, inline or built in, no longer panics "Extension runtime not initialized" when it calls `pi.sendMessage`, `appendEntry`, `setSessionName`, `getSessionName`, `setModel`, `setThinkingLevel` or `getThinkingLevel` under the interactive mode. The mode rebinds its in-process runner after the Session binds it and replaced the Session's actions with three of them; it now binds every action Pi's `_bindExtensionCore` binds, through the code the wire host actions of the same names run. <!-- fix: 81add2436b -->
- The interactive mode no longer rewrites every provider request's system prompt into one head. It set the agent's system prompt for every run and again after every tool change and reload, so a mid-session change to the prompt or the active tools reached the model as a rewritten head with the transcript's system patch messages dropped. Pi sends the prompt as the transcript's sections and patch messages and forces text onto the request only for the `systemPrompt` a `before_agent_start` handler returned; the interactive mode now does the same. <!-- fix: 863981b82a -->
- A Node extension no longer keeps reading an entry the host refused. When the host answers `pi.appendEntry` (or `ctx.sessionManager.appendCustomEntry`) with an error, `getEntries()`, `getBranch()`, `getLeafId()`, `getEntry()` and the other session reads stop showing that entry, also after later calls the host synchronizes; before, the entry stayed in the extension's session reads although the host's log never held it. Only the refused entry is taken back: appends still in flight stay, and an entry the host appended is read with the parent the host linked it to, where the extension could show the refused entry or an older leaf instead. A call that was cancelled or lost with its connection has no answer, so its entry stays until the host's log settles it. <!-- fix: 8a18a6bb2d -->
- A Durable code mode tool call made just before the script launches another call or ends has started before the script goes on, as in Pi: a later call no longer overtakes it, and the end of the script no longer aborts it before its tool ran.
- Markdown bold and italic render next to CJK punctuation in the terminal, for example `**这是测试。**后面` (Pi #10730). Text without CJK keeps the CommonMark flanking rules. <!-- fix: 9076cd5cb8 -->
- A Durable Harness task that went to sleep for a short time, such as the 1 ms backoff before a generation retry, could stay asleep for good on a busy machine, so a `Wait` on its submission never returned (a CI run of the `durable/harness` tests hung until the 10-minute timeout). The Harness recomputes when to wake the process in a goroutine that can run milliseconds after the change, and it read the clock when that goroutine ran, not at the change, as Pi's microtask does. A sleep that had been ahead of a scheduling pass was then already due, so no timer was armed for it, and a host's `onWake` was told that work was active instead of the time to wake at; nothing ran the task again. The recomputation now reads the clock of the change that queued it, so the timer is armed (or the time is offered to `onWake`) and the task wakes. <!-- fix: 94e6796e8d -->
- `pig extension upgrade` finds and rewrites every changed API of an extension in one run. Startup offers each Go extension the upgrade would touch, not only those whose first ten compile errors all name an SDK change: a `len(ctx.GetAllTools())` or `panic(ctx.GetSessionID())` (a builtin that takes one value) is now recognised as the getter's change, and a recorded failure with no classification is read again by type checking. A `Context.ConfigHome` in a function that cannot return the error is rewritten to report the error through the call's own `Context` (`ctx.Notify`) and return, never to discard it; a getter that cannot move before its statement (a `&&` operand, an `else if`, a loop condition, a call after another call) takes a generated helper in `pig_upgrade_getters.go` that returns the old getter's zero value on failure. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- `pig extension upgrade` applies the rewrites that have one safe form and lists the rest with file, line and the form to write, instead of restoring every file when one use has no mechanical form. It exits 1 and reports `partly upgraded` when uses are left. `--dry-run` compiles the rewritten extension in a temporary directory (the go command's overlay, with the staged SDK) and reports `would upgrade` only when it builds; a rewrite that would add a compile error of its own is not written. The load error of a drifted extension names the changed APIs and the exact command. The stale-copy warning also recognises an edited copy by its Go module path. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- A message sent with `deliverAs: "nextTurn"` while a run was starting could join that run instead of the next one. A run takes the pending nextTurn messages when its preparation ends, before the bash results and custom messages held while it started are appended, as Pi does. The Session took them after that, when it claimed the Agent, so a Session event listener that answered a held custom message's `message_end` with a nextTurn message (a Go program embedding the Session; no extension handler sees those events) had it reach the model a run early. <!-- fix: 9a694a51f1 -->
- Markdown bold and italic next to CJK punctuation render in HTML exports and in Markdown that Node extensions render through pi-tui, matching the terminal (Pi #11168, #10730). <!-- fix: 9abc6acfde -->
- An extension's UI closures now run only in the TUI, as in Pi. In RPC mode the Node runtime no longer runs `setFooter`/`setHeader` factories (a throwing render leaked a `notify` error), keeps `setEditorComponent` and `onTerminalInput` handlers, forwards the working-indicator calls, answers `getEditorComponent` with the installed factory, or fails `setTheme(Theme)`; they are no-ops, `getEditorComponent` answers undefined and `setTheme` answers `{success:false,error:"Theme switching not supported in RPC mode"}`. The Go, Python and Rust SDKs no longer run footer and header renderers in RPC, print or JSON mode, and `Custom` in RPC mode resolves nil/None without running the component. <!-- fix: a293b81d2a -->
- Fixed `pig piglet build` failing on a Piglet that carries a single-file extension (a lone `.ts` or `.js` file): locking it read `<file>/go.mod`, which fails with ENOTDIR rather than ENOENT, so the build stopped with "local Go replacements: not a directory". A file extension has no Go replacements and now locks like a directory extension without a `go.mod`.
- Interactive mode now routes a message an extension sends with `sendMessage` while a run streams as Pi does. A message with no `deliverAs` steers: the run delivers it after the current tool batch, before the next model call, where it used to wait until the run would stop. A message with `triggerTurn: false` is held until the turn's tool results are complete and appended without a model call, where it used to be queued as a follow-up that cost the run another model call. `sendMessage` no longer rejects an empty `customType` or an unknown `deliverAs` (Pi accepts the first and steers the second). <!-- fix: a343b2ba32 -->
- An SDK's `ctx.hasPendingMessages()` in interactive mode counts queued user messages only, as Pi does, and no longer counts the custom messages an extension queued with `sendMessage`. <!-- fix: 5df1bac28c -->
- A provider stream could stop for good, with the turn waiting for events that never came, when the goroutine that reads the stream and the goroutine that runs the provider's response were scheduled against each other as the stream started. The reader's goroutine could pick up a reaction loop that belonged to the response, run the reader's code beside a reaction that had not returned, and leave the stream's executor marked busy with nothing running. A goroutine now continues only a reaction loop its own turn holds, so no code runs beside a reaction and every stream ends. It stalled once in CI on Windows (`TestGoogleProviderStreamMatchesPi`); the same stall reproduces on macOS and Linux when the executor's lock hand-offs are delayed.
- PiG no longer hangs for 30 seconds at exit when two extensions share a Node process and one is still waiting on a host call (seen with a global project-trust extension and a project extension).
- A Node extension that changes the active tools or registers a tool now reads the system prompt back as Pi does. `ctx.getSystemPrompt()` and `ctx.getSystemPromptOptions()` answered from the state the host pushes with the next event, so a read after `pi.setActiveTools` or `pi.registerTool` in the same handler returned the prompt of before the write, while the Go, Python and Rust SDKs read the live value. From the first such write on, the reads ask the host. <!-- fix: a003596587 -->
- `ctx.isIdle()` after `pi.sendMessage(..., { triggerTurn: true })` in an `agent_settled` handler of a Node extension now reports `true`, as the Go, Python and Rust SDKs do. The session holds such a message until the handlers return, as in Pi, and Node had assumed the run started. <!-- fix: a96962ee93 -->
- A Node extension whose factory failed, because it has no default export or throws while initializing, could be reported as `connect:process_exited ... exit status 1` instead of Pi's "Extension does not export a valid factory function: <path>" or "Failed to load extension: <message>". The extension's runtime reports that error over its socket and exits; on a loaded machine the host looked after the exit and chose between the connection and the exit by whichever it noticed first, which failed about 1 run in 20 of the upstream extension-discovery tests. The host now accepts the connection the extension made before it exited, in both a packed cell and an isolated process, and reports `process_exited` only for a process that never connected.
- A tool that streams a growing partial result no longer sends the whole result to each subscribed extension on every update; updates carry only what changed, and handlers still receive the full `partialResult`.
- A Durable core request costs the same however many requests are in flight: a notice is tested only against the requests waiting for it.
- A Node extension's `ctx.ui.select`, `confirm` and `input` honor `options.signal`, as Pi's RPC and interactive modes do. A signal that is already aborted resolves the default (`undefined`, or `false` for `confirm`) without showing the dialog or writing an RPC request, and a signal that aborts while the dialog is open resolves the default at once and dismisses the dialog (RPC: the pending request is dropped). Before, the signal was ignored: the dialog stayed open until the user or its timeout answered, and the extension waited for it. <!-- fix: b2a73bfaed -->
- Durable tool calls that the model issues in parallel or nested no longer slow down as the live state grows: a commit no longer decodes the whole live document, and each tool reads only its own call.
- A start could fail with a bare missing-file error when another `pig`'s cache collection removed the cached extension build it had just reused. The collection (the daily automatic one, or `pig extensions cache prune`) decides which entries are expired and removes them a moment later. A start that reuses an old entry in between refreshed its use time but holds no lease until its extension process spawns, so the entry was removed under it, the lease then came back empty instead of failing, and the spawn reported a missing file. The collection now reads each entry again under its exclusive lock and skips one that another `pig` used since, counting it as in use. A reuse takes the entry's lock while it checks and records the entry, so it never reuses an entry that is being removed and builds it again instead. A lease for an entry that is gone fails with `cache_lease_failed` and says the entry was removed. The SDK fingerprint file of a published entry is replaced through a rename and written only when it changes, so a collection can no longer read it half written and expire a build that a start is using. <!-- fix: b856354834 -->
- A tool's `prepareLoadout` hook now runs again before every later turn of a run, as in Pi, so the descriptions it sets and the declarations it hides follow the state the hook reads. The hooks ran when a run started and when the loadout changed, so a hook that read settings or extension state kept the first turn's answer for the rest of the run. <!-- fix: b8c4b2c287 -->
- With an extension `turn_end` handler, each tool result no longer copies the whole session history, and a boundary preview no longer re-encodes every entry: long sessions stay fast at turn ends.
- Fixed the chord host keeping a disposed object reachable until its ready timeout (a minute by default) had passed: a stopped host timer stayed in the runtime's timer heap with its callback, and the callback captured the object. A stopped timer now releases its callback at once. <!-- fix: be21e9ad -->
- The /tree "Summarize branch?" selector and "Custom summarization instructions" editor, the /import confirmation and the missing-session-cwd confirmation of /resume and /import are dialogs on the same stack as an extension's, as in Pi. A dialog an extension opens while one of them is shown takes the keys, closing the prompt shows the earlier dialog again instead of hiding it, each reports its blocked status to the terminal, and a session replacement or `/reload` removes the prompt with the other dialogs. `/bug` keeps its own prompts outside the stack and no longer reports a blocked status for its description editor, as in Pi (Pi 38847563, bug-report.ts). <!-- fix: be6803c903 -->
- The Node extension runtime's `ctx.modelRegistry.runtime` is now a model runtime that a nested Session can share. An extension that passes it to `createAgentSession({ modelRuntime })`, as the pi-goal-x completion auditor does, failed with `this._modelRuntime.hasConfiguredAuth is not a function`. The nested Session now uses the parent's providers and credentials, as in Pi. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- A Durable reference harness turn no longer copies the whole conversation history to extend its context.
- Starting a child host could wait out its 30-second ready timeout ("child not ready in time") although the child was up. The Go port of Node's IPC channel read messages in its own goroutine and dropped any that arrived before its owner had registered a listener. Node cannot lose them, because an owner registers in the tick that creates the channel. A child that opened its channel after the parent's init message had been written could miss the init (so it never answered), and a manager descheduled between spawning the child and registering its control listener could miss the child's ready. The channel now keeps the messages received before its first listener and replays them to it in order, and a forked child instance does the same for its control listeners. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- OpenAI Responses requests keep the prompt cache across tool changes (Pi #11142). Every tool is declared in `additional_tools` items from the head of the input, a removal is announced as text with the still-unavailable tools restated, and synthetic tool search and Kimi tool messages move the top-level tool list only at the last removal or redefinition, so a later tool load no longer rewrites the cached prefix. Applies to `openai-responses`, `azure-openai-responses`, `openai-codex-responses` and Kimi on `openai-completions`. <!-- fix: c371a134cc -->
- Durable: `Submission.Wait`, `WaitForIdle` and `WaitForTask` return only after every event-stream listener has been given the batches committed before the wait, as in Pi. Stopping an event stream right after one of these waits no longer drops the end of a run.
- Selecting a `go.work` member module that is itself an exact factory package resolves that module's factory and keeps the workspace as the compiler closure (#212). Before, the selection scanned every member module and reported all of their factories as ambiguous, so a monorepo with one module per extension and a root `go.work` could not install any member. The workspace root itself stays ambiguous when several members hold factories. An exactly selected member that is a `package main` standalone resolves to that member instead of "spans N modules".
- Fixed the chord host's `Close` waiting forever when a hold that a link was still delivering created an object after `Close` had taken its snapshot of the host's objects: that object's resource open kept waiting for an abort that nothing raised. `Close` now disposes what is left once the links are closed. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- On a file system that refuses file locks (an NFS mount without a lock manager, a FUSE or SMB share), starting an extension failed with `wait for cell build lock: flock: no locks available`, or with a bare lock error from the lease of a cached build, the cache collector or the SDK staging, and nothing named the cause. The error now names the directory, says the file system does not support file locks, and names the remedy: point `PIG_HOME` at a directory on a file system that does. The cache still needs its locks, so a file system without them cannot hold it. <!-- fix: d68f60ece2 -->
- `pig update --extensions` no longer fails when two Git Packages come from one repository. Packages of one repository share a managed checkout, and their updates ran at once: both fetched into one `FETCH_HEAD` and both ran `git reset --hard`, which failed with `Unable to create '.../.git/index.lock': File exists` and `git reset --hard FETCH_HEAD^{commit} failed with code 128`. The packages of one checkout now update one at a time, in the order of the settings file, and the last one decides the commit the checkout is left on. Packages from other repositories still update at the same time, and a package that fails does not stop the others. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- In RPC mode, a toast an extension deferred with `setTimeout(0)` from a `model_select` or `session_start` listener reached the output before the response of the `new_session`, `fork`, `clone`, `switch_session`, `set_model` or `cycle_model` command that ran the listener, or of the prompt whose extension command started the new Session. Pi writes the response first, because its response follows the listeners in microtasks and the timer needs a later turn of the event loop. The host now holds such UI calls (`ui.notify`, `ui.setStatus`, `ui.setWidget`, `ui.setTitle`, `ui.setEditorText`) until the command's response is written. Seen with `@liyu1981/pi-tweaks`' remember-model extension. <!-- fix: d99a513b55 -->
- In RPC mode, a message an extension's `session_start` listener sent while a command replaced the Session was written to the output. Pi subscribes its output writer after `session_start`, so it writes nothing for it; PiG now publishes the new Session's events after `session_start` too. <!-- fix: d99a513b55 -->
- In RPC mode, an extension command that sends a user message and then awaits `ctx.waitForIdle()` (the init command of a Pigpen warden) had the run's `agent_start` written before the prompt's response, for the Go, Python, Rust and Node SDKs alike. Pi's `waitForIdle` returns at once while the run the command started has not begun, so the command returns, the response is written, and `agent_start` follows. The host now settles such a wait at once and no longer counts the handler as suspended. <!-- fix: d99a513b55 -->
- Two Bun runtime installs, or an install and a status check, in different processes no longer leave a moment where `<agent dir>/runtimes/typescript/bun` does not exist: `/bun-runtime` install, remove, verify and status take a lock on the slot, and an install removes the `bun.old-*` directory that a killed install left behind. <!-- fix: da811523f5 -->
- A slow toolchain version check no longer blocks other starts without limit. Go, Rust and Python extension builds probe `go version`, `rustc --version` and the like; the probe is now ended after two minutes, a start that finds another start probing the same tool waits at most five minutes before probing for itself, and two projects with different `go.mod` or `rust-toolchain.toml` selectors each keep their own cached version instead of probing again on every alternate start. <!-- fix: dcb0a11e16 -->
- A `--print` or `--mode json` run that SIGTERM or SIGHUP reaches while its bash tool is starting the shell no longer has a data race on `os.Stdout`. Starting a child process (a shell, an extension command, a Durable child) decided which of the child's files to leave open by reading the `os.Stdout` variable, which the exit path's restore of the output guard assigns at the same time. It now compares them with the files `os.Stdin`, `os.Stdout` and `os.Stderr` held when pig started, which stay open as before. The output and exit status are unchanged.
- A Go, Python or Rust extension tool that awaits a host call such as `exec` before its first dialog no longer holds the first dialogs of the later calls of its parallel batch: the SDK passes the call's dialog place at its first host call of any kind, so the later calls' dialogs open while the `exec` runs and the call's own dialog opens after it, as Pi's single thread does (D187). A handler that blocks on something other than a host call before its first host call still holds them. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- `chord/host`: the Node facet loader no longer writes its child entry under one fixed name in the shared temporary directory. Another process that cleaned that file up, or two processes racing it, made a starting load fail with `child exited before ready`; and any other user could rewrite the file Node ran. Each load now writes the entry into a private `0700` directory of its own and removes it once the child has started.
- A Durable core listing of all or terminal tasks (`ScanTasks`) reads one page from storage per call instead of loading every task: a page costs the same at any task count. Listing a conversation owner's conversations reads only that owner's.
- Two calls of an extension tool in one assistant message now end in the order their tools answer, as Pi ends them. In Pi, two tools that each wait on a dialog (or on timers of equal length) continue in the order their answers arrive, so `tool_execution_end` for the call whose dialog was answered first comes first. PiG woke the goroutines that waited for the two answers in no order, and on most runs the second call ended first, although its dialog was answered second. The host now records the order where the answers are read (a `tool_call` response from the extension's process, an RPC `extension_ui_response` line) and keeps it where the calls continue: the batch's `tool_execution_end` events, and the replies sent to the extension for its dialog calls. Tool-result messages still follow call order. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- Edit diffs and the `edit` tool with many replacements on a large file use far less memory and time: a 16,000-line diff takes a quarter of the time and 2 MB instead of 336 MB.
- Fixed the chord host manager letting other code read it as stopped, or start the child again, before the `onExit` callback for the child that just exited had run: upstream sets the status and calls `onExit` in one step, so the manager now keeps other goroutines waiting until `onExit` returns, while `onExit` itself sees it stopped and may start the child again. <!-- fix: ea96559132 -->
- An extension that set its header or footer, or registered a sprite or a login, from its own goroutine painted the whole screen on that goroutine while the main loop was changing the transcript, so the two could read and write the same transcript state at once. Such a change now asks the main loop for a frame, as Pi's `setExtensionHeader` and `setExtensionFooter` end with `ui.requestRender()`; a change made on the main loop still paints at once. The race detector reported it in about one run in ten of the Node scroll-view footer scenario, which sets its footer while a notification arrives.
- The terminal width and height the extension host tells extensions during `/reload`, and when one starts or restarts, were read while the main loop's frame updated them, and the handler that reports an extension's crash was replaced on each reload while the host's connection goroutines read it. Both now read an atomically published value. The race detector reported the width and height read in about one `/reload` run in twenty, and the crash handler once, in a shutdown from a `session_start` handler.
- Fixed a chord link closed with a reason reporting "peer closed" to its close handlers instead when the peer answered the goodbye before the closing side had marked the link closed; the link now ends first, with the reason it was closed with, as upstream's does. <!-- fix: f102eb98d5 -->
- A compaction, branch summary or bug-report summary request is now sent through the session's own stream function, as Pi sends it. Before, a session whose stream function was the stock one sent these requests straight to the model's provider, so the host never ran `before_provider_headers` for them. `/compact`, automatic and overflow-recovery compaction, `/tree` summaries and bug-report summaries now emit `before_provider_headers`, in every mode. They still do not emit `before_provider_request`, `after_provider_response` or `provider_stream_event`: Pi's summary requests carry none of the Agent's payload, response and stream-event callbacks, and neither do PiG's now (agent requests are unchanged and emit all four). `Agent.EffectiveStreamFunction` is new: the stream function a run sends through, with the default stream in place of the nil `StreamFunction` returns for it. Reported by a user. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- Fixed `pig --mode rpc` exiting 143 instead of 0 when stdin ended while a termination signal's shutdown was still running: the first exit of the shutdown now decides the exit status, as Pi's first `process.exit` does. <!-- fix: dbe25ef5c1 -->
- Deleting members of a large Durable core JSON object no longer costs time proportional to its size for each delete.
- An unclosed `$$` or `\[` display-math block no longer swallows or corrupts every Markdown table after it. A display-math block never spans a table, and the streaming pending-math fallback no longer treats a following table as pending math (Pi 2ed3118b, #11159). <!-- fix: 9c980381eb -->
- The terminal no longer stays in mouse-tracking, focus-reporting, Kitty keyboard, bracketed-paste or alternate-screen mode when pig exits through a path that skips the normal teardown (a direct exit, an emergency exit, an off-loop crash, a panic unwinding through `Main`). The modes are restored in Pi's order, once, and not again after a normal stop (Pi 9df819de, #11170). <!-- fix: 909581ef0b -->
- Concurrent extension `select`, `confirm`, `input`, `editor` dialogs and inline `custom()` components in interactive mode no longer replace each other: a second dialog used to take the editor slot and leave the first one's promise pending. They stack, the last one is visible, closing it shows the previous one again, and a hidden dialog's abort or timeout removes only itself. `/reload` and session replacement dispose every stacked dialog, and an editor-slot custom component restores the editor text it found (Pi 38847563, #11166, #9909). <!-- fix: 90f69085ce -->
- Pasting in the shell no longer hangs after copying text in pig and suspending it with Ctrl+Z on Linux: the `wl-copy`, `xclip` or `xsel` process that keeps serving the clipboard now runs in its own session and is not stopped with pig (Pi f8ab362c, #11179). <!-- fix: 9be7181948 -->
- Codemode scripts no longer fail immediately when both codemode workers are busy, for example with parallel codemode calls or in-process subagents. A script waits up to 60 seconds, in arrival order, for a free worker; the wait counts toward its `timeout_ms` (Pi 885a564b, #11184). <!-- fix: e73e536f2c -->
- Extensions that import `@earendil-works/pi-ai/models` (for example for `clampThinkingLevel`) load in the Node extension runtime, under both the `@earendil-works` and the legacy `@mariozechner` scope (Pi 22dc6c26, #11156). <!-- fix: 0aa31c48e4 -->
- `mcp.json` files that start with a UTF-8 BOM, which Windows PowerShell 5.1 adds when it rewrites a file, load their MCP servers again, and `pig mcp add` and `pig mcp remove` work on them (Pi bdd9f039, #11191). <!-- fix: 00933e586a -->
- A Go or Rust extension edited while it was being built could be cached under the hash of the source from before the edit. The compiler reads the source after the build hashed it, past the wait for another `pig`'s build of the same extension and through its own compile, and nothing read it again, so an edit saved in that time was compiled into a build stored under the original source's hash. Putting the original source back then ran the build of the edit. A build now reads its inputs again when the compiler returns (the extension source, the SDK, workspace modules and toolchain) and caches nothing when they changed: it fails with `changed while it was being built`, and the next start builds the current source. A Rust crate's `Cargo.lock`, which `cargo` writes during the build, does not count as an edit. <!-- fix: fd51f36b79 -->
- A Node extension that registers or unregisters a provider through `ctx.modelRegistry` (or `pi.registerProvider`/`unregisterProvider`) sees the write on the next line, as Pi does: `find`, `getAll`, `getAvailable`, `hasConfiguredAuth`, `getProviderAuthStatus` and `isUsingOAuth` read the registry the registration left, and an unregistration removes the provider's models (a built-in provider keeps its own). The runtime keeps its own writes over the host's registry snapshot until the host answers the call, so a publication that predates the write cannot undo it. <!-- fix: ff590eb41e -->
- `ctx.abort()` aborts the run's `ctx.signal`, and the signal a tool receives, before it returns in a Node extension, as Pi's `agent.abort()` does. The host sends the run's `run_signal` frame ahead of the reply to the `abort` call, so the Go, Python and Rust SDKs read the signal aborted as soon as `abort()` returns instead of when a watcher's frame happens to win the race. <!-- fix: ff590eb41e -->
- `pi.getCommands()` in a Node extension lists a command registered after load by the same turn's `pi.registerCommand`, as Pi's live command Map does. <!-- fix: ff590eb41e -->
- A Node extension tool that awaits real work and then takes the file mutation queue (the upstream `subagent` example, which writes a prompt file under `withFileMutationQueue` after a `mkdtemp`) no longer waits forever when it runs outside the dispatcher's parallel batch of an agent run, for instance when a tool is called directly or its batch runs sequentially. The runtime holds a tool's next queue call after it reports that the handler awaits, until the host says the edit and write calls waiting for the tool's start window registered; a tool that holds no start window had nobody waiting, and the host never said so. <!-- fix: adffe57501 -->
- RPC mode: an `extension_ui_response` whose `value` is not a string, whose `confirmed` is not a boolean, or whose `cancelled` is not a boolean no longer leaves the dialog pending. The dialog resolves with the client's JSON as Pi's does: a Node, Go, Python or Rust extension sees a number, `null`, an object or an array from `select`, `input` and `editor`, and the client's `confirmed` from `confirm`; `cancelled` is read by JavaScript truthiness. <!-- fix: c4bcb663a6 -->
- A YAML date used as a mapping key is named with the time zone names of Node 24.22 and later, the Node Pi runs on (tz 2026e). Morocco and Western Sahara print "Western European Standard Time" and "Western European Summer Time" instead of "GMT+00:00" and "GMT+01:00" (Node 24.22.1 does so from 2026-09-20 01:00 UTC, when Morocco returned to permanent UTC); `EST5EDT`, `CST6CDT`, `MST7MDT` and `PST8PDT` print their offset (`GMT-05:00`) instead of the names of New York, Chicago, Denver and Los Angeles; and the switches between standard and daylight names of Alberta, Manitoba, the Northwest Territories, Colombia (1992) and Iran (1979) follow tz 2026e. Node 22.19 and 24.19 print other names for these zones. <!-- fix: 86cb0929c3 -->

### Security

- PiG's own TLS connections refuse a certificate chain that includes one of the eight eMudhra intermediate CAs whose private keys anyone can recover from their public certificates (Mozilla bug 2083449), or one of the three roots Mozilla removed from NSS 3.131.1 (`emSign ECC Root CA - G3`, `emSign Root CA - C1`, `emSign ECC Root CA - C3`). Sectigo's cross-signed certificates of the same keys are refused too. This covers model providers, OAuth, MCP, package and tool downloads, self-update, the Codex WebSocket and Bedrock. Recorded as D191.
- CI, the parity image and the devcontainer move to Node.js 24.22.1 (current 26.12.1), the 2026-10-21 security releases (CVE-2026-105482, CVE-2026-105483, CVE-2026-106069, CVE-2026-111497 and others). PiG ships no Node.js binary: update the Node.js that runs your TypeScript and JavaScript extensions to 22.23.4, 24.22.1, 26.12.1 or later.

### Known issues

- Node.js 24.22.1 orders the microtasks of `values().next()` after a buffered byte-stream read differently from 24.19.0 (`values-buffered/bytes`); the Web Streams models in `ai` still replay 24.19.0 (`ai/testdata/node-microtasks/oracle-node-version`).

## [0.5.1] - 2026-10-19

## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-14

### Upgrading from 0.4.x

- Go API: `agent.NewAgent` returns an error, as Pi's `Agent` constructor throws. Before: `a := agent.NewAgent(opts)`. After: `a, err := agent.NewAgent(opts)`, where `err` is `agent.ErrNoDefaultStreamFunction` when no stream function is configured. `Agent.Continue(ctx)` returns only an `error`, and the Go extension SDK's `Context.SetEditorComponent` takes an `sdk.EditorFactory` instead of `any`. The other Go API changes are the **Breaking (Go API)** entries under "Changed". The extension SDK getters already returned errors in 0.3.0 and are unchanged.
- `models.json`: a custom model needs an `api` on the model or on its provider unless it replaces a built-in model of that provider; 0.4.x defaulted it. Without one, PiG reports `Provider "<provider>": Provider <provider>, model <model>: no "api" specified. Set at provider or model level.` and ignores that provider's `models.json` entry, as Pi does. See the **Breaking (config)** entry under "Changed".
- Local Packages: a local directory with no `"pi"` manifest in `package.json` and no `extensions/`, `skills/`, `prompts/` or `themes/` directory now loads its own root as an extension, as Pi does. A library-only local package must add `"pi": {}` to its `package.json`, or loading it fails in every session.
- Go extension SDK: in a `before_agent_start` handler, `data["systemPrompt"]` is an `*sdk.SystemPrompt` instead of a string. Before: `prompt := data["systemPrompt"].(string)`. After: `prompt, err := data["systemPrompt"].(*sdk.SystemPrompt).Get()`. A handler that still asserts a string panics, and the `, ok` form reads an empty prompt. `pig extension upgrade` rewrites both forms. See the **Breaking (Go SDK)** entry under "Changed".
- Go API: `agent.TimingSnapshot.Turns` (a `[]time.Duration` with one entry per turn) is replaced by `TurnCount int`. The recorder no longer keeps a duration for every turn, so a long session's memory stays flat; read `TurnCount` for the number of turns. `/cost` output is unchanged.

### Added

- Add the Go form of Pi's `@earendil-works/pi-env`: remote execution environments over SSH. `env` provides `RemoteExecutionEnv`, a Durable `ExecutionEnv` on another machine whose results match `NodeExecutionEnv` on that machine, with lazy `Connection`s that restart after a loss, watchers that report `overflow` and resume after a reconnect, and the SSH bootstrap (`ConnectSsh`, `SshConnection`, `ScanHostKey`, `AcceptHostKey`, `ForgetHostKey`): strict host keys under a fixed alias, platform detection through the login shell or PowerShell, and daemon deployment verified by SHA-256 (base64 lines through PowerShell on Windows). The daemon, `env/cmd/pi-env`, speaks Pi's wire protocol and is a Go program (D96): `make build-env-daemons` cross-builds it for Linux, macOS, Android and Windows on x86-64 and arm64, and `PI_ENV_DAEMON_DIR` names the directory that holds them (D97). The test suites of Pi's package run over a pipe to the daemon, and the SSH tests use a disposable `sshd` or a scripted `ssh`, so no remote host is needed; `PI_ENV_SSH_HOST` runs the conformance suite over a real server.
- `pig --no-mcp` turns off the built-in MCP support for one run.
- `durable/tools.CreatePowerShellTool` adds a `powershell` tool that runs `pwsh`, or else Windows PowerShell, directly through argv `Exec`, with UTF-8 output and the `bash` tool's output window, spill and errors.
- Codemode `tools.read()` on an image file resolves to an image block that `image()` shows. The `read` tool returns the same value as structured content.
- `pig piglet publish <name|path> --to npm` publishes a Piglet's source to npm so that anyone can add it with `pig piglet add npm:<name>`. It validates the Piglet, replaces each local Package with its `npm:<name>@^<version>` reference, sets the package name and version from the Piglet (`release.version`), adds the `pig-piglet` keyword and `pig.piglet` to `package.json`, and includes the README, LICENSE and system prompt file. It is a dry run until `--yes`, refuses a name and version that npm already has, and runs your own `npm`, so npm handles the login, one-time password, passkey or trusted publishing. `--tag`, `--access`, `--otp`, `--npm-name`, `--package-map` and `--binaries github:<owner/repo>` are available. PiG adds `--provenance` when `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` is set. PiG never handles an npm token.
- A Piglet published with `--to npm` records its signed Binary release in `package.json` under `pig.binaries` when it was published with `--to github` on the same machine, or when `--binaries github:<owner/repo>` names the release, so catalogs can show `pig piglet pull` next to `pig piglet add`.
- `pig package publish [<dir>] --to npm` is optional sugar over `npm publish` for a Package: it validates the Package and the `package.json` fields the pi-in-go.dev catalog reads (including the `pig-package` keyword), checks that the version is free, and shows npm's dry run before `--yes` publishes. Plain `npm publish` still works.
- A new documentation page, "Publish a Package or Piglet" (`pig docs show publishing`), covers the keywords, both commands, signed Binaries, how pi-in-go.dev finds published packages, trusted publishing in CI and the npm first-publish notes.
- `agent` exports Pi's standalone loop: `RunAgentLoop`, `RunAgentLoopContinue`, `AgentLoop`, `AgentLoopContinue`, `AgentContext`, `AgentLoopConfig`, `AgentEventSink` and `AgentEventStream`. The `Agent` runs on the same loop. `AgentLoopConfig`, `AgentOptions` and `Agent` take a `GetAPIKey` resolver that runs before every request, as Pi's `getApiKey`; its error fails the run, as a rejected `getApiKey` does. `AgentLoop` logs a failed run on the process log and ends its stream with the error.
- `tui.ParseColor` accepts a number, and `IsKeyRepeat` is exported.
- `durable.WatchDoc[T]` is the typed form of Pi's `watchDoc`: a watch of a document's committed value decoded as `T`, nil once the document retires, and a nil watch for an absent document.
- `durable.DocumentStateOf[T]` is the typed form of `documentState`.
- The Go examples now cover every script of Pi Durable's `test/examples` (16 and 21 to 29, 31): late join, foreground and background subagents, child tasks, compaction, a coding agent, plan mode, a reviewer, a sandbox per conversation, and reload and restart.
- `durable/interop` runs Pi Durable 1.0.4 (the pinned Node packages) and Go on one scripted session and compares the SQLite rows, the JSONL files and the Harness API view, then lets each runtime open and resume the store the other wrote.
- The RPC command `get_extensions` returns `{"paths": [...]}`, the resolved path of each loaded extension in load order, with built-in extensions as `builtin:<name>`. Pi has the same list in process as `getExtensionPaths()` (D101). See `docs/site/docs/rpc-commands.md`.
- The Go extension SDK has `Context.SetEditorComponent`, as Rust and Python have. The factory receives the host's stock editor (`*sdk.Editor`) as the component's `super`, and host keys, mouse clicks, `SetText` and history reach the override.
- `ai.Context.Frozen` declares the messages immutable, so a request shares them instead of copying them, as pi-ai's `normalizeContext` does; `TranscriptContext.Len` and `At` read a transcript without a copy. Durable's generation sets it unless a `beforeRequest` hook receives a copy.
- `durable/interop/bench.mjs` and `bench/durableperf` run the durable-bench workload on pi-durable (Node) and on PiG's Go Durable, with fingerprints and SQL counters.
- `BashSpawnHook`, `BashSpawnContext`, `PowerShellSpawnHook` and `PowerShellSpawnContext` let a Go caller adjust a shell tool's command, working directory or environment before it runs, as Pi's `spawnHook` does. The hook sees the `PI_*` session variables unless the tool hides them.
- `SettingsManager.SetDefaultProjectTrust` and `SetMaskSecretInput`.
- `ai` exports the per-provider catalogs Pi's `providers/<provider>.models.ts` exports (`AnthropicModels`, `AnthropicImageModels`, `AnthropicClassifierModels`, and the same for each of the 42 providers), generated from the runtime catalog by `cmd/gen-provider-catalogs`.
