- `coding` exports Pi's compaction helpers (`DefaultCompactionSettings`, `FindCutPoint`, `FindTurnStartIndex`, `GetLastAssistantUsage`). `--api-key` startup goes through `ModelRuntime.SetRuntimeApiKey`, which synchronizes local state as Pi does. `PIG_PACKAGE_DIR` and `PI_PACKAGE_DIR` override the package directory. `imageprocessing.DetectSupportedImageMimeTypeFromFile` returns its open error and is the read tool's default detection.
- `<agent dir>/exit.log` records every interactive exit that no user action asked for, with the time, pid and parent pid: SIGHUP, SIGTERM, an external SIGINT, a closed terminal, an extension's `shutdown()` and a failed interactive run. A session that the OS killed (SIGKILL, memory pressure, power loss) is reported at the next start. `pig diagnose` prints the crash log, exit log and debug log paths and the newest exit lines (D102).
- `make port-lint` runs 23 analyzers for porting anti-patterns (map key order, nil versus empty JSON, UTF-16 units, regex case folding, numbers, clocks, goroutine and request lifetime, error identity, test isolation) against a ratcheting baseline, and runs in `make check` and CI.
- `RefreshModelsContext.baseUrl` reaches extension-registered providers: the host sends the registered provider's base URL in the `refreshModels` call, and the Go (`RefreshModelsContext.BaseURL`), Python (`base_url`) and Rust (`base_url`) SDKs read it and send it when they invoke a provider object, as Pi 1.2.0 does. Node extensions receive it as `context.baseUrl`.
- Added per-key OpenRouter model lists: with an OpenRouter key, PiG lists only the chat models that key may use, with the key's context size, max output and prices, including guardrails and regional endpoints set as the base URL in `models.json`. The list is saved and used offline.
- Added `PersonalizeOpenRouterCatalog` and `RefreshModelsContext.BaseURL` to the `ai` package: the registered provider's base URL, including configuration overrides, reaches a provider's `RefreshModels`.
- `piglets/aot` (experimental): compiles a session's TypeScript extensions into one executable on your machine with the Pi-Bolt 0.7.4 compiler, in the background, and runs it from the next start. The compiler downloads on first use, verified against a pinned signature key and digests; nothing is redistributed. Linux x64 and macOS arm64. `/aot-runtime` shows and prunes the cache (1 GB default).
- `bench/evals` holds reproducible benchmarks of PiG, Pi, Claude Code, Codex and Copilot CLI against a local scripted model endpoint, with no live key: startup, per-turn overhead, memory, extensions, long sessions, reliability under HTTP faults, kills and disconnects, long-history sessions, and the durable-bench workload. Each arm is installed at a version and checksum pinned in `bench/evals/arms.lock` into a throwaway directory. `make bench-overhead`, `bench-reliability`, `bench-longhistory`, `bench-durable`, `bench-harbor`, `bench-lock-bump` and `bench-test` run them, and each writes raw JSON, CSV and a Markdown report that records the lock hash and seed.
- TypeScript and JavaScript extensions can run on another runtime than Node. The global `extensionsTypescriptRuntime` setting (default `node`) or `PIG_TS_RUNTIME` names a runtime that an extension or Piglet installed under `<agent dir>/runtimes/typescript` (`PIG_TS_RUNTIMES_DIR`); PiG downloads nothing. PiG verifies the executable's SHA-256 against its manifest, and a Bun executable against PiG's runtime lock (Bun 1.4.2 for the eight release targets), and checks that it answers `--version`. A Bun runtime loads neither a project's `bunfig.toml` nor its `.env` files, as Pi's own Bun binaries do not. When the requested runtime cannot run, the extensions run on the runtime its manifest names as `fallback`, else on Node, with a typed reason for each skipped runtime. `pig extensions runtime status [--json]` shows the request, the runtime a launch uses and why, and the installed runtimes. Pi runs extensions inside its own Node process; this is a PiG addition (D19). See [TypeScript extension runtimes](/docs/latest/typescript-runtimes).
- PiG Standard adds `/bun-runtime [status|install|verify|remove]`. `install` downloads stock Bun 1.4.2 for this platform from Bun's GitHub release, verifies the OpenPGP signature of `SHASUMS256.txt.asc` with the pinned Robobun key, checks the archive and the extracted executable against pinned digests, and installs it into the TypeScript runtime slot. Select it with `"extensionsTypescriptRuntime": "bun"` or `PIG_TS_RUNTIME=bun`. Stock PiG does not download Bun.
- `delta.ApplyImmutableBatchesSeq` takes an `iter.Seq[[]Op]`, as Pi's `applyImmutableBatches` takes an `Iterable`.
- `delta.AssertValidOpValue` accepts any value, as Pi's `assertValidOp` takes `unknown`.
- `delta.Verb` and `delta.PathOf` read an operation's verb and path.
- `delta.Ops` is a batch of operations whose `UnmarshalJSON` keeps the key order of the objects the operations carry. Use it for a decoded field; a `[]delta.Op` decoded by `encoding/json` holds Go maps.
- `chord/host` has the Node facet loader of Pi 1.2.0 (`nodeLoader`): `NodeLoader` and `NewNodeLoader(...).Load` are a `FacetLoader` that imports JavaScript facet modules, so `HostOptions.Loader` loads and hot-reloads them. The facets run in an isolated Node child (one per load, over `ForkDriver`): each facet is named by its module URL, a module whose default export is not a facet fails the load with Pi's `TypeError`, a reload imports fresh copies, and the facets of one load share their module graph. The Go host serves the contracts the child's facets serve and forwards each method call to the child; a ChordError a method throws keeps its code and message, and an aborted root aborts the child's call. A child stops at `Close` or once no facet of its load is referenced, so replaced module copies do not accumulate. Contracts with state or opaque members, several variants, `accepts` adapters or state migrations are refused at load.
- `host.WebSocketAcceptOptions.MaxCalls` limits the calls a WebSocket client may have in progress, as Pi's `acceptWebSockets` takes `maxCalls` from `AcceptOptions`. `AcceptWebSockets` passes it to `Accept`: further calls are answered at once with `unavailable` (reason `too_many_calls`) and the link stays open.
- Go package `chord/host` with Chord's contracts (`Remote`, `Local`, `Method`, `Opaque`, `Coverage`, `Bind`), schema helpers (`Fits`, `Normalize`, `CanonicalJSON`, `Hash64`), `ChordError` and the wire frames of Pi main's Chord host runtime; contract fingerprints equal Pi's.
- A durable input submission can name the entry it is placed as: `SubmissionDraft.InputEntry` (`kind`, `data`) places the input as an application entry that carries the user message, instead of `pi.user`. The kind must be a non-empty string outside the `pi.` prefix, or the submission fails with a `TypeError` before any write. A queued input keeps its entry until a boundary places it (Pi `InputEntry`).
- A durable Session bounds its loaded-document cache by use. A document that no watch or document state observes leaves the cache once it was not read or written for the retention period, and the next access loads it from Storage; observed documents always stay. `HarnessSettings.DocumentRetentionMs` and `CreateSessionOptions.DocumentRetentionMs` set the period: the default is ten minutes, `0` drops a document right after use, `+Inf` keeps documents, and anything else that is not a number of milliseconds, 0 or more, means the default. The Harness reads the setting at each use (Pi `documentRetentionMs`).
- `codemode.SandboxOptions` gains `AcquireWorker`, which reserves capacity before each worker VM starts and releases it when the VM actually exits, and `OnWorkerStarted`, whose channel closes on that exit, following Pi 3daa2028.
- Extensions can draw a conversation as the main transcript does, with Pi's conversation components in the component kit: user and assistant messages (with thinking), tool calls, `!` commands and diffs (`docs/plan/extension-component-kit.md` §2.1). PiG draws them with the same code as its own transcript, a node with an id keeps its state (a clicked thinking block, a tool card's elapsed time) across updates, and a frontend such as Tern receives them as its transcript's message, thinking and tool card nodes. Go extensions build them with `kit.NewUserMessage`, `kit.NewAssistantMessage`, `kit.NewToolExecution`, `kit.NewBashExecution` and `kit.NewDiff`; Rust extensions with `kit::UserMessage`, `kit::AssistantMessage`, `kit::ToolExecution`, `kit::BashExecution` and `kit::Diff`; Python extensions with `kit.UserMessage`, `kit.AssistantMessage`, `kit.ToolExecution`, `kit.BashExecution` and `kit.Diff`. Node extensions keep using Pi's own `UserMessageComponent`, `AssistantMessageComponent`, `ToolExecutionComponent`, `BashExecutionComponent` and `renderDiff`, which a frontend now also receives as these nodes.
- A frontend member's session now receives PiG's program status (D91): working, blocked while a dialog or a login waits, then done, error or idle, as the OSC 7501 report Pi 1.2.0 writes to a supporting terminal. A session that implements `frontend.ProgramStatusSession` receives every change, including the clear before `Suspend` and `Close`. While a session may draw, PiG no longer writes the OSC 7501 support query or a report to the terminal, where they bypassed the member. Stock PiG and a Piglet without a frontend member report the status as before.
- Each shipped Piglet (PiG Standard, pig-aot) has a reviewed feature inventory: a test pins its strip list (none in 0.5.0), selected extensions, discovery and the state of every built-in tool, slash command, built-in extension, model API and feature, so nothing is stripped or added silently. A strip stays opt-in: an empty `discovery.extensions` strips nothing (D92).
- `ai.WrapBuiltinProvider` applies a built-in provider's registered stream wrapper to a per-model `Provider`, so a model built outside the registry still resolves its Cloudflare endpoint.
- `coding.SessionOptions.Agent` and `coding.SessionOptions.CacheWarmer` let a caller hand `NewAgentSession` an Agent and a cache warmer it built, as Pi's `AgentSessionConfig.agent` and `cacheWarmer` do. The Session chains the `prepareRequest`, `prepareNextTurn`, `finishTurn` and message-transform hooks the Agent already has, replaces its `BeforeToolCall` and `AfterToolCall` properties as Pi does, and drives the warmer's status, `onWarmed`, settle and mode hooks. `agent.Agent` gains `SetPreparePrompt`, `SetPrepareToolResult`, `SetTransformLLMMessages` and `SetSessionFile`. There is no `extensionRunnerRef` cell (D113): read `Session.ExtensionRunner()`.
- `ai.StreamOptions.AnthropicClient` sends the Anthropic Messages request through a caller's `ai.AnthropicClient`, as Pi's `AnthropicOptions.client` does: the provider skips federation, OAuth and API-key header assembly, and retries the client's `ProviderRequestError`s within `MaxRetries`.
- `chord/context` is now a public package (it was `internal/chord/chordctx`). `Background()` and `TODO()` are Pi's `BACKGROUND_CONTEXT` and `TODO_CONTEXT`, and the contexts the package derives print as Pi's do (`[Context BACKGROUND_CONTEXT].WithValue(first)`).
- `tui.StdinBuffer` has no `getMaxListeners`, `setMaxListeners` or listener-limit warning: any number of listeners register without Node's `MaxListenersExceededWarning` (D114).
- `codemode.LoadQuickJSWasm(path)` returns the embedded QuickJS module for an empty path or `codemode.EmbeddedQuickJSWasmPath`, and an error for any other path: codemode has one module source and never reads a module from the file system.
- `coding.DiscoverAndLoadExtensions(ctx, configuredPaths, cwd, agentDir)` discovers extensions in the project, global and configured locations with Pi's `discoverAndLoadExtensions` order and entry-point rules, loads them in the Extension Host and returns a `coding.LoadExtensionsResult` (with its runtime); `Close()` on the result shuts the host down. It takes no event bus (D117). `tui.StdinBuffer.Process` is Pi's void `process(data)`, and `tui.StdinBufferEventMap` types the arguments of its events.
- `durable.AnyTask` is now Pi's object shape, a struct with the erased `Definition`, instead of an interface. A `Task` erases to it with `task.Erased()`, and `Extension.Tasks`, the registry snapshot and `CreateTaskErased` take it; a task absent from a registry is the zero `AnyTask`. The former interface is `durable.TaskSource`, which `Task` and `AnyTask` satisfy.
- The docs have a new page, Run Local Models on NVIDIA RTX, which converts an NVIDIA Local AI Inference Recipe for llama.cpp into a router preset that PiG can use (Pi 746d995b).
- The Piglets page has a worked example of a read-only reviewer Piglet, and explains that a Piglet's `tools` list limits the active tools but never turns any on, and that its `systemPrompt` replaces PiG's. The Custom providers page has a worked example for adding a local OpenAI-compatible model in `models.json`, the output to expect, and the two silent traps: a provider without `apiKey` is left out without an error, and a down server answers `Connection error.`
- The documentation site has an "Applied use cases" section for people who do not program. It explains that PiG acts without asking, where your files go, and how to undo, and it walks through summarising a folder of PDFs and organising files, each with the exact prompts, what PiG shows and how to check and undo the result. "Your first ten minutes" takes a new user from install through signing in, choosing a model and one finished task.
- Two new documentation pages. "Command line by example" runs the common `pig` commands and shows what each prints and its exit status: version and diagnostics, sign-in checks, print and JSON mode, exporting a session, Packages, MCP servers, Piglets, updates and the offline manual. "Your first extension" creates, checks, runs, edits and reloads a Go extension, with the output of each step.
- The MCP page has a worked example with a complete 20-line stdio server in Python, the `pig mcp add` and `pig mcp list` output, the `/mcp` screen, and how the model reaches the server's tools through the `codemode` tool, with the screen PiG shows for the call.
- The Go SDK page has a worked example that was run: the Session events a prompt produces, how to read the reply, that `Session.Send` returns no error when the provider has no credential (the failure is on the assistant message), and that `coding.DefaultAgentDir()` does not read `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR` the way the `pig` command does.
- The Windows page says which statements were observed and which were only read from the code, and that nothing was run on Windows. It records that the amd64 and arm64 builds link as Windows executables, and the order in which `PIG_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and the profile directory choose the configuration folder: a set `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` moves PiG's folder to `%XDG_CONFIG_HOME%\pig`.
- The manual that ships inside PiG (`pig docs list`) has an "Applied use cases" page: the rules to give a user who does not program, and the prompt, check and undo for summarising PDFs, cleaning a spreadsheet, drafting a document, automating a weekly report and organising files. Asking PiG how to do one of these now finds it.
- The Themes page has a worked example that follows the `/settings` theme screens and shows the `settings.json` they write. The Applied use cases overview has a short "If PiG feels like too much" section with the four things to know and how to ask PiG about itself.
- The keybindings page has a worked example that moves the tool-output key and shows the hint, `/hotkeys` and what happens to the old key, and its sample no longer binds `app.tools.expand` to `ctrl+e`, which is also the editor's "End of line". "Your first extension" ends with an extension that blocks `bash` commands containing `rm -rf` through a `tool_call` handler, with the output to expect.
- The Codemode page has a worked example: a script that reads two files in parallel and returns three numbers, with the screen PiG shows and the tool result the model receives.
- The Sessions page has a worked example of naming, continuing, reopening by ID prefix, forking and resuming conversations from the command line, with each command's output, and the Environment variables page shows how to try PiG in a scratch `PIG_HOME` and how `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` change the paths that `pig diagnose` prints.
- The Providers page has a seven-step worked example with the exact output of `pig auth check`, `pig auth print-api-key` and `pig logout`: no credential, an environment key, the wrong variable for a provider, a stored key that is a command (it wins over the environment), a command that fails (the environment key is used), logout, and the `invalid` state with exit status 2.
- The Terminal UI page has a six-step walkthrough with the screens PiG drew: the plain and colour headers, `/` and `@` completion, a prompt that runs a command, `!` and `!!` (the second keeps its output out of the model's context), Ctrl+O, and the two ways to exit. It records that `@file` completion only inserts the path and does not attach the file.
- The Piglet Binaries page has a worked example: build a Piglet into one executable, check it with `pig piglet verify`, see it in `pig piglet list` and run it, with the build output. The command-line examples page lists the commands it does not run (those that need a network, a login or their own screen) and where each is explained.
- A "Settings by example" page shows the common settings at work: the JSON to write, how to check it and the output to expect. It covers choosing the model and thinking level, the tool list, retry, the shell prefix and proxy, the session directory, cache warming, the project trust prompt and `defaultProjectTrust`, `quietStartup`, branch navigation, compaction and secret masking, and lists every other key with an example value and its Pi source line.
- The JSON mode page has a worked example with real output: the order of records in a run that calls a tool, the system-prompt record at the start, how a failed command shows as `isError` and `exit_code` while `pig` itself exits 0, how to read the final answer, and what an unknown model name does.
- The Termux page shows how to check that PiG loaded your `AGENTS.md` (the `/reload` confirmation and the text the model receives), and says plainly which steps on the page were not run on an Android device.
- The slash commands page gives every command a worked example: what to type, the screen PiG prints, what it changes, and where PiG differs from Pi (`/share` D64, `/bug` D62, masked key entry D80, `/reload --explain`, `/sprite`). It also says that `/debug` writes the whole conversation to its log, that `/tree` and `/fork` never change files, and that `/exit`, `/clear` and `/piglet` outside a Piglet are sent to the model as text.
- The RPC mode page has a worked example you can run from a shell: the records PiG prints for `get_state`, `prompt`, `get_last_assistant_text` and `get_session_stats`, why the `prompt` response comes before the answer, why responses can arrive out of send order, and the errors for an unknown command and for a line that is not JSON.
- "Your first extension" has a second example that adds a command that takes arguments, a dialog, a key binding and a command-line flag, shows the compile error you get from a wrong name, and the output of each part.
- Three more "Applied use cases" walkthroughs in the documentation: clean a spreadsheet, draft and edit a document, and automate a weekly report with `pig -p`. Each gives the exact prompts, the screens PiG shows, how to check the result and how to undo it. The weekly-report page explains why a scheduled run needs the full path to `pig` and a stored login, and that an empty `@` file is skipped without a message.
- `durable.TaskOptions.AbandonOnRestart`, `TaskRecord.AbortReason` and `TaskRecord.AbandonOnRestart`, and `TaskRuntime.AbortOwned`, as Pi's `TaskOptions.abandonOnRestart`, `TaskRecord.abortReason`/`abandonOnRestart` and `TaskRuntime.abortOwned` declare them. `AbortOwned` aborts a task the running task owns and returns once it is terminal. A task-owned child that a tool which is not replay-safe creates through its api defaults to `AbandonOnRestart`; `AbandonOnRestart: false` keeps it off. The scheduler's restart abort-marking that acts on `AbandonOnRestart` follows in a later change.
- Durable: `ToolRegistration.Callers` (the model, other tools, or both), the stored `pi.agent` field `modelTools`, and `Agent.Callable`; `addTools` extends `tools` and `modelTools`. `ToolRegistration.StructuredOutputSchema` declares the structured output, which the Harness checks strictly; a model-issued call keeps its result and loses a broken structured output, and the host is told through `report`. `ToolExecutionApi.RetainedOutput()` returns the output retained so far.
- Durable: `read` returns an image as one image block (PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP within the model's image limits, 2000x2000 pixels and 4.5 MiB of base64 by default) instead of an `unsupported_image` error. Images that need resizing or converting remain errors until an image processor is configured.
- Durable: `ToolExecutionApi.ExecuteTool` lets a running tool call another tool as a nested call. A nested call is its own `pi.tool` task (`kind: "nested"`) owned by the caller; its result (`NestedToolExecutionResult`) goes to the caller through its `pi.tool.nested-call` document (one document per nested call, keyed by the call's key), not the transcript, and the nested calls a caller leaves running, also those an earlier attempt made, are aborted when it settles. `TaskRuntime.OwnedTasks` lists the live tasks a task owns directly (Pi 4ac0bd8c), served by a new `ownedTasks` api op of the core (the ABI id changes). Tool hooks receive `ToolHookCall` with the `Parent` of a nested call. Running nested calls are listed in `pi.live.nestedTools`, and tool events and the snapshot carry them with `parentToolCallId`/`parentTaskId`; every tool event names its `taskId`.
- `pig piglet build --format binary` and `pig piglet publish` build a Piglet that lists no extensions. A base that only strips built-ins and sets defaults, such as a `model`, becomes a Binary of PiG's own parts with that Piglet baked in, and a child that extends it can add extensions. A Piglet that lists extensions that resolve to none still fails with `piglet resolves to no extensions`.
- `pig extension upgrade [<name|path>...] [--all] [--dry-run] [--summary]` rewrites Go extensions written for an older SDK with tested, type-aware rules: the host-backed `Context` getters that now return `(value, error)`, the optional boolean fields that are now `*bool`, and `ContextUsage.Tokens` and `Percent`. It backs up the originals, prints a diff, rebuilds, and reports per extension. A change with no safe rewrite is reported with its remedy and left alone. It is a PiG addition (D109); Pi has no SDK to drift from. The extensions guide, section "Upgrade a Go extension written for an older SDK", shows an extension before and after the upgrade.
- A Go extension whose build fails only on changed SDK symbols now reports `written for an older SDK`, naming each changed API with its old and new shape and the exact `pig extension upgrade` command. Interactive startup asks `Run pig extension upgrade <path>... now? [y/N]` before it exits, and on yes loads the upgraded extensions in the same process; print, JSON and RPC modes print the command and exit 1 with the same standard output as before.
- The global `extensionsAutoUpgrade` setting (default false) runs those rules at startup and after `pig update`, with a backup and one line per extension. A project settings file cannot turn it on, and no agent-based fix runs.
- When two loaded extensions register the same tool and one directory is a copy of the other, startup names both directories and suggests removing the older copy.
- `make sdk-apidiff` fails an incompatible change to any package of the extension SDK module since the pinned release that lacks an upgrade rule or a release note.
- `examples/extensions/node-factory`, a runnable Node example next to the Go, Rust and Python ones.
- A Go, Rust or Python extension that fails while registering now shows its own error in the load message even when the process had only just exited, and a Rust panic is reported with its message (`thread 'x' panicked at src/lib.rs:1:44: <message>`). The cause line was lost for Rust 1.89 and later, which print a thread id after the thread name.
- The Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs now send each frame with one socket write instead of two (length, then body), as the Node runtime already did. The host decodes every wire frame with fewer allocations (a request round trip allocates 13 objects instead of 24; the JSON decode state, the inbound length buffer and the write-result channel are no longer allocated per frame), and the Go SDK shares the cheaper decode.
- A lone UTF-16 surrogate in an extension's tool result (a Node or Python string such as `"a\ud83db"`) is kept as JSON.parse keeps it. A tool result in block form, and any text, image or thinking block read from a session, used to turn it into U+FFFD while the text shorthand kept it. New scenario rows cover unicode, NUL, U+2028, a 180 KB argument and the lone surrogate through every SDK that can express them.
- Tool-call arguments replayed to a provider (OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, Mistral, Google), passed to hooks and shown in the tree are written as JSON.stringify writes them. A call whose objects were already in sorted order, the common case, was written by encoding/json instead: `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 were escaped, integer-like keys sorted as text (`"10"` before `"2"`), and a lone surrogate became U+FFFD (three of them). Arguments a model streams or a session stores keep a lone surrogate as well.
- Conformance rows for tool_call event handlers run through all four SDKs: a block result, a thrown error and a defect each reach the runner with the handler's message.
- `ctx.ui.theme.sourcePath` reports the theme's own source file, so a theme loaded from a file reports it even when the active registry does not hold it. Conformance rows now compare the modifiers, `getFgAnsi`, `getBgAnsi`, `bg`, `getColorMode`, both border colors and `sourcePath` of every SDK against the host's theme.
- The Node conformance fixture names the component kit classes through the `piTui` and `piCodingAgent` namespaces and sets a component's `viewLines` as a member, so the consistency matrix sees the Node cells the kit rows already drive (EveryKind, Conversation, Widget/Header/Footer): 17 kit cells closed.
- The consistency matrix counts an object-literal key as reaching a runtime-read property (`{ filePath }` for Pi's `renderDiff`), and maps Node's `ctx.ui.custom(view)` to the author-facing `RuntimeUI.custom` instead of a runtime-internal field: 2 more component-kit cells closed.
- The matrix maps the Node cells of the kit's tool and entry renderers to the author-facing members; `TestConformance_ComponentKitWidgetHeaderFooterAndRenderers` draws a Node `renderCall` card and a `registerEntryRenderer` entry against the tui ports at every width.
- Conformance rows for `tool_result` handlers in all four SDKs: handlers chain (each sees the earlier edit), a throwing handler is swallowed and later handlers still run, and a handler that returns nothing changes nothing.
- The Go SDK ends the connection on a frame that is not a JSON envelope, as the Node, Python and Rust SDKs and the host do, instead of skipping it and leaving the host waiting for the answer to the request it carried.
- A conformance row pins that a command receives its arguments unchanged in every SDK (leading and trailing spaces, non-ASCII, astral characters, U+2028/9, NUL, 180 KB).
- A command, shortcut, flag, message renderer or entry renderer an extension registers after it loaded (from a handler) now reaches the host in every SDK, as in Pi, instead of being dropped. New `registerDeclaration` wire call; Rust handlers use `Context::register_command`, `register_shortcut`, `register_flag`, `register_message_renderer` and `register_entry_renderer`.
- Go, Rust and Python footer renderers can read Pi's footer data provider (`getGitBranch`, `getExtensionStatuses`, `getAvailableProviderCount`, `onBranchChange`) from their context, as the Node runtime always could: `Context.FooterData()` (Go), `Context::footer_data()` (Rust), `ctx.footer_data` (Python). The footer renders again when the host's footer data changes, and the surface matrix now has rows for the four members.
- The SDK surface matrix now expands the members of a callback parameter's type (`ReadonlyFooterDataProvider`, the third argument of a `ui.setFooter` factory) into rows, so a runtime without them shows as a hole. The generator names the types to expand in `callbackExpandable`.
- The Go, Rust, Python and Node example extensions each add a `count_words` tool with a required `text` parameter and an error result, and the example run test calls it through the real host in all four SDKs.
- Extension components opened with `ctx.ui.custom` receive mouse events in fullscreen mode, as in Pi: clicks, presses, drags, wheel and hover with coordinates local to the component. Go (`sdk.MouseHandler`), Rust (`handle_mouse`), Python (`handle_mouse`) and Node (Pi's `handleMouse`) extensions all get them, and select and settings lists in component-kit views respond to clicks and the wheel. In Tern, clicking an item in an extension's overlay or dock view reaches the extension exactly as the same click in a terminal would, scrolling a list to the item first, and a double click on it arrives as the terminal's second click (`clickCount` 2), so a double click opens or plays a list row as it does in a terminal.
- `--no-stdin` skips reading piped stdin, so a caller that leaves the stdin pipe open no longer hangs startup (Pi #10415). Without the flag PiG still waits for end of input, as Pi does.
- `chord/host`: links deflate frame payloads (call args, result values, state snapshots) whose JSON exceeds `PackThreshold` (64K characters). Memory and IPC links send them as `z: [field, base64]`, WebSocket links as one binary message (`[u32 envelope length][envelope JSON][deflated payload]`). A relaying host forwards the deflated bytes unread; the host that uses the payload inflates it. `AcceptWebSockets` deflates what it sends to clients and refuses packed frames from them; `NewWebSocketLink` takes `WebSocketLinkOptions{Compress, AcceptPacked}`, `WebSocketLike.Send` sends a string as text and a `[]byte` as binary, and `InflateMessage` turns a binary message back into its text frame (Pi 018c87dd).
- `chord/host`: links are flow controlled end to end. Both ends announce `Acks` in their hello and acknowledge what they processed (`AckFrame`, a cumulative frame count). While more than `LinkOptions.Window` (default 256 KiB) of sent frames is unacknowledged, state frames wait at the sender, coalesced per subscription; a slot that outgrows four windows becomes a reset, or a fresh downstream subscription on a relay. Results and other frames are sent at once. `AcceptOptions.MaxCalls` answers calls beyond it with `unavailable` (reason `too_many_calls`) and keeps the link (Pi db6497c3).
- PiG reads and writes Pi's `sessionShareDestination` setting (`auto`, `gist`, or `radius`; Pi cea25743) through `SettingsManager` (`GetSessionShareDestination`, `SetSessionShareDestination`), so a settings file shared with Pi keeps the value. PiG's `/share` still uploads only to `https://pi-in-go.dev` for every value, and `/settings` has no item for it (D64).
- `PI_TUI_WRITE_LOG` records the terminal write stream as Pi does: a file path is appended to, an existing directory gets `tui-<date>_<time>-<pid>.log`, and cursor, title, progress, start and stop sequences are not logged.
- Added the `Model scope: …` line Pi prints before the interactive UI starts when models are scoped (`--models` or `enabledModels`) and the startup details are shown, with the thinking levels and the cycle key in Pi's dim and muted colours.
- `bench/k8s` runs PiG's Durable benchmarks on Kubernetes. `go run ./bench/k8s/cmd/k8sbench` submits one Guaranteed-QoS Job per session, runs PiG and pi-durable interleaved in the same pod on the same pinned cores, records the node's kernel, CPU, cpuset, CFS quota and throttling, and writes durable-report's tracker format. `calibrate` measures the reference's noise floor, and `compare` refuses to report when it exceeds `-max-cv`. `bench/k8s/image` builds the pinned benchmark image. Bundle mode needs no registry: the pod runs the pinned official Node image, and the runner copies a prebuilt artifact bundle (`k8sbench bundle`) into it with `kubectl cp`. The pod checks the bundle's and each artifact's sha256 before it runs. On shared cores the noise gate judges CPU time (the cgroup's usage and each turn's process CPU) and the deterministic store size, and reports wall times as informational (`-gate auto|wall|cpu`). A calibration judges a size only from 12 runs. `-pick-node` runs on the node with the most free CPU (allocatable minus requests), and `-spread N` or `-nodes` run one Job per node at once and report the median over them. With `-nodes`, `-spread` and `-pick-node` choose among the named candidates. Without permission to list pods in all namespaces or to list nodes, picking falls back to the namespace's own pods and the candidates. A profile may have several pig targets; each writes its own tracker line per size under its own rule. A session refuses measured cores whose cpufreq governor scales the clock (`-allow-frequency-scaling` runs it unjudged, and such a calibration cannot vouch for a comparison), and records the cores' clock per process. `k8sbench bundle -agents` also carries PiG, Pi's coding agent installed from Pi's published install lockfile, and a pinned standalone CPython, each checked against its pinned SHA-256. The built-in profile `longhistory` runs `bench/evals`' long-history bench (sessions of about 1M and 3M tokens: RPC open, resume and appended turns, TUI first output, keystrokes, streaming frames, resize redraws and `/resume`) on PiG and on Pi in the pod with `pigbench k8s`. See [Benchmarks on Kubernetes](docs/site/docs/benchmarking.md).
- A Piglet can put a strip list in keep mode with `strip.keep.<list>`: the list names the built-ins that stay, and every other built-in of that list is stripped, including built-ins a later PiG adds, so a minimal base stays minimal. `keep.extensions: []` keeps no built-in extension. One list is in deny mode or keep mode in one Piglet file, keep IDs are checked like deny IDs, keep mode is inherited down `extends`, a child can narrow but not leave keep mode, and `extends.remove.strip` re-enables single IDs in both modes with `extends.allowWiden`. `pig piglet show` prints a keep-mode list as `extensions  keep([])` above the expanded rows.
- Piglet Binary records keep the strip table of the PiG that built them (`stripTable`) and a keep-mode Piglet's keep lists (`stripKeep`). When a Binary of the Piglet was built before, `pig piglet build` and `pig piglet show` print a strip delta report: the built-ins this PiG adds since that build, which a keep-mode list leaves out and which a deny-mode list now includes.
- The component kit's tool cards and `!` commands take Pi 1.2.0's `outputPad` and `setOutputPad`, and a tool result carries the final result's `durationMs`, which a shell card draws as `Took 1.2s` (`docs/plan/extension-component-kit.md` §2.1). Go extensions set `ToolExecution.OutputPad`, `BashExecution.OutputPad` and `ToolResult.DurationMs` and call `SetOutputPad`; Rust extensions call `set_output_pad` and set `ToolResult::duration_ms`; Python extensions pass `output_pad=` and `duration_ms=` and call `set_output_pad`. A Node extension that uses these on Pi's own components keeps its structure for a frontend.
- The `ai` Go package gains `RetryProviderRequest`, the provider retry loop with `noRetryStatuses`, `KnownProvider` constants, and `JsonValue`. Classifier requests retry through the same loop.
- `tools.CreateReadToolDefinition`, `CreatePowerShellToolDefinition`, `CreateEditToolDefinition`, `CreateWriteToolDefinition`, `CreateGrepToolDefinition`, `CreateFindToolDefinition` and `CreateLsToolDefinition` return the built-in tools as extension `ToolDefinition`s, as Pi's `createXToolDefinition` do.
- Pi Durable scans take an `Order` (`durable.ScanAscending` or `ScanDescending`) in `ConversationQuery`, `EntryQuery`, `TaskQuery` and `SubmissionQuery`. A cursor carries its order and rejects a query that asks for the other one. `Conversation.Entries` passes the order through.
- Task records carry `StartedAt` and `EndedAt`, stamped by the Session on the first change to running and on the change to terminal. `session.CreateSession` takes an optional clock.
- A conversation's context can be read as of an earlier entry with `ContextOptions{At}`, the view a fork at that entry starts with.
- `durable/storage/sqlite/cloudflare` adapts the SQLite storage of a Cloudflare Durable Object to Pi Durable's SQLite storage.
- Tools and hooks get the Harness's `Models()`. `HarnessSettings.ContextRetentionMs` (default ten minutes) sets how long an idle conversation keeps its derived context.
- Tool results carry `durationMs`, the milliseconds the tool's `execute` took; `ai.ToolResultMessage.DurationMs` is nil for calls that did not run. `durable/harness.AppendToolResult` takes the duration.
- A `user_bash` handler in any SDK can return Pi's `{ operations }` result. The `BashOperations` object stays in the extension, and the host runs the user's command through its `exec` with `onData`, `signal`, `timeout` and `env`, so an SSH-style extension runs `!` commands remotely. Node returns Pi's object as is, Go returns `sdk.BashOperations`, Python returns an object with an `exec` method, and Rust returns `ctx.bash_operations(...)`.
- `extension.API.RegisterNativeProvider` is the `registerProvider(provider)` overload and takes Pi's `Provider` (`ai.ModelsProvider`).
- The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs have `parseFrontmatter`, `stripFrontmatter` and `Date.parse` (`sdk.ParseFrontmatter`, `pig_sdk.parse_frontmatter`, `pig_sdk::parse_frontmatter` and the date forms), answered by the host's own YAML and date parsers so every language reads a header as Pi does, object key order included. A call made while the factory runs connects to the host itself.
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export Pi's `convertToLlm` and `formatDimensionNote`.
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export `prepareBranchEntries` and `collectEntriesForBranchSummary` (Pi's branch-summarization helpers), checked against Pi's own code in nine cells.
- The Rust SDK's `serialize_conversation_json` reads a conversation from JSON text and keeps an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate, as Pi's `serializeConversation` does; `serialize_conversation` over `serde_json::Value` shares its code.
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export Pi's `serializeConversation`. Go reads raw JSON (`[]json.RawMessage`), because a decoded map loses the order in which a tool call's arguments are printed.
- `tui.Key`, the helper for building key identifiers (`Key.Ctrl("c")`, `Key.Escape`), matching Pi's `Key`.
- `extension.API.OnCacheWarmingDecision` registers a typed `cache_warming_decision` handler (Pi's `ExtensionHandler<CacheWarmingDecisionEvent, CacheWarmingDecisionEventResult>`); the known-gap entries for it are gone.
- `SessionManager.setSessionFile` switches an existing session manager to another session file, as Pi does for resume and branching: it loads a file with entries, initialises an empty file with a header, and starts a new session at a missing path. The manager keeps its working directory, and an in-memory manager never writes the file.
- `DynamicBorder` takes a color function, as Pi's constructor does.
- `ParseSessionEntries` parses session JSONL content as Pi's `parseSessionEntries` does, and `BuildSessionProjection` takes the entries and an optional leaf, as Pi's `buildSessionProjection` does.
- Facet bundle builds accept `Minify`, `Define`, `Platform` and `Target`, as Pi's `bundleFacets` does.
- `make perf-lint` has six more rules: `chan-hop` (a channel send or receive, `make(chan)`, a `go` statement or an `AfterFunc` in a hot function), `open-per-call`, `timer-per-call`, `decode-own-encode`, `nested-marshal` and `pool-uncapped`. A function is hot when its doc comment carries `//perf:hot` (or portlint's `//pig:hotpath`); the event, render, keystroke, extension connection and pipe-read entry points are marked. Their sites are ratcheted in the shrink-only baseline with a class each.
- `BenchmarkPrintPipelineDeltas` (packages `coding` and `coding/cli`) reports ns, process CPU (getrusage), bytes and allocations per 16-byte tool-call delta through `-p` text, JSON with and without an in-process `message_update` extension, and RPC event forwarding, at 1k and 10k deltas.
- `TestStreamWakesPerDelta` ratchets goroutine wake-ups per streamed delta (runtime/trace, GOMAXPROCS=1) at 5.36 measured, ceiling 5.5.
- The scoped-wait benchmarks (`BenchmarkObservation*`) measure the waiting path, with the producer on another goroutine, besides the queued one, and report process CPU.
- `bench/lw/row.sh` runs Pi-Bolt's own bench tools (`bolt-v0.8.5`, pinned commit) against `pig` built from the tree and the Pi-Bolt 0.8.5 release (pinned sha256): `large_write.py --sizes 50,200`, `long_answer.py --sizes 20000,60000`, `benchmark.py --scenarios interactive` and `long_session.py --prompts 75`, with each build twice per round (A/A), N rounds in rotating order, every tool invocation under the global bench lock. It writes the raw logs and one normalized JSONL to `tmp/lw-bench/<wave>/`, prints the table (medians, A/A spread, pig over Pi-Bolt) and checks the `lw.row.*.ratio_vs_bolt` ceilings in `automation/perf/ratchet.toml`.
- `PIG_PROFILE=metrics` records report `goroutines_created` (goroutines started over the process's life) and `auth_file_stats` / `models_file_stats` (revision stats of `auth.json` and `models.json`).
- `TestPrintReadToolsProcessGate` runs Pi-Bolt's headless scenario in `pig -p` (four `read` calls on 47-239 KB files, then the answer) and ratchets its allocations, bytes, GC cycles, goroutines started and `auth.json` stats at the measured values: 85,898 allocations, 13.0 MB, 4 GC cycles, 535 goroutines and 433 `auth.json` stats per run.
- `bench/lw/row.sh --rows headless,startup` runs Pi-Bolt's `benchmark.py` headless and startup scenarios against pig and Pi-Bolt 0.8.5; `rows.py check` holds the headless row to 1.7x Pi-Bolt's instructions on darwin and to Pi-Bolt's CPU time on linux.
- `bench/lw/row.sh` records the peak RSS of each pig and Pi-Bolt run of the interactive, headless and startup rows (`peak_rss_mb` in `runs.jsonl`, from `benchmark.py`'s `ru_maxrss`). On linux, `rows.py check` holds the headless row to 0.50x and the interactive row to 0.46x Pi-Bolt 0.8.5's peak RSS. Those ceilings are the values measured at e85ec79e85 (0.43 and 0.40) plus about 15%.
- A Piglet Binary can carry TypeScript and JavaScript extensions. Before, `pig piglet build --format binary` of any Piglet with a Node extension failed with `node subprocess requires a runtime`, and a Piglet that strips `node-extensions` got that error instead of its strip conflict. The build now embeds each Node extension's sources, and the Binary runs them with the installed Node (22.13 or newer) through the same path Stock PiG uses, so the extension registers and behaves as it does under Stock PiG. Without a suitable Node the Binary reports the extension and the required Node version and exits, as Stock PiG does. See "TypeScript and JavaScript extensions" in `docs/site/docs/piglet-binaries.md`.
- `PIG_PROFILE=metrics` writes `pig-<pid>-metrics.json` at exit: allocation and GC-cycle counts for the whole run, the live heap, peak RSS, and the goroutines, open files and child processes still held at exit.
- `make perf-scoreboard` renders `docs/performance/scoreboard.md`, the list of PiG's performance and resource metrics with their current counts, ratchet gates, targets and owners; `make perf-scoreboard-measure` re-measures the counts and `make perf-ratchet-check` rejects a raised ceiling in `automation/perf/ratchet.toml`.
- Added `pig piglet tree`, `pig piglet lineage` and `pig piglet verify --source` (D170): product-neutral, offline diagnostics. `tree` arranges the installed Piglets by `extends` (current and installed marked, `--depth`, `--json`); `lineage` prints a Piglet's `extends` chain root first with each link's version, digest and what it adds, removes or alters (extensions, packages, skills, tools, permissions, slots, strip, settings), or a signed Binary's recorded identity; `verify --source` recomputes a source's digests and exits 1 when the Binary's signed manifest records different ones.
- `PIG_PIGLET_GITHUB_URL` points `pig piglet pull github:...`, `pig piglet publish --to npm --binaries` and `pig piglet update` at a loopback release server for testing. It requires `PIG_PIGLET_PULL_ALLOW_LOOPBACK_HTTP=1` and refuses any non-loopback host. Unset, nothing changes.
- The `codemode` Go package has `NewInlineSandbox`, a sandbox whose VM runs under an interrupt budget (`InterruptBudget`, default `DefaultInterruptBudget`, 100,000 polls) instead of a worker, as Pi's `InlineCodemodeSandbox` does ([748b351f](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/commit/748b351f)). A script that computes past the budget fails with `ErrorTimeout` and the message `Execution exceeded its interrupt budget of N`.
- The `codemode` tool row shows `Elapsed` while the script runs and `Took` with the recorded duration once it finishes, like bash rows (Pi 014873d7, [#10708](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10708)). The timer is shared with the shell renderers, and a script sends one update before its first nested call so the row's clock starts.
- `extension.ToolDefinition.ReachesTools` and `extension.ToolInfo.ReachesTools` (Pi 24382bed `reachesTools`): a tool that reaches other tools declares which ones a call with a given input may reach (`extension.ToolReach`: all tools, or the words of the input that may name them). `codemode` reports every tool for a script that uses `searchTools`, `describeNamespace`, `describeTool` or `ALL_TOOLS`, and the words of the script otherwise; `tool_search` reports every tool. `extension.ToolNamespace.Aliases` are other names `describeNamespace()` and the `namespace` option of `searchTools()` accept, replacing the rule that matched the part of a name after its last `__`.
- Codemode has a Cloudflare entry, as Pi 1.2.0 does ([748b351f](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/commit/748b351f)): the Go package `codemode/cloudflare` (`CreateInlineSandbox`, `SandboxDurableObject`, `DefaultRemoteMemoryLimitBytes`, `QuickJSWasm`) and a Durable Object for Workers, `CodemodeSandboxDurableObject`, that runs the VM of a remote sandbox in its own isolate while the tools run in the caller. The object is a TinyGo build of the codemode package with QuickJS as a second static Wasm module, because Workers compile no Wasm from bytes. It speaks Pi's remote protocol, so Pi's `RemoteCodemodeSandbox` is a client of it, and so is a PiG `RemoteSandbox` whose `Remote` reaches it (no Go transport to a Durable Object is shipped; the behavior tests drive a `RemoteSandbox` against the module in a Node process). The limits (100,000 interrupt polls, 32 MiB of VM memory) apply to every request. See `durable/core/host/cf/src/codemode/README.md`.
- Durable deferred tools: `ToolRegistration.Deferred` keeps a tool out of the model's tools until `modelTools` names it. `modelTools` gains `{remove?, add?}`, and `control.AddTools` (and `harness.AddTools`) write the loaded names to `add`, so a load only appends to the declared tools. `Agent.Deferred` lists the deferred tools not loaded yet, and the `codemode` section leaves them out (Pi 77332f7a).
- `durable/toolsearch`: the `tool_search` tool and extension for durable conversations, ranking deferred tools with BM25 and loading the matches with `control.AddTools`.
- The Cloudflare entry of codemode carries Pi's TypeScript client (Pi 1.2.0): `CodemodeSandboxBase`, `RemoteCodemodeSandbox`, `InlineCodemodeSandbox` and `createInlineCodemodeSandbox` (`durable/core/host/cf/src/codemode/{cloudflare,portable}.ts`). The inline sandbox runs the script on the caller's thread over the codemode module, and Pi's `sandbox.test.ts` and `portable.test.ts` run against it, with the `wasm` option being the pair of compiled modules.
- `codemode.Sandbox.Start` runs a script deterministically with the caller answering its calls, as Pi 1.2.0's `CodemodeSandboxBase.start()` does ([0fb1aee7](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/commit/0fb1aee7)): the VM runs in UTC with a virtual clock that starts at `StartedAt` and moves with the delivered results, randomness from `Seed`, no `WeakRef` or `FinalizationRegistry`, and results applied strictly in `seq` order. `Run.Deliver` answers a call, `OnStarted` reports the VM's fingerprint, `OnIdle` the calls issued after each result, and a result that ended the script carries `During`. A rejected answer can carry properties for the script's `Error`. The remote protocol carries the same fields. The Cloudflare Worker module runs it too, and the Worker host's TypeScript client has Pi's `start()`, `CodemodeRun` and the types around them (`durable/core/host/cf/src/codemode`).
- `durable/codemode` is durable code mode, as Pi 1.2.0's `@earendil-works/pi-durable/codemode` is: a `codemode` tool (`CreateExtension`/`CreateTool` over a `codemode.Sandbox`) whose scripts call the conversation's tools as nested calls and survive a restart. The script is replayed deterministically (`Sandbox.Start`) from a journal of the results it got, written before each delivery, and reattaches to its nested calls by key; then it continues. Divergence, a changed sandbox fingerprint or module version, and a passed deadline end the call with an error result, and closing the sandbox while a script runs decides nothing, so the next process resumes it. `store()` and `load()` values live per conversation (`StoreDoc`, rewindable, forked as of the fork's entry). The result's details list the script's tool calls (`ToolDetails`). A tool call whose arguments are not an object is rejected inside the script with the tool schema's validation message, as Pi's nested call does, but without a nested task or tool events (ExecuteTool takes a map).
- `durable/codemode`: the code mode tool lists the tools scripts can call (a `codemode` prompt section of TypeScript declarations within a token budget, grouped by namespace), finds the rest with `searchTools()`, `describeTool()` and `describeNamespace()`, lays out a script's result under a `Script completed` header with a token budget, a spill file for the complete text and files for images, gives scripts `models.*` (catalog, `classify()`, `generateImages()` as hidden internal tools with at most four calls at once), and declares the Lark grammar of the script source. A schema tool resolves with its value also on an error result; a schema-less tool with its text. `ModuleVersion` is 2.
- `codemode.Run.Finished` reports whether a `Sandbox.Start` run has its outcome (the script ended, timed out or failed, or the run was aborted), before `Result` returns, as Pi 1.2.0's `CodemodeRun.finished` does. The Worker host's TypeScript client has the same property.
- The `codemode` Go package has `RemoteSandbox` and `ServeRemote`, Pi's `RemoteCodemodeSandbox` and `serveCodemodeRemote` ([748b351f](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/commit/748b351f)). A `RemoteSandbox` keeps the tools, the call records, the deadline and the abort in the caller and runs each script's VM on a `Remote`, such as a Durable Object stub; `ServeRemote` is the remote side, running the VM under an interrupt budget and memory limit capped at the remote's `ServeOptions`. Output is batched, one tool call crosses per exchange, and the caller tells the remote to stop when it finishes first. A remote that fails, vanishes or cannot be reached fails the execution with `ErrorSandbox`.
- `pi-env` daemon: the `listFiles` operation, ported with Pi's ignore-crate semantics (aa499c242): a depth-first listing of a directory tree as `fd --hidden --exclude .git` lists it, without following symbolic links, skipping what `.gitignore` files, `.git/info/exclude` and the global excludes file ignore inside git work trees, bounded by `maxEntries`, `maxBytes` and `timeoutMs` with a `truncated` flag and stopped between entries by a cancel. `hello` reports `features: ["listFiles"]`.
- `env.RemoteExecutionEnv.ListFiles` and `env.LocalExecutionEnv` (a `NodeExecutionEnv` plus `ListFiles` through a daemon on the same machine); a daemon without the feature answers `not_supported`. `env.NodePlatform` maps the daemon's Rust platform and architecture names to Node's; `RemoteInfo.Features` carries `hello.features`. `durable/env` gains the optional `FileLister` capability, and the environment conformance suite checks it when `ListFiles` is set.
- The pi-env daemon runs programs in pseudo-terminals for interactive terminals: `pty` with raw output, ordered input (`ptyWrite`), resizing (`ptyResize`) and the foreground job (`ptyForeground`), with the program's process group killed on cancel or a lost connection. The Go package `env` gains `OpenTerminal`, `RemoteTerminal` and `LocalDaemon`. Terminals are not supported on Windows yet.
- Added `+name` and `-name` entries to `--tools`, which change the default tool selection instead of replacing it, for example `pig -t +codemode`. A reload keeps tools removed with `-name` removed.
- Added program status reporting with OSC 7501: terminals and agent dashboards that support it see whether PiG is working, blocked on a dialog or login, done, or failed. `PI_PROGRAM_STATUS=1|0` overrides detection (see [Terminal setup](docs/site/docs/terminal-setup.md#program-status)). The reported `app` is `pig`.
- Added `aborted` to `agent_settled` session, extension, and JSON/RPC events, so integrations can tell a cancelled run from a finished one.
- Added `durationMs` to the tool render context (`ToolRenderContext`), to `tool_execution_end` events, and to stored tool result messages: how long the tool's `execute()` took, measured with a monotonic clock and excluding hooks.
- Added `durationMs` to assistant messages: `AssistantMessageEventStream` measures each response with a monotonic clock from the start of the request to its final message, for every API implementation. Deferred results fetched later stay untimed.
- Added `outputPad` to the tool render context (`context.outputPad` in Node extensions, `OutputPad`/`output_pad` in the Go, Rust and Python SDKs). Renderers with `renderShell: "self"` apply it themselves.
- Added `Box.SetPaddingX(...)` and `Text.SetPaddingX(...)` to the TUI package.
- `chord/host` ports Pi 1.2.0's Chord state cells (`cell.ts`, `publish.ts`): `Cell` (`NewCell`, `Change`, `ChangeValue`, `Replace`, `Reset`, `Subscribe`, `Events`), `Derived` and the publication queue. A write recomputes dependent derivations before it is adopted, and observers see revisions in adoption order, also when a listener writes again.
- `chord/host` ports Pi 1.2.0's `@earendil-works/chord/node` host manager (`manager.ts`): `CreateHostManager` (`Start`, `Stop`, `Replace`, `Liveness`, `KeepAlive`), `ForkDriver`, which forks a Node child with Node's IPC channel, `ChildControl` and `ProcessLock`. `ForkDriver` and `ProcessChannel` also work on Windows, where Node's IPC pipe is handed to the child as a CRT descriptor and framed as libuv does.
- `chord/host` ports Chord's registries (`Registry`, `DefineRegistry`, `RegistryTable`, `RegistryView`, `Registration`) and the `Handle` interface that a get returns.
- `chord/host` ports Pi 1.2.0's links: the `Link` interface, `MemoryLinkPair` (with `Sever`), `NewIpcLink` over an `IpcChannel` and `NewWebSocketLink` over a `WebSocketLike`. A received value that is not a valid frame arrives as `MalformedFrame`.
- `chord/host` ports Pi 1.2.0's records, connections and remote handles: `ServeOptions`, `Factory`, `Self`, `LinkOptions` and `AcceptOptions`, with the record, connection and remote-handle runtime the host uses.
- `PIG_VERSION_FORMAT=pi` makes `pig --version` and `pig -v` print the pinned Pi version, which is what `pi --version` prints, for launchers that check a minimum Pi version and reject PiG's composite version. Every other value keeps the composite output, and `pig version`, the banner, updates, and SDK versions do not change (D63). Thanks to @PJalv.
- `pig extension upgrade` rewrites a Go `before_agent_start` handler that reads `data["systemPrompt"]` as a string, in a module that requires an SDK before 0.5.0: `prompt := data["systemPrompt"].(string)` and `prompt, _ := data["systemPrompt"].(string)` become `prompt, err := data["systemPrompt"].(*sdk.SystemPrompt).Get()` with the error returned, and the ok form reads `prompt, ok := systemPrompt, true` after the same call. Such a read still compiles against SDK 0.5.0, so the build failure check cannot name it.
- A public bug fix ledger, `docs/site/docs/bug-ledger.md` (also `docs/bug-ledger.json`), lists every bug fixed since the previous release: symptom, root cause, user impact, platform, fix commits, guarding tests, and whether the defect was PiG's own, a Pi defect PiG fixed first (with its divergence number, Pi issue, and the condition that removes the difference), or a Pi fix PiG ported. `make bug-ledger` generates it from the commit history, the changelog and the divergence records. `make bug-ledger-check` runs in CI and fails when a fix has no entry or an entry names a test that does not exist.
- `make release-notes-draft` writes draft release notes for the current release from the changelog, the bug fix ledger and the divergence records, and never overwrites an existing draft.
- The documentation drift gate now covers the README, the website and bundle pages, and the SDK and Piglet pages. It extracts every fenced code sample and checks it: JSON, YAML and TOML must parse, Go samples must parse and import real packages, complete Go programs must compile, JavaScript, TypeScript and Python must pass their interpreters' syntax checks, and Piglet YAML must pass PiG's own parser. It checks `pig` command lines, flags, `pig docs show` topics, settings keys, keybinding IDs, environment variables, slash commands, `make` targets, Go identifiers, relative links and anchors, and the pinned Pi and toolchain versions against the binary and the source.
- The install pages document the variables `install.sh` reads (`PIG_VERSION`, `PIG_INSTALL_DIR`, `PIG_API_BASE`, `PIG_LATEST_RELEASE_URL`, `PIG_DOWNLOAD_BASE`, `PIG_UPDATE_URL`, `PIG_HOME`).
- The bug fix ledger accounts for every divergence from Pi added since the previous release: a divergence that fixes a Pi defect links to its entry, with the Pi issue it cites and the condition that removes it, and the others are listed as deliberate differences. `make bug-ledger-check` fails when a divergence is missing.
- `coding.DefaultResourceLoaderOptions` takes `ExtensionFactories` and `EventBus`, as Pi's `DefaultResourceLoader` options do. The loader runs each `builtin:<name>` path with the built-in entry of that name and loads the other factories last as `<inline:name>`. It leaves out a replaceable built-in that another extension replaces, with a warning. File, built-in and inline extensions register against one runtime per reload, and a trust reload runs each factory once.
- The new `coding/extensionset` package holds the in-process half of the extension set that `pig` and `DefaultResourceLoader` share: inline and built-in factory loading and the replaced-extension filter.
- A Piglet can name a Go frontend member under `slots.frontend` (D91). `pig piglet build` fuses it into the Piglet Binary, and the member then draws the interactive mode in place of the ANSI renderer from a retained tree of nodes (`extensions/sdk/frontend`). Stock PiG fuses no member and paints as before. See "Frontend members" in `docs/site/docs/piglet-binaries.md`.
- Added `PI_CLIPBOARD_OSC52=1`, which sends copied text to the terminal with OSC 52 after the local clipboard write, so copies reach the clipboard of a remote client that PiG cannot detect (a multiplexer attached from another machine, a remote IDE daemon).
- `getAllTools()` carries each tool's `reachesTools` to extensions of every runtime, as Pi 1.2.0's `ToolInfo` does (`types.ts:2122-2130`, `agent-session.ts:1507`), so a `tool_call` handler can ask which tools a call may reach whether the tool is built in, another extension's or its own. The host marks such a tool `"reachesTools": true` and runs the hook for the new `tool.reachesTools` call; the Node runtime turns the mark into a synchronous function in the same member position, the Go SDK adds `ToolInfo.ReachesTools` (it panics with the host's error, as the hook throws), the Rust SDK adds `ToolInfo::reaches_tools` (a `ToolInfoReachesTools` whose `call` returns `io::Result<ToolReach>`), and the Python SDK's `get_all_tools()` puts a callable under `reachesTools`. The Node runtime now answers `tool_reaches_tools` on receipt, so an extension that runs its own or a packed neighbor's hook from inside a host call is not stuck waiting for itself. Rust code that builds a `ToolInfo` literal needs the new field.
- A Piglet can strip built-ins with a `strip` key: built-in tools, slash commands, built-in extensions (`mcp`, `codemode`, `tool-search`, `pig-login`, `llama.cpp`), model APIs (`bedrock-converse-stream`, `google-vertex`, `mistral-conversations`) and features such as `themes`, `skills`, `node-extensions`, `syntax-highlight`, `export-html`, `self-update`, `changelog` and `docs`. Strip lists inherit through `extends` and come off with `extends.remove.strip`. `pig piglet show` lists each stripped ID in its `Slots` section. Stock PiG and a Piglet without `strip` are unchanged (D92).
- A Piglet Binary leaves the code of a stripped built-in extension, model API or feature out of the executable. A request for it reports `<what> is stripped from this Piglet (strip.<list>: <id>)` (D92).
- A stripped built-in leaves every surface that offers it: `--help`, `pig piglet` usage, `pig config`, `/hotkeys`, the startup hints, slash autocomplete, `/settings`, `/login`, the system prompt docs section, the extension API and RPC `get_commands`. A stripped command's keys do nothing, and a stripped `bash` tool also stops `!` and `!!` user bash (D92).
- The models of a stripped model API are not offered by `--list-models`, `/model`, model cycling, `--models`, the startup default, RPC `get_available_models` or an extension's model registry, including `models.json` models on that API. `--model` or `--provider` naming one reports the strip (D92).
- A Piglet frontend member receives a tool result's images on its tool card (`ToolCard.Images`), the ones Pi shows after the output: while `terminal.showImages` is on, each with its bytes, MIME type, SHA-256 and the width `terminal.imageWidthCells` gives it. The frontend draws them as native images that open in its viewer on a click. Terminal output is unchanged.
- A Piglet frontend member also receives the images a user message carries, such as a pasted screenshot (`MarkdownText.Images`). The frontend shows them as thumbnails above the message that open in its viewer on a click. The terminal still shows only the message text, as Pi does.
- A Piglet can strip the `/piglet` inspection command with `strip.commands: [/piglet]`, so a Piglet that wants only Pi's commands can remove the one PiG-only command an active Piglet adds. The command then leaves autocomplete, RPC `get_commands` and an extension's `getCommands`, and typed `/piglet` goes to the model as an unknown slash command does in Pi. The Piglet's `--piglet` flag, tool scoping and system prompt are unchanged (D92).
- Add `make test-changed`, which tests only the packages a change can affect: the packages that hold, embed or name the changed files, and every package whose tests import them. Edited packages run under `-race`. It prints the output of failed tests, including a test still running when its package timed out or crashed, and the slowest tests, and keeps the `go test -json` events in `tmp/test-changed/`. `docs/project/test-economy.md` describes which tier of tests to run when.
- `make test` now fails a test group that leaves a process running after `go test` returns, and kills that process.
- `pig acp install` builds the `pig-acp` companion from the installed `npm:@pi-in-go/pigpen-acp` Package with the local Go toolchain, offline, and puts it next to `pig`. The `/acp` screen now says plainly when `pig-acp` is missing and how to get it. Reported by @klonuo (#210).

### Changed

- **Breaking (config):** Custom models in models.json now need an api at the model or provider level, as in Pi; PiG previously defaulted it. Add "api" to the provider or model to fix the startup error. The error reads `Provider "<provider>": Provider <provider>, model <model>: no "api" specified. Set at provider or model level.`, as in Pi. A model whose contextWindow or maxTokens is zero or negative is rejected the same way (`Provider <provider>, model <model>: invalid contextWindow`).
- PiG follows Pi 1.1.0. `pig --version` prints `<PiG release>+1.1.0`.
- The model catalog follows Pi 1.0.4.
- `--tools` keeps MCP tools unless an entry starts with `mcp__`, so `pig --tools codemode` no longer loses every MCP server. `--tools` and `--exclude-tools` accept `*` patterns, for example `--tools read,codemode,'mcp__radius__*'`. `--no-tools` and `--exclude-tools 'mcp__*'` remove MCP tools. See `docs/cli.md`.
- The default system prompt leaves tools that a tool hides with `prepareLoadout` out of its tool list and rules. The skills hint names no tool when the file reader is hidden. Codemode shows each tool's prompt guidelines with its declaration. `ToolLoadout` gains `getPromptGuidelines()` in the Go, Python, Rust and Node SDKs.
- **Breaking (durable):** `NodeExecutionEnv.Watch` fails with `permission_denied` when a target, or a watched directory, cannot be read for lack of permission; before, it was watched as if missing. A later rescan that hits this reports `permission_denied` instead of `invalid`. The environment conformance suite checks three more `Watch` behaviors, so a custom environment that passed the 1.0.3 suite may need changes.
- **Breaking (Go API):** `agent.NewAgent` returns `(*Agent, error)`. It fails with `ErrNoDefaultStreamFunction` when no `StreamFn`, `DefaultStreamFn` or configured default exists, as Pi's `Agent` constructor throws. A model's `Provider` no longer stands in for the stream function.
- **Breaking (Go API):** The TUI uses Pi's names and input pipeline: `OpenOverlay` is `ShowOverlay` and `FocusedComponent` is `GetFocusedComponent`; `AddInputListener`, `RemoveInputListener`, `OnTerminalColorSchemeChange`, `SetTerminalColorSchemeNotifications`, `RunInputListeners`, `ConsumeTerminalColorSchemeReport` and an exported `CompositeTuiLine`. `Start` and `Stop` re-enable and disable terminal light/dark reports, so the external editor, suspend and renderer swap no longer write them by hand.
- Durable turns no longer re-read and re-decode the whole stored history for every model call. SQLite storage keeps a bounded working set of decoded entries (64 MiB by default; `NodeSqliteStorageOptions.WorkingSetBytes` changes it, 0 turns it off) that commits write through and a change made through another connection resets, and the Harness extends each conversation's derived context by the entries committed since. A warm turn on a 3,500-turn history reads about 540 rows (pi-durable 1.0.4 reads about 212,000), and the store format is unchanged (D104). On the durable-bench workload at 3,500 turns, a warm PiG turn went from about 9.6 s to about 0.37 s on one host with the default GOMAXPROCS, and to about 0.2 s with GOMAXPROCS=1.
- Stored entries are decoded in one pass; any record the fast decoder does not recognize goes to the existing reflection codec, so reading is unchanged.
- The `ai` JSON clone and validation of plain tool arguments no longer round-trip through marshaling, and the faux provider's usage estimate no longer copies the transcript.
- `/settings` is Pi's settings selector: rows come from a `SettingsConfig`, each change reaches a `SettingsCallbacks` function, and the theme, warnings and per-model thinking submenus open inside the list. Changing the HTTP idle timeout or cache warming mode shows Pi's status line. The follow-up row names the key bound to `app.message.followUp`, not a fixed `alt+enter`.
- **Breaking (Go API):** `tools.ComputeEditsDiff` returns `EditDiffResult` or `EditDiffError` (`EditDiffOutcome`), as Pi's `computeEditsDiff` does, in place of `EditsDiffPreview`.
- `ReadOperations`, `GrepOperations` and `LsOperations` let a caller replace the filesystem under the read, grep and ls tools, as Pi's `operations` options do.
- `ToolsOptions`, the per-tool option types and `CreateReadTool` to `CreateLsTool` build tools as Pi's `createXTool(cwd, options)` do. `CreateCodingTools`, `CreateAllTools` and `CreateReadOnlyTools` take `(cwd, *ToolsOptions)`; `ToolsOptionsFromSettings` builds the options for a settings manager.
- `SkillFrontmatter` and `ParseSkillFrontmatter`; `extension.EventBusController` and `extension.CreateEventBus`.
- **Breaking (Go API):** Renamed to Pi's names: `InteractiveOptions` → `InteractiveModeOptions`, `StatusLine` → `FooterComponent`, `coding.Services` → `AgentSessionServices`, `coding.NewServices` → `CreateAgentSessionServices`, `coding.ServicesOptions` → `CreateAgentSessionServicesOptions`, `CLIFlags` → `Args` (its `Args` field is now `Messages`).
- `extensiontest.NewMemBus` returns an `extension.EventBusController`; a handler that panics no longer propagates to the emitter.
- **Breaking (Go API):** `SettingsManager.GetProviderRetrySettings` returns `*int` for `TimeoutMs` and `MaxRetries` (nil when unset); `RetryConfig.MaxDelayMs` is `MaxAgentDelayMs`. An unset provider `maxRetries` now reaches the request as unset.
- `Theme.Fg`, `Bg`, `FgText` and `BgText` panic with `Unknown theme color: <token>` for an unknown token, as Pi throws; `Theme` gains `Bold`, `Italic`, `Underline` and `Strikethrough`.
- `tools.GetShellConfig` takes the custom shell path, as Pi's `getShellConfig` does. `CreateSyntheticSourceInfo` builds the synthetic `SourceInfo` of CLI resources, tools and skills.
- **Breaking (Go API):** `Session.Compact` returns `(*CompactionResult, error)` and rejects a session with nothing to compact, as Pi's `compact()` does; `CompactResult` is gone. `Session.ExecuteBash(ctx, command, onChunk, *ExecuteBashOptions)` replaces `ExecuteBash`, `ExecuteBashWithUpdates` and `ExecuteBashWithOperations`. `Session.Prompt` returns only an error, as Pi's `prompt()` returns `void`.
- `extension.ExtensionUIDialogOptions` and `extension.WorkingIndicatorOptions` are typed structs instead of `any`. `ModelRuntime.GetAllAvailable` is added and `ModelRuntime.GetModels` takes an optional provider. `codingagent.PiSlashCommand` is `SlashCommandInfo`; the `/help` summary type is `HelpCommandInfo`.
- `KeybindingsManager.Get`, `ResolvedBindings` and `Conflicts` are `GetKeys`, `GetResolvedBindings` and `GetConflicts`; a conflict names its `Keybindings`. `GetDefinition` and `GetUserBindings` are added. `frontmatter.Strip` is upstream's `stripFrontmatter`.
- `extension.SourceInfo` and `extension.SlashCommandInfo` are typed structs. `extension.RegisteredTool` and `extension.ToolInfo` gain `Source` for PiG's per-tool source name, which used to be stored as a string in `SourceInfo`.
- **Breaking (Go API):** The coding-agent components in `tui` take Pi's names, with their constructors: `ModelSelector` → `ModelSelectorComponent`, `OAuthSelector` → `OAuthSelectorComponent`, `LoginDialog` → `LoginDialogComponent`, `UserMessageBlock` → `UserMessageComponent`, `BashExecutionBlock` → `BashExecutionComponent`, `AssistantMessageBlock` → `AssistantMessageComponent`, `UserMessageSelector` → `UserMessageSelectorComponent`, `TreeSelect` → `TreeSelectorComponent` (`NewTreeSelect` → `NewTreeSelectorComponent`), and the session selector is the exported `SessionSelectorComponent`.
- `BorderedLoader`, `ShowImagesSelectorComponent`, `ThemeSelectorComponent`, `ExtensionInputComponent`, `ExtensionSelectorComponent`, `ExtensionEditorComponent`, `CustomMessageComponent`, `UserMessageSelectorComponent`, `OAuthSelectorComponent`, `ModelSelectorComponent`, `LoginDialogComponent`, `AuthURL`, the Earendil announcement, `ScopedModelsList`, `SelectSubmenuComponent`, `SteppedSubmenu`, `UserMessageComponent`, `AssistantMessageComponent` and `BashExecutionComponent`, `ConfigSelectorComponent`, `TreeSelectorComponent`, `SessionSelectorComponent` and `ToolExecutionComponent` embed `tui.Container` and are built from child components as in Pi; their `Render` methods are gone and the output is unchanged.
- `tui.Container.InsertBefore(ref, comp)` is new; a custom session entry added while an assistant message streams now appears before that message, as in Pi.
- Custom session entries render through the new `CustomEntryComponent` (Pi `custom-entry.ts`): Ctrl+O re-runs the entry renderer with the new expansion state for every renderer, not only subprocess proxies, and a panicking renderer shows a `[type] renderer failed: ...` box.
- `codingagent.InteractiveSessionHandle` requires `Compact(ctx, customInstructions) (*CompactionResult, error)`, and `coding.CompactionResult` is an alias of `codingagent.CompactionResult`.
- The `/sprite` picker draws the pig of the highlighted sprite right after its option rows (a few cells past the longest row, top-aligned with the first option, and dropped on narrow terminals), so the choice shows its face before you make it. `Create your own...` draws no pig and keeps the full-width list. The list stays the one it was, and a UI context without a custom-component host (RPC) still gets that plain list (D2).
- The interactive startup header keeps its drawn lines until its width, theme, key bindings, sprite, expansion or startup details change, as Pi's header keeps its text. Before, every frame redrew the pig head and re-wrapped the key hints, about 60% of the objects a streaming turn allocated.
- Wrapping styled text no longer allocates a slice of parameters for every SGR code it tracks; the tracked state and the codes it re-emits are unchanged.
- `tui.JSNumberString` prints the integers 0 to 255 from a table, so truecolor sequences no longer format each channel; the output is unchanged.
- `tui.NewTreeSelectorComponentFrom` builds the tree selector with the arguments of Pi's `TreeSelectorComponent` constructor: the root nodes, current leaf, terminal height, `onSelect`, `onCancel`, `onLabelChange`, initial selected ID and initial filter mode. An empty tree calls `onCancel` after 100ms, on a timer goroutine. `NewTreeSelectorComponent(title, root)` is unchanged.
- A completions request encodes its history into one reused buffer and writes the token of each cached large string from the encoder instead of copying the payload with the tokens in it: at 75 turns of four 48 KiB reads, building the request allocates 233 KB where it allocated 617 KB (1.29 skeletons instead of 3.42). The request body is the same bytes.
- An errored Anthropic tool result now keeps its place for each image as the text note `[image omitted: error tool results cannot contain images]`, following Pi 34a8bb38 ([#10794](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10794)); PiG previously dropped the images and sent `(error)` for a result without text.
- The full-screen renderer reuses the last frame's processed row (line resets, tab expansion, width cut) for a row that did not change, which lowers the CPU of every frame, notably the spinner ticks and streamed answers. The bytes written to the terminal are unchanged.
- A streamed Anthropic `content_block_delta` record that holds one non-empty text, thinking, partial JSON or signature member is decoded without reflection; any other record (unknown or case-folded member names, members of another type, empty members, malformed JSON) is decoded exactly as before.
- Tool-call cards skip the validating parse of the argument text received so far until it can be a whole JSON value (it starts with `{` and ends with `}`, and so on). Streaming a 100 KB `write` call over 4000 deltas takes 2.7 ms in the card instead of 309 ms. The header appears when the last delta completes the object, as before.
- Each tool-call delta copies the received arguments once for the card and its renderers instead of twice.
- The proxy `contentIndex` member decodes into a number or a string, not an interface value.
- New gate `make any-trap` (`automation/ci/anytrap`) counts the places that decode JSON into `any`, `map[string]any`, `[]any` or a type parameter. The counts in `automation/ci/anytrap/baseline.toml` only shrink.
- Go SDK: `sdk.ToolNamespace.Aliases` is a `sdk.ToolNamespaceAliases`, built with `sdk.NewToolNamespaceAliases("docs")` and read with `Names()`, so `sdk.ToolNamespace` stays comparable (usable with `==` and as a map key) as it was in v0.4.1. The aliases still go on the wire as Pi's `aliases` string array, left out when there are none.
- Deeply nested Markdown emphasis (for example a run of 4,000 `*` around one letter, 8 MB of styled output as in Pi) renders in 0.16 s and 19 MB of allocation instead of 17 s and 16 GB, also inside thinking blocks, quotes and headings; the bytes are identical to Pi's. Ordinary Markdown allocates about 11% less.
- The embedded Node runtime archive is 7.6 MB instead of 10.6 MB (solid compression, identical files); first-run extraction takes the same time and later starts do not read it.
- A turn with an unchanged tool set no longer copies or serializes every tool schema to detect changes: 56 allocations instead of 4,418 for twelve tools.
- Building a session's context allocates about 40% fewer objects, and reloading a long session allocates about 13% fewer.
- An interactive session no longer re-validates and re-copies the whole transcript three times per model turn: a tool result's details were marshaled and decoded again at each step, so CPU for five prompts with file reads fell by about 30% (PiG 2.8 s to 2.0 s of CPU, turn time 1.9 s to 1.2 s on the Pi-Bolt interactive benchmark).
- A session's per-turn CPU no longer grows with its history: the agent loop shares its history with the request and validates each tool result's details and call arguments once, not on every turn. A 20-prompt interactive session with file reads used 8.3 s of CPU before and about 5.5 s after the first and second round; the cost of the remaining turns is the request itself.
- Long sessions send each request faster: the OpenAI-compatible request encodes each large message text once and reuses the encoding on later requests, the context estimate reads the branch's messages without copying them, and the model lookup no longer decodes message bodies. In a 75-prompt session with file reads (a 4.3M-token request) CPU per prompt fell from 0.84 s to 0.34 s on top of the earlier fix. The request body is the same as before.
- The OpenAI-compatible request is sent from the pieces it is built from instead of being copied into one buffer first, which saves one copy of the whole conversation per request (about 14 MB after 60 prompts with file reads).
