A failed check is never recorded, `pig verify` and `pig piglet verify` always hash the whole file, and a cache that cannot be written prints a warning without stopping the Binary. The cache cannot detect in-place corruption that keeps the file's size and times, or an in-place write that restores the modification time on a file system without a status-change time such as FAT, and the startup check does not stop anyone who can rewrite the Binary. See "Verification cache" in `docs/site/docs/piglet-binaries.md`.
- `make port-lint` and `make interface-gaps` replay the stored passing run while no tracked, modified or untracked file has changed. Failures always rerun. CI and `PIG_GATE_CACHE=0` always run them.
- Outside CI, `make test` and `make test-changed` report cached results for unchanged packages. Under `CI` every test runs.
- The `make test` cache warm-up compiles with `go list -export` instead of `go build`, so it no longer relinks every command on each run.
- Setting `PIG_NODE_MODULES_STORE` makes `make parity-deps` install each locked Node tree once per host and link checkouts to it.
- The test suite passes when built with `-trimpath`, which lets checkouts share one Go build cache.
- The Durable TinyGo core spends less CPU on V8's Wasm tier-up during warm turns: the watch, view, task-graph and commit-change frames, the document actions and the operation walk are compiled apart from the functions that run on every commit, so V8 compiles 51 KB of Wasm instead of 80 KB in the first turns of a session. Steps and stored records are unchanged.
- A resumed session that uses the default tools applies `--tools` `+name`/`-name` entries to the tools active when it was saved, following Pi 6f2f164a ([#10785](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10785)); PiG previously applied them to the default selection and dropped the saved tools. Restoring the saved tools on resume is now Pi behavior rather than a PiG divergence.
- A streamed assistant update copies the provider's partial message once instead of twice when the agent builds its message (85 allocations per tool-call delta drop to 44: the copy's own 43 plus the message).
- Delivering a streamed update to extension or SDK listeners no longer copies the message and the partial again when the stream has not changed since the update was built (36 allocations per update drop to none).
- The interactive transcript keeps its Markdown transformer list (Mermaid, then the extensions') until the theme or the extension runner changes, instead of building it for every Markdown render of a streaming answer (4 allocations per render drop to none).
- Typing in the fullscreen interactive view allocates two fewer objects per frame (about 1 KB less at a 48-row screen): the frame resolves the editor's fake cursor in the screen it built instead of copying the frame, and rewrites the cursor row in one pass. The rows written to the terminal are unchanged.
- Go, Python and Rust SDK: `ProviderStreamOptions` gains `OnProviderStreamEvent` (`on_provider_stream_event`) and loses `TransformHeaders` (`transform_headers`). A Go extension that read `ProviderStreamOptions.TransformHeaders` no longer builds; `pig extension upgrade` names the change. Pi's model runtime applies `transformHeaders` itself and never passes it to `Provider.stream`, so the host never offered it.
- Rust SDK: `on_event` and `on_event_result` lose the `can_block` argument, and Python SDK `on_event` loses `can_block`. Pi has no such concept, the Go and Node SDKs never had it, and the host never read it. The register message no longer carries `can_block` or a command `args` hint.
- The extension host sends `reload_started` and `runtime_input_end` only to Node runtimes. Both end waits that only the Node runtime has; the Go, Python and Rust SDKs never acted on them.
- A write tool card decodes its arguments once per redraw instead of three times (the header, the path and the content): redrawing a 136 KB write drops from 1.74 ms and 912 KB to 0.71 ms and 353 KB. The card reads the same.
- A documentation test now requires every adapted-pi page to keep each section heading of the pinned Pi page, with a reasoned allowlist for the sections PiG pages intentionally differ on. Reported by @SkrOYC (#195).

### Deprecated

- `delta.ApplyImmutableBatches(target, [][]Op)`: use `delta.ApplyImmutableBatchesSeq`. It still works.
- `delta.AssertValidOp(Op)`: use `delta.AssertValidOpValue`. It still works.

### Removed

- The experimental server, client and plugin stack (`pig_experimental` builds: `pig server`, `pig client`, the coordinator, session workers, the durable agent and its TUI) is gone, as Pi 1.2.0 removed it. Stock `pig` never shipped it.
- The Piglet strip feature `experimental-server` is gone with it; a Piglet that lists it no longer parses. Remove the entry.
- The Chord 1.1.0 service API in `internal/chord` is gone; `chord/host` is the Chord 1.2.0 runtime.

### Fixed

- On Windows, a session's private snapshot file is created in the temporary directory instead of the session directory, where it was listed while the session was open. <!-- fix: b8df7eb579 -->
- On Windows, an image fallback links a path that starts with a slash but no drive (`/tmp/x.png`) as Pi does, since `path.isAbsolute` counts it absolute. <!-- fix: b8df7eb579 -->
- Alt-screen frames outside the Kitty protocol no longer read the environment for WezTerm on every render, which allocated on Windows. <!-- fix: b8df7eb579 -->
- On Windows, a refused connection reads as Node's `connect ECONNREFUSED` and is not retried, as in Pi; it was reported as a retryable `fetch failed`. <!-- fix: 83fea11dbf -->
- In print and JSON mode, a Node extension no longer runs a timer or other callback between a prompt's last extension call (its command, a handled input or `agent_settled`) and `session_shutdown`, nor between `session_shutdown` and the runtime going stale. Pi runs those steps without an event-loop turn between them. Before, pi-goal-x's `create_goal` could start a goal turn while the run was ending, print `Extension error (<runtime>): This extension ctx is stale ...` and leave the goal paused.
- Codemode scripts that patch built-ins, for example `Array.prototype.toJSON = ...`, no longer break the tool call: built-ins are frozen before the script runs, and a malformed message from the sandbox fails the run with a `sandbox` error.
- Syntax highlighting keeps its colors after the first line of multiline strings and comments in fenced code blocks.
- MCP OAuth sign-in no longer fails with `invalid_redirect_uri` on OpenID Connect servers: client registration sends `application_type`, `native` for loopback and custom-scheme redirects.
- Bedrock requests that fail with `The pending stream has been canceled` after a stalled HTTP/2 connection are retried.
- The durable `read` tool reads a file that grows while it is read, such as an active log, instead of failing with "changed while it was read".
- Bounded durable tool output drops a byte-order mark only at the start of byte output, and keeps a U+FEFF at the start of the retained tail.
- `NodeExecutionEnv.Watch` gives a directory replaced at the same path a new watcher, reports changes to the file a symbolic-link target points to, no longer lets a non-recursive target stop an overlapping recursive target from covering subdirectories, and installs no watcher once closed. On macOS it reports changes to the file that a watched symbolic link points to.
- The durable `powershell` tool fails with "No command to run" when its program list is empty, instead of reporting success.
- `getSystemPromptOptions().hiddenTools` lists hidden tools in the order the `prepareLoadout` hooks hid them, as Pi does, instead of sorted.
- `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates` and `read_mcp_resource` return every member a server sent for a resource, template or content entry, in Pi's order and in the form `JSON.stringify` writes: integer-like keys first, numbers such as `2.0` as `2`, and `<`, `&` and `>` unescaped. Before, members PiG did not know were dropped and the known ones were reordered.
- MCP tool descriptions, resource MIME types, a pasted sign-in redirect URL and the resource tools' `server` and `uri` arguments are trimmed as JavaScript trims them: a byte order mark (U+FEFF) is removed and U+0085 is kept.
- An MCP tool or resource link with an explicit empty `title` keeps it, as Pi does, instead of falling back to the annotation title or the name.
- When an MCP server drops tools or is disabled, its tools are hidden in the order the server listed them.
- The error of an MCP stdio server that fails to start ends with the last 2000 UTF-16 code units of its standard error, as in Pi.
- An HTTP MCP client opens its server-to-client stream before it sends the next request after `notifications/initialized`, as Pi does. A server that treats the first GET as a stream reconnection no longer fails about one connection in ten.
- MCP OAuth discovery and resource selection now read URLs the way Node's `URL` does (default ports, dot segments, backslashes, punycode hosts), and `WWW-Authenticate` challenges with quoted or odd values parse as Pi parses them.
- MCP OAuth token and client registration responses keep empty lists, read `expires_in` like JavaScript's `Number()`, treat `null` timestamps as absent, and keep registration members PiG does not model. A registration response keeps `token_endpoint_auth_method` and the other registered members when one member has an unexpected type, so the token request uses the authentication method the server registered, as in Pi.
- MCP OAuth metadata documents with a member whose name differs from a modeled one only in case (for example `Scopes_Supported`) are no longer rejected; the member is kept as an unmodeled member, as in Pi.
- A JSON-RPC request or notification with a malformed `error` member is no longer rejected, and a `method` that is not a string is. A JSON-RPC error response whose `error.code` or `error.message` is `null` is rejected as an invalid message, and one whose code is beyond the float64 range is accepted, as in Pi.
- Codemode `@options` errors report V8's JSON error text, tool and schema declarations keep unpaired surrogates, and an explicitly empty global signature renders as Pi renders it.
- `StreamProxy` merges the final tool call and reports abort and timeout as Pi does, and it no longer changes a delivered message when the server sends more after the terminal event. The stdin buffer drops an incomplete sequence before a bracketed paste, the editor calls `onChange` as Pi does, and overlays hide the cursor and request a frame as Pi does.
- A family document member accepts a typed Go value as its seed, as `CreateTask` input and snapshots already do.
- `Submission.Wait`, `WaitForTask` and the idle waits return after every commit subscriber has run for the commit that settled them, as Pi's promise continuations do.
- A system message with an empty sections object is stored as `"sections": {}`, as Pi stores it after a reset.
- On Windows, an npm self-update no longer fails when a virus scanner or indexer holds `pig.exe` open for a moment. The update retries moving the loaded images for up to 10 seconds; any other error fails the update as before (D99).
- `ModelRuntime.Close` cancels the remote model catalog's refreshes and waits for them, so none runs on after `Close` returns, and a refresh requested after `Close` does not start.
- Crash attribution reads Go goroutine traces as well as JavaScript stacks, so a crash in the PiG process carries stack frames (D100).
- The `tui` package's default keybindings no longer depend on the platform: undo is `ctrl+-` and the alt-screen prompt and search keys are Pi's `TUI_KEYBINDINGS` everywhere. The Windows and WSL keys stay in the coding agent's table, as in Pi.
- A context an extension kept across a session replacement or reload now raises Pi's stale message from `cwd`, `mode`, `hasUI` and `model` in the Go, Python and Rust SDKs, as Pi throws it. Go and Rust panic with the message and Python raises `RuntimeError`; the handler's error carries it. The Rust SDK's `handler_panic` error now includes the panic text.
- Extension `ctx.ui.theme.fg`, `bg` and `getBgAnsi` throw `Unknown theme color: <token>` in every SDK for a token the palette lacks, as Pi's theme does; a theme with no palette still leaves text unstyled.
- A panic in a background goroutine of an interactive session no longer ends PiG with only a stderr dump. PiG restores the terminal, prints the error, records it in `crashes.json` and exits 1, as Pi does for an uncaught exception. Any other goroutine panic or runtime fatal error is copied to `<agent dir>/crash-output/` and becomes a crash record at the next start.
- The prompt-cache refresh that fires minutes after a prompt now treats a panic in the extension decision or in the refresh request as Pi treats a thrown error there: it keeps Pi's decision or skips that refresh and keeps warming, instead of ending the session.
- When the terminal's input ends while its output is still there, PiG keeps running as Pi does instead of exiting 1 with `Error: EOF`.
- An extension provider whose `streamSimple` hands its request to pi-ai's compat `streamSimple` (or `stream`) for a stock API, as `pi-commandcode-provider` does, no longer re-enters its own `streamSimple` without bound. The request now reaches the stock API's endpoint, as under Pi, instead of growing the Go and Node processes until the extension stopped answering heartbeats and the packed cell was killed (#164, reported by @xinaps-dev).
- A provider registered with its own `streamSimple` runs that callback only for its models whose `api` is the provider's registered `api`, as under Pi. A model of that provider that names another API, such as `openai-completions`, now reaches that API's implementation instead of the callback. This applies to extension registrations and to `ModelRuntime.RegisterProvider`.
- The edit tool's prepared arguments keep the members in the order the model sent them. Legacy `oldText`/`newText` arguments are folded into `edits` after the other members, and a JSON-string `edits` stays where it was, as in Pi. A `tool_call` handler used to see the members sorted, with `<`, `>` and `&` escaped.
- The legacy credential migration writes `auth.json` and the rewritten `settings.json` with the key order Pi writes (array-index names first, then the order in the old files) and without escaping `<`, `>` and `&`. The files used to be written with every key sorted.
- An OpenAI Responses request for a conversation with no messages sends `"input": []` instead of `"input": null`.
- The provider retry transport no longer replaces `Body` on the caller's request when it retries. Each retry sends its own request clone with a fresh body, so a server that answers 503 with `Connection: close` before reading a large request body no longer races the HTTP transport's body writer.
- Saving a setting keeps the key order of the existing `settings.json`, at the top level and inside a patched object, as Pi does. A replaced setting stays where it was and a new setting is added at the end; the file used to be rewritten with its top-level keys sorted.
- The edit tool no longer accepts a JSON-string `edits` value that starts or ends with non-JSON whitespace such as a vertical tab or a no-break space; Pi's `JSON.parse` rejects it.
- `settings.json`, `trust.json` and `auth.json` are written as Pi's `JSON.stringify` writes them: `<`, `>` and `&` stay literal instead of `\u0026`-style escapes, so a command-backed key or a `shellCommandPrefix` such as `source x && ` keeps its text. Saving a setting also rewrites numbers and strings in Pi's form, and `trust.json` sorts its paths by UTF-16 code units as Pi does.
- A legacy `oauth.json` or `settings.json` that starts with a byte order mark migrates to `auth.json`, as in Pi.
- The `/tree` label of a custom tool call shows its arguments in the order the model sent them, with `<`, `>` and `&` literal, and truncates at 40 UTF-16 units as Pi does. It used to sort the members and could cut a multi-byte character.
- A Node extension can default-import TypeBox (`import Type from "typebox"`, and likewise `typebox/value`, `typebox/compile` and `@sinclair/typebox*`), as it can in Pi. The import failed to link in an `.mjs` extension and returned the wrong object in a `.ts` extension.
- A key pressed while an extension's custom overlay is open no longer freezes `pig` when the key reaches the overlay on the main input loop. The loop read the overlay's state by queueing a task on itself and waiting for it, so the key never reached the extension. A caller already on the main loop now runs that work inline, as Pi's single event loop does, and a task the loop posts to its own full queue no longer blocks.
- An extension that calls `ctx.shutdown()` from a `session_start` handler, or from a `session_before_switch` or `session_before_fork` handler during `/new`, `/resume` or `/fork`, ends the session as Pi does: extensions get `session_shutdown`, `pig` prints the resume command when the session has a file, and exits 0 without waiting for the handler that asked for the shutdown. `pig` had restored the terminal and then waited forever for that handler, and `/new` exited 1 as if creating the session had failed.
- A `!` or `!!` command's block draws the bytes Pi's `BashExecutionComponent` draws: its rows are padded to the terminal width, the exit status, cancelled and truncation lines use the theme's error and warning colors instead of fixed red and yellow, the collapse hint uses the theme's key hint with the configured expand key, the spinner names the configured cancel keys (`escape/ctrl+c` by default) instead of `Esc`, no blank row follows the bottom border, the output Pi would cut for the model's context (the last 2000 lines or 50 KB) is cut the same way, and ANSI escapes and carriage returns in the output are cleaned as Pi cleans them.
- Escape cancels a running `!` or `!!` command again, as in Pi: the command stops and its block ends with `(cancelled)`. Escape had left the command running until it finished on its own. While an agent run streams, Escape still aborts the run and leaves a command started during it running, as Pi does.
- A resumed session draws its `!` and `!!` command blocks, as Pi does. `--session`, `--continue`, `/resume`, `/tree`, `/fork`, `/clone`, `/reload` and a compaction rebuild drew nothing for a persisted command. Each block now shows its output, exit status, cancelled or truncation line as when it ran, starts collapsed, and keeps the dim borders of a `!!` command.
- A running `!` command or bash tool command no longer outlives `pig` when the session ends because its terminal closed or `pig` crashed. The command runs in its own process group, so the terminal's hangup never reached it, and `pig` now kills it before it exits, as Pi does.
- With an active Piglet, an extension that denies every tool with `SetActiveTools([])` is no longer widened back to the Piglet's full tool scope. The Piglet scope only narrows what an earlier extension selected. `getActiveTools` replies and the replicated state carry `[]` for a session narrowed to no tools, never `null`.
- On Windows, refreshing an MCP server's OAuth token no longer fails with `EPERM` when another `pig` process is still deleting the refresh lock. Lock acquisition that waits for a lock now retries every error, as Pi's lock does, and reports the most frequent error when it gives up.
- Starting `pig` no longer waits for the daily cleanup of the extension cache. The cleanup now runs in the background after the session is running, stops when `pig` exits, and skips entries that are in use or that the file system reports busy (such as NFS `.nfs*` files) instead of failing and repeating at every start. `pig extensions cache prune` reports the skipped entries, and `pig extensions cache stats` shows the last background failure.
- `pi.exec` called while an extension loads, before the session binds, no longer fails with `extension connection closed or replaced`. Load-time exec runs with Pi's default working directory, as Pi's loader serves it (#165, reported by @luiul).
- A Node extension's `tool_call` handler can edit `event.input` in place and the tool runs with the edit, as in Pi: the host rewrites the call's input in place, and assigning a new object to `event.input` is ignored, as in Pi. The Go, Python and Rust SDKs follow the same rule, and a `tool_call` reply in an unexpected shape now blocks the call (#165).
- Tool-call input keeps its object members in the order the model sent them through events, tool execution, `tool_result` and the session file, where an edited input was sorted by key (#165).
- RPC mode now ends by the signal when a termination signal arrives while a quit `session_shutdown` handler is pending. The extension processes PiG stops first could complete the pending shutdown or report a drained event loop, and the process exited 0 instead of by the signal.
- Markdown resolves link reference definitions as Pi does: `[text][label]`, `[label][]` and `[label]` link to a `[label]: url` defined anywhere in the message, and the definition line is no longer printed. A list item whose text starts more than four spaces after its marker, as after a tab, renders as an indented code block.
- A listener that fails while agent_start is dispatched now ends the run as in Pi (failure messages, no turn_start or prompt), and the failure message carries the model's api (agent.ts handleRunFailure).
- `ai.CloudflareStreams` drops the deferred fetch and cancel operations of the API it wraps, as Pi's `cloudflareStreams` does, so a Cloudflare-wrapped API reports that it does not support deferred responses. Before, it passed them through without materializing the Cloudflare endpoint placeholders.
- Fixed tool-call arguments losing a field and every field after it when the model wrote an invalid `\u` escape such as `\usepackage`; the escape is now kept as literal text like other invalid escapes (Pi 4b994d5e, [#10868](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10868)).
- Migrating a version 1 session file that holds a line parsing to a number, string, array or `true` no longer fails while finding the header. Like Pi, an array record stays as written and still takes a place in the migration's id chain, and a number, string or boolean record stops the migration with the same `Cannot create property 'id'` error Pi throws.
- Fixed OpenRouter router models such as `openrouter/auto` showing a cost of -$1,000,000 per 1M tokens; OpenRouter's negative placeholder prices are now 0.
- An explicit `anthropic-beta` header, from a model's `headers` or the request, no longer makes a managed-effort Claude model (claude-opus-5, opus-5-5, sonnet-5-5, haiku-5-5, fable-5-1) fail with 400. The override still replaces the default betas. PiG now sends `thinking.block_binding` only with `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01`, and the mid-conversation effort markers only with `mid-conversation-output-config-2026-07-01`. Without the markers, the effort is sent as the top-level effort. Pi sends both regardless (Pi #10759, D130).
- The Anthropic event reader decides message_start and message_stop from the parsed event type as Pi does (not the SSE event name), and a JSON null event fails with the TypeError Pi reports (a CRLF split across chunks still waits for the LF, D121). <!-- fix: 40bc651bae -->
- The anthropic-messages and google-generative-ai requests build their URL as @anthropic-ai/sdk and @google/genai do (query rewritten through qs or the form serializer, WHATWG normalisation, alt=sse placement), and a base URL with credentials is refused as fetch refuses it. <!-- fix: a8ff0c7afe -->
- The Anthropic workload-identity token endpoint check parses the base URL with the WHATWG parser, as the Anthropic SDK does: loopback hosts reached with userinfo, mixed case or unusual spellings are accepted, and a base URL containing a backslash, quote or non-ASCII text appears unescaped in the error message.
- The per-API stream entry points (`ai.AnthropicMessagesAPI()` through `ai.PiMessagesAPI()` and the deprecated `ai.StreamX`/`ai.StreamSimpleX` aliases) end a failure inside the API's own stream the way Pi's API module does: the final message carries the module's `api` (its own API for azure-openai-responses, bedrock-converse-stream, google-generative-ai, google-vertex and openai-codex-responses, the model's `api` otherwise), and a request aborted before it starts stops with `aborted` instead of `error`. Only `streamSimple`'s missing-key check stays a setup error carrying the model's `api`.
- A panic in an extension error listener is now named on stderr instead of vanishing, and extension SDK terminal-input handlers and overlay invalidators that panic are reported in the extension log.
- MCP notifications no longer swallow every panic from the notify callback; only the "MCP failed to load" message ignores a throwing notify, as Pi does.
- Interactive startup paints the editor and footer first and adds the startup header once the terminal reports its colors or 100 ms pass, as Pi does. On a terminal that never answers the color query, the first frame no longer waits for that timeout.
- `pig auth check` and `pig auth print-api-key` no longer reject `--api-key` that another option takes as its value (`--model --api-key`, `--provider --api-key`): only an `--api-key` that is itself a flag counts, as in Pi.
- `auth.json` keeps every provider's entry as written, as Pi does. An entry the credential types cannot hold (a wrong-typed field such as `"key": 5` or `"env": {"A": 1}`, an OAuth entry without `refresh` or `access`, a value that is not an object) no longer makes every credential read, list and write fail with `auth: parse: …`, and an unrelated login or logout no longer rewrites that entry into the typed form (`"type": ""`, empty `refresh` and `access`).
- The Azure OpenAI base URL is parsed and serialized with the WHATWG URL parser, as Pi does: hosts are lowercased and converted from IDN form, any scheme Pi's `new URL` accepts is accepted (and one it rejects is rejected), and the `/openai/v1` rewrite works on the normalized host and path.
- Fixed the interactive bash block stripping escape sequences with the wrong rule: `BashExecutionComponent.AppendOutput` now removes ANSI like Pi's `stripAnsi` (ansi-regex), so an unfinished `ESC[38;5;` loses only `ESC[38;5` and a digit ends a sequence, instead of using the terminal-width scanner's rule. The regular expression moved to `internal/text.StripANSI`, which the tool output path shares.
- A stored models catalog whose `contextWindow` or `maxTokens` is written as `128000.0` or `1.28E5` loads like Pi's JSON.parse reads it, instead of failing to decode the model.
- Provider HTTP error bodies are decoded like `response.text()`: ill-formed UTF-8 becomes one U+FFFD per maximal subpart and a leading byte order mark is dropped, so an error message no longer carries raw invalid bytes (Anthropic, OpenAI completions and Responses, Azure, Google, Mistral, pi-messages, Codex).
- A Gemini (or Bedrock/native) response streamed in many small chunks no longer hangs the stream intermittently: the body reader goroutine no longer runs the completion that starts the next read.
- The Bedrock endpoint region is read from the WHATWG hostname of the base URL, as Pi's `new URL` does: full-width and mixed-case hosts, tabs and newlines inside the URL, and other schemes are handled as in Pi, and a URL without a scheme no longer yields a region.
- Bedrock model matching follows Pi: separator runs in a model id or display name become a single `-` (so "Opus  4.6" and "sonnet...5" match the adaptive-thinking and caching families), no-break spaces count as separators, and a model counts as Anthropic Claude only when its id carries an `anthropic.claude` or `anthropic/claude` prefix or its display name mentions claude.
- `durable/interop/bench.mjs` and `sqlhost-bench seed|sample` report the process's own peak RSS on Linux (`VmHWM`), as `bench/durableperf` already did. getrusage's maxRSS lagged it by 6 to 20 MiB, and it took on the launcher's peak when that was larger: Go, Python and Node all spawn with vfork, so the child's maxRSS starts from the parent's. `peakRss` in the pi and pig-native lines of earlier Linux runs is low by that lag.
- With the Bun TypeScript runtime (`PIG_TS_RUNTIME=bun`), `/reload` now evaluates an edited ES module extension (`.mjs`, or `.js` under `"type": "module"`) and the extension modules that import it again, as it does on Node (D93). Before, Bun kept the first evaluation until PiG restarted.
- The TypeScript extension runtime no longer depends on Node-only behaviour: its entry modules do not await the admission loop at top level (Bun never reaches `beforeExit` while an entry module's top-level await is pending, so the quit drain after RPC input ended hung), it hooks `v8.Serializer.prototype.writeValue` only where the runtime has it, it keeps a native method when the runtime froze built-ins, and it loads its lazy modules through one list that a bundling runtime can serve. On Node, a serve failure still ends the extension process with exit code 1; a runtime process whose event loop empties while it waits for the next admission now exits 0 instead of Node's unsettled-top-level-await code 13 with its warning.
- Cache warming no longer refreshes at once when a model's promptCache lifetime is far larger than the one-hour safety limit. The delay is computed in floating point as upstream does, so the run stops with "one-hour safety limit reached" instead of overflowing to a one-millisecond refresh.
- The OAuth callback server reads an absolute-form request target with a scheme other than http or https, a relative reference against a base that has a path and query, and a `\` path the way Node's `new URL(request.url, base)` does, instead of falling back to Go's `net/url`.
- Pasting a ChatGPT sign-in callback URL with a `ws:`, `wss:` or `ftp:` scheme, a backslash path or an unusual host gives Pi's message: "Paste the full callback URL from the browser" only where `new URL` rejects the text, and "The pasted callback URL must start with ..." otherwise.
- Sign in with ChatGPT now reads the token endpoint's responses as Pi does: a failed response quotes the body decoded as `fetch` does (an unreadable body falls back to the status text), a body that is not JSON or cannot be read fails the request instead of reporting that it is not an object, and a leading byte order mark or ill-formed UTF-8 is read as `fetch` does.
- `delta.AssertValidOp` checks path safety as Pi's `assertValidOp` does: it rejects a negative, fractional, boolean, null or nested path segment, and `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype`. Before, it accepted them, and only `ApplyImmutable` rejected them while applying. Its messages are Pi's (`path is empty`, `t shape`).
- `delta.ApplyImmutable` sets a numeric path segment on an object as that key, as Pi does. Before, it reported `unresolvable path`.
- `PathError` and `UnsafePathError` messages print paths as `JSON.stringify` does: `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 stay unescaped and `-0` prints as `0`.
- An `"m"` operation whose permutation a Go caller builds as `[]int` validates and applies again, as it did in 0.4.1. Before this fix, `AssertValidOp` reported `m permutation is not an array`.
- `durable.DocumentContent` decoded by `encoding/json` keeps the key order of the objects its operations carry, as Pi's storage `JSON.parse` does.
- A `delta.Tracker` no longer keeps memory alive that Pi's tracker releases. A settled `Change`, a draft handle kept after its change settled, and a `Prepared` no longer keep the draft, the base revision or the `Tracker` alive. An aborted change no longer stays registered with its tracker until the next adoption, and an open change the caller drops is reclaimed. Each case is one of Pi's `retention.test.ts` scenarios.
- `delta.CloneJSON`, `chord.CopyJSON` and draft placements report a Go struct or pointer with Pi's message for a value that is not a plain object (`Value must contain strict JSON plain objects or arrays`), and a Go func as `Value contains a non-JSON function; expected strict JSON`. Before, the message named the Go kind.
- Durable document, conversation-view and task-graph states report failing state listeners to the Session's report, as Pi does, instead of dropping them.
- `-e <local Package>` now contributes its prompts, skills and themes, loaded before the project, user and Package resources, so they win same-name collisions as in Pi. They survive `--no-prompt-templates`, `--no-skills` and `--no-themes`. A directory with an `extensions`, `skills`, `prompts` or `themes` directory is a Package and no longer fails to load as an extension.
- `--prompt-template` and `--theme` paths are now merged after the project, user and Package resources, as in Pi, so a same-name resource there wins over them.
- Stopping a codemode script or reaching its deadline no longer waits up to half a second for a built-in that does not poll the interrupt flag ([#10748](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10748)).
- Repeated codemode timeouts or aborts can no longer pile up running VMs: at most two codemode VMs are alive across sessions in one process, and a cancelled VM holds its slot until it exits ([#10748](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10748)).
- A finished code-mode execution no longer keeps its closed VM reachable through its stopped halt-grace timer, which stays in an idle scheduler's timer heap until that scheduler next runs. Each such VM held its whole linear memory (the heap limit plus 64 MiB, 320 MiB at a 256 MiB limit) until then. After 16 concurrent executions on a 196-CPU Linux host, the live Go heap after a collection was 0 to 6 of those memories above the start before the fix (up to 1.9 GiB) and under 0.1 MiB after it.
- The `codemode-store` session entry lists the keys of its `set` object as Pi does (array-index keys ascending, then the others in the order the script first wrote them) instead of in sorted order (execute.ts, host.ts parseStoreWrites).
- The OpenAI Codex login reads the account id from the access token the way Pi does: the token payload is decoded as standard base64 with Pi's padding and whitespace rules (a payload using the URL-safe `-` or `_` characters is not accepted), and the decoded bytes are read as Latin-1 characters, so a non-ASCII account id comes out as Pi's text.
- OpenAI Codex and Azure OpenAI requests parse their URL as fetch does again: an upper-case scheme, a space or a dot segment in a configured base URL reaches the server as the serialized URL, and the fragment is not sent. Codex reports a base URL with credentials with fetch's own message. <!-- fix: 8070fcc38b -->
- OpenAI Codex device-code login now reads the server's responses the way Pi does: a field of an unexpected JSON type reports the response as invalid (quoted as `JSON.stringify` writes it, not as the raw body) instead of a Go decode error, `interval` strings follow JavaScript `Number()` (hex, exponent and whitespace forms), any 2xx status counts as success, error bodies and a leading byte order mark are read as `Response.text()` and `Response.json()` do, and an unparsable body fails the login instead of reporting a failed poll.
- OpenAI Codex and OpenRouter OAuth responses read ill-formed UTF-8 and a body that fails to read as `fetch` does: one U+FFFD per maximal ill-formed subpart, and a truncated body is never parsed (an unreadable error body reads as empty text, an unreadable success body as invalid JSON).
- An OpenAI Codex login or refresh whose success response is not JSON reports JavaScript's own message, as Pi does (for example `Unexpected token 'n', "not json" is not valid JSON`, without a PiG prefix), and one whose body is cut off reports `terminated`, as Node's fetch does.
- The OpenAI Codex base URL keeps the whitespace around it, as Pi does (only an all-blank value falls back to the default), and its WebSocket URL is built with the WHATWG URL parser. The Vertex API-version check reads the parsed URL path.
- In print and JSON mode, SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a tool call stops the run where Pi does while the extensions' `session_shutdown` handlers finish without waiting: no further provider request and no tool result. A handler that waits on a timer or I/O lets the run continue, as in Pi, and text mode then prints the run's result.
- In RPC mode, SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a tool call likewise stops the run while the `session_shutdown` handlers finish without waiting, as Pi does: a handler that throws no longer lets the run persist the tool result and send another provider request before the exit.
- In print and JSON mode, an extension whose `session_shutdown` handler throws after SIGTERM or SIGHUP now has its error reported, as Pi does, instead of "connection closed".
- In print and JSON mode, SIGTERM and SIGHUP always leave their line in `exit.log`.
- In `--mode json`, SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a tool call now stops the output and the session file where Pi does: no tool result, aborted turn or `agent_end` is written after the signal, and extensions still receive `session_shutdown`.
- A `pig_strip_bedrock_converse_stream` Piglet Binary no longer links the AWS SDK through the Bedrock retry delay.
- An extension that calls `registerTool({})` or `registerTool(null)` now fails to load with Pi's error text (the missing parameter schema, or the TypeError of reading `parameters`), not a PiG-only message.
- OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions keeps the `"text":""` member of a user text part whose text is only a lone UTF-16 surrogate, as Pi writes it after sanitizing (a part that is empty to begin with is still dropped). Found by widening the payload oracles' random transcripts with lone surrogates and decoding and encoding them with the SDK-compatible JSON package.
- `grep` with a large `context` no longer holds every context line in memory: its memory is bounded by the 50KB output it returns, with the same text and truncation details (#9276).
- Subprocess extensions no longer slow down each streamed token in long sessions: the state sent before each event reuses the context usage estimate and the session name until the session changes.
- Long sessions no longer slow down each streamed frame in the main screen: a frame redraws from the first line that changed instead of re-rendering and comparing the whole transcript, with the same terminal output.
- SDK: `Session.Dispose` now releases the Session as Pi's `dispose` does. Its `Events` stream closes, publishing and flushing events stop waiting, and the model registry no longer keeps a disposed Session reachable.
- Long streamed answers render each frame faster: the Markdown view re-wraps and re-renders only the lines and inline text that changed since the last frame, with the same output.
- Pasting a large text that arrives in many terminal reads no longer stalls input: the paste is joined once instead of being copied and searched again on every read.
- Streaming long code blocks no longer grows memory with every frame: the syntax-highlight cache keeps one copy of a streamed block and is bounded in bytes.
- A running `!command` that prints a lot, the `/resume` search over many long sessions, and streamed answers without Mermaid diagrams cost less per frame or keystroke, with the same output.
- Very long streamed answers no longer slow down with their length: each streamed frame renders only the last blocks of the message that can still change instead of lexing, wrapping and laying out all of it, so a 60,000-character answer costs about 5% of the CPU it did, with the same output.
- PiG, the extension SDKs and the sprite selection use one configuration root: `PIG_HOME`, else `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pig`, else `~/.pig`. The Python SDK's `config_home` ignored `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, the Rust SDK's `config_home` still read the retired `GOPI_HOME`, and the Go SDK, the sprite selection and several host paths each had their own copy of the rule, so an extension could read or write a different directory than the CLI. An empty `PIG_HOME` or `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` now falls through to the next choice, a leading `~` or `~/` expands to your home directory everywhere, and any other value is used as written. If the configuration root needs your home directory and PiG cannot find it, PiG reports an error and exits (`pig --version` and other commands that need no root still run) instead of writing under a path relative to the working directory. Pi-sharing mode (`PIG_USE_PI_DIRS=1`) still moves only the agent and project directories, so a fresh `pig` in that mode writes its documentation and stages the extension SDKs under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/pig`. Reported by @baggiiiie (#200).
- Constrained-sampling errors quote tool and property names as Pi does (raw text between double quotes, not Go-escaped), and the strict JSON-schema conversion treats an explicit `null` `properties`/`items` and a `type` array holding non-string elements like Pi when deciding whether a union variant is an object or array.
- Fixed context edits replacing the content of a system message: as in Pi's `projectContextEntry`, only user, assistant, tool-result and custom messages take the replacement content, so an edit entry that targets a system message leaves it unchanged in the projected context.
- GitHub Copilot model-catalog fetches now report a failed response as Pi does (`<status> <reason>: <untrimmed body>`), report a malformed catalog as `Invalid Copilot models response`, and fail on a body that cannot be read, while a policy POST that is not rate limited no longer fails because its body cannot be read.
- GitHub Copilot login now reports failed HTTP responses as Pi does (`<status> <reason>: <body>`, not `HTTP <status>: <trimmed body>`), reads a device-flow `error` that is an empty string as a failed device flow, reads numbers past the double range as JavaScript does, and reads ill-formed UTF-8 or a truncated body as `fetch` does.
- GitHub Copilot, ChatGPT and OpenAI Codex logins report what Pi reports when a server's response cannot be used: JavaScript's SyntaxError text for a body that is not JSON, `terminated` for a body that is cut off, the server's own reason phrase for an empty error body, and a Copilot device-flow `error_description` of any JSON type printed as JavaScript prints it (for example `5` or `[object Object]`).
- GitHub Copilot login sends the browser to the verification URL exactly as Pi does (`new URL(...).href`: the default port dropped), and a device-flow failure appends a non-string `error_description` as Pi prints it.
- The crash notice prints the year of a crash time the way Node's toLocaleString does: unpadded, and counted back from 1 for years before 1 CE. <!-- fix: 20aee29744 -->
- `auth.json` keeps each credential's properties in the order they were read or returned by an extension's login, as Pi keeps the object it stores and writes it with `JSON.stringify`. Before, PiG rewrote every credential in a fixed key order whenever `auth.json` was saved, including credentials of providers that were not changed. A `config.oauth` login's credential gets its `type` after the flow's own properties, as Pi's `{ ...credential, type: "oauth" }` does. A credential that one of PiG's built-in logins creates still uses PiG's own key order, not the order of Pi's object literal.
- A subprocess extension's `ctx.ui.custom` component now learns when another component shown over it takes focus and when focus comes back, as Pi's `setFocus` tells it: the overlay handle's `isFocused()` follows, the component's `focused` flag is cleared and set again (Node, Go `SetFocused`, Python `focused`) and the component renders again. This holds for Node, Go, Rust and Python extensions. Before, the component kept reporting focus until its next key arrived.
- A second `ctx.ui.custom` component opened while one is shown now opens over it at once, as in Pi, whether it is an overlay or not and whichever extension process opens it. The component on top takes focus and the keys, and when it closes focus returns to the component below, which receives the next key. Before, a subprocess extension's custom component waited until the shown one closed, and a subprocess custom component kept reading the keys while another custom component shown over it had focus.
- Session and crash timestamps are read like Date.parse of a JavaScript string: U+00A0 and U+FEFF between tokens are white space, and U+2028 and U+2029 are not, as in V8. <!-- fix: bada2af6dc -->
- Durable (Pi 89e5c2c0): a generation waiting on a blocking threshold compaction fails with `model_error` (`Compaction failed: <error>`) when that compaction failed, faulted, was orphaned, or was aborted directly, instead of sending a request over a context it judged too large; a compaction that found nothing to compact or was declined still lets the request go ahead. `pi.live.compactionFailure` (`LiveState.CompactionFailure`) keeps the newest failure until a compaction produces a summary, `SnapshotEvent.CompactionFailure` carries it, and `CompactionEndEvent.Error` is set for a failed compaction. `GenerationCheckpoint.Threshold` marks the prepare phase that waits for the compaction.
- An extension's direct call into a pi-ai API module (`openAICompletionsApi()` and the other bridged APIs) sends the model's base URL as given, as Pi's API modules do. Only the Cloudflare providers' stream wrapper materializes `{CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID}` and `{CLOUDFLARE_GATEWAY_ID}`.
- `coding.DefineTool` returns the tool definition it is given, as Pi's `defineTool` does.
- `codemode.CodemodeJsonSchema` names the raw JSON of a tool's JSON Schema document, as Pi's `CodemodeJsonSchema` does.
- `durable/images` is the image processor of pi-durable: `images.CreatePhotonImages()` (also `images/node` and `images/cloudflare`) is a `tools.ImageProcessor` that turns an image upright by its EXIF orientation, scales it to the limits with a Lanczos3 filter, converts BMP to PNG, and re-encodes PNG or JPEG at falling qualities until it fits. `tools.CreateReadTool(&tools.ReadToolOptions{Images: p})` and `tools.CreateCodingTools(...)` pass it to `read`; `tools.CreateReadTool` now takes its options (nil for none), as `CreateBashTool` does.
- `durable.StorageRequestError` and `durable.SessionFailed` are Pi's error classes of the same names (`errors.ts`). The built-in storages return a `StorageRequestError` for a read of an unknown conversation, a document without retained history, or a cursor or scan order they did not write; the driver matches both names in a remote error.
- `harness.ToolTaskInput.Kind` and `ToolTaskResult.Kind` are the named string type `harness.ToolTaskKind` with `ToolTaskModel` and `ToolTaskNested`, and `harness.ToolHookCall` carries `Type` ("toolCall") like Pi's `ToolHookCall`; build one with `harness.NewToolHookCall`.
- Device-code login polling now does its time arithmetic in floating-point milliseconds as Pi does, so a NaN, infinite or very large interval or expiry waits as Node's `setTimeout` would instead of overflowing a Go duration.
- An extension dialog (`select`, `confirm`, `input`, `editor`) opened while a focused `ctx.ui.custom` component holds the terminal now shows at once and takes focus, as in Pi. Closing the dialog gives focus back to a custom overlay, which receives the next key. A custom component in the editor slot that the dialog replaced stays off screen, and focus goes to the editor. Before, a subprocess extension's dialog waited until the custom component closed. A dialog from an in-process extension opened at once, but the custom component kept focus, and a subprocess custom component kept reading the keys meant for the dialog.
- Fixed the edit tool's diff card for files with CRLF line endings: a diff row that ends in a carriage return is now wrapped like Pi's `Text` (which splits on CR as well as LF), so the CR no longer reaches the terminal and the row is followed by Pi's empty wrapped row.
- A durable directory reader now refuses a `maxEntries` above `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` with an invalid `FileError`, as Pi's `NodeDirReader.next` does.
- The Skills page said your own skills live in `~/.pig/skills`, that a skill's text is added to the system prompt, and that a bare skill name works in a Piglet. PiG reads `~/.pig/agent/skills`, lists each skill's name, description and file in the system prompt (the model reads the text on demand, or you load it with `/skill:<name>`), and `pig piglet validate` rejects a skill with no origin. The Skills and Prompt templates pages now carry worked examples with the output PiG produces.
- The documentation said PiG starts without a first-time setup screen and that Stock PiG has no built-in sprites. PiG shows a three-step setup (theme, sprite, anonymous usage data) on the first interactive start when no `settings.json` exists and `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR` is not set (D88), and the Stock binary includes fifteen built-in sprites and `/sprite`. The Quickstart, the embedded onboarding page and the concepts page now say so, and "Your first ten minutes" shows each setup screen.
- Durable entries keep a present empty `edits` list (`"edits":[]`) when they are appended, stored and read back, as Pi does. Pig previously dropped it.
- `durable/images.CreatePhotonImages`, and `durable/images/node.CreateNodePhotonImages` with `Worker: false`, which returns it, prepare images one at a time in call order on the caller's goroutine, as Pi's `createPhotonImages` does on its calling thread. Before, concurrent calls prepared side by side. A caller whose context is cancelled while it waits returns the context's error and does not hold up or reorder the calls after it.
- Durable: a JSONL store whose task or submission record carries a status outside the closed set (a damaged file) now fails to open with "Invalid committed state at sequence N", as Pi's does, instead of loading the record under an unknown status.
- Durable: a JSONL line that starts with a byte order mark now opens, as in Pi, instead of failing with "Malformed complete ... line N". When recovery reclaims a sidecar file it writes the records it keeps in canonical JSON, as Pi does, instead of copying their original bytes.
- Durable: reading the history of a conversation whose parent conversation is not stored now fails with Pi's error ("Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'parent')") at the point where Pi's lineage walk reaches the missing record, instead of returning a truncated history. The SQLite storage reports the same failure with Pi's SQLite error ("Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'id')") instead of "Fork parent conversation is missing".
- A durable transaction that settles a submission with a detail that is not strict JSON now counts as having written a table, as Pi's `settleSubmission` does: later table reads in the same commit are refused, and on a settled transaction the settled error is reported before the JSON check.
- A Pi Durable SQLite file written by PiG can be read by Pi, and the reverse, when a task kind, request ID, or document kind or key contains `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 or U+2029. PiG now stores indexed columns and records as `JSON.stringify` writes them. Before, a lookup by kind, request ID or document address missed rows written by the other implementation. Stored document records list `createdAt` and `retiredAt` after the other members, as Pi stores them.
- Durable (Pi eb2fce43): a wait that closes a cycle through waits and held owners now fails the task's commit with `Task <id> cannot wait on <member>: <cycle> is a wait cycle`, which names the cycle. Before, every task on such a cycle waited forever. The only cycles caught before were a task waiting on itself or on a task in its owner chain. A background task may now wait on the owner of its conversation, because that wait is no cycle, and a task may wait on an ended owner.
- The footer context percentage is refreshed after a `!` command is recorded, as Pi's footer asks the session on every render.
- A `bashExecution` entry records `excludeFromContext: false` when the caller passed `false`, as Pi does; an RPC `bash` command without the member records none. The Go fields are `*bool`.
- Queueing a steering or follow-up message while the agent streams prints no status line, as Pi does.
- A live `/skill:` message renders as the collapsed `[skill] name` block instead of the raw `<skill>` XML.
- Ctrl+U, Ctrl+K and the word-delete keys leave an open autocomplete list open, as Pi's editor does.
- A branch-summary entry writes `fromHook: false` in Pi's member order, and the summary has its Spacer. "Session compacted N time(s)" is shown only when a session is opened or its transcript is rebuilt.
- RPC and session JSON write an assistant message's members in the order Pi's providers assign them: `reasoning` before `totalTokens` in usage, and `responseId` after `timestamp`, in message events, `get_messages`, `get_entries`, `get_tree` and the session file.
- Fixed `Editor.SetText` keeping the previous scroll offset: as in Pi, replacing the text puts the view back at the top, so a long editor that was scrolled no longer renders with the wrong rows and scroll indicators after `setText`.
- An `env` request whose context is already cancelled is now always sent to the daemon and then cancelled, as in Pi, instead of failing about half the time with a local `aborted` error before it was sent. A request cancelled while the daemon is starting now waits for the start, as in Pi.
- An `env` SSH command that runs past its timeout now receives SIGTERM, as in Pi, instead of SIGKILL, and the operation fails as soon as the timeout expires, even when a process started by `ssh` keeps its output open.
- Closing an `env` connection, or losing it, now ends its daemon process with SIGTERM, as in Pi, instead of SIGKILL, so a daemon reached through `ssh` can close its remote side.
- Provider error text is written the way Pi writes it. A JSON error body keeps U+2028 and U+2029 as they are instead of `\u2028`, writes `-0` as `0`, keeps lone surrogates, and lists integer-like keys first (`JSON.stringify` rules). Text that is cut to a length limit (error bodies, Radius gateway text, Pi message text) keeps a replacement character where the cut falls inside an emoji or other pair of UTF-16 units, as Pi's `slice` leaves half of the pair, instead of dropping the half.
- Background runs of pi-subagents (and other helpers an extension starts as a detached Node process) complete under PiG. The harness package now carries the host peer packages Pi's pi-coding-agent depends on, and a process with no extension runtime streams model requests through a bridge process it owns (the internal provider-bridge command), D74.
- The Go extension SDK accepts a native Provider object with no auth methods, as Pi's `ProviderAuth` (both `apiKey` and `oauth` optional) allows. It used to panic at load with "Provider requires auth", while the Node, Python and Rust SDKs accepted it.
- An extension that writes a large amount to stderr no longer grows its diagnostic log without limit. The log keeps the first 1 MiB and the last 64 KiB of the output and says how many bytes it dropped, so a startup failure and a crash line both survive a flood.
- Fixed the extension editor dialog to size its text window from the terminal rows (`max(5, floor(rows * 0.3))`) as Pi does instead of always showing five lines, and its title and the image fallback line to close only the foreground colour (`ESC[39m`) rather than resetting every attribute.
- An extension dialog (`select`, `confirm`, `input`, `editor`) that opens while another is shown now replaces it, as in Pi. This includes dialogs from subprocess extensions. The replaced dialog stays pending until its signal aborts it. Previously the second dialog failed with "another extension dialog is already open" or waited for the first to close. <!-- fix: 74721716a1, ef0d4cb621 -->
- A failed extension shortcut handler is now reported as `Error: Shortcut handler error: …` in the error color, as in Pi. Previously it was shown as a warning.
- In a Node extension's `before_agent_start` handler, `event.systemPrompt` and `ctx.getSystemPrompt()` now render the current options, as in Pi. The handler's own edits to `event.systemPromptOptions` and an earlier handler's returned `systemPrompt` both show.
- A Node extension's `setWidget`, `setHeader` and `setFooter` factories receive the same `tui` as a `ctx.ui.custom` factory, as in Pi: `showOverlay`, `hideOverlay`, `setFocus` and `terminal` work there, and `tui.requestRender()` also redraws the overlays the extension shows.
- Fixed lists drawn by the submenu and filterable selectors (settings submenus, model and thinking lists) showing one label cell in a terminal narrower than five columns; Pi shows the cursor prefix alone.
- A Package configured as a settings object reads a resource kind its `pi` manifest does not declare (or declares empty, when patterns are given) from the conventional directory, and a `pi` manifest field that is not an array of strings counts as absent, as in Pi's `collectPackageResources`.
- The find tool trims each fd result line like JavaScript trim(): a name ending in U+FEFF loses it and a name ending in U+0085 keeps it. <!-- fix: eb34085200 -->
- RPC mode: when an extension command calls `pi.sendUserMessage()` or `pi.sendMessage(..., { triggerTurn: true })` without awaiting it, the run's `before_agent_start` handlers run before the command's `prompt` response, and `agent_start` follows the response, as in Pi. PiG used to emit `agent_start`, `turn_start` and the run's first messages before the response.
- `tool_search` and codemode tool search match Chinese, Japanese and Korean queries: each such character is a search term, where Pi's tokenizer dropped them (earendil-works/pi#10816; D173). Text in other scripts tokenizes as before.
- An OpenAI-compatible provider that streams a tool call without an id or a function name (OpenRouter and GLM do) no longer leaves the session failing every later turn with `tool messages must include a non-empty string tool_call_id`. PiG gives the call a unique id, leaves a call without a name or id and its results out of every provider's replayed request, and a stored session that already holds such a call works again (D120, pi-mono #10299). <!-- fix: a3859aa658 -->
- A CRLF that a proxy splits across two network chunks no longer aborts an Anthropic turn with `Could not parse Anthropic SSE event message_start` (D121, pi-mono #10390). <!-- fix: 1fbdabb354 -->
- OpenRouter's signature-only `reasoning.text` delta for Claude is kept and replayed with its reasoning block (D122, pi-mono #9534). <!-- fix: cb002735d6 -->
- A turn through OpenRouter (Chat Completions or Anthropic Messages) now reports the cost OpenRouter billed, including the upstream cost of BYOK requests, instead of the catalog-rate estimate; the HTML export shows the recorded total. This ports pi-mono's unmerged `open-router-cost` branch (D124, pi-mono #9980). <!-- fix: 0d0a3efd10 -->
- Model requests reuse their HTTP connection. Every provider build used to create its own transport, so each request and tool round trip opened a new TCP (and TLS, and proxy CONNECT) connection and left an idle socket with its goroutines for two minutes. Providers now share one transport per TLS mode; a changed idle timeout or proxy environment replaces it, as Pi's configureHttpDispatcher replaces its single dispatcher. <!-- fix: aeaaf97297, 04b10bcaa0 -->
- The xAI, Kimi Code, Meta, Radius, OpenAI Codex, Sign in with ChatGPT and GitHub Copilot OAuth requests send their form fields in Pi's order and with Pi's encoding (`*` kept, `~` encoded as `%7E`) instead of in alphabetical order. <!-- fix: e1ad272ec4, 01b94a8249 -->
- A single server-sent-event line larger than 32 MiB (for example a base64 image chunk) no longer fails the stream with `token too long` on the providers that use PiG's SSE decoder. A line is now bounded where Pi's runtime bounds it, at Node's maximum string length (512 MiB), so a server that never ends a line cannot exhaust memory (D134). <!-- fix: bdb7ad8292, c715193fab -->
- The assistant-message frame encoder no longer re-parses and re-serializes a whole tool call's arguments for every delta while it catches up to the start snapshot (8,000 deltas allocated 19 GB; they now allocate 12 MB). The frames it emits are unchanged. <!-- fix: 5e36d8772b -->
- Anthropic requests whose strict `prefer` tools exceed a strict tool limit ("The compiled grammar is too large", "Too many strict tools", too many union parameters) no longer fail every turn: PiG retries once with the `prefer` tools non-strict, keeps `require` tools strict, and records an `anthropic_strict_tools_fallback` diagnostic, as Pi 1.2.0 does (pi-mono #10409). <!-- fix: d983b74821 -->
- A Mistral stream is no longer aborted mid-generation by the request timeout: the timeout now covers only the wait for response headers (D127, pi-mono #10609, ported from Pi commit 6b07b4e5). <!-- fix: 2c1959cb36 -->
- OpenRouter image models that only the Images API serves (for example `openai/gpt-image-2.5-flare`) now generate: when `chat/completions` refuses the model, PiG retries through `/images` (D128, pi-mono #10652). <!-- fix: b73020581b -->
- An OpenAI Responses function call streamed with an empty `call_id` no longer makes every later request fail with an empty `call_id`: it gets a generated id, and a Responses request omits a stored call without one (D129, pi-mono #10299, Responses path). A call streamed without a `call_id` is stored as `undefined|<item id>`, as in Pi. <!-- fix: 0dff068cfd, 66765600a6 -->
- OpenRouter generated-image data URLs are matched exactly as Pi does (`^data:([^;]+);base64,(.+)$`): a URL with media type parameters, an empty media type or a line break in the payload is skipped, and a payload that is not valid base64 is kept instead of dropped. <!-- fix: b0608a98b9 -->
- A provider stream that keeps sending keepalive comments or pings but no event now ends with an error after `httpIdleTimeoutMs` instead of hanging for ever; the timeout restarts on each event, and on keepalives while the model is reasoning (D131, pi-mono #10761, #10762). <!-- fix: 3651a9096a, 4b6ef3e320 -->
- Session directories (the agent directory, `sessions` and each project's directory) are created owner-only (0700) instead of world-readable (D132, pi-mono #10770). <!-- fix: 25c854d4cd -->
- A resource pattern that starts with `~` (`!~/claude-backup/skills/pdf`, `+~/…`, `-~/…`, wildcards) now expands the home directory like a plain entry instead of silently matching nothing, and a `file://` URL pattern is its path, as Pi 1.2.0 does (pi-mono #10765). <!-- fix: 5b1d5b1382 -->
- An MCP OAuth authorization response with an `iss` parameter is now rejected when no authorization server metadata was discovered, so a malicious MCP server cannot receive a code another authorization server issued (as Pi c5f5b328 does). <!-- fix: 61322774f1 -->
- A streamed tool call whose arguments are valid JSON but not an object (`[]`, `null`, `5`, `"s"`) keeps them as the model sent them, so the model is told `root: must be object ... Received arguments: []` and the session stores them, as Pi does; they used to be replaced by `{}` and reported as missing required properties. <!-- fix: af36fe827b -->
- A tool call whose arguments are not an object is written back to each provider as Pi writes it: Anthropic and Google send `{}` for `null` (`arguments ?? {}`), Mistral sends `{}` for `null`, `0`, `false` and `""` (`arguments || {}`), Bedrock sends the value itself; a Gemini function call with `"args": null` is the empty object, and the agent proxy keeps Pi's `parseStreamingJson(...) || {}` and the streamed arguments of a `toolcall_end` that omits them. <!-- fix: d53cd09a75 -->
- A replacement request body from a `before_provider_request` hook on Anthropic Messages, and every OpenAI Codex WebSocket `response.create` message, now leave in Pi's member order (`type` first for the WebSocket message) instead of sorted keys.
- A `before_provider_request` replacement that is not an object leaves Anthropic Messages as Pi's `{ ...value, stream: true }` (`null`, a number or a boolean send `{"stream":true}`, an array or string its index members), `"betas": null` sends no `anthropic-beta` header, array-index keys lead a replacement body as in JavaScript, and a Codex WebSocket body's own `type` keeps the first position; a Codex payload hook that returns `null` no longer crashes PiG.
- A resumed session keeps the tools that were active when it was saved (an optional or deferred tool a command or extension had enabled no longer drops out and gets recorded as removed on the next turn); an explicit `--tools` list, `--no-tools` or `--no-builtin-tools` still wins (pi-mono #10785).
- An OpenAI Chat Completions chunk with a member of an unexpected type (a number for `delta.content`, `delta.tool_calls` or `function.arguments`, a non-object `choices` or `delta`, a non-string `delta.role` or tool call `type`, a falsy `custom`, a tool call element that is not an object) is read as Pi's JavaScript reads it: the member is ignored, tool-call arguments append their string form, a `tool_calls` string makes one empty tool call per character, any other `tool_calls` value that is not an array ends the turn with `choice.delta.tool_calls is not iterable`, non-empty array content (or an object with a positive `length`) appends its string form, a truthy `finish_reason` that is not a string ends the turn with `Provider finish_reason: <its string form>`, a usage that is not an object resets the counts to 0, a falsy count or a details member that is not an object reads as 0, and a prompt count that is not a number counts as its Number() when that is an integer. An Anthropic `content_block_delta` with a missing or non-numeric index matches no block and its text, thinking, partial-JSON and signature members append their string form. An Anthropic `message_start`, `content_block_start` or `message_delta` is read the same way: a usage, delta or content block that is not an object reads as empty, a falsy `message_start` count is 0, a block's text, thinking or signature that is not a string starts with its string form, a model that is not a string becomes the response model's string form, and a truthy `stop_reason` that is not a string ends the turn with `Unhandled stop reason: <its string form>`. A null or missing message, message usage, content block or delta ends the turn with Pi's `Cannot read properties of null` (or `undefined`) error instead of streaming on. Other mistyped members still end the turn with the decode error.
- Compacting a history longer than the model's context window no longer fails with the provider's "prompt is too long" error: the history is summarized in window-sized chunks, each chunk updating the summary of the one before, a split turn's over-window prefix joins those chunks, and a chunk the provider still refuses is halved (Pi 1.2.0 sends the whole history in one request and fails; D167).
- A `/tree` branch summary the provider refuses as too long is retried with the newest half of the request, never less than the newest message, until it is accepted (D167).
- `coding.DefaultAgentDir()` (Pi's `getAgentDir`) now returns the directory named by `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR` (`PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` with `PIG_USE_PI_DIRS=1`), tilde expanded, as the `pig` binary does; before, SDK embedders, `--skill` loading, the default skills directory and the interactive default ignored the variable and used `$PIG_HOME/agent`.
- An OpenAI Responses stream event or output item with a member of an unexpected type is read as Pi's JavaScript reads it instead of ending the turn with `openai-responses: invalid SSE JSON` or silently skipping the item: a delta appends its string form (`null` and a missing delta append `null` and `undefined`), a finished message or reasoning item joins its parts' string forms and ends the turn with `item.content?.map is not a function` (or `item.summary?...`) when they are not an array, a function call's id interpolates a non-string `call_id`, non-string function-call arguments end the turn with Pi's `partialJson.trim is not a function` or `startsWith` error, a usage that is not an object reads as zeros, a status that is not a string ends the turn with `Unhandled stop reason: <its string form>`, and a failed response's non-string error code and message are written in their string form. OpenAI Codex streams read their events the same way. A missing or `null` output item ends the turn with Pi's `Cannot read properties of ... (reading 'type')` error, a message's text signature records its id and any non-empty phase as Pi's `JSON.stringify` writes them, and an output item whose mistyped member Pi keeps as-is ends the turn with the decode error instead of being dropped from an otherwise successful answer.
- A Gemini stream chunk with a member of an unexpected type is read as Pi's JavaScript reads it instead of ending the turn with `google: invalid SSE JSON`: a text that is not a string (or null) appends its string form, a `thought` or `thoughtSignature` of the wrong type is ignored, a `candidates`, candidate, content or `functionCall` that is not the expected shape reads as empty, `parts` that are a string are skipped and other non-arrays end the turn with `candidate.content.parts is not iterable`, a null part ends it with Pi's `Cannot read properties of null` error after the parts before it, a finish reason that is not a string ends it with `Unhandled stop reason: <its string form>`, and usage that is not an object reads as zeros, and a `candidatesTokenCount` of `true` counts as 1, as Pi's `+` makes it.
- A Mistral stream chunk with a member of an unexpected type is read as Pi's JavaScript reads it instead of ending the turn with a JSON decode error: a finish reason that is not a string ends the turn with `Provider stopped with: <its string form>`, a content item's non-string text ends it with Pi's `text.replace is not a function`, a content item that is not an object is skipped, a `tool_calls` value that is not an array ends it with `toolCalls is not iterable` (a string with Pi's error for its first character), a usage that is not an object reads as zeros, a prompt count sent as a string counts as its number, and a cached-token count that is not a number reads as 0.
- An OpenAI Responses event's `output_index` matches an output item as a key of Pi's `Map` does: a string, `null`, a boolean or a missing index is its own key rather than a decode error or item 0, and an object never matches.
- The npm release packer reads `npm pack --json` output from npm 12, which keys the result by package name, as well as npm 11's array (Pi 6fb2e781).
- `grep` with `context` no longer loads each matched file into memory. A search over a directory of very large logs used to exhaust the heap; the output is unchanged (Pi #9276).
- A codemode script that calls `text()` tens of thousands of times no longer blocks the process: adjacent text items are joined in linear time (Pi #10678).
- The bash truncation notice for a last line over 50 KB that ends with a newline reports the line's size instead of `line is 0B` (Pi #10015).
- A `read` call whose `offset` or `limit` arrives as a string shows the right line range (`:25-37`, not `:25-2512`) in the TUI and in exported HTML (Pi #9887).
- An MCP server `url` can use environment variables (`"url": "${TOOLS_URL}"`): PiG checks and connects to the expanded URL, as it does for `headers` and `env` (Pi #10654).
- PageUp and PageDown move the selection by a page in selection lists and selectors (settings, model, extension, session, tree, trust, login and the open editor autocomplete) and no longer scroll the fullscreen transcript behind them (Pi #7629, PR #7680).
- MCP OAuth reads a non-HTTP redirect or server URL as Node's `new URL` does: dot segments are removed, a default `ws` or `ftp` port is dropped, and a malformed percent escape such as `/%zz` is kept.
- Sorting names with `localeCompare` (MCP servers, sessions, login providers, codemode namespaces, model lists, Hugging Face results) no longer shares one collator between goroutines, which raced when sessions were listed concurrently, and uses ICU root collation for every name instead of an ASCII weight table.
- RPC model objects of built-in models carry `"type":"chat"` (`get_available_models`, `get_state`, `set_model`, `cycle_model`), as Pi's do, and so do Radius models; a `models.json` model has no `type`, even when its definition writes one.
- A URL of a non-special scheme keeps an explicit port 0 (`ssh://git@host:0/repo`, an MCP OAuth redirect such as `myapp://host:0/cb`), as Node's `new URL` does.
- `PIG_DEBUG` writes its log with mode 0600 and refuses a symlink or a file owned by another user in the shared temporary directory; `PIG_DEBUG_KEYS` logs typed text and pastes only by length, so a key entered at a masked login prompt no longer reaches the log; `pig-render.log` is private (0600).
- RPC model objects of built-in models match Pi's: each keeps its catalog entry's own member order and spelling (`"type"` last for most models, first for others; `inputLimits.images` as the entry spells it), and a `models.json` override keeps the changed members in place, with merged objects listing the catalog's members first. <!-- fix: 364cc57510 -->
- MCP OAuth URLs are WHATWG URLs, as in Pi. An empty fragment (`#`) or a malformed fragment escape now stays in the authorization URL that is shown and opened, and in the insecure-endpoint error. The authorization parameters replace repeated names, and an opaque authorization server URL fails with `Invalid URL`. The protected-resource check compares origins, which are `null` for custom schemes.
- A tool result from a Rust or Python extension can set the collapsed-view `preview` the Go SDK already had (`ToolResult::with_preview`, or a `"preview"` key of the result dict), and the preview footer reads `Took 1m 15s` as the shell renderers do instead of `Took 75.0s`.
- Display text that PiG cuts at a length (the `/tree` listing, the Piglet system-prompt preview, an extension load error cause) is cut at characters, not bytes, so a multi-byte character at the cut no longer becomes invalid UTF-8.
- Filters that Pi writes with `toLowerCase()` (select lists, the config selector, file autocomplete, `/model` search, minimatch `nocase`) lowercase as JavaScript does (`İ` becomes `i̇`, a final sigma `ς`), not as Go's `strings.ToLower`.
- On Windows, PiG no longer searches `C:\etc\pig\piglets` or `C:\opt\pig\piglet.yaml` for a Piglet. A rooted path without a volume is drive-relative there, so any local user could plant a Piglet that every other user's `pig` loaded.
- `toUpperCase` ports use a shared JavaScript `jsstring.ToUpper` (matches Node over every code point). minimatch `nocase` folds `ᾀ` and `ß` as JavaScript does.
- RPC mode no longer hangs when a Node extension command calls `pi.sendMessage(..., { triggerTurn: true })` while a `before_agent_start` handler is subscribed: the handler runs inside the call, as in Pi, and the run starts after the command's response.
- RPC mode no longer hangs when a Node extension command calls `pi.appendEntry` and then `pi.setSessionName` while a `session_info_changed` handler is subscribed.
- In RPC mode, a run that an extension command starts with `sendMessage` or `sendUserMessage` now emits `agent_start` after the command's response, as in Pi, when the command is written with the Go, Python or Rust SDK, not only on Node. A command that awaits after the send still lets the run start first.
- In RPC mode, the Go, Python and Rust SDKs no longer let the host move on while a `notify`, `setStatus`, `setWidget`, `setTitle` or other immediate UI call is still in flight. A nested handler's UI request is now written before the response of the command that triggered it, as in Pi.
- `PIG_PROFILE=metrics` reports the exact cumulative allocation counts. Before this fix it missed allocations still held in the runtime's per-processor caches, so a record could be as much as half a megabyte low, depending on whether a garbage collection happened to run shortly before exit.
- The Go, Python and Rust extension SDKs' `serializeConversation` (`SerializeConversation`, `serialize_conversation`) omits thinking blocks, as Pi 1.2.0's does and the Go host already did: an assistant message prints only its visible text and tool calls, with no `[Assistant thinking]` line.
- `chord/host`: a link that is already closed when a host accepts or routes to it is no longer kept as a connection. Before, a route that returned a dead link (a child that exited before its link was wrapped) made every later call to that host name fail with `unavailable` until restart, and started a ping timer for the dead connection (Pi 7f022043).
- `chord/host`: a child host forked by `ForkDriver` no longer inherits its parent's `CHORD2_LOCK`. Before, a child host that forked its own children passed its lock on, and children without a lock of their own failed to start with `locked` (Pi a4d040d3).
- `chord/host`: `ProcessLock` no longer fails with `invalid argument` on macOS and the BSDs when the temporary directory is too deep for a socket path (sun_path holds 104 bytes); the lock socket then lives in a private per-user directory, `/tmp/chord2-<uid>` (Pi cd6b2a55).
- Fixed the footer's context percentage keeping the usage of a reply that a context edit had dropped (a response cut off by the token limit): it now refreshes when the edit is appended, as Pi's footer does on its next render.
- Fixed frontmatter parsing when the opening `---` is followed by a multi-byte character (for example U+0085 or U+2028) or an astral character instead of a line break: the header now starts after one UTF-16 code unit, as Pi slices it, rather than at byte 4.
- A `tui` input listener or light/dark listener that is added while input is dispatched now sees that input, and one that is removed before its turn no longer does, as Pi's listener sets iterate.
- A git source whose ref has no repository (`git:#v1`) no longer panics, and a ref split out of an opaque-path URL (`git:bitbucket:git+https://@v1`) leaves the repository whole, as Pi's `parseGitUrl` does.
- GitHub Copilot login reads an enterprise domain the way Pi does: an IPv6 host keeps its brackets, an internationalized name becomes its `xn--` form, a numeric host is normalized (`1.2.3` is `1.2.0.3`) or refused when out of range, a backslash ends the host, and a port above 65535 refuses the domain. A domain given as a URL of a scheme other than `http`, `https`, `ws`, `wss` or `ftp` keeps its host as `new URL` reports it: the case as written, non-ASCII characters percent-encoded, and an empty host for `file://localhost`. The Copilot token's `proxy-ep=` claim is found anywhere in the token and used as written, as Pi does, instead of only at the start of a `;`-separated part and trimmed.
- OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses and Codex request bodies list their fields in the order Pi writes them, and sampling parameters from `models.json` or the request replace a field in place and append new fields after the rest, as Pi's `Object.assign` does, instead of sorting every key of the body.
- Provider request bodies (OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses, Codex, Anthropic, Google and Mistral) write `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 as they are, as Pi's `JSON.stringify` does, instead of as `\u` escapes.
- A surrogate pair split across two concatenated string halves is written as its character in session files, extension frames and Durable records, as Pi's `JSON.stringify` writes it, instead of as two `\u` escapes.
