- Assistant messages in session files and RPC output list `responseId` before `responseModel`, Codex's `endTurn` before `rawStopReason`, and `rawStopReason` before `errorMessage`, the order in which Pi's providers create them.
- Tool schemas in OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses, Codex and Mistral request bodies keep the members in the order the tool declares them. Strict schemas add `required` and then `additionalProperties`, as Pi does. `cache_control` comes after the members of the tool or text part that carries it.
- Anthropic and Bedrock assistant messages write `providerThinkingLevel` after `model`, and write `cacheWrite1h` and `reasoning` after `cost` in `usage`, as Pi does.
- Anthropic tools list `eager_input_streaming` and `strict` before `input_schema`, and the input schema and Google function declarations keep the tool's member order (`type`, `properties`, `required` for the legacy Anthropic schema), as Pi does. A strict schema adds `required` and `additionalProperties` after the members it was authored with, also when those were authored in sorted order.
- Google and Vertex request bodies list their members in the order the `@google/genai` SDK writes them, also when an extension handles `before_provider_request`: body members (`contents`, `systemInstruction`, `tools`, `toolConfig`, then `generationConfig`), `generationConfig` members, `parts` before `role`, and the members of each part and function call. A function response lists `parts` before `id`, as Pi does.
- Amazon Bedrock request bodies match Pi's: PiG writes the ConverseStream body itself in the member order Pi's AWS SDK writes (`messages`, `system`, `inferenceConfig`, `toolConfig`, `additionalModelRequestFields`; `role` before `content`; `toolUseId`, `name`, `input`), tool schemas and tool call inputs keep their authored member order, and a tool schema with an empty property name no longer fails the request.
- RPC output writes summed usage (a tool result's usage with nested model calls, a compaction summary's usage) with `cacheWrite1h` and `reasoning` before `totalTokens`, as Pi's `combineUsage` creates them, instead of after `cost`.
- When an extension's `before_provider_request` handler replaces an Anthropic request, or a Codex WebSocket request carries raw JSON, nested values are written as Pi's `JSON.stringify` writes them after `JSON.parse`: `\u00e9` becomes `é`, and a nested object's repeated key keeps its first place with its last value. PiG copied the replacement's text unchanged.
- Amazon Bedrock `requestMetadata` keeps its keys' insertion order on the wire, as Pi's AWS SDK writes the object. Go callers set the new `StreamOptions.RequestMetadataOrder` (keys missing from it follow in sorted order); an extension's `requestMetadata` keeps its order through `streamModel`, `complete` and an extension provider's options.
- Mistral request bodies list their members in Pi's order (`model`, `stream`, `messages`, ..., then the renamed `max_tokens`, `tool_choice`, `reasoning_effort`, `prompt_mode`, `prompt_cache_key`; a tool result's `tool_call_id` last; thinking parts as `type`, `text`) instead of sorted, and the body is built about 3 times faster.
- When an extension's `before_provider_request` handler returns a replacement body for OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses, Codex, Pi Messages, Mistral or Google, PiG sends it as Pi's `JSON.stringify` writes what `JSON.parse` read: `\u00e9` becomes `é`, `1.50` becomes `1.5`, a repeated key keeps its first place with its last value, and array-index keys come first. PiG sent the handler's text unchanged.
- `make port-lint` has three more checks: `regexpinfunc` (a constant pattern compiled inside a function), `jsonclone` (a value encoded to JSON and decoded again in one function) and `ctxbackground` (`context.Background()`/`TODO()` in a library function with no context). Today's findings are in the ratchet baseline.
- The color level check reads `TERM` with JavaScript's case-insensitive matching, as Pi's chalk does: a name such as `ſcreen` (long s) no longer counts as `screen`. Ten regular expressions that were compiled on every call are now compiled once.
- Text that holds ill-formed UTF-8 is written with one U+FFFD for each ill-formed sequence, as Node decodes it, instead of one per byte (Durable core records and the new `internal/jsparity` writers).
- Ill-formed UTF-8 in a string that PiG re-encodes as JSON (an extension's raw JSON, tool schemas, code-mode keys, Bedrock and Google request members) is written as `JSON.stringify` writes the decoded text: one literal U+FFFD for each ill-formed sequence, instead of one `\ufffd` escape per byte.
- A Google Generative AI or Vertex request with no `contents` (a history of system messages only, or one user message without content) now fails with `contents are required` before it is sent, as Pi's `@google/genai` SDK does, instead of reaching the API and failing with HTTP 400. An `onPayload` hook that adds or removes `contents` is checked as sent.
- Gemini model-id checks follow Pi's lowercasing: an id spelled with a dotted capital İ or a long ſ is no longer treated as a Gemini 3 thinking-level or version-matched model, and a Gemini major version too long for an integer (such as `gemini-99999999999999999999`) counts as version 3 or later, as `Number.parseInt` does.
- A Google provider built from a model id without catalog metadata now sends its own earlier thinking blocks as thoughts with their signatures, as Pi does for the same provider, API and model. Before, the missing API made every earlier turn look like another model and the signature was dropped (found by the provider-wire parity scenario against the pinned Pi).
- Google streams replace an ill-formed UTF-8 sequence with one U+FFFD per maximal subpart (the WHATWG TextDecoder rule) and drop a leading byte order mark, as Pi's genai SDK does; the decoder used before emitted one replacement character per bad byte.
- Inline images render in Herdr again: `TERM_PROGRAM=herdr` detects the Kitty graphics protocol, as in Pi 1.2.0. Hyperlinks stay on, and truecolor still follows `COLORTERM`/`TERM`.
- `.gitignore`, `.ignore` and `.fdignore` rules that skills, packages and extension entry discovery read now follow the `ignore` package Pi uses: a pattern ending in a slash ignores directories only (a file named `build` survives `build/`), patterns match case-insensitively, a path inside an ignored directory stays ignored, a pattern with a slash before its end is anchored, `[a-c]` and `[!a]` bracket expressions work, and `**/x`, `x/**` and `x/**/y` follow Pi's forms. A leading space in a pattern is kept, as in Pi.
- `pig extension upgrade` now rewrites a `Context` getter call that is the only argument of a variadic call, such as `fmt.Println(ctx.ConfigHome())`. That call still compiles against the current SDK because the value and the error both become arguments, so the error was printed or passed on instead of handled and the upgrade reported nothing. It is now rewritten like the other uses: the function returns the error, or, for `ConfigHome` in a function that does not return an error, the line is listed for you to change.
- Selecting a model or cycling models with Ctrl+P no longer freezes the interactive mode when an extension's `model_select` handler registers a tool. As in Pi, the mode no longer waits for the handlers, whether the change comes from Ctrl+P, the model selector or a typed `/model`. The new model applies at once, and keys typed while the handlers run reach the focused component: the model selector, which stays open until the change settles (Escape closes it at once), or the editor. A prompt submitted meanwhile starts at once on the new model. A second change applies its model at once, and its handlers run alongside the first change's. An extension dialog opened meanwhile closes the selector, so later keys reach the editor. Quitting while a handler still runs no longer waits for it, and a failed model switch shows the error's message, as Pi does.
- `pig extension upgrade` now rewrites a `Context.ConfigHome()` call in an `if` or `switch` init statement and in a call with other arguments, such as `if err := c.save(ctx.ConfigHome()); err != nil` or `planDispatch(ctx.Cwd(), ctx.ConfigHome(), name)`. A `Cwd`, `Mode`, `HasUI`, `Width`, `Height` or `ToolCallID` call before the getter moves out with it, so the calls run in the same order as before.
- `pig extension upgrade` no longer discards the error of `Context.ConfigHome` with `home, _ := ctx.ConfigHome()`. The old `ConfigHome` could not fail, and an empty configuration root would make its paths relative to the working directory. A function that returns an error now returns it. In a function that does not, the upgrade lists the line for you to change. It also lists a getter passed as a function value where the old signature is wanted, instead of reporting it as an unrelated compile error. Listed lines are line numbers in your source as it was before the upgrade.
- `pig extension upgrade` is all or nothing. When writing a file or rebuilding the upgraded extension fails, it restores every file it changed and says so, instead of leaving the extension half rewritten.
- A `session_start` handler that calls `ctx.shutdown()` during `/reload` ends the session and exits 0, as Pi does, instead of leaving the reload screen up.
- A Go extension whose `go.mod` replace (or a Rust extension whose `pig-sdk` path dependency) points at an SDK an earlier pig staged under its configuration root, for example through a link made with `pig reload --sdk-path go` before updating pig, now builds against the SDK the current pig staged. pig never deletes the SDK directories older versions staged, so such a link kept resolving and the extension compiled against the old SDK, failing at load with `decode register payload: json: unknown field ...`. An extension written for an older SDK now gets the `written for an older SDK` report instead. A replace that points anywhere else, such as your own SDK checkout or copy, still wins.
- `pig extension upgrade` no longer changes, breaks or rejects a Go extension that already builds against the current SDK. A getter passed straight into a variadic call, such as `fmt.Println(ctx.GetActiveTools())`, is current code when the getter returned its error in the SDK the extension's `go.mod` requires, so it is left as it is. The upgrade now rewrites only `ConfigHome` calls of this kind, in extensions that require an SDK older than 0.5.0, and lists such a call rather than discard its error. A call whose parameter for the error has the type `error` stays. Rewriting one of these calls no longer also rewrites `ContextUsage.Tokens` and `Percent` uses that were already correct. A getter in a `select` case is listed instead of making the upgrade fail with `the rewrite does not parse`.
- A local package source that is a file Node cannot import, such as a packed `.tgz`, fails to load as in Pi (`Failed to load extension: Unknown file extension ".tgz" for <path>`, exit 1) instead of being run as a program and failing with "permission denied". Like Pi, `pig install ./x.tgz` still records the path; Pi does not unpack tarballs either.
- `pig install --validate-only` on a Package root validates every extension the Package names in `pi.extensions` (or its conventional directories), in any language, as an install loads them. It no longer treats the root as one Node extension and fails with "declares pi.extensions directories with no extension entry file" for a Go member.
- The editor now enters bash mode (bash border color, Escape clears it) when the text starts with `!` after any JavaScript whitespace, including blank lines and non-breaking spaces, as in Pi. PiG looked only at spaces and tabs on the first line.
- Rebuilding the transcript (resume, `/tree`, display settings) now places cache-miss notices as Pi does in a session with branches: each response is compared with the request before it in the session file, as the live notice and `/session` do. PiG compared only within the shown branch.
- Pressing Escape twice with only whitespace in the editor now opens the tree or fork selector, as in Pi, which trims the editor text before the double-Escape check.
- The `/model` picker now keeps its model list when the catalog refresh times out, as in Pi. It showed models the aborted refresh had added, and a refresh that ignored the 15 second timeout kept the picker on "Refreshing model catalogs…" until it ended.
- The `/session` block now follows a later theme change and shows the cache-warming countdown as of each redraw, as in Pi. It kept the colors and the status text from the moment the command ran.
- Extension widgets now appear in the order they were set when Node or other extension-process widgets and Go in-process widgets share a side of the editor, as in Pi. The extension-process widgets came first.
- Case-insensitive matching of tool names (Claude Code tool casing), the `anthropic-beta`, reserved Bedrock and OpenCode session header names, Bedrock GovCloud regions and model ids, the Codex usage-limit error codes, the merging of model and caller header names and the Bedrock data-retention hint now lower-case with JavaScript rules, so `İ` is no longer treated as `i`.
- Blank-key detection for compat API keys, the Vertex API key and the Vertex custom base URL trim whitespace the way JavaScript does: a value that is only a byte-order mark counts as blank, and a leading or trailing U+0085 is kept, as in Pi.
- A JSON syntax error reports what V8's `JSON.parse` reports in two cases it got wrong: a number or string where a `true`, `false` or `null` literal breaks off (`t1`, `fals"`) is "Unexpected number" or "Unexpected string in JSON at position N", and a backslash followed by a character above U+00FF in a string is "Unexpected token" rather than "Bad escaped character".
- `pig piglet schema` lists the `apis` strip list.
- A `keybindings.json` key named like a property every JavaScript object inherits (`constructor`, `toString`, `__proto__` and the rest) is renamed on load the way Pi renames it (to that property's printed text), instead of being kept as written.
- The keybindings manager's key text and key matching now cover every keybinding the merged table defines, including the `tui.*` ones, and no keys for an id no definition names, as Pi's `KeybindingsManager.getKeys`/`keyText`/`matches` do. Before, a `tui.*` id had no key text and matched nothing through the coding-agent manager, and an unknown id echoed its user keys.
- Kimi Code OAuth login and refresh now read the server's response the way Pi does: a body that is not a JSON object or array counts as absent, a field of an unexpected JSON type is reported as invalid or missing (instead of a Go decode error), an error body is quoted as `JSON.stringify` writes it (member order, number and string forms), error descriptions keep an empty value, `slow_down` carries the server's `interval`, a leading byte order mark is ignored, the failure texts match Pi's (`Invalid Kimi Code device authorization response`, `Kimi Code token refresh response missing fields: …`, the status and body of a 5xx during login), and a refresh with an empty stored token sends the request as Pi does.
- Kimi Code and xAI OAuth decode a response body as fetch does: ill-formed UTF-8 becomes one U+FFFD per maximal ill-formed subpart (not one per byte, nor one per run), in quoted error bodies and in fields such as `error_description`; a body that fails to read (a connection closed mid-body) is never parsed, so a truncated token response is no longer accepted.
- A faux provider error message that carries a response id writes `responseId` after `errorMessage` in session JSON, in the key order Pi's `fauxAssistantMessage` uses. Before, `responseId` followed `model`.
- In-process extensions that call `pi.getCommands()` now receive the extension commands, prompt templates and skills of the session in every mode, as Pi's session binds them.
- `ctx.compact` answers `onComplete` with the compaction result's members in Pi's order (`summary`, `firstKeptEntryId`, `tokensBefore`, `estimatedTokensAfter`, `usage`, `details`); they were written alphabetically.
- `thinking_level_select` and `turn_start` handlers are covered through the production Session: a handler sees the clamped effective level only when it changes, and `turn_start` carries the per-run turn index and the time of the turn.
- A scripted faux response can carry `DurationMs`. It stays on every partial and the final message, as in Pi, and the stream measures a duration only when none is scripted.
- The experimental client takes Pi's `ByteTransportFactory` shape: `ClientOptions.TransportFactory` returns a `ByteTransport` whose `Send(ctx, chunk)` returns when the write settles. The unix transport uses it, and a transport that admits sends itself (`Submit`) is handed each frame before `Connection.Send` returns.
- Changing the thinking level in interactive mode (cycle key, `/thinking`, the settings selector) goes through the Session like Pi's `session.setThinkingLevel`: the level is clamped to the model, the `thinking_level_select` event carries the clamped level, and a handler that is slow to start no longer holds the input loop.
- Durable: a tool's `PrepareArguments` result of a typed object (a struct, or a map of another element type) reaches validation and execution as that object; it reached them as no arguments.
- Durable: a `PrepareArguments` result that is not an object fails validation as an `invalid_arguments` tool error whose `Received arguments` shows the returned value, as Pi does; it panicked the core.
- A tool or command that throws an `Error` with an empty message reports an empty message in the Node runtime, as upstream does (`err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)`); it reported `Error`.
- The Go and Python `serializeConversation` keep an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate in a message, a tool-call name or argument, and when the 2,000-unit cut splits a surrogate pair, as Pi does; the conformance test now compares those cases against Pi's own code.
- The host answers a parsed frontmatter with `<`, `>`, `&` and U+2028 as they are (as `JSON.stringify` writes them) instead of `\u003c`-style escapes, and a packed cell's helper without a `Context` keeps a live connection when the first extension to start has stopped (Go, Rust and Python SDKs).
- The Rust and Go SDKs keep an unpaired surrogate through `prepareBranchEntries` (`prepare_branch_entries_json`, `PrepareBranchEntriesJSON`, `Context::get_entries_json`), and the token estimate of all three SDKs counts it as `JSON.stringify` does in a tool call's arguments.
- The Rust SDK parses every JSON number as the nearest double, as `JSON.parse` does; it could land one unit in the last place away (`123456789012345680000` was read as `123456789012345670000`).
- A tool argument or event payload with a number outside the double range (`1e400`), at any nesting depth, no longer fails the call in the Go SDK or ends a Rust extension; it reads as Infinity (Go) or `null` (Rust), as `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify` do.
- The Rust build failure test asserts that a killed rustc is named by its program name, not its full path.
- The serializeConversation parity row records that Rust session entries still read an unpaired surrogate as U+FFFD, and is partial until that is decided.
- The liveness conformance harness answers the host-parser helper calls a factory makes before the register frame, so the recording of the five SDK cells is unchanged.
- A pi-ai call made while a module loads is answered in every extension host: SDK sessions, the extension validation, the authentication inspection and the login preview no longer hang on it, and any extension call with no handler now gets an error result instead of silence.
- A Rust extension build that ends without a compiler diagnostic (cargo or rustc killed, cargo unable to start) now says how it ended: the exit status or signal, the crate whose rustc was killed, how long cargo ran and whether the build's context had ended, instead of only "cargo build failed".
- The Rust SDK keeps every session entry when one entry of the session file holds an unpaired surrogate (it read the whole file as empty).
- The Go SDK's typed branch entry keeps the text, thinking and tool calls of an assistant message whose tool calls carry object arguments (Pi's `ToolCall`), and the Rust SDK's `BranchEntry` decodes Pi's nested session entries (role, text, thinking, tool calls, provider, model and usage were all empty before) and gains `id`, `first_kept_entry_id` and `UsageInfo::total_tokens`. <!-- fix: 18daf7fed2 -->
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export Pi's compaction helpers `calculateContextTokens`, `shouldCompact`, `getLastAssistantUsage`, `estimateTokens`, `findCutPoint`, `findTurnStartIndex` and `DEFAULT_COMPACTION_SETTINGS`; the Rust SDK also exports `UsageInfo`. <!-- fix: 058e7a0e4b -->
- An extension that calls a pi-ai function such as `completeSimple` at top level or in its factory no longer hangs startup; the Host answers the model stream while the module loads.
- The Go SDK writes NaN and infinities as `null` (and negative zero as `0`) in the frames it sends, as `JSON.stringify` and the Node, Rust and Python SDKs do; a Go tool that returned one used to fail with an encoding error.
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export Pi's `getLanguageFromPath`. <!-- fix: 68d062839c -->
- The Node SDK's `messageRole` now returns `""` for a message whose `role` is not a string, as the Go, Rust and Python SDKs do (it returned the value, e.g. `5`).
- The Rust SDK's `get_model_info()` reads Pi's `maxTokens` and `cost` (it reported 0 for the output limit and every price) and returns an error for a field of the wrong type, as the Go SDK's `ModelInfo` does. <!-- fix: 5260e5c15f -->
- The Go and Rust SDKs now answer a tool result past the 128 MiB frame limit with a `frame too large` error, as the Node and Python SDKs do; the call used to hang until the run was aborted.
- The Python SDK sends NaN and infinities as `null` (as `JSON.stringify` does) instead of the bare words `NaN`/`Infinity`, which the host refused and which ended the extension's connection.
- The Rust SDK decodes tool arguments, notifications and host-call results nested past 128 levels (up to 4096) instead of dropping its host connection.
- The Rust SDK no longer drops its host connection when a frame (a tool argument, event payload or object key) holds an unpaired UTF-16 surrogate; it decodes the unit as U+FFFD, as Go does for a `string`.
- The Go, Rust and Python SDKs export Pi's `CURRENT_SESSION_VERSION`, `VIRTUAL_MODEL_STATE_ENTRY`, `getLatestCompactionEntry`, `parseSkillBlock` and `formatSkillsForPrompt`. <!-- fix: 50f526d69e -->
- An extension's `ui.setHiddenThinkingLabel` now changes the label shown for hidden thinking in the chat, including assistant messages already on screen. It only changed a footer string before.
- Tab at the start of a line no longer forces file completion while the text before the cursor is a slash-command name.
- The `/login` and `/logout` provider picker and the `/tree` selector show the terminal cursor in their search and label inputs only while they hold focus, as Pi does. The picker's search input claimed focus even when it had none.
- A built-in extension named with `-e builtin:<name>` loads before the extensions from settings and the extension directories, as in Pi, and keeps that place after `/reload`. Before, every built-in loaded after the file extensions. The order decides which extension owns a shared command, tool or flag name and the order of event handlers.
- Read-only credential storage reports an unreadable or malformed `auth.json` as Pi does: `Failed to read auth.json: ` followed by Node's errno text (for example `EISDIR: illegal operation on a directory, read`) or the JSON.parse SyntaxError text (for example `Unexpected end of JSON input`), instead of Go's error text.
- Keybinding conflict detection ignores user bindings for actions the table does not define, as Pi's `KeybindingsManager.rebuild` does, so a typo'd action no longer appears in a conflict (oracles `TestKeybindingsManagerMatchesPi`, `TestAppKeybindingsManagerMatchesPi`).
- The `/settings` search edits like Pi's search input: the cursor moves with the arrow keys, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+K, Ctrl+Y, paste and the other input keys work, and any key the list does not own returns the selection to the first match. Confirm and cancel keys that a user binds to the same key as up or down now run up or down first, as Pi does.
- The login provider list selects with Enter when `tui.select.confirm` is rebound, as Pi's search input submits it, and starts its search cursor at the beginning of a prefilled query.
- The `/thinking` selector edits its search like Pi's input, selects with the input's submit key, and no longer repeats a selection or cancel on the next navigation key.
- The cancel hint of extension loaders (`/share`, `/bug`) shows the key bound to `tui.select.cancel` instead of always `escape/ctrl+c`.
- Pressing the login dialog's cancel key again cancels again, and Enter after a cancelled prompt leaves the field unchanged, as in Pi.
- The session picker checks its keys in Pi's order when bindings overlap (delete before rename, list actions up, down, page up, page down, confirm, cancel), and Enter typed into the search box selects the highlighted session even when `tui.select.confirm` is rebound.
- An unmatched tool result row in the /tree selector shows the tool's name, as in Pi (`[bash]`), instead of `[tool]`.
- Pressing Enter in the model picker's search box selects the highlighted model even when `tui.select.confirm` is rebound.
- Single-line inputs (login prompts, search boxes) ignore xterm modifyOtherKeys text sequences, as Pi's Input does; the multi-line editor still accepts them.
- The /fork message picker checks its keys in Pi's order (up, down, confirm, cancel) when bindings overlap.
- Settings submenus: the type-to-filter box no longer shows a hardware cursor marker (Pi never focuses it), and the highlight callback (theme preview) now fires on every up/down like Pi's SelectList, including when the highlight stays on the same item.
- Filterable selector lists (theme, images, tree-filter-like pickers) check up/down before confirm/cancel like Pi's SelectList when a key is bound to several of them.
- Durable error values (FileError, ExecutionError, JsonlCorruptionError, ReadAfterWrite, StorageRejected, ConversationBusy) report Pi's `name` and a V8-style `stack` when built by their constructors; the JSONL corruption constructor is now exported as NewJsonlCorruptionError.
- Durable: resolving harness settings no longer shares the default retry policy's max agent delay between harnesses.
- Extensions' `ctx.ui.setHiddenThinkingLabel()` now changes the collapsed thinking label of every assistant block (it was stored but never read) and is reset when the session is replaced.
- /settings warnings submenu: a warning the settings never set shows as on and a toggle always reports the warning as set (Pi's `?? true`).
- /scoped-models: an empty search no longer makes enable-all/clear-all act on every model, clearing every model from an all-enabled list now leaves none enabled (not all), and the save key is checked after the navigation keys like Pi.
- Config selector (`pig config`): every key that reaches the search input re-filters and resets the selection to the first item even when the query is unchanged, Tab no longer edits the search when project mode is unavailable, and the search is a callback-mode Input so Enter no longer freezes it, all as in Pi's `ResourceList.handleInput` (`tui/config_selector_oracle_test.go`, 648 probes against pinned Pi).
- `SettingsManager.getTransport` and fifteen sibling getters (settings-manager.ts: getSteeringMode, getFollowUpMode, getDefaultThinkingLevel, getTheme, getDefaultProvider, getDefaultModel, getHideThinkingBlock, getCompactionEnabled, getImageAutoResize, getBlockImages, getShowImages, getEditorPaddingX, getAutocompleteMaxVisible, getCodeBlockIndent, getEnableSkillCommands): new live-Pi oracle `TestSettingsManagerGettersMatchPi` (`internal/codingagent/settings_getters_oracle_test.go`, 1,500 global/project file pairs setting, omitting or conflicting on each key, nested objects merged one level) compares every getter's value, defaults included, with Pi's. No bug found. 8 mutations red (transport/steering/follow-up defaults, showImages default, compaction/blockImages/hideThinking inversions, editorPaddingX). Not compared: environment-reading getters (hardware cursor, clear on shrink), wrong-typed values and fractional numbers (Settings is typed), and a codeBlockIndent of "" (Go's string cannot tell "" from absent, so GetCodeBlockIndent returns the default where Pi keeps ""). Closes the flip:c2cc995c row (`SettingsManager.GetTransport`) at v1.1.0.
- `SettingsManager` structured getters (settings-manager.ts getBranchSummarySettings, getRetrySettings, getProviderRetrySettings, getCacheWarmingMode, getQuietStartup, getDefaultProjectTrust, getDoubleEscapeAction, getTreeFilterMode, getOutputPad, getMermaidRenderingMode, getImageWidthCells, getTuiMode, the fullscreen getters, getEnabledModels, getNpmCommand, getTrackingId and others): new live-Pi oracle `TestSettingsManagerStructuredGettersMatchPi` (2,000 layered global/project file pairs). Two real fixes: (1) a project settings file's `enableAnalytics` and `trackingId` were ignored by the merge (deepMergeSettings lets every project key override), so a project `enableAnalytics: true` read as false; (2) `terminal.imageWidthCells` of 0 or a negative number read as the 60 default, where Pi's getImageWidthCells reads `Math.max(1, Math.floor(width))` = 1; an authored value is now tracked (`imageWidthCellsSet`), merged from the project file and written back. Regression `TestSettingsWriteKeepsAnAuthoredNonPositiveImageWidthCells`. 15 mutations red. Not compared: environment-reading getters, wrong-typed values and fractional numbers (Settings is typed), empty strings.
- `ReadOnlyAuthStorage`: new live-Pi oracle `TestReadOnlyAuthStorageMatchesPi` (`ai/auth_storage_readonly_oracle_test.go`; 800 auth.json files x 1-5 operations over read, list, refused modify and delete, and a file rewrite between operations that a loaded store must not see; valid and invalid credential shapes: non-string key, non-string env value, env null/array, OAuth missing access/refresh/expires or with a string expiry, null/array/scalar entries and roots). Results and thrown messages match; no bug found. 9 mutations red (key/env/OAuth validation, command-key bypass, refused modify, null root, error text). Equivalent mutants: the finite-expiry check (Go's JSON decoder already rejects `1e999`), the empty-key and API-key type guards in Read (`resolveStoredCredential` repeats both). Not compared: V8 text after "Failed to read auth.json: ".
- `DefaultResourceLoader.reload`: new live-Pi oracle `TestDefaultResourceLoaderReloadMatchesPi` (`coding/resource_loader_reload_oracle_test.go`; 400 random trees of user, project, ancestor `.agents`, home and settings-listed prompt templates, skills, themes, SYSTEM.md, APPEND_SYSTEM.md and AGENTS.md; trusted and untrusted; no-skills/prompts/themes/context switches; systemPrompt, appendSystemPrompt and additional-path options incl. missing and home-relative paths). Compares prompts, skills and themes (names, descriptions, contents, paths, source info), collision/validation diagnostics (including Pi's installed theme JSON validator messages), system and appended prompts with sources, and context files. No bug found. 12 mutations red (discovery trust, dedupe winner/loser, message, append/source handling, no-prompts/skills/themes/context switches, mergePaths dedupe, global discovery, missing-path message). Equivalent mutants: the `|| len(paths) > 0` prompt/theme gates (loading zero paths yields nothing), `CanonicalizePath` in mergePaths (corpus has no symlinks or case-folded duplicates), the missing-path diagnostic skip and the DefaultSourceInfoForPath fallback (every corpus path carries resolved metadata). Not compared: extensions, Packages (npm/git), symlinked duplicates.
- `AuthStorage` file semantics: new live-Pi oracle `TestAuthStorageFileMatchesPi` (`ai/auth_storage_file_oracle_test.go`; 600 auth.json files x 1-5 operations over read, list, modify and delete: absent, empty, corrupt, byte-order-marked and non-object files, `$VAR`/`${VAR}`/`!command`/credential-env key resolution, OAuth and unknown types, integer-index provider order). Real fixes for a file whose JSON is not an object (Pi keeps whatever `JSON.parse` returns): `null` made PiG treat the file as empty where Pi's read, list, modify and delete all throw (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'x')` / `Cannot convert undefined or null to object`); an array was rejected where Pi reads and lists it as an index-keyed object, turns it into an object on modify and rewrites it as an array (deleted element `null`) on delete; a number or boolean was rejected where Pi reads it as empty and a delete rewrites it as itself; a `null` credential entry made `list` return an entry where Pi's `credential.type` throws. 10 mutations red; one equivalent (the root shape of the post-write read cache is never rewritten). Not compared: V8's JSON parse error text, string-valued files (JavaScript indexes their characters), file modes, cross-process locking.
- `SettingsManager` collection getters (getPackages, getExtensionPaths, getSkillPaths, getPromptTemplatePaths, getThemePaths, getAllModelThinkingLevels, getThinkingBudgets, getTerminalCapabilityOverrides, getWarnings, getHttpIdleTimeoutMs, getWebSocketConnectTimeoutMs, getExternalEditorCommand): new live-Pi oracle `TestSettingsManagerCollectionGettersMatchPi` (2,500 layered global/project file pairs; throws compared by message). Two real fixes: (1) an authored `"httpIdleTimeoutMs": null` was dropped (Pi keeps it, overrides the other layer, and getHttpIdleTimeoutMs throws "Invalid httpIdleTimeoutMs setting: null"; websocketConnectTimeoutMs already did this); (2) a project `"externalEditor": ""` did not override a global editor (Pi's merge keeps the empty string, so the editor falls back to VISUAL, EDITOR, nano); both now survive load, merge and encode. Regression `TestSettingsEncodeKeepsAnAuthoredNullHTTPIdleTimeoutAndEmptyExternalEditor`. 11 mutations red. Not compared: wrong-typed array members, fractional numbers, a thrown value that is neither number nor string; getWarnings is compared as the consumer reads it (`anthropicExtraUsage !== false`).
- `SettingsManager.getCompactionSettings` (+ `getCompactionReserveTokens`, `getCompactionKeepRecentTokens`), `getShellPath`, `getSessionDir`, `getThemeSetting`, `getTheme`, `getModelThinkingLevel`: new live-Pi oracle `TestSettingsManagerResolvedGettersMatchPi` (3,000 layered global/project file pairs; model override beats ordinary setting beats default; invalid authored token values and unsafe integers compared by thrown message; path settings through normalizePath with home expansion and file URLs; a theme containing "/" reads as unset). No bug found. 9 mutations red (override ignored, either validation removed, override key, object message, theme slash rule, normalizePath bypass, model-level key). Not compared: wrong-typed values, empty strings.
- `markdown.codeBlockIndent`: Pi's getter is `markdown?.codeBlockIndent ?? "  "`, so an authored `""` means no indent and an authored `null` (which survives the project merge and overrides the global value) reads the default. `MarkdownSettings.CodeBlockIndent` was a plain string, so `""` read as the two-space default (lg-review-i1 note on r3). Now `*string` with an authored-null flag (custom Unmarshal/Marshal), merged, cloned and encoded; the r3 oracle `TestSettingsManagerGettersMatchPi` now compares `""` and `null` (red before: 4+ mismatches per 1,500 cases), regression `TestSettingsKeepAnAuthoredEmptyOrNullCodeBlockIndent` (load, project merge, merged and global encode); 8 mutations red; one equivalent (none remaining).
- Bug report files against live Pi: new `TestBugReportFilesMatchPi` (`internal/codingagent/bug_report_files_oracle_test.go`; `collectBugReportDiagnostics` over a `SessionManager` opened on 500 generated session files plus a crash log, and `bugReportFiles`; bytes of diagnostics.json, member list, session.jsonl and summary.md). Two real fixes, both red first (128 of 500, then 13): (1) report.json and diagnostics.json went through `json.Encoder`, which writes U+2028/U+2029 as `\u2028` where `JSON.stringify` writes them literally (and kept Go number text); `bugReportJSON` now canonicalises through `stringifyIndented` (`internal/codingagent/bug_report.go`); (2) a diagnostic's `details: {}` was dropped by `omitempty` (`ai.AssistantMessageDiagnostic` now has a `MarshalJSON` that keeps an empty, non-nil Details; regression `TestAssistantMessageDiagnosticKeepsAnEmptyDetailsObject`). 14 mutations red (crash `notified` strip needed the crash corpus to be a JSON array, as `readCrashLog` expects). Findings left for a ruling (questions/lg-help-4.md): PiG's `AssistantMessage` cannot tell an empty `errorMessage`/`rawStopReason` from an absent one (Pi keeps `""`), and a diagnostic's `details` is a Go map, so non-index key order is sorted, not insertion order; both are excluded from the corpus and named in the test comment. Not compared: report.json content (metadata tests), the zip container (Pi writes a classic layout, Go's archive/zip a data-descriptor layout; the members' content is what a reader sees).
- `/cost` lists its tools in a stable order: `formatCostSummary` ranged over `snap.ToolTotals`, so the `Tools:` lines came out in a different order on every call (Go map iteration). They are now sorted by name. Regression `TestFormatCostSummaryListsToolsInStableNameOrder` (red before: fails on the first run with 24 tools). `/cost` has no Pi counterpart, so the name order is PiG's choice. Found by scanning production code for map ranges that feed output; the same pattern (a map range whose first failing entry names the error) remains in `coding/packagecontent` (`validateMCPDocument`, `validateComposeClosure`) and `coding/hookconfig.Parse`, which belong to other lanes.
- `ProjectTrustStore` reads entries in JavaScript order: `readTrustFile` named whichever invalid value Go's map iteration reached first, so a trust.json with several wrong-typed values reported a random key; Pi's `Object.entries` order (array-index keys, then insertion order) decides, and `read` now walks `orderedjson` keys. New live-Pi oracle `TestProjectTrustStoreMultiEntryFilesMatchPi` (`internal/codingagent/trust_store_multi_oracle_test.go`; 1,200 trust.json files x 1-5 get/getEntry/set/setMany operations; symlinked cwd, missing paths, astral and BMP-high keys); red before the fix (4-5 of 1,200, varying with map order); 8 mutations red (key order, null rejected, false-as-true, non-object check, UTF-16 key sort, null entry stops the ancestor walk, null delete, BOM). Extends `TestProjectTrustStoreMatchesPi`, which passed before the fix. Not compared: the V8 text after `Failed to read trust store <path>: `, file modes, cross-process locking.
- `FileModelsStore` roots and index keys: new live-Pi oracle `TestFileModelsStoreNonObjectRootsAndIndexKeysMatchPi` (`ai/models_store_file_oracle_test.go`; 900 models-store.json files x 1-5 operations; null, array, number and boolean roots, integer-index provider ids, valid files holding null/number/string/array entries; TypeError text compared). Real fixes: PiG rejected every non-object root where Pi accepts JSON arrays (read as an index-keyed object, written back as an array: a deleted or unset element is `null`, an index write extends the array) and numbers/booleans (read as empty, delete rewrites the scalar, write throws `Cannot create property 'x' on number '5'`), and a null root now throws the per-operation TypeErrors (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'x')`, `Cannot set properties of null (setting 'x')`, `Cannot convert undefined or null to object`). `TestFileModelsStoreRejectsInvalidJSONAndCancelledContexts` asserted the old `[]`-is-corrupt behaviour; it now uses corrupt text. 11 mutations red (+1 untestable: the 2^32-1 array-index bound). Not compared: a stored value that is not a catalog reads back as itself in Pi (PiG's typed entry cannot hold it, so such files are only written and deleted), string-valued roots, V8 JSON parse text.
- Cache warmer pure functions against live Pi: new `TestCacheWarmerPureFunctionsMatchPi` (`internal/codingagent/cache_warmer_format_oracle_test.go`; `getCacheWarmingDelayMs`, `getPromptCacheTtlMs`, `isReplayable`, `formatCacheWarmingStatus`, `formatCacheWarmingUsage`; 6,000 inputs under three process `PI_CACHE_RETENTION` values; TTLs around the 10 s margin, tier values 0/fractional, empty request env falling through to the process one, adaptive vs budget thinking, every status state with rounding-boundary probabilities and dollars). No bug (formatCacheWarmingStatus was previously only reached through the /session oracle). 19 mutations red; 5 equivalent: empty-API guard in `IsReplayable`, `Phase`/`Action` closed-union comparisons, the `seconds > 0` clause when no larger unit printed (`nextWarmAt > now` forces at least 1 s), and the `none` retention check (a model's `promptCache` holds only short/long). Not compared: NaN/infinite TTLs.
- `String.prototype.trim` vs `strings.TrimSpace`: four Pi `.trim()` sites used Go's `strings.TrimSpace`, which trims U+0085 and not U+FEFF where JavaScript trims U+FEFF and not U+0085 (`jsstring.Trim` is the JS rule, already used in 100 other places). Fixed where the difference is observable: `Text` blank check (`tui/text.go`, text.ts:57), assistant-message text/thinking runs (`tui/assistant_message_block.go`, assistant-message.ts:110-125: a leading BOM is now trimmed before the markdown renders, U+0085 is kept), replayed empty-thinking filter (`agent/transform.go`, transform-messages.ts:111) and the proxy `data:` line (`agent/proxy.go`, proxy.ts:181). Regressions `TestTextBlankCheckTrimsLikeJavaScript`, `TestAssistantMessageComponentTrimsRunsLikeJavaScript`, `TestReplayAndProxyLinesTrimLikeJavaScript` (each red with the old call, 3 sites checked by swapping the call back). `parseStreamingJsonValue`'s guard (`ai/streaming_json.go`) was probed and left: the result is the same either way. Left for owners: `stripThinkingSignatures` (`ai/anthropic.go`, PiG's own D37 recovery, no Pi counterpart), the Pi `.trim()` sites in other providers if any still use `TrimSpace`. `./tui -race` (1,219 s) failed only the flaky `TestSurfaceRendersAnEditorBeforeAnythingAsksForItsTerminal` (race in `tui_surface_program_status_test.go:213`, passes alone) and `./agent` only `TestAgentClassMatchesPi`/`TestAgentLoopMatchesPi` (the oracle cannot import `@earendil-works/pi-ai` from `durable/interop`, a missing node_modules in this worktree).
- `getTextOutput` against live Pi: new `TestGetTextOutputMatchesPi` (`internal/codingagent/tools/render_text_output_oracle_test.go`; 2,500 seeded tool results of text blocks with ANSI, CR, control characters and empty text, and PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP/garbage/empty image blocks with matching, mismatching, empty and unknown mime types, with images off, kitty or iterm2 and showImages on or off). No bug; 11 mutations: 9 red, 2 equivalent (the `Data != ""` and `MimeType != ""` guards before `GetImageDimensions`, which returns nothing for empty data or an empty mime type anyway). Not compared: an explicit empty-string mime type (not representable in `ai.ImageContent`).
- Image resize strategy against live Pi: new `TestResizeImageDimensionStrategyMatchesPi` (`internal/imageprocessing/resize_strategy_oracle_test.go`; `resizeImageInProcess` through Photon vs `ResizeImage`; 200 seeded PNGs 1-2,600 px, maxWidth/maxHeight 1-3,000, default/huge/1-byte maxBytes, unset/explicit mime). One real fix, red first (15 of 300): a scaled side that rounds to zero (8000x1 under the 2000 limit gives a height of round(0.25) = 0) made PiG clamp it to one pixel and return an image, where Pi's Photon resize throws and `resizeImageInProcess` returns null, so the image is dropped; `ResizeImage` now returns `ErrImageTooLarge` (`internal/imageprocessing/images.go`). Regression `TestResizeImageReturnsNoImageWhenAScaledSideRoundsToZero`. 5 mutations: 4 red; the 0.75 shrink factor survives because the shrink loop is reached only when every candidate misses maxBytes, which depends on the encoders (named as not compared). Not compared: the encoded bytes, which candidate wins a tight maxBytes, BMP input (PiG-only branch), the shrink steps.
- `processImage` against live Pi: new `TestProcessImageMatchesPi` (`internal/imageprocessing/process_image_oracle_test.go`; stacked on r16 because it needs the zero-side fix; 400 seeded inputs: PNG/JPEG/GIF/BMP/TIFF/WebP, garbage and empty bytes, declared types with parameters/case/spaces, unsupported and mismatched types, resizing on/off with small limits; ok, mime type, ordered hints and omission message compared). One real fix, red first (9 of 400, 8 of them this): `ResizeImage` excluded BMP bytes from the "already within every limit" shortcut (`&& srcFormat != "bmp"`, undocumented, from the first release), so BMP bytes declared as a supported type (png/jpeg/gif/webp) were re-encoded to PNG with a dimension note where Pi returns the bytes unchanged under the declared type; the clause is gone (a BMP declared `image/bmp` is converted to PNG before resizing, in both). 9 mutations red; 1 equivalent (`from == to` in the conversion hint: an unsupported declared type never equals the PNG/JPEG result). Not compared: the returned bytes.
- `find` and `grep` trim like JavaScript: `FindTool` trimmed each fd output line with `strings.TrimSpace` where find.ts:270 uses `String.prototype.trim`, so a file whose name ends with U+FEFF lost nothing in Pi but a name ending in U+0085 lost it in PiG (and the reverse). `find`'s lines and stderr message and `grep`'s stderr message and blank-line check now use `jsstring.Trim` (find.ts:252,270; grep.ts:220,252). New live-Pi oracle `TestFindTrimsOutputLinesLikeJavaScript` (`internal/codingagent/tools/find_trim_names_oracle_test.go`: names beginning or ending with U+FEFF, U+0085, U+00A0 or a space, and names that are only U+FEFF or U+0085); red before the fix (3 of 7 directories), green after; `./internal/codingagent/tools -race` green. The stderr and ripgrep blank-line trims are not reachable by the oracle (they need fd or rg to fail or emit a blank JSON line) and are changed for the same rule only.
- Tool-call header argument preview no longer splits a character: `truncateArg` (`tui/tool_execution.go`) cut to `max-1` bytes, so a non-ASCII argument longer than the budget ended in a half character that the JSON quoting turned into a visible U+FFFD before the ellipsis (`text:"ééé…é�…"`); it now backs up to the start of a character. Regression `TestFormatToolArgsTruncatesAtACharacterBoundary` (red before: é, 日 and 🙂 at four byte offsets; both mutations, no back-up and cutting at `max`, red); `./tui -race` green (1,139 s). Found by scanning production code for byte `len()` comparisons against limits. `HeaderForTool`'s fallback (`FormatToolArgs`: `name path:"x"`) is PiG's own format for a tool with no builtin formatter and no definition, where Pi renders `formatToolCallWithArgs`; that mismatch is the open "tool card with no definition" ruling request in questions/lg-help-4.md.
- `formatToolCallWithArgs` against live Pi: new `TestFormatToolCallWithArgsMatchesPi` (`tui/tool_call_args_oracle_test.go`; real dark and light themes; 3,000 seeded argument documents as JSON text with whitespace, repeated keys, escapes, integer-like keys, odd number text, tabs/CR/newlines/emoji, nested values and non-object arguments; collapsed and expanded, colour escapes included). No bug (the earlier port is faithful, including the 100-unit cut and the 97-unit ellipsis); the cut can split a surrogate pair, which PiG keeps as WTF-8 until the terminal write, so the test compares `jsstring.StreamText(got)`. 11 mutations: 10 red; `muted` vs `toolOutput` is equivalent (both resolve to the same colour in the dark and light themes). Related finding, not changed (no production caller): `agent.EstimateTokens` / `agent.EstimateContextTokens` (agent/context_tokens.go) count UTF-8 bytes where Pi's estimateTokens counts UTF-16 units; the production path is `internal/codingagent/compaction` (oracle-tested), so the `agent` copy is a caller-free duplicate.
- `formatThousands` (`tui/compaction_summary.go`, compaction-summary-message.ts:40 `tokensBefore.toLocaleString()`) wrote a negative count as `-,123` (the separator rule counted the minus sign as a digit); it now keeps the sign out of the grouping. New live-Pi test `TestFormatThousandsMatchesPi` (28 values, both signs, up to ±2^53; red with the sign handling off or the grouping shifted). A negative `tokensBefore` only comes from a hand-edited session file.
- Live-Pi oracles for `utils/headers.ts`: `TestHeadersToRecordMatchesPi` (800 header sequences over case variants of 15 names, repeated names, set-cookie, empty values; compares `headersToRecord` with Pi's `Headers.entries` record) and `TestProviderHeadersToRecordMatchesPi` (1,500 source lists with null deletes and spelling replacement; `providerHeadersToRecord`), both in `ai/headers_utils_oracle_test.go`. No bug found; 8 of 9 mutations red (last-set-cookie, ", " join, lowercase key, set-cookie special case, delete-before-set, later spelling, undefined result, null handling); the ninth (dropping the empty-value-list skip in `headersToRecord`) is equivalent because `Headers` never holds a name with no values. Not covered: two spellings of one name inside a single `ai.ProviderHeaders` source, which Pi resolves by insertion order and a Go map cannot express (the later key wins in Pi; PiG's result depends on map order).
- openai-completions thinking formats now match Pi (`ai/openai.go`, openai-completions.ts:885-985 and 1034-1075), found by the new live-Pi oracle `TestOpenAICompletionsThinkingFormatsPayloadMatchesPi` (3,000 probes: every `thinkingFormat`, `compat.chatTemplateKwargs`/`chatTemplateArgs` with scalars, null, `$var` placeholders, `omitWhenOff`, arrays, and model `thinkingLevelMap` entries; 32 of 1,200 probes differed before any fix, 4 more after widening the corpus). Fixes: (1) `chat-template` with no resolved value sent `"chat_template_kwargs": null` (a typed nil map in an `any` field defeated `omitempty`) where Pi omits the member; (2) `resolveChatTemplateValues` dropped a placeholder whose `$var` is not `thinking.enabled`/`thinking.budget` or a `thinking.effort` that resolves, where Pi maps every other object (unknown `$var`, `{}`, arrays) to the mapped effort; (3) the `baseten` format sent `reasoning_effort: "none"` when the model had no `off` mapping (Pi sends nothing) and sent the clamped level for a level mapped to `null` (Pi sends nothing), now `templateEffort`; (4) the `ant-ling` format with no requested effort ignored Pi's fall-through to the off-value `reasoning_effort` (:970-975); (5) a `thinkingLevelMap` value of `""` was dropped by `omitempty` where Pi sends `reasoning_effort: ""`, so the request field is now `*string`. Regressions: the oracle plus `TestResolveChatTemplateValuesMapsEveryOtherObjectToTheEffort`; 8 mutations red, 1 not applicable. `./ai -race` green except the env-only `TestSDKProvidersRequestURLMatchesSDK` and `TestOpenAIStreamDecoderMatchesSDK` (need `npm ci` in `ai/testdata/sdk`). Hot file: `ai/openai.go` belongs to go-idioms.
- Live-Pi oracle `TestOpenAICompletionsCompatAndCacheOptionsPayloadMatchesPi` (`ai/openai_completions_compat_options_oracle_test.go`, 2,500 probes) for the openai-completions compat members and options the other payload oracles never draw: supportsFinishReason, zaiToolStream, supportsOpenAIGrammarTools, supportsMidConvoSystemMessages/ToolAdditions, supportsThinkingTokenBudget, thinkingTokenBudgetField, cacheControlFormat, supportsLongCacheRetention, openRouterRouting, vercelGatewayRouting, cacheRetention and sessionId, over OpenAI/OpenRouter/Vercel/Z.ai/xAI hosts and 0-2 mid-conversation system messages (text, sections, toolsAdded, toolsRemoved). No payload difference found. Mutations: 9 red (zaiToolStream, both routings, long-cache gate, 24h retention, thinking-budget default gate, mid-convo system collapse in both directions, mid-convo tool-addition gate off, prompt-cache gate); equivalent: the `true &&` form of the tool-addition gate (no system message survives collapse without the system flag) and the `compatBool` default arguments (detected compat always sets each flag). Not reached by this oracle: grammar-tool compat (covered by constrained_sampling_oracle_test.go) and supportsFinishReason (a response-parsing flag). Not covered: `sessionId` set to the empty string, which Pi sends as `prompt_cache_key: ""` and `ai.StreamOptions.SessionID` cannot tell from unset.
- anthropic-messages payload now matches Pi for four cases found by the new live-Pi oracle `TestAnthropicMessagesCompatAndOptionsPayloadMatchesPi` (`ai/anthropic.go`, anthropic-messages.ts:1132, 949-990, 1154-1164; 1,500 probes over `supportsMidConvoEffort`/`supportsMidConvoSystemMessages`/`supportsMidConvoToolChanges`/`allowedFallbackModels`/`forceAdaptiveThinking`, `interleavedThinking`, `thinkingBudgets`, env cache retention, object tool choices, odd metadata, model `maxTokens`, `thinkingLevelMap`, and mid-conversation system messages with sections and tool changes): (1) the initial system message is the transcript's first message as given, so a mid-conversation system message after a dropped errored/aborted assistant turn is no longer taken for the prompt; (2) a managed-effort request without `forceAdaptiveThinking` takes the effort `high` (streamSimple derives it only for adaptive models); (3) an unmapped `xhigh`/`max` level is `high` (mapThinkingLevelToEffort has no such case); (4) a managed-effort simple request still gets the budget-adjusted `max_tokens`. openai-codex-responses now picks the instruction role of a mid-conversation system message from the selected model's `reasoning` flag instead of always `developer`; the bedrock, google and responses payload oracles now draw mid-conversation system messages too. Mutation-checked: 10 mutants red (initial from transformed list, effort default, managed max_tokens, xhigh case, fallbacks model/beta, interleaved beta, mid-convo effort beta, native tool changes anchor).
- Live-Pi oracle `TestMistralMessagesPayloadMatchesPi` (`ai/mistral_messages_payload_oracle_test.go`, mistral-conversations.ts toChatMessages and options; 1,200 random transcripts with thinking, errored/aborted turns, foreign tool calls, tool results with images and mid-conversation system messages, thinking level maps, sampling options, tool choices): the earlier Mistral payload oracles drew one user message, so the message conversion was only checked by two hand-written bodies. No payload difference. Mutation-checked: dropping the initial-system slice, the tool-result error text and the image data-URL prefix go red; `sanitizeSurrogates` on the system prompt is not drawn (generated text has no lone surrogate).
- openai-codex-responses reads `instructions` from the initial system message of the resolved transcript before `transformMessages` drops an errored or aborted assistant turn (`ai/openai_responses.go`, openai-codex-responses.ts:551): a transcript that starts with such a turn followed by a system message no longer sends that message as the prompt. Found by drawing the responses compat members the payload oracle never drew (`supportsDeveloperRole`, `supportsAdditionalTools`, `supportsToolSearch`, `supportsExplicitPromptCacheMode`, `sessionAffinityFormat`, `supportsOpenAIGrammarTools`) in `checkResponsesPayload`; 5 mutants of those flags and the codex initial-system source go red (grammar tools stay inert there: the drawn tools carry no `constrainedSampling`, covered by `constrained_sampling_oracle_test.go`).
- The /config selector keeps the first-seen order of groups that tie on origin, scope and source (auto resources from different base directories); it followed Go map order (`tui/config_selector.go` BuildResourceGroups, config-selector.ts buildGroups; regression `TestBuildResourceGroupsTiesKeepFirstSeenOrder`).
- Extension shortcut conflict warnings name both extensions ("registered by both A and B. Using B."), and shortcut keys are lower-cased with JavaScript rules so `İ` no longer collides with `i` (`coding/extension/host/inproc/runner.go` Shortcuts, runner.ts:673-716; live-Pi oracle `TestRunnerShortcutsMatchPi`, 800 probes; mutations on the message, both ToLower sites and the reserved-wins rule fail it).
- `ModelsError` appends the cause detail the way Pi does: an Error cause with an empty message contributes its name ("load failed: TypeError") instead of nothing (`ai/auth_resolve.go` NewModelsError, models-error.ts withCauseDetail; live-Pi oracle `TestModelsErrorMessageMatchesPi`, 3,000 probes, 147 red before; three mutations red).
- `/hotkeys` lists extension shortcuts, and the extension shortcut listener tries them, in the order of Pi's `getShortcuts` Map (load order, registration order, a re-registered key keeps its first position) instead of path then key / Go map order (`coding/extension/host/inproc/runner.go` OrderedShortcuts, `internal/codingagent/interactive_hotkeys.go`, `interactive_helpers.go`; `TestRunnerShortcutsMatchPi` now compares Map order, regression `TestHotkeysCommandListsExtensionShortcutsInRegistrationOrder`).
- Live-Pi oracles `TestExtensionSelectorMatchesPi` (4,320 probes) and `TestExtensionInputMatchesPi` (1,144 probes) (`tui/extension_selector_oracle_test.go`, `tui/extension_input_oracle_test.go`): every frame and callback after each key against the pinned Pi component, default and rebound select keys, descriptions, wide/empty content, focus; no bug found; six and seven mutations red (the lone-LF confirm needed its own probe).
- The extension editor dialog (`ctx.ui.editor()`, `/tree` summary prompt) shows the hint keys the user bound for submit, newline and cancel instead of the default key names (`internal/codingagent/extension_editor.go`, extension-editor.ts keyHint; extended `TestExtensionEditorHandleInputMatchesPi` renders the hint row under five rebindings, red before).
- A tool card whose `app.tools.expand` key is unbound shows an empty key in "... (N more lines, to expand)" as Pi does, instead of the default `ctrl+o` (`tui/keybinding_hints.go` AppKeyText falls back only when no resolver is installed; `TestBuiltinToolCardsMatchPi` gained rebound/multi-key/unbound expand probes, 5 red before).
- `TestMcpManagerViewMatchesPi` renders the /mcp manager footers under rebound, multi-key and unbound `tui.select.confirm`/`cancel`/up/down (a quarter of its 120 probes, against the merged tui and app table); no difference; the hard-coded-hint mutation is red (a matching-path mutation is not observable in the rendered rows).
- `TestLlamaViewMatchesPi` runs a quarter of its 700 scenarios under rebound, multi-key and unbound `tui.select.*` bindings (hint rows and the progress view's cancel key); no difference; mutations on the hint key, the stop hint and the progress cancel match are red.
- `TestCodemodeCardsMatchPi` and `TestMcpToolCardsMatchPi` repeat their collapsed probes under rebound, multi-key and unbound `app.tools.expand` (the expand hint of the codemode and MCP cards); no difference; hard-coded-key mutations are red.
- `TestBashExecutionComponentMatchesPi` probes the collapsed expand hint under rebound, multi-key and unbound `app.tools.expand` with the merged tui and app table; no difference; the fixed-key mutation is red.
- `TestSummaryAndSkillMessageComponentsMatchPi` probes the compaction, branch summary and skill messages under rebound, multi-key and unbound `app.tools.expand`; no difference; fixed-key mutations in each component are red.
- `TestSettingsSelectorMatchesPi` walks every row under rebound, multi-key and unbound `app.message.followUp` / `app.thinking.cycle` and rebound select keys, so the row descriptions that name those keys are compared; no difference; fixed-key mutations on both descriptions are red.
- The `ExtensionSelector` and `ExtensionInput` oracles also run with the Kitty keyboard protocol active (CSI-u enter, escape, ctrl+j, ctrl+c, shift+enter, letters, modified arrows) in Pi and Pig; no difference.
- `TestSelectListInputMatchesPi` also runs under the Kitty keyboard protocol (CSI-u enter, escape, ctrl+j, ctrl+c, shift+enter, modified arrows, rebound select keys); no difference; a literal-LF confirm mutation is red only in Kitty mode.
- `TestSettingsSelectorMatchesPi` opens each row under the Kitty keyboard protocol with CSI-u keys; no difference.
- The `/scoped-models` search input stays editable after a submit key reaches it (LF under the Kitty keyboard protocol, or Enter with `tui.select.confirm` rebound): it was a host-owned input that finished on submit and ignored all later typing; Pi builds `new Input()` with no `onSubmit` (`tui/scoped_models_list.go`; `TestScopedModelsSelectorMatchesPi` now also runs 336 Kitty-protocol probes, red before; regression `TestScopedModelsSearchStaysEditableAfterASubmitKey`).
- The login dialog now holds TUI focus while it is shown, so the prompt cursor appears in the right place for IME input.
- `SessionBeforeTreeResultSummary.Usage` is typed as `ai.Usage`.
- Tool cards rebuilt when a session is resumed now follow the `showImages` and `imageWidthCells` settings.
- A Radius gateway model's `samplingParamsByThinkingLevel` now reaches the `pi-messages` model and the composed catalog entry (Pi's `getRadiusModelsFromConfig` spreads each gateway model); it was dropped before.
- `client.Client.Disconnect` takes the reason string as Pi's `Client.disconnect(reason)` does (it was an `error`); the connection fails with a `DisconnectedError` carrying it.
- OpenAI Responses prices a response that reports no `service_tier` at the requested tier, as Pi's `processResponsesStream` does (`response.service_tier ?? options.serviceTier`); only Codex did before, so a flex or priority request to OpenAI Responses was billed at the default rate.
- `createPowerShellTool` now uses the `operations` option. The PowerShell tool dropped it and always ran commands through the local PowerShell.
- The editor's autocomplete list takes its colors from one `getSelectListTheme` equivalent, the same function the other select lists use.
- `llama-cpp-classify` answers match Pi bit for bit: the softmax uses `jsmath.Exp`, V8's fdlibm `Math.exp` (Go's `math.Exp` differs in the last bit for some arguments on amd64). Its requests are written with their members in Pi's order, and a failed lookup no longer cancels the sibling requests of the same `Promise.all`, so the server receives what it receives from Pi.
- `llama-cpp-classify` answers match Pi bit for bit: the softmax uses `jsmath.Exp`, V8's fdlibm `Math.exp` (Go's `math.Exp` differs in the last bit for some arguments on amd64). Its requests are written with their members in Pi's order, and a failed lookup no longer cancels the sibling requests of the same `Promise.all`, as in Pi.
- The login dialog renders its one input at every prompt position until a submit replaces them with the submitted text, and keeps it after a cancel, as Pi's `LoginDialogComponent` does; an unanswered earlier prompt no longer renders blank.
- In print and JSON mode, Node extensions in separate processes now keep Pi's order when a later extension's `session_shutdown` or `agent_settled` handler awaits a timer, however busy the machine is. The immediate and the timer an earlier extension's handler queued ran before that handler resumed only if the earlier process read the forwarded `loop_turned` in time; a process the OS kept waiting ran them after the handler answered, with a stale ctx once the runner was invalidated. The yielding process now stays in its turn until every other Node process reports it took the turn (`loop_turn_taken`), after the immediates and due timers it queued before the turn ran.
- Session lines (and every other JSON.stringify-mode encode) now write ill-formed UTF-8 the way Pi does: one raw U+FFFD per WHATWG maximal subpart (what a JavaScript string decoded from those bytes holds, and what JSON.stringify writes). PiG used to write one `\ufffd` escape per bad byte, so `"a\xe2\x82b"` became `"a\ufffd\ufffdb"` where Pi writes `"a�b"`. Nested `\ufffd` escapes from PiG's own types are written raw as well. Session files written before this change keep the old form and read back the same strings. On the extension wire (the codec's `Marshal`/`MarshalJS`), ill-formed UTF-8 also counts one replacement per maximal subpart rather than per byte; the `\ufffd` escape form there is unchanged.
- A tool card shows the arguments of a tool call while they stream, as Pi does: a streamed write card shows its first lines and the "(N more lines, M total" hint as the content arrives, instead of a bare `write ...` header until the last delta. The card reads the arguments the provider's streaming JSON parser has already decoded and encodes them only when it draws, so a delta costs the same however long the arguments are (10 allocations and 1.7 KB per delta at 1 KB and at 504 KB received).
- A streamed write card no longer slows down as its content grows. Since streamed tool cards started drawing their partial arguments, every frame encoded the whole argument object and decoded it twice, and the write renderer split the whole content again, so a 400 KB write cost O(n²) (pauses write:400: 15 s CPU and 395 MB own memory, against 6.6 s and 60 MB before). The card now hands the built-in write renderer the object the streaming parser already holds, and the renderer extends its line count and highlighted lines from the previous frame, highlighting only the lines a frame draws. A frame allocates 77 KB at 400 KB received, as at 40 KB (3.1 MB before, 6.4 times the 40 KB frame). A settled write card keeps no highlighted copy of its content.
- Markdown: a rule written with the list's own marker (`* * *`) after a blank line ends the list with its blank row, and a line after a three-level nested list is a lazy line only where each enclosing item allows it, as in Pi.
- Markdown: a list item line that is a rule (`- a`, a blank line, `  - - -`; or `- * * *`) renders as a rule inside the item instead of hanging the renderer.
- Markdown: an indented line that a table delimiter row (`---`) follows joins the paragraph before it with its indent removed, instead of rendering as a separate code block, as in Pi.
- Markdown: a text that ends in one line of spaces after an indented code block, an HTML block or a table no longer renders the extra empty row, a fence right after an HTML comment gets no spacing row, and an indented line after an empty quoted line starts a code block in the quote, as in Pi.
- Markdown no longer adds a blank row after a quote or block when only a repeated link definition follows it, and a reference before its definition no longer changes that.
- Markdown: a line that continues a nested list item's paragraph (`- a`, `  - b`, `  x`) renders at the nested item's text column, and a bullet with an empty first line followed by a nested list keeps its empty bullet row, as in Pi.
- Markdown list items lex their text line by line like Pi: an HTML block starts at any item line, and an item line indented four or more spaces past the content joins the text before it (so a heading, quote, rule, fence, table or bullet there stays text) and leaves an empty row before the text that follows. <!-- fix: 74fb98c362 -->
- Markdown no longer adds an empty row after a table that is too narrow to draw, when the table is inside a list item and another block follows it.
- Markdown: list items whose markers are indented by different amounts (0 to 3 spaces) form one list, so a loose list keeps its empty rows between them, as in Pi.
- Markdown rendering shows a horizontal rule (`- - -`, `* * *`) inside a nested list item as a rule, as Pi does, instead of an empty bullet.
- Markdown nested lists treat a less-indented continuation line the way Pi does: a line indented to a nested item's content relative to its parent stays in that item, and a `--- | ---` line no longer ends the item.
- An indented line after a quoted heading, rule or fence in a blockquote renders as the code block Pi shows. <!-- fix: 677ded1860 -->
- A table delimiter row on a lazy blockquote line no longer splits the quoted paragraph above it unless it forms a table with a lazy header of the same cell count.
- A setext underline on a lazy line after another lazy line in a blockquote now renders as Pi does, joined to the quoted paragraph indented by four spaces.
- Markdown: a blockquote whose last quoted line holds only spaces or tabs (`> q`, `>  `, `x`) now renders the lazy line in the same paragraph, with the whitespace line kept as an empty row, as Pi does.
- Markdown rendering matches Pi on blank lines at the start and end of the text, empty headings and closing `#` runs, and unterminated code fences: a text that starts with a blank line begins with one empty row, a trailing line of spaces after a list, table or quote adds one, a closing run of `#` is dropped from a heading, an empty heading keeps its row, and an unterminated fence does not show the empty line its final newline splits off.
- An empty list item ends its list: a following unindented line is no longer taken for its lazy continuation, and spaces ending a list's last line render the empty row Pi's space token gives.
- A list item whose first line is another list item is rendered as a nested list with no bullet of its own, and an indented line that lazily continues a quoted paragraph loses up to four leading spaces, as in Pi.
- An indented continuation line of a list item loses up to four spaces, a lazy line after a quoted list item that ends in spaces is a paragraph of its own, the last list item's text is trimmed, and a text of only link definitions or an empty quote followed by blank lines renders its empty row.
- Text after an empty heading follows the same space-token rule as after any other block: blank lines or spaces after a final empty heading add the empty row Pi shows.
- A lazy line no longer continues a quote whose last quoted line is empty (`>\nnext` is an empty quote then a paragraph), and a quote that renders no rows still adds its spacing row.
- A lazy line joins a list item as written (indentation kept), four spaces after the marker make the first line indented code that no lazy line continues, the break test uses the content indent to limit rules/headings/fences, a blank-after-slice line no longer ends the item, a lazy line inside an item's fence stays in the fence, and a LaTeX block that takes the end of the text leaves no trailing space row.
- A nested list whose last item is empty but for spaces after its marker is followed by an empty row before the item's next text (`- - \n  a`).
- A task list item whose first line looks like a heading, quote, rule, bullet, fence, table row or HTML tag renders that line as text, and a setext heading over it keeps the checkbox marker, as Pi does. <!-- fix: fc749adcb9 -->
- A list item whose text is `[ ] ` or `[x] ` with nothing after the spaces on the line is plain text, not a task item (marked's listIsTask needs a non-whitespace character after the spaces), so `- [ ] ` renders as `- [ ]` like Pi. <!-- fix: ca675e0af0 -->
- Markdown task lists whose later item is a heading (`- [ ] b` over `===`) keep and strip checkbox markers on the same items as Pi.
- Markdown rendering follows Pi for lines made only of Unicode spaces (U+00A0, U+3000, U+2003 and the others JavaScript's trim removes) in and after lists, a list item whose first line is one such space, a heading line holding U+2028 or U+2029, a table whose last line is such a space at a narrow width, and a `$$` or `\[` block whose text is only whitespace. <!-- fix: c3a6eca380 -->
- A line of only spaces and tabs that starts with a tab or four spaces, alone or after a blank line or in a blockquote, is blank in markdown rendering instead of an empty code block, as in Pi.
- MCP HTTP servers and the OAuth requests of a sign-in now report a connection that fails before any response as `fetch failed`, as Pi does (Node fetch rejection), instead of Go's `Post "<url>": dial tcp ...` text in `/mcp`, `pi mcp list` and `pi mcp login`.
- A subprocess extension's markdown transform that was busy past the five-second renderer inactivity boundary no longer leaves the message untransformed until something else draws it again: the host asks again once the extension answers the abandoned transform, or after a backoff that starts at five seconds and doubles to one minute for an extension that keeps missing the boundary, and the message transforms again on its next frame.
- A Go, Rust or Python extension that calls `parseFrontmatter` or `Date.parse` while its factory runs, in a runtime cell shared with other extensions, no longer waits forever when another member of the cell has already connected. The helper now uses the connection of the extension that owns the socket the runner names, and dials that socket when the extension has not connected yet, instead of reusing a sibling's connection that the host serves only later.
- A session message whose members are in another order than Pi writes them, or that holds members PiG does not model, now reaches RPC `get_messages`, extension events and the built session context exactly as Pi returns it: the object parsed from the file, written with `JSON.stringify`. Before, PiG rewrote such a message in its own member order and dropped unknown members. Context edits and handler replacements without content keep the content member in place, as the JavaScript spread does. A system message without content is kept and gets empty content, as in Pi; before, PiG dropped it. Messages Pi wrote are unaffected and retain no extra memory.
- Meta OAuth login now reads the server's responses as Pi does: an invalid device-authorization response is quoted as `JSON.stringify` writes it (an array body as the array, members in the server's order, numbers as JavaScript prints them), a number past the double range is an unusable interval instead of voiding the whole body, a leading byte order mark or ill-formed UTF-8 is read as `fetch` does, and a body that fails to read counts as `null`.
- Case-insensitive glob matching compares as a JavaScript regular expression does: `İ` no longer matches `i`, the Kelvin sign no longer matches `k`, and the Angstrom sign no longer matches `å` (the engine lower-cased both sides).
- The Mistral request URL for a base URL with a non-special scheme or a host or port the WHATWG parser rejects (for example a port above 65535) follows Pi's `new URL`: it fails where Pi throws and keeps the base path where Pi does.
- `/login` offers a provider object's own login methods (Pi's `registerNativeProvider`, the `registerProvider(provider)` overload). A provider whose `auth` declares an OAuth method now offers its account login under the flow's own `loginLabel` (the generic "Sign in with an account" label is replaced), and it stays offered after the provider's catalog refresh. Reported and diagnosed by @balcsida, whose branch the login carrier started from (#201).
- A login flow's `select` prompt opens a selector in the login dialog, as Pi's does, instead of ending the login as "Login cancelled". Cancelling the selector cancels the login and returns to the menu it was started from. This applies to provider objects and to `config.oauth` providers.
- A provider object's own `auth.apiKey.login` runs in the login dialog, as Pi's `showApiKeyLoginDialog` runs it, so a provider that asks for its base URL before the key stores both. Each answered prompt stays visible in the dialog. A provider object without its own API-key login keeps the generic API-key prompt.
- A provider object's login that sends a prompt or event type Pi does not define fails with an error that names the type, instead of being answered as a text prompt or shown as progress.
- A cancelled prompt in a `config.oauth` login now rejects with Pi's `Login cancelled` in every SDK (it was `oauth prompt cancelled`, and Node's `onSelect` resolved `undefined`), so `/login` returns to the menu instead of reporting a failed login.
- A `config.oauth` login's `onManualCodeInput` asks "Paste the authorization code", as Pi does, and a provider object's `manual_code` prompt shows its own message and placeholder.
- An extension login callback (`onPrompt`, `onSelect`, `onManualCodeInput`) whose prompt fails for a reason other than the user's cancel now reports that error to the flow. Before, every failure reached the flow as a cancel.
- A login flow's text prompt accepts an empty answer, as Pi's does, instead of failing with "<prompt> is required".
- A provider object's OAuth login receives Pi's login options, as `oauth.login(interaction, options)` does in Pi. `options.getDeviceId()` returns the app's device ID, and the app creates that ID only when a flow first calls it. `options.agentName` is passed when the app sets it. Before, a provider object's flow got no options, so a flow that needs the device ID could not get it.
- NO_PROXY entries and ports are trimmed with JavaScript's whitespace rules, as in Pi: an entry or port that starts with U+0085 is no longer stripped, and one that starts with U+FEFF is.
- On Windows, path normalization and joining follow Node 24's reserved device name handling: a path such as `CON:` or `\\?\COM1:` keeps its device, `path.join` leaves a part naming one unnormalized, and a `\\?\name` or `\\.\name` device path no longer gains a trailing backslash.
- Where Pi calls `new URL(value)`, Pig now parses a `ws:`, `wss:`, `ftp:` or `file:` URL, and a bracketed IPv6 host of any other scheme, instead of refusing it. This affects package and repository sources (`coding/source`), the OAuth paste and GitHub Copilot enterprise-domain inputs.
- A parsed http, https, ws, wss or ftp URL reports its fragment percent-encoded, as Node's `url.hash` does.
- `/login` runs a provider object's OAuth login with Pi's login interaction, so an `info` event shows in the login dialog with its message and links, as Pi shows it. Before, PiG showed the message as a progress line and dropped the links. A `secret` prompt in that login now follows the **Mask secret input** setting, as it does in an API-key login.
- An extension's API-key login no longer shows "Waiting for authentication..." after an authorization URL. Pi shows that line only after a device code.
- Pasting an OAuth authorization into the Anthropic, OpenAI Codex or OpenRouter login now reads it the way Pi does. A `code#state` paste keeps only the text up to the next `#` as the state. A bare query string with a leading `?`, a `;`, or a malformed `%` escape keeps the code instead of dropping it. A URL of any scheme that `new URL` refuses, such as one whose port is above 65535, is read as a plain code, not as a callback URL. An empty pasted state (`code#` or `state=`) reaches Anthropic's token request as an empty state, as in Pi, instead of being replaced by the login's own state.
- OpenAI ChatGPT and Codex OAuth token failures with an empty body report the reason phrase the server sent (`response.statusText`), as Pi does, instead of the canonical text for the status code.
- The Kimi Code, Meta and xAI device logins judge the verification URI the way Pi does, and Meta and xAI open the URL Pi opens.
