A `https:host` or backslash form is read as a URL; a port above 65535, an out-of-range numeric host, or a space in the host is refused. The opened URL is normalized as `new URL` does (lowercase scheme and host, default port and empty userinfo dropped, an empty path becomes `/`) instead of being echoed as received.
- `pig --piglet piglets/standard/pig-standard.yaml` no longer warns on every start that its `piglogin` extension registers `/sprite`, so the built-in `pig-login` extension was not loaded. PiG Standard now strips the built-in `pig-login` (`strip.extensions: [pig-login]`), and its own `piglogin` extension owns `/sprite`.
- `pig piglet add` now names the offending bundled path and where it came from (for example `Package herdr origin "local:../../components/herdr"`) and says it leaves the added subdirectory, instead of a bare "must be a portable relative path" error.
- `pig piglet build --format binary` from a Pig source directory that is not a Git checkout (for example `PIG_SOURCE_ROOT` pointing at an extracted archive) now says the directory must be a Git checkout of PiG, names it, and suggests cloning PiG or unsetting `PIG_SOURCE_ROOT`, instead of failing with `resolve Pig source revision: exit status 128`. Other git failures now include git's error line.
- `pig setup` now flags a go on PATH older than Go 1.26, the extension SDK minimum, naming the version found and the fix, and extension builds use the toolchain `pig setup go` installed instead of that older go (pig additive, D20).
- `pig auth --help` names `pig auth ...` instead of `pi auth ...`.
- `pig extension --help` lists `pig extension init --login`.
- `pig --help` lists `pig login`, `pig logout`, `pig package`, `pig extension upgrade`, `pig reload`, and `pig diagnose`.
- OpenDirReader on a FIFO now fails with not_directory at once instead of waiting for a writer.
- OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions requests resolve `chat_template_args` (the `baseten` thinking format) and `chat_template_kwargs` (the `chat-template` format) as Pi does. A `thinking.budget` variable in `chat_template_args` now sends the clamped thinking budget instead of being dropped. A `thinking.effort` variable sends the model's `thinkingLevelMap.off` value when reasoning is off and is left out when that entry is absent or not a string, instead of sending `"none"`. A `null` entry for the requested level also leaves it out, instead of sending the level name.
- OpenAI-compatible chat completions requests keep the tool-call ids of the same model's earlier calls as they were sent. As in Pi, only a tool call of another model is normalized: a `|`-separated id is shortened, and on the `openai` provider an id over 40 characters is truncated. Before, every id in the request was rewritten, including the model's own.
- `openai-decisions` writes the image input's user message, text part and image parts with their members in Pi's order (`role` before `content`, `type` before the value), not in Go map order.
- OpenAI Responses streams follow Pi's `processResponsesStream` on malformed and edge events, checked against the pinned Pi in a new oracle test: a failed response records only its status (no usage or response id), `end_turn` is recorded only for Codex, an `error` event without a code or message reads `Error Code undefined: undefined`, a `response.created` or terminal event without a response reports Pi's TypeError text, and a reasoning signature backfilled with `encrypted_content` keeps its original key order.
- The openai-completions, openai-responses and openrouter-images requests build their URL as the openai SDK does (base query rewritten through qs, one slash dropped, WHATWG normalisation) and a URL with credentials is refused as fetch refuses it ("Connection error."), where Pig sent the credentials as Basic auth. <!-- fix: 2ea8ad24ae, a8ff0c7afe -->
- The openai stream decoder follows openai 7.19.0, the version packages/ai declares: the stream ends at [DONE] without waiting for the connection to close, the last event is delivered when the server omits its blank line, [DONE] must be the whole data, and an event named error throws an APIError. <!-- fix: 24a821d908 -->
- OpenRouter image generation reads the chat completions body as Pi does, checked against the pinned Pi in a new oracle test: a body with no `choices` (OpenRouter sometimes answers 200 with an error object) fails with Pi's TypeError instead of returning an empty success, content, image and usage members of other types are skipped or fail with the same message, and an unparseable body reports the JSON syntax error.
- OpenRouter OAuth key exchange now ignores a leading byte order mark in the response body, as Pi's `Response.json()` does.
- MCP servers whose names are numbers (`"9"`, `"42"`) load and report their errors in the order Pi does: a JSON object lists its array-index keys first, in ascending order, then the others in file order. The ordered JSON object the MCP configuration, tool arguments and other raw objects use now follows that rule.
- A request sent after `/tree` navigation to a point between parallel tool calls and their results no longer fails with "unexpected tool_use_id in tool_result blocks". A branch summary, compaction summary, bash execution or custom message now ends the pending tool calls like any user turn, so the placeholder results come before it. A system message between a tool call and its results is now held until after the results, as in Pi.
- Fullscreen double-click selects the word Pi selects: words come from the same ICU segmentation as Intl.Segmenter (Thai, Lao, Khmer and Burmese dictionary words, Devanagari conjuncts, emoji ZWJ sequences), and thinking-block wrapping and Mermaid widths split only between Intl.Segmenter graphemes.
- A local file listed as a Package with `autoload: false` still loads as one enabled extension, and when a project lists a Package with `autoload: false`, every user-settings entry for that Package is kept, as in Pi's `resolveLocalExtensionSource` and `dedupePackages`.
- Repairing a partial tool-call arguments text follows partial-json's `obj["__proto__"] = value`: a `"__proto__": null` member leaves the object without a prototype, so a later `"__proto__"` member is an ordinary member, as in Pi (it was dropped).
- Tool-call arguments that arrive as incomplete or malformed JSON are now recovered exactly as Pi's partial-json parser recovers them. When a value inside an object or array is unreadable, the containers around it keep their members and read on, so text after the bad value is kept; a truncated number is cut at a lowercase `e` only (`1E` is not an exponent); `Infinity`, `-Infinity`, `NaN` and out-of-range numbers such as `1e400` are read as values and become `null` (Pi serializes them as `null`, and Go cannot serialize them); a `__proto__` member is dropped from the recovered object, as in Pi.
- Fixed the queued steering and follow-up lines, the dequeue hint and the startup hint separator to use Pi's colours: the pending lines use the `dim` theme colour (not `muted`) and both close only the foreground colour (`ESC[39m`) instead of resetting every attribute.
- A `turn_end` or `agent_before_settle` handler in an extension process receives each `context.contextEntries[].sourceEntry` with its members in the order the session wrote them, as in Pi, instead of sorted by name.
- RPC mode now writes the `PIG_PROFILE` files when it exits; it ended the process before they were written.
- A large file written through a tool call no longer slows down as it streams: each streamed piece of the tool-call arguments is parsed on its own instead of re-parsing all the arguments so far after a provider opened the call with empty arguments, so the cost of the whole call grows with its size instead of with the square of its size.
- A `session_compact` handler's passive custom message reaches the overflow retry (D138, Pi #9051).
- A run that `sendMessage({ triggerTurn: true })` starts while idle emits `before_agent_start`, checks compaction first, applies the handlers' result and delivers pending `nextTurn` messages. The event carries `messageType` (`"user"` or `"custom"`) and, for a custom run, `message`. A user prompt's run, like a custom one, is busy from its start, as in Pi 1.2.0: a message sent while its compaction check and `before_agent_start` handlers run joins it, a prompt or `sendUserMessage` sent meanwhile must name `streamingBehavior`/`deliverAs`, bash results recorded meanwhile are appended after the handlers, and an abort aborts the run at `agent_start` instead of letting its request complete (Pi #5581, Pi PR #10739).
- Compaction summaries, branch summaries and bug reports omit thinking blocks (Pi 15e7bdc1, Pi #9602): replayed thinking is often not in the live context, so including it could make the summarization request larger than the context it summarizes and compaction fail on every attempt.
- RPC `set_model` applies after its auth check, as Pi's awaited `setModel` does, so a `cycle_model` in the same input batch cycles from the previous model and answers first. A session resumed without credentials no longer restores its saved model: it starts on the unknown placeholder, as Pi does. <!-- fix: 5bc4773278, 2df65dffcc -->
- A `waitForIdle()` waiter registered during a run stays pending through `agent_settled` handlers and the prompts they defer, and an abort while settlement is in progress discards those prompts (D142, Pi #10704, #10705). An `agent_settled` handler that awaits a compaction no longer deadlocks on the Session lock.
- A device-attributes reply delivered in slow reads no longer reaches the editor as input: an `ESC[?` prefix waits for its final byte (D143, Pi #10657).
- A response stream dropped mid-run now reports `terminated (cause: ...)` instead of a bare `terminated` (D144, Pi #10697).
- Codemode output and returned values keep a nested Error's name and message, with its own enumerable fields, instead of `{}` (D145, Pi #10650).
- RPC `get_state` sent right after `new_session`, `fork`, `clone`, `switch_session` or `export_html` now answers first from the old Session, as in Pi; PiG still answers the replacement when stdin ends, which Pi does not (D146).
- Two `compact` commands of one RPC input batch no longer cancel each other; they run one after another (D147).
- A `new_session`, `fork`, `clone` or `switch_session` in the same RPC batch as a `compact` aborts the compaction instead of waiting for it (D148).
- Clearing the queued messages (RPC `clear_queue`, the SDK, the interactive restore-to-editor key) no longer destroys custom messages that an extension queued with `sendMessage({ deliverAs })`. They stay queued and run with the active run (D135, Pi #9886).
- Omitting a failed retry attempt no longer makes the context usage jump to an estimate of the whole history. A context edit that only touches entries after the last usage keeps that usage (D136, Pi #10287).
- A request carries at most the model's `inputLimits.images.maxPerRequest` images. Older images become a text placeholder in the request and stay in the session (D137, Pi #10162).
- The pi-messages and classifier (System One, decisions) requests are sent to the URL as written and a URL with credentials is refused as fetch refuses it, where Pig sent the credentials as Basic auth. <!-- fix: 3804a5a2ab -->
- `pig piglet build --format binary` from a Pig checkout with a `go.work` builds a Piglet whose fused Go extension imports an external module, such as an `a2a-go` v2 package. Before, the build stopped with `go: updates to go.sum needed, but go.sum is part of the overlay specified with -overlay`, because workspace mode needs checksums that `go mod tidy` does not write to the extension's `go.sum`. The build now adds them to its own overlay and still never writes the checkout.
- A Piglet Binary that strips `bedrock-converse-stream` links the AWS SDK again: the Bedrock retry backoff (`ai/bedrock_retry_backoff.go`) had no `pig_strip_bedrock_converse_stream` build tag and imported `aws-sdk-go-v2` (27 packages). It carries the tag now, like the rest of the Bedrock provider.
- Codemode rejects a `// @options:` line that is not the first line of the script with an error before the script runs. The line used to be ignored, so its `timeout_ms` never applied and the script ran without a deadline (Pi #10631).
- A trap of the codemode module on a Worker no longer loses the output the script printed before it: the host holds the output items, and the crash message is delivered behind them, as Pi delivers its buffered output with a crash. A script that printed 100,000 one-character items used to trap the module on its heap and now completes (a batch of the same items took 1.06 s, against 1.56 s with the module holding them; the exchange count and the other timings are unchanged).
- The durable code mode joins the text items of a script's output in linear time (Pi f11e50f7, [#10678](https://github.com/earendil-works/pi/issues/10678)): 100,000 items took about 3 s because every item read the end of the growing joined text. The coding-agent code mode already joined each run once.
- `pig mcp login --help` described `--timeout` as only the wait for the browser; it limits the whole sign-in, as Pi 148622bc now says.
- MCP tool calls no longer fail with "Invalid MCP tools/call structured content" when the server returns structured content that is not an object (an array, `null`, or a primitive, as MCP 2026-07-28 allows), as Pi 67996ab7 now does.
- MCP servers with resources registered no tools until every resource was listed, so a slow listing (a large drive) kept their tools out of the first prompt. PiG no longer lists resources when connecting; the resource tools still list and read them on demand.
- Reduced context-limit request failures by estimating input at 3.5 characters per token instead of 4 when calculating output limits.
- `/mcp` now opens at once while servers are still connecting, and connection actions (reconnect, enable, disable) run in the background so the manager stays usable. Reconnects and replacements are serialized, direct MCP tool calls wait for their own server's connection, and a call prepared earlier uses the server's current connection when it runs.
- MCP OAuth sign-ins can now be cancelled with Esc at every step, session shutdown stops a running sign-in, and each request to the authorization server times out after 15 seconds. An aborted token refresh no longer falls back to a new authorization.
- Shutdown no longer waits up to 15 seconds to refresh an MCP OAuth token that was about to expire, only to close the server's session. Closing a streamable HTTP session reuses the token of the last request.
- Fixed MCP stdio servers staying alive after PiG exited because the terminal closed, it crashed, or it received a termination signal. Pi ends their process groups from an exit hook; PiG now does the same on those exits.
- Fixed interactive sessions leaving extension prompt sections, such as the MCP `mcp_servers` list, out of the transcript's system message. Print mode and Pi record them; interactive mode now publishes its prompt run to the session, which appends the sections before the first request.
- MCP tool results whose content blocks carry members of another type than the protocol's (a numeric `text`, a `size` string, a missing `uri`, a `null` block, a `resource` that is not an object) now convert as Pi converts them: the model sees what Pi's would, and a block Pi cannot read ends the tool call with Pi's error instead of a placeholder.
- Fixed the error message of a failed lazy API setup, such as a module load or auth failure, using its failure time as `timestamp` instead of the request start.
- Fixed bash and PowerShell results losing `Took` after reloading a session, and the live `Took` including wall-clock steps; both now show the recorded execution time.
- Fixed the message-format page: it said PiG does not carry the `nestedCalls` record on tool result messages, which it has written since 1.0.4, and it now lists the field; the page also stops citing a `replace` field that Pi's page no longer lists.
- Fixed Markdown links not being clickable in Herdr: `TERM_PROGRAM=herdr` is now detected as supporting OSC 8 hyperlinks, with images off.
- Fixed the fullscreen text selection and multi-click state surviving a session switch or other transcript rebuild, which highlighted unrelated text in the new transcript.
- Fixed `!` and RPC `bash` output keeping fragments of color codes, such as a stray `m`, when a code was split across output chunks.
- Ctrl+V pastes clipboard text in Termux. PiG read `termux-clipboard-get` only where the platform is `linux`, and Termux reports `android`. A failed copy in Termux now also shows the Termux:API install hint on Android.
- `!` command and RPC `bash` output no longer keeps fragments of color codes, such as a stray `m`, when an escape sequence is split across output chunks. PiG holds a trailing unfinished sequence of up to 256 characters until the next chunk or the end of the command.
- `chord/host.Forbidden("")` keeps the empty message, as Pi's `forbidden("")` does; `Forbidden()` still says "forbidden". `IsChordError` takes the code as an optional argument and, like `ToWireError`, no longer looks through wrappers, so an aggregate of ChordErrors goes on the wire as `unavailable`, as in Pi. `ChordError.Name` is "ChordError".
- An MCP request over Streamable HTTP that is cancelled or times out while the server's authorization is being refreshed is no longer sent again after the refresh; other requests waiting on the same refresh still complete.
- `PIG_PROFILE=heap` and the `heap_live_bytes` of `PIG_PROFILE=metrics` count only memory PiG retains. They now run two garbage collections before measuring. After the single collection used before, buffers that pools keep for reuse still counted as live, such as the JSON encoder buffer that holds the last provider request. At a 1M-token session that buffer is about 4.5 MB.
- Anthropic: a server-requested retry delay above the cap fails with `Server requested Ns retry delay (max: Ms). <status> <body>` like the SDK's error, not the HTTP client's `Post "<url>": … 500 Internal Server Error.` text.
- OpenAI Codex: an error body whose `error` member is absent or falsy (an object without it, an array, `null`) keeps the raw body as the message instead of the usage-limit text.
- The pi-messages, Cloudflare Workers AI and TypeSafe System One, and OpenAI decisions request URLs are built with the WHATWG URL parser, as Pi's `new URL` does: hosts are normalized, a base URL with a query or fragment resolves against its path, and `debug=1` is added to an existing query in order and in form encoding instead of with sorted keys.
- Provider streams read integers written as `12.0`, `1.2e1` or `12E0` (a token count, an index) as JavaScript's JSON.parse does; Go's decoder refused them for an integer field and ended the stream with an error. Anthropic, OpenAI completions and Responses (Azure, Codex), Google, Mistral and pi-messages are covered.
- A Radius gateway config cached on an OAuth credential is sanitized as Pi does. A model whose `contextWindow` or `maxTokens` is `null` is dropped (it was kept). A model with a fractional or out-of-range window, a non-object cost tier, `headers` that are not a string map or another member Pig cannot represent is kept (it was dropped): a window keeps its integer part, clamped to the integer range, and any other such member is left at its zero value. `baseUrl`, `models` and each model member are read by their exact key, so a member such as `ID` or `contextwindow` no longer replaces `id` or `contextWindow`.
- Radius gateway and OAuth authorization URLs are parsed and serialized with the WHATWG URL parser, as Node's `new URL` does. Hosts are lowercased and converted from IDN, IPv4 and IPv6 forms, userinfo is percent-encoded, a URL without a scheme or with a bad port is rejected, and an empty `search` keeps a `?`. Before, Go's `net/url` accepted or reshaped many of these.
- Radius OAuth login now reads the gateway's responses as Pi does: an error body that is a JSON string, number or array reports just the HTTP status (not the body text), a `null` body reports the JavaScript TypeError text, a JSON `null` `expires_in` counts as zero, device-authorization members of an unexpected JSON type report the missing-fields error, and ill-formed UTF-8 or a truncated body is read as `fetch` does.
- Radius OAuth login again signs in when the gateway's token response has no refresh token, as Pi does. A success response that is not JSON, or whose body is cut off, reports the same message Pi shows (JavaScript's SyntaxError text, or `terminated`), and so does a discovery error whose body is cut off. Every OAuth login reads a body that starts with two byte order marks as Node's fetch does: both are dropped.
- The Radius relay WebSocket URL is built with the WHATWG URL parser, as Pi's `new URL` does: gateway hosts are normalized, userinfo and ports follow Pi, an unparseable gateway reports "Invalid URL", and an unsupported scheme is reported with its trailing colon.
- PiG no longer names documentation that does not exist. When the config root (`PIG_HOME`) cannot be written and holds no documentation bundle of the running build, the system prompt has no docs section, and login help and the codemode description name no docs page. Before, they named `<config root>/docs` files that were never written. `pig docs show` still prints the embedded pages.
- A read-only config root no longer prints warnings at every start. The extension SDK staging warning stays silent there, because each build reads only its own staged SDK directories and nothing another build staged can stand in for them, and a signed Piglet Binary no longer warns that it cannot write its verification cache; its next start checks it in full again. Other write errors still warn. The agent directory must stay writable, as in Pi.
- A Go MCP client that declares an empty capability, such as `sampling` or `elicitation`, now sends it as `{}`. The capability used to be left out of `initialize`, so a server could not see that the client supports it. <!-- fix: 397866f7f2 -->
- A `bash` shell that starts at the moment `pig` handles an emergency exit, a crash or a signal is now killed with the others. A shell that had started but was not yet tracked could be missed and outlive `pig`. <!-- fix: 1971aaebd8 -->
- A Piglet or Package installed under a symlinked agent directory keeps the origin integrity recorded in its npm lockfile. Lockfile entries that npm writes relative to a symlinked install root were not found. <!-- fix: e32c60c500 -->
- Cycling models in the interactive UI goes through the Session as in Pi: the new model persists as the default, extends a non-empty `enabledModels` scope, keeps scope keys for model IDs that start with their provider, and checks Anthropic subscription auth after the switch. <!-- fix: af1fe1a8fe -->
- The Go program in the SDK page now compiles. It called `coding.NewServices` and `coding.ServicesOptions`, which do not exist, and the page named `coding.Services` for `coding.AgentSessionServices`. The Go quickstart extension in the extension API page now compiles as well: its tool schema is an `sdk.Schema` map and its `session_start` handler returns `(any, error)`.
- The bundled command page (`pig docs show commands`) and the website's CLI reference now agree with `pig --help`. The bundled page lacked `pig mcp`, `pig piglet prune`, `pig extensions cache prune --failures` and the `+name` and `-name` forms of `--tools`; the website page omitted `pig piglet pull`, `update` and `publish` from its Piglet verbs.
- `pig docs show divergences` lists D110 and D111, and the README names the pinned Pi release (1.1.0) and its commit. The README gave the pin of two releases ago.
- The Termux page's link to the Quickstart reaches its "Authenticate a provider" section.
- The extension SDK surface matrix and the extension API pages say that `setEditorComponent` and `getEditorComponent` exist in the Go, Rust and Python SDKs and that the getter returns the factory the extension installed. They described the Go getter as always returning nil and the Rust and Python getters as missing. <!-- fix: d6e0dca953 -->
- After `await ctx.reload()`, a command's captured `pi` and `ctx` now throw Pi's stale message for every member, in every SDK (Node, Go, Python and Rust). Before, members an SDK answers locally, such as `ctx.cwd`, `getFlag`, `getCommands`, `getThinkingLevel`, `getSystemPrompt` and `isProjectTrusted`, kept answering for the replaced extension instance.
- A subprocess extension's message, entry or tool renderer, or tool renderer resolver, that was busy past the five-second renderer inactivity boundary no longer leaves its card on the fallback until the next resize: the host asks again once the extension answers the abandoned request, and an extension that keeps missing the boundary is asked again after a backoff that starts at five seconds and doubles to one minute.
- Provider request bodies write a lone UTF-16 surrogate as its escape, as `JSON.stringify` does, instead of U+FFFD for each byte of its Go form. It matters for the strings Pi does not sanitize: the Codex `instructions`, now sent as Pi sends them, and the reasoning fields of an assistant message. A body with no U+FFFD character encodes as before.
- The request-string walk that splices cached strings into a request body finds a cycle by the pointer, map or slice it is inside instead of stopping at a depth of 1,000: a payload that holds itself still fails as the marshaler fails, and a payload nested deeper than 1,000 levels has its large strings spliced like any other (the body is the same bytes).
- A provider request whose URL path Go cannot parse no longer sends the URL fragment to the server; fetch never sends it. OpenAI Codex again reports a base URL with credentials with fetch's own message.
- Resource resolution follows Pi's `DefaultPackageManager.resolve` in two cases: built-in extensions come after file and package extensions, and a Package listed twice in the same project settings uses its last entry (and its filters), keeping the first position.
- A model request on a reused connection gets the full HTTP idle timeout to receive its first response bytes, as in Pi. Before, the time the connection had spent idle in the pool was deducted from the next request's timeout.
- A panicking notify while MCP reports startup problems is shown as "MCP failed to load" instead of ending the process, as Pi's startup chain catches it.
- The Python SDK reports a component whose `set_invalidate(None)` raises during cleanup in the extension log, as the Go SDK does.
- The Anthropic stream reader takes an event's type only from a member named exactly `type`, as Pi does; a member such as `TYPE` no longer marks the start or end of a message.
- A provider HTTP error body that starts with two byte order marks reads as `response.text()` reads it: both marks are dropped, so a JSON error message is found. Pig dropped one.
- A pi-messages stream event that is a number, string, boolean or array is skipped, as Pi skips an event of unknown type, instead of ending the reply with a JSON decode error.
- An OpenAI Responses, Azure OpenAI Responses or Google stream event whose JSON is a number, string, boolean or array is skipped as Pi skips it, instead of ending the reply with a decode error; a null Responses event fails with Pi's TypeError message.
- A `models.json` whose `contextWindow`, `maxTokens` or a model override writes an integer as `128000.0` or `1.28E5` loads as Pi's JSON.parse reads it; Pig refused the whole file with "Failed to parse models.json".
- Classifier (typesafe-system-one, llama.cpp) and Radius gateway config responses are decoded as `response.text()` and `response.json()` decode them: two leading byte order marks are dropped and ill-formed UTF-8 becomes U+FFFD. A classifier answer behind two marks failed with a JSON syntax error, and a Radius config behind a mark was reported invalid.
- Amazon Bedrock streams read integers written as `12.0` or `1e0` (a content block index, a token count) as the AWS SDK does after JSON.parse; Pig ended the stream with a decode error.
- An OpenAI Codex HTTP error with an empty body reports the reason phrase the server sent, or "Request failed" when it sent none, as Pi does; Pig used Go's status text, which is empty for a code such as 529. A Codex error body behind three or four byte order marks is now read as Pi reads it, through `response.text()` twice.
- In print and JSON mode, SIGTERM or SIGHUP no longer hangs when a Go, Python or Rust extension's `session_shutdown` handler makes a host call that waits for the agent, such as `wait_for_idle`. Such a call lets the interrupted run continue, as a Node handler's `await` does.
- A Cloudflare model built with `coding.BuildModel` and streamed through its own `Provider` resolves `{CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID}` and `{CLOUDFLARE_GATEWAY_ID}` from the stored credential's env again, under the request env, as Pi's `Models.applyAuth` merges the resolution env before `cloudflareStreams` runs. Since `ai.WrapBuiltinProvider` the wrapper read only the request env, so such a request went to the literal placeholder URL.
- "Your first extension" now says that `pig install --validate-only` exits 1 when the extension does not build. Before, it said 0.
- The terminal UI worked example now describes the typed `/model` prefix in words. A docs check no longer reports a nonexistent command from that page.
- The settings documentation now says that `images.blockImages` replaces each image sent to the model with `Image reading is disabled.`, as PiG does. Before, it said PiG still sent images. The embedded onboarding page no longer says that skipping the first-time setup with `Esc` makes it appear again.
- The Go SDK page now says that leaving `AgentDir` empty in `CreateAgentSessionServicesOptions` makes a program read `PIG_CODING_AGENT_DIR` the way the `pig` command does.
- The "Your first ten minutes" and Quickstart pages now describe the first-time setup screen (theme, sprite and usage data) that PiG shows on a fresh install (D88). Before, they said PiG had no setup screen. The first-run page also explains that `Enter` in the model list picks a model for one conversation and `Ctrl+S` makes it the default, what the footer fields mean, and what a `401` error after `/login` means.
- The "Applied use cases" page in the manual that ships inside PiG now links the Security page on the website. Before, it named a `security.md` page that the shipped manual does not have.
- The Termux page shows the `[Context]` line as PiG prints it, `~/.pig/agent/AGENTS.md`.
- A JavaScript or TypeScript extension loads when its `registerCommand` options carry a member Pi does not define, such as `args`. Pi ignores such members; PiG forwarded `args` to the host, which rejected a value that was not a string, and the extension failed to load.
- The MCP client uses JavaScript's `trim()` and `\s` where Pi does: in stdio and Streamable HTTP blank-line and blank-event checks, HTTP error bodies, the `insufficient_scope` challenge check and the `offline_access` consent check. The env SSH helpers and the durable compaction summary also trim as Pi does. A byte-order mark counts as whitespace and U+0085 does not.
- MCP response headers are read as Node's `fetch` returns them, one Latin-1 character per byte. This applies to `Content-Type`, `WWW-Authenticate` and `McpAuthRequiredError.WWWAuthenticate`.
- A JSON-RPC number id is a JavaScript number. The unknown-response message renders the id as `String(id)` does, and the id is written as `JSON.stringify` writes it. A request with id `-0` is cancelled by `requestId: 0`.
- The external editor (`ctrl+g`) now edits `prompt.md` inside a private `pi-editor-*` directory under the system temp directory and removes the directory afterwards, as Pi does. It previously wrote a `pig-editor-*.md` file directly in the shared temp directory.
- The built-in bash, read and list tool cards now show an image block that has no MIME type as `[Image: [image/unknown]]`, as Pi's `getTextOutput` does. They previously showed `[Image: []]`.
- A text file passed as an `@file` argument that holds invalid UTF-8 now reaches the prompt as Pi sends it: each ill-formed byte sequence becomes one U+FFFD replacement character. Pig previously copied the raw bytes, which later became one replacement character per byte.
- Escape over an open autocomplete list during compaction now closes the list and lets compaction continue, as in Pi. Previously it aborted the compaction and left the list open.
- A double-quoted YAML string in a skill, prompt template or `parseFrontmatter` header now reads a surrogate-pair escape such as `"\uD83D\uDE00"` as the one character it encodes, as Pi does. Before, each half became U+FFFD.
- A key hint for an app action the user unbound shows no key, as in Pi, instead of the action's default key. For example, the extension editor dialog showed `ctrl+g external editor` after `app.editor.external` was bound to `[]`; it now shows ` external editor` (`tui/keybinding_hints.go` AppKeyText, keybinding-hints.ts keyText; probe in `TestExtensionEditorHandleInputMatchesPi`, red before).
- An extension custom message whose content is a block array (text or images), or an empty string, now reaches the model as a user message, as in Pi; it was dropped before. Branch summaries, compaction summaries, bash executions and custom messages are now sent as text-block arrays, so Anthropic Messages and OpenAI Completions requests carry the same content shape as Pi. Any of these messages ends pending tool calls like a user turn.
- A frontend member's main region now matches the transcript after an extension's custom entry is placed before the streaming message and a later child of the transcript changes in the same frame. `Container.InsertBefore` did not report the children it kept, so the frame spliced a stale range: the session kept the removed entry and missed the inserted one. An insert alone also walked the whole transcript instead of the inserted entry.
- RPC mode starts the `--tools` list active in its own order when it names extension tools, as Pi does. `--tools grep,my_tool,read` declares `grep`, `my_tool`, `read` to the model and lists them in that order in the system prompt; RPC mode listed the built-in tools first and appended the extension tools.
- The MCP extension's `tool_call` handler passes a tool's `reachesTools` the call's input in the member order the model wrote it, as Pi passes `event.input`, instead of sorted by member name.
- On arm64 and other CPUs with a fused multiply-add, the OKHSL saturation of a color (used when generating the system theme's colors) rounds `0.8 * c0` before adding it, as Pi does. Before, it differed from Pi's in the last bit for about 9% of sRGB colors.
- On Windows, saving credentials to `auth.json`, `models.json` or the MCP OAuth store no longer fails at once with `EPERM: operation not permitted, mkdir '….lock'` when another process has just released the lock and Windows still holds the directory pending deletion. As in Pi 1.2.0, the lock is retried: the synchronous path within its ten attempts, the cancellable path up to five times. Settings and trust locks still fail on the first `EPERM`, as in Pi.
- Extensions receive each model (`ctx.model`, `model_select`, `ctx.modelRegistry` reads, scoped models, virtual-model routes and provider-object calls) as Pi's model object, with its members in Pi's order, instead of sorted by member name with `null` for members the model does not set.
- A models.json cost that sets `tiers: []` (a custom model's cost, or a `modelOverrides` cost) keeps `cost.tiers: []` in an extension's `ctx.model` and model lists and in RPC `get_state` and `get_available_models`, as Pi keeps the authored cost, instead of dropping the member. A cost without `tiers` still has none.
- A Gemini or Mistral stream chunk with a member of an unexpected type no longer sorts the members of a tool call's object arguments: the call keeps the order the model wrote them in, as Pi's `JSON.parse` read does.
- An extension's `ctx.model` and model lists keep a model's `cost.tiers` when the model's record has it as an empty array (a provider object's or a models store's model), as Pi keeps the authored cost, instead of dropping the member.
- Changelog link rewriting and `git` source parsing match a URL scheme case-insensitively as Pi's regex with the `i` flag does: `ſ` (U+017F) and `K` (U+212A) no longer count as `s` and `k`.
- An agent assistant message that holds an empty `diagnostics` array writes it as `[]`, as Pi does, instead of leaving the member out.
- Chord host timers (reload vote, gate and retire deadlines, link pings, service linger, host manager grace, ready timeout and keepAlive interval) wait as Node's `setTimeout` does: a delay above 2147483647 ms, below 1 ms or zero runs after 1 ms, and fractional milliseconds are truncated. A deadline above the limit used to never fire and a zero one fired at once.
- Models an extension's provider object returns keep the member order the extension wrote them in, as Pi keeps the returned objects, instead of being sorted by member name. A model without a `provider` gets the provider's id after its own members.
- A classifier response that reports more than 2,147,483,647 input or output tokens keeps the reported count and its cost, as Pi does, instead of capping it.
- A tool namespace registered with `aliases: []` keeps the empty array in `pi.getAllTools()` and the replicated tool list, as Pi returns the authored namespace object. `extension.ToolNamespace.Aliases` omits only a nil slice.
- `/export` to a directory that cannot be created on a read-only file system (such as macOS's root) reports `ENOENT: no such file or directory, mkdir ...` as Pi does, not `EROFS`.
- Started from a directory reached through a symlink, `pig install`/`remove`/`list`/`update` resolve local sources against the physical working directory, and `--session` with an empty file records the physical directory as the session cwd, as Pi does.
- On Linux, clipboard reads accept every `DISPLAY` spelling Pi's `xcb_connect` accepts: white space or a sign before the display or screen number (`: 1`, `:+1`), a negative screen, which selects the first screen, and display numbers that wrap as libxcb's `strtoul` and 16-bit port do (`host:65536` reaches port 6000). Previously these spellings failed, and image and text paste reported the clipboard as unavailable.
- On arm64, `tool_search` and `searchTools()` scores use the same natural logarithm as Pi running on Node for arm64, whose V8 math library fuses each multiply-add in one step. Before, the logarithm in the BM25 weights differed from Node's in the last bit for about 1 in 500 arguments, which could change scores and break ties in a different order.
- A native extension provider's `filterAllModels` receives the models its `getAllModels` (or `getModels`) returned, with their members in the order the provider wrote them, instead of copies with members sorted by name.
- MCP tools declare the `CallToolResult` scripts receive as Pi 1.2.0 does: content blocks with their `type`, `text`, `data` and `mimeType` fields, `structuredContent` always (typed `unknown` when the server declares no output schema), no `_meta`, and room for fields newer protocol versions add. Local `$ref`s in a server's output schema are rewritten to point below `structuredContent`, so they resolve to the same definitions. Before, `describeTool()` and the `Codemode:` line of a directly exposed MCP tool listed `_meta?`, left out `structuredContent` without an output schema, and lost the types behind such references (Pi 1.2.0 `mcp/tools.ts` createMcpResultSchema, #10416).
- The MCP extension decides which tools reach MCP tools by their `ReachesTools`, as Pi 1.2.0 does: another extension's `codemode` or `tool_search` that declares `reachesTools` is activated for MCP servers and waits for the servers its call may reach. Before, only the built-in tools of those names were recognized, by their source path (Pi 1.2.0 `mcp/index.ts`).
- Code mode: a deterministic run's clocks round like `Math.round` (0.49999999999999994 ms read 1 ms), and a tool declaration's `@default`/`const`/`enum` values keep the schema's authored member order instead of sorted members.
- Print mode (`-p`) now prints the partial text, writes a warning to stderr and exits 1 when the final response was cut off at the model's output token limit, instead of exiting 0 without a warning.
- Delayed terminal replies (OSC color, XTVersion, Kitty graphics) that arrive in pieces are no longer typed into the editor or read as Ctrl+G; batched Alt+]/P/_ keystrokes still time out as keys (Pi 1.2.0 stdin-buffer).
- In the alternate screen, wheel input that a focused overlay does not handle scrolls the transcript (Pi 1.2.0 #10769).
- On arm64 and other CPUs with a fused multiply-add, Kimi, Radius and xAI OAuth credentials store the `expires` time Pi computes for a fractional `expires_in`, the device-code login deadline matches Pi's, and the ChatGPT usage-limit message names the same "Try again in ~N min." as Pi: each `x * 1000` product is rounded before it is added, as JavaScript does. Before, the expiry could differ in the last digit and the minutes by one at a half-minute boundary.
- `pi.getSettings()` in a subprocess extension lists the settings in Pi's order: the global settings file's members as written (after the legacy-key migration), then members only the project file sets, with nested objects merged the same way and setters and overrides adding members after the others. PiG sorted them alphabetically.
- A bash or PowerShell tool card reads the result's `truncation` details by their exact keys, as Pi does: a member such as `TRUNCATEDBY` no longer stands in for `truncatedBy`.
- Path autocomplete after resuming a session created in another directory now lists files from that session's directory instead of the launch directory.
- Started from a directory reached through a symlink, `/export <file>.jsonl` and `/bug` now write and name their files under the physical working directory, as Pi does.
- Started from a directory reached through a symlink (a `$PWD` naming a link), pig now uses the physical working directory as Pi does, so `@file` arguments are named by their physical path.
- `/logout` reads stored credentials with Pi's 15-second signal, so an unreadable `auth.json` reports "Could not read stored credentials" instead of "No stored credentials to remove".
- On arm64 and other CPUs with a fused multiply-add, the cache-warming expected savings (shown by `/session`, passed to extensions and compared with the $0.05 threshold), the "Sign in with Radius" shimmer and the 3D easter egg's blocks flying home match Pi: each product is rounded before it is added, as JavaScript does. Before, they could differ from Pi in the last digit, which changed some shimmer colors.
- In RPC mode, runs that an extension command's handler sends with `sendMessage` or `sendUserMessage` start in the order it sent them, as in Pi, when the handler suspends or returns; before, two such runs could start in either order.
- A durable nested call keeps its arguments in the member order the caller wrote them, as Pi does: the stored call and execute intent, the `pi.live` nested slot, tool events, validation messages, and code mode's "took a different path" message (`tools.name({...})`) no longer sort them. Code mode's `models.*` calls no longer store an extra `order` argument.
- Switching to or importing an empty session file from a directory reached through a symlink starts the session in the physical working directory, as Pi does.
- `ai.ValidateToolArguments` and `ai.ValidateToolCall` list a failed validation's errors in the order the tool's schema declares its properties, as Pi does, instead of sorted by property name.
- `tool_search` and the Durable `searchTools()` ranker compute the same BM25 scores as Pi on arm64: the length normalization `k1 * (1 - b + b * length / averageLength)` is rounded before the term count is added, instead of being fused into one multiply-add, so scores no longer differ from Pi's in the last bit and documents that tie in Pi keep their order (Pi 1.2.0 `tool-search/tool.ts` Bm25Ranker, `durable/src/search.ts` rankDocuments).
- `pi.exec` gives the command PiG's environment unchanged, as Pi does, so a command started in a symlinked directory no longer gets that directory as its `PWD`; `pwd` there now prints the physical directory, as in Pi.
- Settings and `models.json` compat objects keep the members PiG does not model, as Pi 1.2.0 does. `getGlobalSettings()`, `getSettings()` and the settings JSON now include keys such as `$schema` or an extension's own setting, project values merge over global ones member by member, and only the legacy keys a migration rewrote (`queueMode`, `websockets`) are dropped. A provider or model `compat` object keeps keys such as a custom API's `customOption`, and the composed model carries them, so a custom API reads its own compat settings. Before, both were dropped on load (Pi 1.2.0 `settings-manager.ts` getGlobalSettings and deepMergeSettings, `provider-composer.ts` mergeCompat, `config-schemas.test.ts`).
- `pi.exec`'s `timeout` starts once the command has been spawned, as in Pi, so time spent finding and starting the program no longer counts against it.
- Go, Python and Rust extensions now draw an overlay component (`ctx.custom` with `overlay: true`) at the overlay's width, 80 columns by default, as Pi and Node extensions do. They drew it at the full terminal width, so a full-width line was cut off at the overlay's edge.
- `pig -e <directory>` now loads a Node extension whose entry file is `index.mjs` and that has no `package.json`, `index.ts` or `index.js`, as Pi does.
- A Node extension that calls `pi.appendEntry` now sees the new entry in `ctx.sessionManager.getEntries()` in the same handler, as in Pi. RPC mode and interactive mode still report the append (`entry_appended`).
- Pressing a key in a subprocess extension's overlay no longer freezes interactive mode when the key reaches the overlay on the main loop (public PR #197).
- When an extension in a shared Go, Rust or Python process crashes, the error for each stopped extension now says that `/reload` restarts it. Before, the message could only say "packed member connection closed".
- `pig install <dir>` of a local directory with no package manifest and no `extensions/`, `skills/`, `prompts/` or `themes/` directory now loads the directory itself as an extension, as `pi install` does. Previously Pig recorded the Package and loaded nothing from it.
- When a Go, Rust or Python extension that shares a process registers under a different name than the one it was selected by, the error now names both identities, the source, and how to fix the selection, as it already did for an extension in its own process. Before, it said only `packed extension registered as "<name>"`.
- `pig install <dir>` now installs a local Go, Rust or Python extension folder and loads it as an extension, as it already did for a Node folder. Before, it refused the folder with "is an extension, not a package". An npm or Git source whose root is a bare extension is still refused (D57).
- Pressing Escape while an extension tool runs now lets the tool finish: a tool that stops on its abort signal reports its own result or error, as in Pi. A tool that ignores the signal fails with "Operation aborted" after 3 seconds, and the session becomes usable again; Pi waits for such a tool forever (D111). Before, every extension tool call failed at once with "context canceled".
- A Node extension that calls `pi.appendEntry` many times in one handler no longer slows down with the size of the session: each append now costs the same, as in Pi.
- A Go, Python or Rust extension that calls `appendEntry` in a handler that already read the session log now sees the new entry in its next `getEntries()`, as in Pi. Before, it saw the entry only on its first read of the log or after the next host request.
- The RPC test harness no longer races on its recorded output error (found by a `go test -race ./coding/...` run); test-only.
- Tool-argument validation compares JSON values as JSON Schema does: a number is never equal to a string. `const`, `enum` and `uniqueItems` no longer treat `1`, `1.5`, `16` or `0.5` as equal to `"1"`, `"1.5"`, `"0x10"` or `"1/2"` (the schema library parsed the string as a number), matching Pi's validator.
- Go, Python and Rust extensions: in a `before_agent_start` handler, the event's `systemPrompt` and the context's system prompt getter now return the prompt the shared options render when read, as Pi and Node do. A handler sees its own edit to `systemPromptOptions` at once, a later handler sees it, and after a handler returns `systemPrompt` later handlers read exactly that prompt; before, they read the prompt rendered before the handler ran, and the context getter returned the Session's prompt.
- A session entry timestamp that is not RFC 3339 but that JavaScript Date reads (a space for the T, an RFC 2822 date) is read for compaction staleness, summary message times and cache-warm entries, and an unreadable compaction timestamp no longer marks every assistant message as older than the compaction.
- A Session listener that queues more messages than the Session's event buffer holds (64 by default), while it runs on the goroutine that emitted its event, publishes its `queue_update` events in order. An update could reach `Events` ahead of the one published just before it, so a subscriber saw a queue with one message too many. Every event published after such a burst now runs after it.
- A goroutine that waits to run Session events, such as the Session's event goroutine while a producer runs a listener itself, now blocks on a channel receive instead of a `sync.Mutex`, so `testing/synctest` counts it as durably blocked. A synctest test that waited while a listener was held no longer hangs until the package timeout.
- A session file with invalid UTF-8 inside a record loads with one U+FFFD per maximal invalid subpart, as Node's UTF-8 decoder reads it, instead of one per byte.
- A Session delivers each event to its listeners the way Pi's `AgentSession` does. A listener that unsubscribes itself or another listener during delivery changes which listeners that delivery still reaches, and a listener subscribed during delivery receives the same event. Before, every delivery used the list as it was when the delivery began.
- PiG opens a session file that holds a valid JSON line that is not an entry (an array, `true`, a string) or an entry whose `id` or `parentId` is not a string, as Pi does, instead of failing with `json: cannot unmarshal … SessionEntryBase`. The leaf, the branch, the context and the `parentId` of the next entry follow Pi: after a line with no `id` the next entry has no `parentId` member, after an entry whose `id` is `5` it has `"parentId":5`, and a falsy `parentId` ends a branch.
- A session message whose integer member, such as `timestamp`, a usage count or `durationMs`, is spelled with a fraction or an exponent (Python's `json` writes `1767225603000.0`), holds a fraction, or is beyond the 64-bit range is now kept, as Pi keeps it. Before, PiG dropped the message from the session context and RPC `get_messages`. The message is written as `JSON.stringify` writes the parsed number.
- A `read` result shown in the transcript no longer keeps the file text a second time as its structured content. Renderers, including extension renderers, still receive the structured content the tool returned. In a 75-prompt session of file reads (about 4.2M tokens of context), memory at the end drops by another 20 MB or so.
- A resumed session no longer holds its whole file in memory. PiG keeps a small index of every entry and decodes only what the active context needs (the latest compaction's kept messages and everything after them); older entries are read back from the session file when a command such as `/tree`, `/fork` or export needs them. On a real 314 MB session, an interactive resume measured 150-190 MB after loading and 225-285 MB after opening `/tree` (two profiling runs on macOS), where PiG 0.4.1 took 1.2-1.3 GB and Pi 1.1.0 about 700 MB. History outside the active context is not kept decoded, so a long-open session file does not accumulate it in memory.
- A prompt in a long session no longer costs time in proportion to the session's history: the current branch and its context are cached instead of rebuilt from the whole path several times per prompt (630 ms to 50 ms per prompt at 50,000 entries).
- Extension session reads (`getEntry`, `getLabel`, `getChildren`, `getBranch`, `buildSessionContext` and the others) decode only the entries they return instead of the whole session on every call.
- `/tree` opens and searches a 300 MB session faster than Pi: it reads the session's index and each message's search text instead of decoding every entry. RPC `get_entries` and `get_tree`, extension session reads and branch summaries read message records from the session file only when they need them, so these whole-history reads no longer decode the history.
- A session without a file (`--no-session`) keeps the records of entries behind its latest compaction compressed instead of decoded.
- RPC `get_entries`, RPC `get_tree` and extension session reads now fail with an error naming the entry when the session file was changed under PiG, instead of returning the entry's base members alone.
- A long session no longer holds each tool result twice. The session now keeps the tool result the agent produced instead of a second copy decoded from its record, and a truncated `read`, `ls`, `find`, `grep` or `bash` result keeps its kept output once instead of again in its truncation details. In a 75-prompt session of file reads (about 4.2M tokens of context), memory at the end drops from about 245 MB to about 170 MB.
- Appending a tool result whose details are a tool's own value (a truncated `read`, `bash` output details, an extension's details object) no longer parses the session record it has just written. Appending a 1 MiB truncated read now takes about 3 ms instead of 72 ms.
- A large message's session record no longer keeps a regrown buffer when its text has escapes such as newlines, tabs or quotes. A truncated 1 MiB read used to keep 1.6 MB of unused space for the life of the session and now keeps about 160 KB; a 1 MiB source-code message kept 226 KB and now keeps 88 KB.
- A session keeps reading its history as it loaded it when another program rewrites, truncates, replaces or removes its session file, as Pi does. PiG reads history from a private snapshot of the session file. The snapshot is a file system clone where the file system supports one (Btrfs, XFS, APFS); elsewhere it is a copy that takes the file's size on disk. Before, a read of an entry behind the latest compaction failed or returned only the entry's id, parentId and type.
- Fixed the `re:` mode of the session selector search to read its pattern as JavaScript does (`new RegExp(pattern, "i")`): assertions that cannot be repeated (`\b{2}`, `^*`, a quantified lookbehind) and class ranges with a class escape (`[a-\d]`) now give Pi's results, a pattern is matched as UTF-16 code units so a quantifier binds to the second half of an astral character, `\b`, `\w` and `\s` use JavaScript's ASCII and WhiteSpace sets, case-insensitive matching uses JavaScript's case mapping (`İ` no longer matches `i`), `(?s:` and `(?m:` modifier groups are accepted, and an invalid pattern reports V8's message (`Invalid regular expression: /…/i: Nothing to repeat`).
- Fixed the session selector's selected row after a down or page-down key over an empty list: as in Pi the index becomes -1 and stays out of range after the scope or search changes (no row is highlighted and the position reads `(0/N)`) instead of snapping to the first row, and enter, delete and rename no longer act on a row that is not selected. Reloading the list in that state no longer panics.
- A session message with a member of another JSON type than Pi writes is now kept, as Pi keeps it. Examples are a string `timestamp`, numeric `content`, a text block whose `text` is a number, a usage count written as a string, `isError: "no"`, a numeric or missing `role`, and a message that is a number, a string or an array rather than an object. Before, PiG dropped the message from the session context, the projection and RPC `get_messages`. The message is written exactly as it was read. PiG reads a wrong-typed member as absent. An object or array member keeps the parts that have the right types. A content block with an unknown or wrong-typed `type` is left out of the provider request, as Pi's provider conversions skip it.
- A configured number in settings text prints as JavaScript's `String(number)`: `-0` is `0`, and a magnitude of `1e21` or more, or below `1e-6`, gets an exponent (the fullscreen wheel scroll lines label and choices, and the invalid compaction token setting message).
- A Node extension's `ctx.ui.custom` factory can call `tui.showOverlay`, `tui.hideOverlay` and `tui.setFocus`, as in Pi. `showOverlay` shows the component as a raw overlay at once and returns a synchronous handle (`hide`, `setHidden`, `isHidden`, `focus`, `unfocus`, `isFocused`, `getBounds`), so Pi's overlay-focus, overlay-passive and overlay-streaming example extensions work. `tui.requestRender()` redraws every component the extension shows. A raw overlay ends with its handle's `hide()`, or when the extension's process exits or reloads.
- Hiding an overlay whose preFocus is another overlay, such as a panel focused when the overlay was shown over it, focuses that panel as an overlay, as Pi does: its handle reports `isFocused()` and its own `hide()` moves focus on.
- A long session keeps less memory for each turn it has compacted away. The `/tree` search text of an entry that compaction moves out of the context is no longer built and kept when the entry leaves memory: the first search reads it from the entry's record, as it already did after opening a session, and a session nobody searches keeps none. The cache of encoded tool-call arguments releases an encoding when the arguments it encodes are no longer reachable, where it kept the encodings of dropped calls until its 16 MiB limit. The agent's turn timer keeps the number of completed turns and the last duration instead of one duration per turn. In a 21,000-turn RPC session with automatic compaction on a 32k window, live heap growth falls from about 1.7 KiB per turn to the index of the session's entries (slots, compressed records, compaction entries), which Pi's in-memory session also keeps, as whole entries.
- In print and JSON mode, an extension in its own Node process no longer runs its callbacks with a stale ctx when a later extension's handler awaits a timer or I/O. Pi runs every extension on one event loop, so the immediate or timer that one extension's `session_shutdown` or `agent_settled` handler queues runs while the next extension's handler waits, before the runner is invalidated. The Node runtime now reports such a wait with a `loop_turned` notification, which the host forwards to every other Node process as its next step. Packed extensions already shared one loop and behave as before; RPC and interactive mode are unchanged.
- In print and JSON mode, Node extensions that share one process now keep Pi's order at disposal. Before, an earlier extension's queued callback could run before a later extension's `session_shutdown` handler started, or a timer could run with a live ctx after the runner was invalidated, depending on the order in which the process read its sockets.
- A terminal's reply to the startup color query now applies the colors and ends the startup wait before the next input is read, as in Pi. A prompt typed right after the terminal answered, for example by a client that types as soon as the editor shows, reaches the ordinary submit handler instead of reporting "Startup is still in progress".
- Interactive startup no longer reads terminal input while it checks for fd and rg unless one of them is being downloaded, as in Pi. When both tools are present, or offline mode skips the download, a prompt typed during startup reaches the ordinary submit handler instead of reporting "Startup is still in progress".
- Interactive startup writes the terminal color query before its first frame, as Pi does: starting the renderer only requests the first frame, which paints while startup waits for the terminal's colors. In fullscreen the alternate screen is still entered first. The color round trip is now under way when the editor first shows, so input typed at the first frame waits on the same startup work as in Pi.
- A system message's `sections` object now enumerates integer-like section names first, in ascending order, when read from or written to JSON, as a JavaScript object does, so prompt section replay and planned section patches keep Pi's order.
- Without `HOME` set, the project trust check takes the home directory from the user database, as Pi's `os.homedir()` does, instead of treating the working directory as the home directory. A working directory's `.agents/skills` now requires project trust in that case too.
- A Piglet Binary with a fused Go extension starts within a few milliseconds of Stock PiG. On an Apple M4 Pro (median of 20, faux provider), `-p` took 72.1 ms against 31.7 ms for Stock PiG and now takes 33.0 ms, and the first typed key showed in the editor after 71.0 ms against 33.6 ms and now after 33.4 ms. The extension host encoded the whole model catalog (more than 1 MB of JSON) three times at startup for every extension. Now it builds and sends that snapshot only to Node extensions, which answer `ctx.modelRegistry` reads from it. Go, Rust and Python extensions read the registry from the host when they ask, as before, so Stock PiG with such extensions starts faster too. See D19 in `docs/additive-features.md`.
- After `pig piglet update` installs a new release of a pulled Piglet Binary, `pig piglet list` shows the Piglet once and `pig piglet show <name>` lists every installed release, including the one the update installed. Before, each release of a Piglet without a source was listed as its own Piglet and `show` printed only the first.
- A durable task that panics with a value whose `String` method panics now faults with the message `[object Object]`, as Pi's `errorMessage` gives for a thrown value that cannot be formatted, instead of Go's `%!v(PANIC=...)` text.
- A configured thinking budget of zero (or below) now wins over the default in the OpenAI-compatible, Google and Bedrock Claude request builders and the shared simple-options helpers, as in Pi. This applies to `thinkingBudgets` in settings and to an extension model request's `thinkingBudgets`. `ai.ThinkingBudgets` gains a `Set` field and `ai.NewThinkingBudgets`, and reads and writes Pi's `{"minimal":n,...}` JSON object with only the configured levels. A Claude budget of zero is floored to 1024 and is not lowered further by the context clamp (`thinkingBudgetTokens || 1024`).
- `~/dir/` expands to the home directory with the trailing separator kept (`$HOME/dir/`), as Node's `path.join` does in Pi's path normalization; it was cleaned to `$HOME/dir`.
- `toPrecision` rounds a value just below a power of ten (1e-11 to 16 digits, 9.999999999999998e9 to 16 digits) to the digits Node prints (`9.999999999999999e-12`, `9999999999.999998`) instead of carrying it up to the power of ten.
- Tool argument validation errors follow Pi: at most eight errors are listed (TypeBox's `maxErrors`), and errors for additional properties, pattern properties and property names come in the arguments' member order instead of sorted order.
- Validation errors for `propertyNames` name the failing member in their path, `contains` reports only "must contain at least 1 valid item", and a failing `then` branch reports only `must match "then" schema` while a failing `else` branch also lists its errors, as in Pi.
- Tool argument validation asserts the `format` keyword for the formats TypeBox checks (date, time, date-time, duration, email, ipv4, ipv6, iri, iri-reference, json-pointer, json-pointer-uri-fragment, relative-json-pointer, uri, uri-reference, uri-template, url, uuid) with TypeBox's own rules. Before, a value that violated a format passed validation.
- Tool argument conversion for TypeBox schemas lower-cases with JavaScript rules (an `İ` in `UNDEFİNED` no longer converts to null) and matches Record `patternProperties` keys as the anchored expression `^pattern$`, as TypeBox does.
- A tool call whose arguments hold a key with an escaped lone surrogate (`"\ud800a"`) keeps that key's place in the member order, instead of moving it last.
- A streamed tool argument of `-0` is held as `0`, so the serialized arguments write `0` as Pi's `JSON.stringify` does instead of `-0`.
- Fixed a failing tool call's result always opening fully expanded: like Pi, an error result collapses to the renderer's preview (a failing bash command shows its last five lines and the expand hint) until the tools are expanded with Ctrl+O or a click.
- The agent loop now gives a custom stream function Pi's messages (errored and aborted turns kept, no synthetic "No result provided" results); real providers repair orphaned, repeated and out-of-order tool results themselves (D48).
- A real provider's tool-flow repair (D48) no longer moves a tool result back to an earlier unanswered call that has the same id, and no longer keeps a result in a turn that never made its call. A request whose tool flow Pi sends intact reaches the provider unchanged, even when a provider reuses call ids every turn.
- Gemini 2.x models (Google Generative AI and Vertex) no longer reject a conversation in which a parallel tool call other than the last returned an image. PiG keeps all function responses of one model turn in a single user turn and sends the image turns after them. Pi 1.2.0 splits that turn (D115).
- Amazon Bedrock Converse requests merge adjacent messages that have the same role. A prompt sent after an abort during tool execution, or after a dropped assistant turn, no longer makes every later turn fail with a role-alternation error. Pi 1.2.0 sends two consecutive user messages (D116).
- Replaying a thinking block that holds only U+FEFF drops it and one that holds only U+0085 keeps it, as JavaScript trim() decides in Pi. <!-- fix: f73c00c391 -->
- Fixed the tree selector's label prompt drawing an input row one cell too wide in a terminal two columns wide; the input now gets Pi's `width - 2` cells.
- The tree selector label time of a session entry whose timestamp is not a valid date prints "aN/NaN/NaN NaN:NaN", and a timestamp is read in any form Date.parse accepts, not only RFC 3339. <!-- fix: b54f9aa86a -->
- Fixed the session tree selector keeping its cursor inside the rows. As in Pi, Up and Page Down over an empty filtered list leave the selection at -1 (Down leaves it at 1), and a later filter change keeps it there until a row has been selected: the list then shows `(0/N)` with no highlighted row.
- A project whose only configuration is a project `mcp.json` (`.pig/mcp.json`, or `.pi/mcp.json` with `PIG_USE_PI_DIRS=1`, D2) now requires project trust, as Pi's `TRUST_REQUIRING_PROJECT_CONFIG_RESOURCES` does. PiG left `mcp.json` out of that list, so such a project counted as having nothing to trust and the commands of its stdio MCP servers started without a prompt in every mode. Interactive mode now asks for trust. Print, JSON and RPC modes, without a saved decision or `defaultProjectTrust: "always"`, now ignore the file, as they do for other project configuration.
- A Piglet found by name in `<project>/.pig/piglets` now follows project trust: it is used only when the project has a stored trust decision of yes or the run passes `--approve`, and an ignored workspace Piglet is named in the error. A bare `--piglet <name>` is never read as a file in the working directory. Before, a cloned repository could shadow the user's Piglet of that name, with its system prompt, extensions and model, with no prompt and no flag.
- `tui.Box` no longer invalidates its children when a child is added or removed or the box is cleared. Pi's `Box` drops only its own render cache there, and an extension component inside a box no longer receives an `invalidate` call that Pi does not make. `Box.Invalidate` still invalidates every child.
- An auto, manual or overflow compaction status shown in the editor's top border now takes the editor border color, like the working status, as in Pi 1.2.0; it used to keep the accent spinner and muted label.
- Markdown now nests a `**strong**` run inside `*emphasis*` as Pi does, renders a line underlined with `===` or `---` as a heading, and leaves a blank row between a paragraph and a blockquote, heading, rule or math block that directly follows it.
- Key matching follows Pi for three terminal inputs. A modified `escape` (`ctrl+escape`, `shift+alt+esc`) matches no key binding, as in Pi, although the same input still parses to that name. An xterm modifyOtherKeys sequence (`ESC [ 27 ; mod ; code ~`) matches only when its modifier is exactly the binding's, so a Caps Lock or Num Lock bit no longer matches. An unmodified letter, digit or symbol binding no longer matches a modifyOtherKeys sequence.
- Key matching follows Pi for more terminal inputs and key identifiers. Unmodified `enter` and `tab` no longer match an xterm modifyOtherKeys sequence. A sequence number with a sign (`ESC [ 1 ; +5 A`) or an event field with no digits (`ESC [ 1 ; 5 : A`) is no longer read as a key. Modifier and codepoint numbers are read as JavaScript numbers, so very large values wrap and round as in Pi. A key identifier part that names no modifier, such as `meta` in `meta+a`, is ignored as in Pi instead of making the identifier match nothing, and identifiers are lowercased as JavaScript's `toLowerCase` does.
- SettingsList draws Pi's bytes: its scroll indicator, description and hint rows are no longer padded to the width, and a row or hint that does not fit ends in `...` as pi-tui's truncateToWidth ends it.
- A `!` command block draws Pi's bytes: two consecutive blocks are separated by one blank row instead of two, the running hint names the `tui.select.cancel` keys (`escape/ctrl+c to cancel`) as Pi's does, and its rows are padded to the width with Pi's SGR closes. A quoted line followed by a lazy `===` stays quoted text, as in Pi, instead of becoming a heading.
- A running `!cmd` block keeps only the last 2000 lines or 50 KB it shows instead of every byte the command prints, so a long build or `!cat` of a large file no longer grows memory with its output (32 MB of output: 33.3 MB retained before, 0.2 MB now). The rows drawn and the output saved to the session are unchanged. `tui.BashExecutionComponent.GetOutput` is replaced by `ContextOutput`, which returns the shown window.
- Fixed the card of a tool call for a tool that does not exist (a name the model made up): it now shows the tool name, the arguments as indented JSON and the result right below, as Pi does, instead of a compact `key:value` summary with a blank row before the result.
- A URL host with an `xn--` label that is empty after IDNA mapping (`ws://xn--。`, `https://..\ufeffxn--`) or that holds non-ASCII text after the prefix (`https://xn--é/`) is rejected, as Node's `new URL` does; the first was read as a dropped label, the second re-encoded.
- Tool arguments that TypeBox's Convert wraps in an array (an object where the schema asks for an array, such as `edits`) keep the member order of the arguments' text, in the returned arguments and in the order of the validation errors, as in Pi. They came out byte-sorted.
- Two PiG builds that share a config root (two installed versions, or stock PiG beside a Piglet Binary) no longer rewrite each other's documentation at every start. Each build writes its docs to its own directory, `~/.pig/docs/bundle-<hash>/`, and the system prompt, login help and codemode name that directory; `pig docs path` prints it. A sync keeps the four most recently used bundles.
- Two PiG versions that share a config root (two installed versions, or PiG beside a Piglet Binary) no longer restage each other's extension SDKs at every start or delete each other's extension builds. Each version stages its SDKs into its own directory under `state/pigsdk`, named for the SDK's content hash, and reads only that directory, so a Python extension also always runs against its own version's SDK. After staging, PiG keeps the 4 most recently used SDK directories per language and drops only the extension builds whose SDK is gone. `pig reload --sdk-path` prints the running version's directory, and `pig diagnose` shows it.
- A provider defined only by virtual models reports `configured` with source `environment` and label `virtual` from `ModelRegistry.GetProviderAuthStatus`/`ModelRuntime.GetProviderAuthStatus`, and `GetProviderAuth`/`GetAuth` resolve it with source `virtual` instead of nil, as Pi does.
- Provider order puts a provider of only virtual models where Pi's `providerIds` does (the place of its models.json ID when that file loaded, else after the others), `allModels`/`allAvailable` list its models there, and the virtual models of a provider with no credentials are not available.
- A model an extension registers with `registerProvider` reaches `ctx.modelRegistry`, `ctx.model` and RPC as Pi's object: the definition's members in the order the extension wrote them, then `api`, `provider` and `baseUrl`, and its `input` as written. PiG sent the members in its own order and filled in `input: ["text"]` when the definition left it out.
- Extension widgets share one key namespace across extensions, as in Pi: an extension that sets a widget key another extension (in the host process or in its own process) already uses replaces that widget, and clearing the key clears it whoever set it. Two extension processes setting the same key used to both show.
- Package manifest globs match as Node's `fs.globSync` does in Pi: brace sets and sequences, extglobs such as `*.@(md|txt)`, POSIX classes, `.` and empty path segments, and case-insensitive wildcard segments on Windows and macOS.
- Package filters, manifest `!`/`+`/`-` overrides, settings resource patterns and built-in extension overrides follow minimatch 10.2.6 and its brace-expansion 5.0.12 limits, as in Pi.
- A pattern that Pi cannot compile now fails where Pi fails: a local Package keeps only the resource kinds collected before it, an npm or git Package (configured or `-e`) stops startup and `/reload` with the JavaScript error, and so does a settings pattern or an override on auto-discovered resources.
- An npm or git `-e` source that fails to install stops startup and `/reload` before any extension factory runs, and later `-e` sources are not installed. Each `-e` source resolves once per load.
- On macOS, reading an image or file paths from the clipboard and writing text to it no longer risk passing AppKit an address in freed memory. When Go moved the goroutine stack during the call, AppKit could read a stale array or text buffer. These buffers now stay in place for the call.
- `pig package publish --to npm` and `pig piglet publish --to npm` no longer run an `npm` that the published directory contains. PiG finds npm in the absolute directories on `PATH`, so publishing from inside a Package that ships its own `npm` works and uses your npm (or resolves a relative `npmCommand` against the directory you run `pig` in) and starts that file. Before, Windows' `cmd.exe` could pick a Package's own `npm.cmd` ahead of the author's npm, and a relative `npmCommand` was resolved inside the Package directory.
- On Windows, publishing to npm now works when npm is an `npm.cmd` at a path with spaces, such as `C:\Program Files\nodejs`. Arguments now reach the shim the way Pi's package commands pass them. An `npmCommand` that names a script now fails with a hint to name its interpreter first.
- On Windows, the bash tool no longer risks waiting forever after a command exits while a process it started in the background still holds the output pipe. The check that asks Windows whether the command's job still has processes could read an empty answer when Go moved the goroutine stack during the call, and the tool then waited for the pipe to close. The buffers these job-object calls pass to Windows now stay in place.
- A prompt now fails with Pi's error when the Session's history holds a system message whose `content` is a number, a boolean or an object (`content.filter is not a function`), whose content array holds `null`, or whose `toolsAdded` or `toolsRemoved` is a number, a boolean, an object or an array that holds `null` (for example `number 5 is not iterable (cannot read property Symbol(Symbol.iterator))`). Before, PiG read such a member as absent and sent the request. In RPC mode the prompt response reports the error, and in interactive mode the error is shown; no run starts.
- Go, Python and Rust SDK: `theme.bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `inverse` and `strikethrough` now draw as Pi's chalk does: an empty text stays empty, a line break closes and re-opens the style, and an inner close of the same style re-opens it. Before, they wrapped the whole text once, so a multi-line or nested string lost the style past the first line or inner close, and an empty string came back as two escape sequences.
- A Provider object registered by an extension (`pi.registerProvider(provider)`) now receives the session's `onResponse` and `onProviderStreamEvent` callbacks in its stream options, as in Pi, so `after_provider_response` and `provider_stream_event` fire for its requests. The host answers each Provider callback by its name; before, every callback was treated as `onPayload`. A Go caller's `StreamOptions.OnResponse` reaches the Provider from `ModelRuntime.Stream` and `StreamSimple` the same way: the observer gets the response status, headers and model, the Provider awaits it, and an observer error ends the stream with that error. Reported by @JihongWEI, whose reproduction is now a regression test (#206). <!-- fix: 051a79d333, 33ce911946 -->
- A Provider object's stream options no longer carry a stray `hasOnPayload` value.
- A provider an extension registers with `refreshModels` and no `streamSimple` streams through its API, as in Pi. Before, every request failed with "Provider ... has no stream implementation".
- `insecure: true` on a provider an extension registers with `refreshModels` now reaches the request transport. A re-registration that omits `insecure` keeps an earlier opt-in, as Pi keeps every field a re-registration leaves undefined; before, a provider registered without `refreshModels` ignored an `insecure: true` re-registration.
- A restarted extension, isolated or in a recovered packed cell, now offers the commands, flags, shortcuts and tools its new process registered; before, it kept the first registration's. Shortcuts keep their registration order.
- `setWorkingMessage` with no message restores the default working label and an empty string shows an empty label, as in Pi (`message ?? defaultWorkingMessage`). Both used to show the default. The Go, Rust and Python extension SDKs gain `ClearWorkingMessage()`, `clear_working_message()` and `set_working_message(None)` for the no-argument form; `SetWorkingMessage(message)` still takes a string.
