A `UIContext` can implement the new `extension.WorkingMessageClearer` interface to tell the two apart; one that does not keeps the old behavior, so existing implementations still compile. `setHiddenThinkingLabel` follows the same rule (`ClearHiddenThinkingLabel()`, `clear_hidden_thinking_label()`, `set_hidden_thinking_label(None)`; `extension.HiddenThinkingLabelClearer`). A Session replacement now also resets the working message, visibility and indicator an extension set, as Pi does.
- xAI OAuth login and refresh now read the server's response the way Pi does: a body that is not a JSON object counts as empty, a field of an unexpected JSON type is reported as that field being invalid (instead of "xAI OAuth returned invalid JSON"), a `refresh_token` or `expires_in` that is present but `null` is rejected rather than treated as absent, a leading byte order mark is ignored, and `verification_uri_complete` is validated before the required fields. A refresh with an empty stored refresh token now sends the request as Pi does.
- A scalar that names a collection tag (`!!map 1`, `!!seq true`) inside a complex mapping key stays plain in the key's text, as the yaml library prints it; it used to be quoted (`!!map "1"`) as a string scalar is, so the object key differed.
- A YAML parse error on a block scalar used as an implicit key (`name: x` followed by `|description: ...`) draws the carets of the `Implicit keys need to be on a single line` message as the yaml library does when the scalar's content holds a non-ASCII character: the range ends one UTF-16 unit per character after the key's offset, where it used to count bytes and print one caret or too many.
- Frontmatter YAML parsing matches the `yaml` library on text outside ASCII and on unterminated quotes: an unterminated quoted scalar reports its error at the end of the input, a line that starts with a non-ASCII character no longer changes the continuation indent of the lines after it, `Invalid escape sequence` quotes UTF-16 units, an implicit key's 1024-character limit counts UTF-16 units, anchors may not contain Unicode whitespace, and `-0` is `0` as an `!!omap` or `!!set` key.
- A streaming run no longer hangs, rarely and mostly under load, when a response turn resumes just as the drain that granted it ends: a pausing coroutine put back a drain flag the ending drain had already cleared, which left every goroutine asleep with a reaction still queued.
- Codemode no longer reserves its whole 256 MiB script-memory limit when a session starts: the QuickJS linear memory now grows on demand, so an idle session with one small script allocates about 4 MiB instead of about 359 MiB (Go heap held, 321 MiB before, 4 MiB after), while a runaway allocation still fails inside the script at the same limit. Reported by @kayis-rahman (#211)
- The built-in models page lists the real thinking levels (off, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max) instead of a five-step ladder that started at none. Reported by @SkrOYC (#194).
- The built-in models page documents the `models.json` provider and model fields (`models[]`, `contextWindow`, `maxTokens`, `modelOverrides`) with an example, plus provider cost tiers, local models and troubleshooting. Reported by @SkrOYC (#193).
- The built-in docs gain the compaction summary format, branch summaries and extension hooks, the RPC, JSON mode, CLI integration, session format, message types, llama.cpp, security and usage pages, and "PiG" replaces "Pig" throughout. The platform pages stay on the site only, by choice. Reported by @SkrOYC (#195).
- The packages page says how to build and run `pig-acp`, which `npm:@pi-in-go/pigpen-acp` needs but does not install, and how to point Zed at it. Reported by @klonuo (#210).
- Codemode peak memory is now measured and guarded through the normal tool path in a fresh process with 40 extension tools exposed. Peak RSS is about 62-67 MiB cold (no compilation cache) for a no-op with or without extension tools, about 36 MiB with the compilation cache, about the same for a script passing a 96-record, 18 KB state to a tool, and 41 MiB for the two concurrent scripts the worker limit allows; before the on-demand memory fix a concurrent run reached about 355 MiB. Reported by @kayis-rahman (#211)
- A provider request that fails in transport now records its cause: the failed message carries a `provider_transport_failure` diagnostic (the same shape Pi writes for its Codex WebSocket failures) with the Go cause and a Node-style code such as `ECONNRESET`, `ETIMEDOUT` or `ENOTFOUND`, so a reset, a dial timeout and a DNS failure can be told apart in the session file and `/bug`. The error message, stop reason, retries and events are unchanged, and existing sessions are untouched. Reported by @ShoichiTect (#87)
- The Codex catalog entry for GPT-6.1 Sol reports the 500K context window the Codex models endpoint returns; other Codex models keep 272K (carried Pi fix, Pi 5712b134, #10840).
- Durable: the system baseline written after a compaction now leads the model context when the kept history includes an assistant message, as does a first baseline written after earlier answers, instead of following the new input; Anthropic and OpenAI Codex requests no longer lose the initial prompt and tool set. Other system messages after an assistant message stay in place. Carried from Pi 5713c950 (earendil-works/pi#10904)
- OpenCode Zen and OpenCode Go use the `opencode.ai/inference` endpoints, which fixes OpenCode Go Gemini models failing with a 404; OpenCode Go MiniMax M2.7 uses the Anthropic Messages API, and OpenCode Go DeepSeek V4 Flash is no longer listed (carried Pi fix, Pi 195120a9).
- Empty quoted prompt-template arguments (`""` or `''`) are kept as empty arguments, so later positional arguments no longer shift and `${1:-default}` defaults apply (carried Pi fix, Pi 9fe3f371, #10867).
- A tool call whose finished arguments are not a valid JSON object no longer runs with the parsed prefix: its arguments are `{}` and `argumentsError` holds the reason and the text the model sent, which tool validation reports back to the model. Carried forward from Pi 87f9a364 (earendil-works/pi#10944, #10897), past the 1.2.0+3daa2028 pin.
- Xiaomi MiMo models send only the thinking on/off toggle (no `reasoning_effort`) and expose only the `off` and `high` thinking levels, fixing the 400 at `minimal`. Carried forward from Pi 6374c2ba and 381a2d8e (earendil-works/pi#10818), past the 1.2.0+3daa2028 pin.
- OpenAI-compatible streams where one chunk carries reasoning and content (LiteLLM and similar proxies) now place the thinking block before the text. Carried forward from Pi 63035734 (earendil-works/pi#10926), past the 1.2.0+3daa2028 pin.
- Gemini 4 and later models send `thinkingLevel` instead of `thinkingBudget`, fixing Gemini 4 Argon on Vertex and the ignored thinking level on the Gemini API. Carried forward from Pi 1692bba6 (earendil-works/pi#10890), past the 1.2.0+3daa2028 pin.
- Automatic retry now triggers for the gateway error "Unable to forward upstream stream". Carried forward from Pi 7efebff1 (earendil-works/pi#10920), past the 1.2.0+3daa2028 pin.

### Known issues

- `models.classify()` keeps the order of the script's questions but not the key order of its `state`: `ai.ClassifierContext.State` is a Go map, so the order is lost for every caller. Nested calls of other tools whose `Execute` reads a map are unordered in Go too (a Pig-wide property of `args any`).
- The Pig OAuth credential store (D40, `has_credential_store`) is available to Go, Python and Rust extensions but not yet to Node extensions. A Node extension's OAuth provider keeps its credentials in `auth.json`, as in Pi.
- On Windows, an image that an application puts on the clipboard only as a bitmap (for example .NET's `Clipboard.SetImage`) can paste upside down. Windows synthesizes the `CF_DIBV5` copy with its rows top-down under a bottom-up header. Pig reads the clipboard as Pi does (the `PNG` format first, then `CF_DIBV5`, then `CF_DIB`), so Pi shows the same image. Screenshot tools that also offer `PNG` paste correctly.

## [0.4.1] - 2026-10-05

### Added

- Azure Foundry Chat Completions: `azure/deepseek-v4-pro` is built in, and other Foundry models can be added under the `azure` provider with `"api": "openai-completions"` and a `"baseUrl"`. `AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT_NAME_MAP` applies to both Azure APIs.
- The durable execution environment follows Pi 1.0.3. File systems open bounded binary readers (`OpenBinaryReader`, with `NoFollow` to refuse a final-component symlink and a one-pass `ScanLines`) and paged directory readers (`OpenDirReader`), and report changes to files and directories with `Watch`. `NodeExecutionEnv` watches natively and falls back to snapshot polling on Windows and on network and FUSE file systems.
- `Exec` takes a string or a `[]string` argv, which runs a program without a shell, and every output chunk names its stream. An environment may omit shell output outside the tail a tool keeps (`ShellExecOptions.Window`) and report the omission; the bash tool passes the window along.
- `settings.progress` sets how often generation partials and running tool output are committed (defaults stay 100 ms).
- `durabletest.RegisterEnvConformance` checks a custom `ExecutionEnv`, as `RegisterStorageConformance` checks storage.
- A running session warns once, in place of the `/bug` hint, when an update or another `pig` replaced or removed the `pig` executable, or another `pig` pruned an extension cell or runtime file the session uses: `The pig installation this session runs from was removed or replaced. Features that load code on demand can fail until restart.`, with the `pig --session <id>` command that continues the session. PiG checks only when an error is shown (an assistant error, a failed tool call, an error or extension error notice). This is PiG's version of Pi 1.0.3's install-change warning.
- `samplingParamsByThinkingLevel` for custom models and `modelOverrides` in `models.json`: sampling parameters for each thinking level, merged between the model's `samplingParams` and the request's. OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses and Azure OpenAI Responses requests apply the entry for the request's effective thinking level. See `docs/models.md`.
- Durable conversations persist a provider session ID (`pi.provider` document) and send it with every generation and compaction request, for prompt caching and session affinity. Conversations created before this release get one before their next request.
- Project overrides for MCP servers: a `.pi/mcp.json` entry without `command`, `url` or `type` changes only `enabled`, `exposure` and `toolExposure` of the user-level server with the same name. `/mcp` offers "Enable in this project" and "Disable in this project", and `pig mcp list` shows the override. See `docs/mcp.md`.
- MCP OAuth `clientRegistration: "cimd"` signs in with Pi's Client ID Metadata Document on pi.dev instead of dynamic client registration, with a server-specific callback path when the authorization server does not send the `iss` parameter.
- `pi.registerToolRenderer()` in every SDK (Node `pi.registerToolRenderer`, Go `ToolRenderer`, Python and Rust `tool_renderer`) and for in-process Go extensions: resolvers choose how calls to any tool are drawn, in extension load order, in the transcript and in HTML exports. A resumed session draws calls to MCP tools whose server has not connected yet with the MCP renderers. An extension process answers once per tool, off the interactive loop, and `next()` cannot be wrapped (D89).
- Ctrl+X (`app.message.copy`) copies the sign-in URL on the `/login` dialog and the MCP sign-in screen.
- On Kitty-protocol terminals, non-PNG images are converted to PNG; a tool image that cannot be converted shows its text description instead of nothing.
- `check-model-data -hydrate <models.all.json>` hydrates a package's provider data from a published model catalog.
- Extension-registered models (`pi.registerProvider`) also carry `samplingParamsByThinkingLevel`, as in Pi 1.0.2, so their per-level sampling parameters reach OpenAI-compatible requests.
- `coding.ModelRuntime.Login` logs in built-in and `models.json` providers as well as extension-registered ones, and takes optional `ai.LoginOptions` (for example the installation ID), as Pi's `ModelRuntime.login` does. An extension OAuth provider with its own credential store saves there (D40) (#147).
- `docs/terminal-setup.md` documents PiG's own herdr agent support (`custom:pig`, through Pigpen's herdr Piglet) and why herdr's built-in Pi integration only attaches to a process named `pi` (#136).

### Changed

- PiG follows Pi 1.0.3. `pig --version` prints `<PiG release>+1.0.3`.
- **Breaking:** the Azure provider is `azure`, not `azure-openai-responses`, because it now serves Chat Completions as well as the Responses API. The `azure-openai-responses` API ID and the `AZURE_OPENAI_*` variables are unchanged. PiG does not migrate old entries, as Pi does not. Rename the provider key in `auth.json` (or run `/login` again), in `models.json`, and in `settings.json` (`defaultProvider`, `enabledModels` patterns and `modelThinkingLevels` keys). A session that used the old provider falls back to another model when resumed, and its prompt cache is not reused. See `docs/providers.md`.
- The model catalog follows Pi 1.0.3: the `azure` provider, Azure Foundry DeepSeek V4 Pro, Bedrock Claude Sonnet 5.5 regions, and price updates.
- Codemode `image()` also saves each image to a temp file and names the path in the result, so later turns can copy or move generated images.
- Output files (the full text of truncated tool output, binary MCP resources and codemode images) are readable only by the user.
- `Home` and `End` always move the editor cursor. In fullscreen, the transcript top and bottom moved to `Ctrl+Home` and `Ctrl+End`, which no longer move the editor cursor.
- The durable `read` tool reads only the file header, one line scan and the lines it shows, instead of loading the whole file. Its results are unchanged.
- The model catalog follows Pi 1.0.2: NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Super, OpenRouter's Cloudflare Clef and Perplexity decider classifiers, and Llama 3.3 70B prices.
- llama.cpp models whose chat template supports thinking offer the off, minimal, low, medium and high thinking levels, and each level sends its own `thinking_budget_tokens`. Pi offers only off and medium (D90). See `docs/llama-cpp.md`.
- In fullscreen mode, `/arminsayshi` and `/pigsayhi` play Pi's 3D easter egg with the sprite's pig head in place of Armin: the screen dissolves, the pig grows out of the center, spins and slides its blocks around as a puzzle. Outside fullscreen mode they draw the inline pig head as before (D87).
- Anthropic requests that define tools after the first turn send them inline as tool definition blocks with the `inline-tools-2026-09-15` beta, as Pi 1.0.1 does.
- Bedrock adaptive thinking on Claude Opus 4.7, Opus 4.8, Opus 5, Sonnet 5 and Fable 5 drops thinking blocks whose prefix no longer matches (`block_binding`), outside GovCloud.
- The model catalog follows Pi 1.0.1: Bedrock prices above 272k input tokens, Cloudflare AI Gateway model IDs with dashes, the Together DeepSeek V4 Pro rename, Cloudflare's Clef classifiers, and NVIDIA's default model `nvidia/nemotron-3-ultra-550b-a55b`.
- Anthropic workload identity federation joins the request's betas and the OAuth beta with `,` and identifies as `Anthropic/JS 0.129.0`, as the SDK Pi 1.0.1 pins does.
- Sign-in URLs end their terminal hyperlinks with ST instead of BEL.
- The PiG text mark's letters are colored like Pi's wordmark.

### Fixed

- A subscription login such as Sign in with ChatGPT no longer fails with `refresh_token_invalidated` after a request is cancelled during an OAuth token refresh. The caller's cancellation now stops only the wait for the credential lock; a refresh that has started finishes and saves the rotated token, bounded by its 15 second timeout. An extension's OAuth refresh signal is no longer cancelled with the request.
- A foreign tool call replayed to an OpenAI Responses provider outside OpenAI, Codex and OpenCode keeps only the characters the API accepts in its ID, as Pi does.
- Interactive sessions no longer report a crash, or ask you to run `/bug`, when the terminal goes away while the session reads from it or enters raw mode, for example after closing the window or resuming a suspended `pig` in a closed terminal.
- `FlushFile` on a directory fails with `is_directory` on Windows, as on POSIX.
- Tail-retained tool output no longer depends on when progress commits happened: a snapshot compacted the stored output to the kept window, which could move where a later window's first line started.
- In interactive mode, an extension's `model_select` and `thinking_level_select` handlers see the newly selected model in `ctx.model` and in the subprocess SDKs' model state, so a custom footer no longer shows the previous model (#128).
- In interactive mode, an extension's model stream request without `reasoning` no longer thinks at the thinking level the session had when extensions loaded; it streams without thinking, as in Pi and in PiG's other modes.
- "Selected model is at capacity" provider errors are retried.
- Cloudflare's System One classifiers that answer directly no longer fail to parse.
- Sign in with ChatGPT fails with a clear message when port 1455 is taken by another login or the Codex CLI.
- `--models` ignores empty entries, such as a trailing comma.
- In WezTerm fullscreen mode, a change to a row under an image no longer erases the image.
- Codemode stops a script whose output passes 16 MiB or 100,000 `text()`, `image()` and `console` calls, so a script printing in a loop cannot exhaust memory.
- `AZURE_OPENAI_DEPLOYMENT_NAME_MAP` is read as Pi reads it: a later entry for the same model wins, text after a second `=` in an entry is ignored, a leading byte order mark is trimmed, and a model mapped to an empty name uses the model ID.
- A custom model in `models.json` under the `azure` provider (or any built-in provider whose models have no base URL) without a `baseUrl` now reports `"baseUrl" is required when defining custom models.` and keeps the built-in models, as Pi does, instead of being dropped without a message.
- A Durable `Submission.Wait` that started while the harness was closing could block forever; it now fails with `Harness is closed`.
- `pi.exec` in extensions no longer waits for a background process that keeps the command's output open. It returns once the command has exited and its output has been idle for 100 ms, as in Pi.
- `pi.exec` timeout and cancellation now send SIGTERM to the command alone (Pi's behavior), so a SIGTERM handler runs. A command that a signal ended, or on Windows that the kill ended, reports exit code 0, as in Pi.
- An extension that registers a provider override for a built-in provider (`pi.registerProvider("anthropic", { streamSimple })`) and then reads `ctx.modelRegistry.getProvider()` now sees the registration at once, as in Pi. After it unregisters the provider, the same reads see the built-in or models.json provider at once. `pi-background-tasks` 2.6.9 no longer stops startup with `pi_anthropic_attribution_install_failed`.
- Extension `input` handlers no longer run on the loop that owns the terminal. A handler that opened a dialog and waited for the answer deadlocked the UI: Enter did nothing, no frame changed, and no key responded again. When an extension registers an `input` handler, the handlers now run off that loop, one dispatch at a time, and the rest of the prompt path is applied on the owner loop when they return. With no `input` handler loaded the prompt path stays synchronous, and delivery is decided again after the handlers return, so a run that started while they ran receives the text instead of a second concurrent turn.
- `pig status` no longer reports duplicate resource errors for package resources that settings filters disable; only enabled resources can collide.
- Start every program PiG runs on Android through `/system/bin/linker64`: `pig install` and `pig remove` for `npm:` and `git:` packages, MCP stdio servers, tools, editors, and the Go, Piglet and git helpers failed with `fork/exec ...: permission denied` on Termux from Google Play.
- Make `pig setup go` on Android say that Go publishes no archive for `android/arm64` and to run `pkg install golang`, and make `pig setup status` give the same next step.
- Let `PIG_CELL_BUILD_TIMEOUT` bound `pig install <dir> --validate-only`, which stopped a slow extension build after a fixed two minutes.
- Sign in with ChatGPT works again: `pig login` and `/login` pass the installation ID (`deviceId`) to the OAuth flow, as Pi does (#146).
- Escape or Ctrl+C in a login dialog cancels the login before ending its pending prompt, so the login reports that it was aborted instead of failing (Pi's `login-dialog.ts`).
- The pending-messages rows list only queued user input, as in Pi: an extension's custom messages no longer show as blank `Steering:` or `Follow-up:` rows (#141).
- On Windows the bash tool keeps every line of a command's output: once the command exits and its job has no live processes, PiG reads to the end of the output instead of stopping after the 100 ms grace.
- A subprocess custom editor that sets `embedWorkingStatus` shows the working, compaction, retry and branch-summary status inside the editor, as Pi does (D94) (#135).
- Extension processes are stopped before the dead-terminal emergency exit, so they no longer outlive a closed terminal.
- `/reload` notices an edit to a Python extension file that keeps its size and lands inside one file-system timestamp tick: the reload check also compares a hash of the source.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-10-03

### Added

- PiG follows Pi 1.0.0. `pig --version` prints `<PiG release>+1.0.0`.
- Codemode and tool search as built-in extensions written in Go. The `codemode` tool runs model-written JavaScript in a QuickJS sandbox (compiled to WebAssembly and run by wazero, so it needs no Node) that calls PiG's tools, and `tool_search` finds tools that were not declared to the model. Enable codemode with `defaultTools` or `--tools`; `codemode.mode` and `codemode.inlineBudget` configure it. See `docs/codemode.md`.
- The built-in `mcp` extension runs the native MCP client in print, JSON, RPC and interactive mode: it loads `mcp.json`, registers `mcp__<server>__<tool>` tools and the resource tools, adds `/mcp`, and lists servers in a system-prompt section. Servers with the default `codemode` exposure connect in the background and do not delay the first prompt. `pig mcp add` takes `--description` and `--oauth-client-name`, and an HTTP server can use `"auth": {"provider": "<provider>"}`. See `docs/mcp.md`.
- Sign in with ChatGPT: `/login openai` uses a ChatGPT subscription with the OpenAI provider. GPT-6.1 Sol is in the catalog.
- The `system` theme, now the default, takes its colors from the terminal's palette and follows a light/dark switch. Theme files accept `#rgb`, `oklch()` and `okhsl()` colors.
- Anthropic workload identity federation from `ANTHROPIC_FEDERATION_RULE_ID`, `ANTHROPIC_ORGANIZATION_ID` and `ANTHROPIC_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE`; `/reload` enables tools newly added to `defaultTools`.
- User documentation for MCP, codemode and virtual models (`pig docs show mcp`, `codemode`, `virtual-models`), and a test that every document the system prompt cites ships in the docs bundle.
- `coding.CreateModelRuntimeOptions.Credentials` supplies the `ai.CredentialStore` a ModelRuntime uses for authentication instead of `auth.json`, as `ModelRuntime.create({ credentials })` does in Pi. When it is set, no `auth.json` is opened; `Services.Credentials()` returns the store in effect and `Services.Auth()` is nil. `ModelRegistry.SetCredentialStore` accepts any credential store.
- `Context.Signal()` in the Go SDK (a `context.Context`, nil while no run is active), `ctx.signal` in the Python SDK and `Context::signal()` in the Rust SDK (a `ProviderSignal`, `None` while no run is active), and `Context.Signal()` for in-process Go extensions. `Done`, `Err` and `is_cancelled` keep reporting the handler's own request.
- Add `agent.AgentToolResult.MemberOrder` and `MemberNames()`: the members of a tool's result object in the order the tool wrote them, which is the order Pi's `tool_execution_update` and `tool_execution_end` events carry.
- `defaultTools` accepts `+name` and `-name` entries. A project list of only such entries adjusts the user selection instead of replacing it, and a lone `["+codemode"]` adds a tool to the built-in defaults.
- Settings `fullscreenWheelScrollLines` (`"auto"` or 1 to 100 lines per wheel event), `codemode` (`mode`, `inlineBudget`) and `deviceId` (a random installation ID kept in the global settings file and left out of bug reports).
- Go API: `SettingsManager.GetFullscreenWheelScrollLines`, `SetFullscreenWheelScrollLines`, `GetOrCreateDeviceID` and `GetSettings`, and `Session.EntryCount`.
- Add the typed model accessors of upstream 0.99.1's `ModelRuntime` to `coding.ModelRuntime`: `GetProvider`, `GetModelsOfType`, `GetModelOfType`, `GetAllModels`, `GetAvailableOfType`, `GetModelAuth` and `GenerateImages`. Built-in OpenRouter image models are listed separately from chat models, keep working under `models.json` overrides, and use the provider's key and headers.
- List a classifier model next to each chat model for llama.cpp and keep the last known context window of a model llama.cpp has not loaded, unless the server arguments set `--ctx-size`, `-c` or `-ctx`.
- Built-in extensions. `pig config` lists them under a "Built-in" group (or "Built-in (project override)" for a project scope), and the `extensions` setting enables or disables one with `+builtin:<name>` or `-builtin:<name>`. `-e builtin:<name>` loads one for a single run, and `--no-extensions` disables all of them. They load after file and package extensions, and a replaceable built-in extension is left out, with a warning, when another extension registers the same tool, command or flag.
- An extension package whose `package.json` lists host-provided packages (`@earendil-works/*`, `@mariozechner/*`, `typebox`) under `dependencies` now produces a startup warning. A `package.json` that cannot be parsed fails the load.
- The system prompt's documentation list ends with the MCP servers document.
- Extensions register MCP servers (`registerMcpServer`, `unregisterMcpServer`, `getMcpServers`, the `mcp_servers_change` event) and virtual models (`registerVirtualModel`, `unregisterVirtualModel`) as in upstream 0.99.1. A registration made while the factory runs is applied when the factory succeeds, an extension owns the names it registers, and its registrations leave the runtime when the extension stops or a reload replaces it without them.
- A tool call receives an `extension.ToolContext` (`extension.ToolContextFromContext`) with `Tools()` and `ExecuteTool(name, args, options)`, which runs another tool through the same validation and hooks as a model-issued call. Nested-call updates and cancellation reach subprocess extensions through the `executeTool` host call.
- Tools declare `outputSchema`, `exposure`, `namespace`, `annotations`, `defaultActive` and `prepareLoadout`. `tool_result` handlers carry and return `structuredContent`. Extensions receive `provider_stream_event` for each parsed provider stream event before normalization (upstream 0.99.1 `sdk.ts:372-386`).
- Nested tool calls: a tool calls other tools through the extension context's `executeTool`, and the calls and their summed usage are recorded on the tool result as `nestedCalls` (`ai.NestedToolCalls`, `agent.ToolResultMessage.NestedCalls`), as upstream 0.99.1 does. Tool execution events carry `ParentToolCallID`. Compaction counts file operations of nested calls.
- Virtual models: `ModelRuntime.RegisterVirtualModel`/`UnregisterVirtualModel`/`ResolveModel`/`GetPhysicalModel`, `Session.RoutedModel`, and Session routing of each request, retries, compaction summaries and restored selections.
- Tool exposure (`direct`, `codemode`, `deferred`, `hidden`), `defaultActive`, `prepareLoadout` and `Session.CallableToolNames`.
- Go extensions can use upstream's `pi.events` bus. `ext.Events()` (before the extension runs) and `ctx.Events()` (from a handler) return an `EventBus` with `On(channel, handler)`, which returns an idempotent unsubscribe function, and `Emit(channel, data)`. The bus is the one Node extensions already share, so a Go extension receives events that Node extensions emit and emits events that Node listeners and Go listeners in other processes receive. Listeners run in registration order across all extensions, an unsubscribe removes only its own listener, a listener's error or panic is reported as `Event handler error (<channel>):` and reaches neither the emitter nor the later listeners, and a listener can emit from inside its handler. Payloads cross by value as JSON: a Go listener receives a Node emitter's `JSON.stringify` view (`toJSON` runs in the emitter when that listener runs, so the view includes what earlier listeners wrote, and `undefined` is `nil`), and a payload with no JSON form (a BigInt or a cycle) fails only the native listener's delivery. Call `Emit` through `ctx.Events()` from a handler so the nested emit is ordered with that request. The in-process `extension.EventBus` (`Publish`/`Subscribe`) is unchanged.
- The Go SDK carries the upstream 0.99.1 extension API additions. `Extension` and `Context` gain `RegisterMcpServer`, `UnregisterMcpServer`, `RegisterVirtualModel` and `UnregisterVirtualModel`; `Context` gains `GetMcpServers`, `GetSettings`, `Tools` and `ExecuteTool`; `Extension` gains `OnProviderStreamEvent` and `OnMcpServersChange`. `ToolDefinition` gains `OutputSchema`, `Exposure`, `Namespace`, `Annotations`, `DefaultActive` and `PrepareLoadout`; `ToolResult` gains `StructuredContent`. Registrations made before `Run` are applied when the extension loads; the host validates them.
- Node extensions can use the extension API of upstream 0.99.1: `pi.registerMcpServer`, `pi.unregisterMcpServer`, `pi.getMcpServers`, `pi.registerVirtualModel`, `pi.unregisterVirtualModel`, `pi.getSettings`, the tool definition fields `outputSchema`, `exposure`, `namespace`, `defaultActive` and `prepareLoadout`, `structuredContent` in tool results, and `ctx.tools` and `ctx.executeTool()` in a tool's context. Pi's own `examples/extensions/jev-router.ts` now loads and routes. `ctx.modelRegistry.classify` and the other typed registry methods it uses for its first request are not available yet.
- The Python extension SDK covers the extension API of upstream 0.99.1: `register_mcp_server`, `unregister_mcp_server`, `get_mcp_servers`, `register_virtual_model`, `unregister_virtual_model`, `get_settings`, `ToolDefinition` fields `output_schema`, `exposure`, `namespace`, `annotations`, `default_active` and `prepare_loadout`, `ctx.tools`, `ctx.execute_tool(name, args, ExecuteToolOptions(...))`, and the `provider_stream_event` and `mcp_servers_change` events. `ext.events` and `ctx.events` bridge `pi.events` to the other extension realms of a session. Existing extensions keep working.
- The Rust extension SDK exposes the upstream 0.99.1 extension API: `Extension::register_mcp_server` and `register_virtual_model` while loading, and `Context::register_mcp_server`, `unregister_mcp_server`, `get_mcp_servers`, `register_virtual_model`, `unregister_virtual_model`, `get_settings`, `tools` and `execute_tool` afterwards. `get_settings`, `get_mcp_servers` and `tools` are answered from the state the host replicates, as Pi's are synchronous. `execute_tool` names the calling tool call, delivers partial results to `ExecuteToolOptions::on_update` in order before it returns, and cancels the nested call with `ExecuteToolOptions::signal` (by default with the calling request). A tool declares `prepare_loadout` and the SDK answers the host's `tool_prepare_loadout` request. `EVENT_PROVIDER_STREAM_EVENT` and `EVENT_MCP_SERVERS_CHANGE` name the two new events.
- A native Model Context Protocol client in Go: `mcp` (client, stdio and streamable HTTP transports), `mcp/oauth` (OAuth discovery, PKCE, dynamic client registration and refresh) and the built-in MCP extension `coding/mcpext`, ported from upstream 0.99.1's `packages/mcp` and its built-in extension. It loads `mcp.json`, connects servers with `codemode`, `direct`, `deferred` or `hidden` exposure, registers `mcp__<server>__<tool>` tools and the `list_mcp_resources`, `list_mcp_resource_templates` and `read_mcp_resource` tools, and keeps OAuth credentials in `mcp-auth.json`. The built-in `mcp` extension bullet above describes how sessions use it.
- `anthropic` authenticates with workload identity federation when `ANTHROPIC_FEDERATION_RULE_ID`, `ANTHROPIC_ORGANIZATION_ID` and `ANTHROPIC_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE` are set and no key or authorization header is available, as upstream 0.99.2 does. See [Anthropic workload identity federation](../docs/site/docs/providers.md#anthropic-workload-identity-federation).
- `coding.SessionOptions.ResourceLoader` and `coding.SessionStartOptions.ResourceLoader` supply the skills, prompt templates, context files and system prompt text a Session expands, reports and builds its default system prompt from, as `createAgentSession({ resourceLoader })` does in Pi. The Session reads the loader on every use and `Session.ResourceLoader()` returns it. When none is supplied, the Session uses a `coding.DefaultResourceLoader` over the Services' cwd, agent directory and settings, which reloads the Services' settings (dropping transient `ApplyOverrides`, as `createAgentSession` does) and discovers resources at construction. It resolves settings-configured paths and override patterns, `~/.agents/skills`, ancestor `.agents/skills`, configured Packages (installing a missing npm or git Package unless `PI_OFFLINE` is set) and `SYSTEM.md`, `APPEND_SYSTEM.md` and `AGENTS.md` files, records each resource's `sourceInfo` from its metadata (a Package keeps the path it shares with a settings entry and ranks after settings and auto-discovered resources), resolves Packages against the loader's own cwd and agent directory, and skips project resources while the project is untrusted. `coding.DefaultResourceLoaderOptions` accepts `AdditionalSkillPaths`, `AdditionalPromptTemplatePaths`, `NoSkills`, `NoPromptTemplates`, `NoContextFiles`, `SystemPrompt`, `AppendSystemPrompt` and the `*Override` callbacks. It does not load extensions or themes. `coding.NoResources` binds none and leaves the system prompt to the caller. `Session.SetPromptResources` replaces the loader's skills and prompt templates with the collections it is given.
- `extension.ToolContext.AppendEntry` appends a custom entry to the session log and reports it as an `entry_appended` event, as `pi.appendEntry()` does. A declarative Go tool has no `pi` to close over, so it uses this method; without a bound action it returns `ErrRuntimeNotInitialized`. `extension.ToolActions.AppendEntry` is the action behind it.
- `inproc.Runner.BindSessionManager` rebinds the log that `ctx.sessionManager` exposes to contexts made afterwards. A replacement Session binds its own log through it.
- Codemode scripts can generate images with `models.generateImages(model, { input })`. It runs image models such as OpenRouter's with the session's credentials and returns base64 image blocks that `image()` attaches to the result. Usage counts toward the session cost like `models.classify()`. See `pig docs show codemode`.
- Extensions can generate images with `ctx.modelRegistry.generateImages()` in every SDK (`ModelRegistry.GenerateImages` in Go, `generate_images` in Rust and Python). It uses the session's credentials and reports failures as a result with `stopReason` `error` or `aborted`.
- MCP servers accept `oauth.authServerMetadataUrl`, the authorization server metadata document to use instead of discovery, for servers that advertise a wrong authorization server or none. It must use https, except on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `[::1]`. See `docs/mcp.md`.
- MCP OAuth credentials are stored per server name and URL, so servers with the same URL can sign in with different accounts. Credentials stored by URL alone move to the first server that uses them.
- `mcp/oauth`: `StepUpScope`, the `Iss` and `AuthorizationServerMetadataURL` flow options, `AuthorizationServerMetadata.AuthorizationResponseIssParameterSupported`, and `DiscoveryOptions.AuthorizationServerMetadataURL`.
- Go API: `Session.ReapplyActiveTools` applies a Session's selection again after a mode rebuilt the agent's tools, without dropping tools that are pending registration.
- Anthropic `/login` offers copy code login for setups where the browser runs on another machine: sign in through the printed URL and paste the code Anthropic shows.
- `/login` offers **Sign in with Radius** as the last top-level option, with its status. After a Radius sign-in, `/login` offers to configure the Radius MCP server in the global `mcp.json` with `"auth": { "provider": "radius" }` and reloads.
- `/sprite` chooses the pig from fifteen sprites (`/sprite list`, `/sprite set <id>`, or a picker): the default green pig, eight colors, and the characters Pigrogu, Darth Vader, Kratos, Piglet, Spider-Ham and Sheriff PiG. `/sprite preview [id]` shows a sprite's full art with the `PiG.` wordmark. The choice is saved in `$PIG_HOME/state/pig-standard/login.json`, where the Pigpen games read it. `/sprite` comes from the built-in `pig-login` extension, which `pig config` lists with the other built-in extensions.
- In fullscreen mode, clicking the pig head in the startup header (or the `PiG.` mark) plays PiG's version of Pi 1.0's logo animation: the screen dissolves into braille dust and the pig head flies to the center, grows and spins, then turns into a big side-view pig, drawn in the same braille dots, that runs in place, and lands back on the header when you press Escape or Ctrl+C (press again to skip). The dust, timing, starfield and keys are Pi's; the pig takes the colors of the sprite chosen with `/sprite`.
- `/pigsayhi` is a second name for `/arminsayshi`.
- Go packages port Pi 1.0.0's `@earendil-works/pi-durable` with its upstream tests: `durable` (types, IDs, errors, entries, documents, tasks and truncation), `durable/storage` (memory, JSONL and SQLite storage; SQLite runs on the pure-Go `modernc.org/sqlite`, so `CGO_ENABLED=0` builds keep it), `durable/harness` (agents, conversations, generation, scheduling, compaction, tasks and tools), `durable/tools` (read, write, edit and bash over a durable environment) and `durable/env`. The experimental services run on the durable harness.
- `durable/session`: the pi-durable Session kernel (mutation line, transactions, document trackers, forks, document states and watches) and `chord/delta`, the Chord overlay draft tracker and immutable operation applier.
- `telemetry`: the pi-telemetry schema data types, `DefineTelemetrySchema` and `CreateTypedSpanStarter`, and `telemetry/telemetrytest`, the runner-independent conformance cases for telemetry adapters.
- Extensions and Piglets add sprites to `/sprite` with `ctx.ui.registerSprite` (Go `RegisterSprite`, Rust and Python `register_sprite`): a 16-by-14 pig, which the header draws and `/sprite preview` shows beside the wordmark, and a palette. Registered sprites are listed after the built-in ones and saved like them; while the extension is not loaded, the header shows the default pig and the saved choice stays.
- Port the durable Harness generation path from Pi 1.0.0 (`packages/durable/src/harness`): the task scheduler with structured ownership, waits, completing holds, and bottom-up aborts; generation with recovery, retries, deferred polling, and overflow compaction; live `pi.live` deltas; exactly-once submissions; the `pi.inbox` queue; compaction; the `pi.usage` ledger; and the built-in tool task.
- PiG installs and runs in Termux on Android (arm64), including the Google Play build. `curl -fsSL https://pi-in-go.dev/install.sh | sh` detects Termux and installs the new `android-arm64` release into `$PREFIX/bin`, `npm install -g @pi-in-go/pig` installs the new `@pi-in-go/pig-android-arm64` package, and `pig update` updates a script installation. The Android binary is built with cgo against Android's C library; Termux never runs `linux-arm64`. Android on other CPUs has no release. See the Termux page of the docs.
- In Termux, PiG sets `TMPDIR` to `$PREFIX/tmp` and `SSL_CERT_FILE` to `$PREFIX/etc/tls/cert.pem` when they are unset.
- When Termux starts PiG through `/system/bin/linker64`, PiG starts the programs it launches the same way, runs scripts through their interpreter with `/bin` and `/usr/bin` read as `$PREFIX/bin`, and finds its own path without `/proc/self/exe`.
- `pig piglet build --format binary` works on a binary-only install. Without a PiG checkout or `PIG_SOURCE_ROOT`, a release binary fetches the PiG source of exactly its own version through the Go module proxy (`go mod download`), checked by `GOSUMDB` and cached after the first build. A development build still asks for a checkout or `PIG_SOURCE_ROOT`.
- A built-in `container` builder runs Piglet Binary builds in Podman or Docker. It is used when the native builder is not ready, or with `--builder container`, so hosts without Go and other `linux/<arch>` targets can build. `PIG_CONTAINER_ENGINE` selects `docker` or `podman`; otherwise Podman is preferred.

### Changed

- Interactive mode starts in fullscreen by default, as upstream 1.0.0 does. Set `tuiMode` to `"regular"` in settings, or pass `--tui-mode regular`, to keep the terminal's normal scrollback.
- `quietStartup` accepts `"header"`: the startup header stays visible and the loaded-resource listing is hidden. `true` hides both and `false` shows both. `/settings` offers the three values.
- In Apple Terminal the startup header shows the one-line `PiG.` mark and the version on its first line and the key hints below, following Pi 1.0.0's text-wordmark layout (D2).
- Break the exported Go tool-progress API to match Pi's `AgentToolUpdateCallback`. `agent.ToolUpdateCallback` is now `func(partial agent.AgentToolResult)` instead of `func(content string, details any)`, and `agent.ToolExecutionUpdateEvent` carries `PartialResult agent.AgentToolResult` instead of `Content string` and `Details any`. Migrate a tool that reports progress from `update("text", details)` to `update(agent.AgentToolResult{Content: []ai.ToolResultMessageContent{ai.TextContent{Text: "text"}}, Details: details})`, and report a progress update without blocks (Pi's bash startup and codemode updates) with an empty `Content`. Read an update's text with `partial.Text()`. The same change applies to `extension.ExecuteToolOptions.OnUpdate` values, which are `func(agent.AgentToolResult)` or `func(agent.AgentToolResult) error`, and to `agent.ToolUpdateSink`, which is now `func(partial agent.AgentToolResult) error`.
- Share one `pi.events` bus across every Node extension process, including explicitly isolated extensions and exact standalones. A listener in another process receives the emitter's original payload through cross-process references, so synchronous mutations, reentrant emits, retained and post-await aliases, and callbacks behave as in Pi. D77 is retired; D83 names the remaining cross-process boundaries.
- Record owner-approved 0.3.x upstream test-porting and review gaps without marking unfinished work ported. The test-porting release gate lists each approved deferral and its missing cases, reports the count, and still rejects unlisted gaps, source drift and baseline regressions. Existing runtime tests and parity comparisons remain unchanged.
- Break the Go `ai` model API to match upstream 0.99.1 model types (`packages/ai/src/types.ts` `AnyModel`, `ImageModel`, `ClassifierModel`; `packages/ai/src/models.ts` `CreateProviderOptions`). Go has no covariant slices, so no compatibility alias is possible. Migrate as follows:
  - `CreateProviderOptions.Models` is `[]ai.AnyModel`, and `FetchModels` returns `[]ai.AnyModel`. Convert an existing `[]*ai.Model` with `ai.AnyModels(models)`, or write a `[]ai.AnyModel{...}` literal. `ModelsProvider.GetModels` and `FilterModels` still list only chat models; `GetAllModels` and `FilterAllModels` cover every type.
  - `ImagesModel` is `ImageModel`, `ImagesAPI` is `ImageAPI`, and an image model's provider is a plain `string`. `ImagesCost` is removed: `ImageModel.Cost` and `ClassifierModel.Cost` are `ai.ModelCost`, and both types gain `InputLimits`.
  - The `ImagesModels` collection is removed. Generate images with `Models.GenerateImages`, which resolves provider auth like `Stream` and reports every failure as an error or aborted `AssistantImages` result.
  - `CreateProvider` panics when it receives no chat, image or classifier implementation, as Pi's `createProvider` throws. A `*ProviderStreams` with no function set does not count.
  - `HasApi` and `Models.GetModelAuth` take `ai.AnyModel`. `GetModelAuth` merges the headers of every model type.
- Create the session file when the first user message is added, not after the first assistant reply, as upstream 0.99.1 does. Setup entries alone (a model or thinking-level change) still leave no file. A crash after the first prompt and before any reply now keeps the prompt on disk and the session resumable. A fork or clone writes its file at once when the retained branch already holds a user or assistant message.
- Report `This session has not been saved yet. Send a message before cloning or forking it.` when `/clone` or `/fork` runs before the session has a file.
- Go API: `Settings.DefaultTools` holds the merged raw `defaultTools` list, which can contain `+name` and `-name` entries. Read the selection to start with through `SettingsManager.GetDefaultTools()` or `Settings.GetDefaultTools()`, which apply the entries; do not read the field directly.
- Break the Go extension provider API to match upstream 0.99.1 (`provider-composer.ts` `ProviderConfigInput`). `ProviderConfigInput.Models` and the value `RefreshModels` returns are `[]ai.AnyModel`: convert an existing `[]*ai.Model` with `ai.AnyModels(models)` or write a `[]ai.AnyModel{...}` literal. A registration can also carry image and classifier models with their implementations in `Images` and `Classifiers`. A model list replaces every model type of the provider, including built-in image models.
- Change the default models of `openai-codex` to `gpt-6.1-sol`, `fireworks` to Kimi K3, `together` to Kimi K3 and `opencode-go` to Kimi K3, as upstream 0.99.1 does.
- Reject a model whose type is not chat at the chat entry points of `coding.ModelRuntime` before any provider lookup: `Stream`, `StreamSimple` and their `Complete` forms.
- The `sourceInfo.path` of a built-in tool is `builtin:<name>`, and the built-in llama.cpp extension has the path `builtin:llama.cpp` and the source `builtin`. This replaces `<builtin:<name>>` and `<inline:llama.cpp>`. Migration: match on the new paths.
- Git package dependencies install with arguments for the detected package manager (`npm install --omit=dev --legacy-peer-deps`, `pnpm install --prod` with the pnpm config flags, `bun install --omit=dev --omit=peer`, otherwise `install`). An `npmCommand` whose package manager cannot be determined is now an error. `packagemanager.NpmCommandName` is replaced by `PackageManagerName`, which returns `(string, error)`, and `GetGitDependencyInstallArgs` returns `([]string, error)`. No shim remains.
- A temporary git checkout of a pinned ref lives in a directory whose name includes the ref.
- Break the Go extension API to match upstream 0.99.1's extension API, with no compatibility shim. `extension.EventBus` uses Pi's names and semantics: `Publish(topic, payload)` is now `Emit(channel, data)` and `Subscribe(topic, handler)` is now `On(channel, handler)`, which returns the unsubscribe function (`core/event-bus.ts:3-6`). `extension.API` gains `GetSettings`, `RegisterMcpServer`, `UnregisterMcpServer`, `GetMcpServers`, `RegisterVirtualModel`, `UnregisterVirtualModel`, `OnMcpServersChange` and `OnProviderStreamEvent`, so an `extension.API` implementation must add them (`extensiontest.Fake` does). `ToolDecl.Annotations` on the subprocess wire is `*extension.ToolAnnotations` (Pi's `ToolAnnotations`) instead of a string map. `extension.ProviderModelConfig` is a chat, image or classifier model chosen by `Type`, and its image and classifier variants serialize without the chat-only fields.
- **Breaking:** `Session.Steer` and `Session.FollowUp` return `(QueuedInputDisposition, error)`. `PromptOptions.PreflightResult` is `func(PromptDisposition)` and is called only for a prompt that Pi accepts (`started`, `handled` or `queued`). There is no shim.
- Manual and automatic compaction resolve summarization credentials only when Pi summarizes the session itself, so an extension-supplied summary needs none.
- `Session.SetActiveToolsByName` de-duplicates names and ignores hidden tools.
- `NewSession` binds the extension core at construction, so virtual models an extension registered while loading are selectable immediately.
- Break the Rust extension SDK's `ToolDefinition` and `ToolInfo` to carry upstream 0.99.1's tool fields, with no compatibility shim. A `ToolDefinition` gains `output_schema`, `exposure`, `namespace`, `annotations`, `default_active` and `prepare_loadout`, so a struct literal must set them; build one with `ToolDefinition::new` and assign the fields you need. A `ToolInfo` from `Context::get_all_tools()` gains `exposure`, `namespace` and `annotations`. `ToolResult` is unchanged; `with_structured_content`, `with_details` and `with_is_error` add fields to it.
- The Rust SDK's `Provider` struct gains the public fields `generate_images` and `classify` (`Option<ProviderOperationFn>`), which declare a provider's image generation and classification. Struct literals of `Provider` must add them, with `None` when a provider has neither, as they had to add `fetch_deferred`.
- Send Pi's collection-complete `systemPromptOptions` to extensions, keep an explicit `constrainedSampling: false` in transcript tool declarations, and echo and arm RPC dialog timeouts as JavaScript numbers.
- Codemode costs fewer prompt tokens, as in Pi 1.0.0. The `codemode` description lists the script globals in one line each and points to the codemode page of the docs bundle for the `models` API. Declared tools say in one line how scripts call them and what the call resolves to, instead of repeating their declaration, and the system prompt's codemode guidance and MCP server section are shorter.
- Codemode errors say how to recover: reading a tool or `models` member that does not exist names the close matches (`tools.Bash` suggests `tools.bash`), `models.classify()` and `models.generateImages()` reject malformed arguments with the expected shape, an unknown model points to `models.getAvailableOfType()`, an oversized `store()` value explains what the store is for, and a script that generates images without showing them gets a note. Scripts that probed for a tool with `typeof tools.name` must use `"name" in tools`.
- **Breaking:** as in Pi 1.0.0, `McpOAuthCredentialStore.ForServer`, `Tokens` and `Remove` take the server name before the server URL, and `OAuthIssuerMismatchError.Received` is a `*string`, nil when an authorization response lacks the `iss` its server promised. There is no shim: a URL-only store would write the legacy key that the name-keyed store takes over.
- Cancelling a sign-in method prompt in `/login`, including the Amazon Bedrock and Google Vertex AI method prompts, or closing a provider's setup note returns to the menu the login was started from.
- `/login` and `/logout` label providers without credentials "not configured" instead of "unconfigured", and label OAuth sign-ins that are not subscriptions (Radius, OpenRouter) "account" instead of "subscription".
- OAuth browser pages show the color logo.
- The `mcp_servers` system prompt section explains only the ways of reaching tools (codemode, `tool_search`) that its servers use.
- The `bash` and `powershell` output schema descriptions match Pi 1.0.0.
- Login dialogs show each sign-in flow's own paste prompt, and Escape in an OAuth login dialog reports `Failed to login to <provider>: This operation was aborted`.
- The startup header shows the sprite's pixel pig where it showed Pi's logo, so PiG never presents itself as Pi. The pig is seven lines tall in the logo's place, with the version, the key hints and the onboarding line beside it; the hints, expanded help and loaded resources are otherwise unchanged. 256-color terminals and panes too narrow for the head get a one-line `PiG.` mark in the logo's 4 cells, and Apple Terminal gets Pi's Apple Terminal layout with that mark. `setHeader(undefined)` and `/reload` restore the PiG header, and an extension can still replace it with `setHeader`.
- `/arminsayshi` draws a pig head labeled `pigsayhi` with Pi's Armin effects, instead of Armin's portrait.
- Syntax highlighting uses a Go port of highlight.js 10.7.3, the highlighter Pi uses, instead of chroma. Code blocks in messages, tool output and codemode cards now get the same token colors as in Pi for every language, and `pig` starts faster. As in Pi, the 20 most common languages highlight at startup, and the rest load after the first screen, which then repaints.
- The experimental Session worker publishes each transcript change as the durable revision's own operations, as Pi does, instead of re-encoding and re-sending the whole conversation view on every commit.

### Removed

- The Go packages `agent/harness/...` and `agent/search` are removed, as Pi 1.0.0 removed the agent harness and search from `@earendil-works/pi-agent-core`. The durable harness is `durable/harness`. `ai.UUIDv7` replaces `agent/harness/session.UUIDv7`, as Pi exports `uuidv7` from `@earendil-works/pi-ai`.

### Fixed

- On Android, commands in `!command` config values run through `/system/bin/sh`, as Node does, instead of the missing `/bin/sh`.
- Container builds of a Piglet with local extensions, skills, or prompt files no longer fail with a read-only file system error when the engine mounts them.
- A Piglet extension whose origins do not resolve is now a startup extension load error, as Pi reports an extension that fails to load: `Error: Failed to load extension "<path>": ...`, the `-ne` hint, and exit status 1. It was a stderr warning that the alternate screen hid, and the Piglet started without the extension.
- An OpenRouter image-generation HTTP error reports `<status>: <error JSON>` like chat providers, `<status> status code (no body)` for an empty body, and no longer returns the raw response body.
- OpenRouter image generation calls the `onResponse` hook only for a successful, parseable response, as Pi does. An HTTP error or an unparseable body no longer calls the hook, and a failing hook no longer replaces that error.
- An OpenAI-compatible HTTP error whose JSON body has no `error` member, or a null one, reports the whole body, for example `401: {"message":"..."}`, instead of `<status> status code (no body)`. This applies to chat completions, Responses, Azure OpenAI Responses and OpenRouter image generation.
- An extension keeps the terminal height after `/reload`. A packed Go extension received a height of 0 after a reload until the next resize. Thanks to @nicholas-recht (#115, #116).
- The host delivers width and height changes to each extension that has not seen the value, so a resize during `/reload`, a crash restart of one extension, and a packed Node extension whose ready message waits for every factory no longer leave an extension on an old height.
- On Windows, retry publishing a Node extension cell (and any other cached runtime cell) when the final directory rename fails with `Access is denied`, a sharing violation or a lock violation, instead of stopping extension loading. Anti-virus scanners and indexers hold freshly written files open for a short time, and Windows refuses to rename the directory that contains them. PiG now retries with backoff for up to 10 seconds, adopts the cell if another PiG process publishes it meanwhile, and reports any other error, or the last error after the 10 seconds, unchanged. Other platforms are unaffected. Thanks to @worldofgeese for the detailed report (#106).
- An extension tool's `prepareArguments` now runs before PiG validates the model's arguments, as in Pi, in the Node, Go, Rust and Python SDKs. An extension that spreads Pi's `createEditToolDefinition` (for example the `minimal-mode` and built-in tool renderer examples) accepts the legacy flat `{path, oldText, newText}` edit again instead of failing with a validation error.
- The tool calls of a parallel batch now start in the model's order in extension tools, as in Pi. A `write` followed by an `edit` of the same file in one batch no longer fails with `ENOENT` because the edit started first.
- Anthropic workload identity federation counts as configured authentication everywhere: `--list-models`, default model selection, `-p` and the provider auth status accept the federation variables alone, and an extension's builtin `anthropic-messages` request with only those variables is no longer rejected for a missing key.
- A direct `anthropic` stream no longer sends `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` itself, so it federates when the federation variables are set. The auth resolver still reads the variable first for model runtime requests.
- A federation token endpoint that cannot be reached reports `Failed to reach token endpoint <url>: TypeError: fetch failed`.
- `/llama` is now a command of the built-in `llama.cpp` extension, as in Pi, instead of a built-in slash command. The slash popup lists Pi's 24 built-in commands (`1/24`) and shows `/llama` only while that extension is loaded, so it no longer appears with `--no-extensions` or `-builtin:llama.cpp`.
- `get_commands` and the slash popup list the built-in extensions' commands in Pi's order: `/llama` before `/mcp`, following `llama.cpp`, `codemode`, `tool-search`, `mcp`.
- Color the bash execution block, the compaction and branch summary label, the editor thinking-level border (low, medium, high), and the settings list cursor from the active theme, as Pi does. Light and custom themes now apply their own colors to these elements instead of the dark theme's fixed values. Thanks @jkerdreux-imt.
- In fullscreen mode, a built-in selector shown in the editor slot (`/model`, `/settings`, `/resume`, `/tree`, `/fork` and the others), or an extension's custom UI in the editor slot, no longer takes `PageUp`, `PageDown`, `Home`, `End`, the mouse wheel or `ctrl+shift+f`. These keys move the transcript viewport or open transcript search first, as in Pi. Before, `PageDown` in `/model` reapplied the search query and selected the first match.
- Fullscreen (alternate-screen) mode keeps the Kitty keyboard-protocol flags on the active screen, as Pi does. The main and alternate screens keep independent protocol stacks, so the flags pushed for the main screen did not apply in fullscreen: `Shift+Enter` degraded to a bare Enter and `ctrl+digit` extension shortcuts stopped matching. PiG also no longer leaves the Kitty keyboard flags set in the shell after it exits fullscreen, where `ctrl+c` arrived as an escape sequence instead of `^C`. Thanks @jkerdreux-imt.
- Preserve CLI provenance for extensions selected with `-e` from temporary Git and npm sources. Their commands report `cli`/`temporary`/`top-level` metadata without a Package base directory.
- RPC mode loads extensions before it resolves `--model` and `--models`, so a model registered by a `-e` extension, including a temporary Git source, can start an RPC session. `--mode rpc --list-models` lists extension-registered providers, and each model scope warning prints once.
- `--help` and `--list-models` print and exit 0 in every mode when an extension fails to load, as Pi does. Other startup still reports the extension error and exits 1.
- Give an extension's `ctx.signal` the signal of the run in progress, as Pi does, in every SDK. It is undefined while no run is active (a command or `session_start` while idle), is one object for the whole run, and is aborted with the run, even for a handler that is still in flight. A Node tool's `signal` parameter is that same object, and a nested call whose caller passed `options.signal` runs with it, as in Pi. Before, the Node SDK handed every handler a signal of its own that was always defined and aborted only when the host cancelled that handler.
- A Node extension's `ctx.executeTool(name, args, { signal: null })` runs the nested call with the calling tool's signal, as Pi's `options.signal ?? signal` does. Before, the call was sent and then failed with a `TypeError`.
- An extension's `ctx.signal` follows the run at the moment it is read, as Pi's getter does. Before, a runtime learned of a run only when its next request arrived, so a timer that read `ctx.signal` after a run ended still got the ended run's signal, and a run that began was invisible to it. Print, JSON, RPC and interactive mode now report each run start and end to every runtime, in every SDK.
- A Node extension's context throws Pi's stale-context message from every member (`ctx.signal`, `ctx.cwd`, `ctx.ui`, `ctx.isIdle()`, `ctx.newSession()` and the rest) once its runtime is stale, as Pi's `assertActive` does. The command context's `waitForIdle`, `newSession`, `fork`, `navigateTree`, `switchSession` and `reload` throw synchronously, as in Pi.
- Interactive mode no longer races the extension host's `ctx.signal` read against a Session replacement.
- A Node extension's `ctx.thinkingLevel` is an enumerable own property, as in Pi's `createContext`, so `{ ...ctx }` keeps it. Before, the spread copy had no `thinkingLevel`.
- Read Amazon Bedrock ConverseStream event streams over HTTP/1 in Pi's order, so the first `message_start` an RPC client receives matches Pi's (the text, thinking or tool-call block after the second event, with its streaming `index` and `partialJson` fields), and report the configured provider id for a custom `bedrock-converse-stream` provider.
- Keep a Bedrock stream alive when the response arrives before the transport finishes writing the request; the SDK's early close of the request body could end the stream with `terminated`.
- A Bedrock request aborted while its stream is open now ends with `aborted` (or `Request was aborted` when the body had already been read), as Pi does.
- Make the partial message that an RPC `message_start` carries over Codex SSE match Pi. The Codex stream now runs as one ordered turn transcribed from Pi's `parseSSE`, `mapCodexEvents` and `processStream` awaits instead of a free goroutine, so the state is deterministic and equal to Pi's for buffered and pending responses.
- Parse Codex SSE records as Pi does: split only on a blank `\n\n` line, skip non-object records, report `null` records as a TypeError, and use `JSON.parse` wording in `Invalid Codex SSE JSON` errors.
- Fixed the assistant message a consumer observes at each `mistral-conversations` delivery differing from Pi's, including the `message_start` an RPC client reads. The provider loop now spends the same number of microtasks per await, yield and rejection as Pi's `readMistralEvents`, its `for await` and its async return, so every event describes the message at the same point of the stream. A tool call also exposes its `partialArgs` scratch buffer while it streams, as in Pi.
- Fixed Mistral streams reporting Go's `context canceled` instead of Pi's `This operation was aborted` when the request is aborted, and Go's JSON error text instead of V8's `JSON.parse` message for a malformed record.
- Fixed Mistral streams skipping empty text deltas: an empty `content` string now opens a text block and pushes a `text_delta`, as in Pi.
- Fixed a Mistral `finish_reason` of `error` or an unknown reason ending the stream early. Pi records the reason and keeps consuming the body before it reports the error, and it reports the error without closing open blocks.
- Fixed Mistral usage, the stop reason and the raw stop reason being assigned only when the stream ended. They are set as each chunk arrives, and the usage object is updated in place.
- Fixed `pi-messages` streams reporting Go's JSON error text and `context canceled` instead of Pi's: a malformed backend record now fails the turn with V8's `JSON.parse` message, and an aborted request reports `This operation was aborted`.
- Fixed `pi-messages` inventing a `start` event for a backend that sends none. The stream now carries exactly the events the backend sent, as Pi's does, and a second `start` is forwarded.
- Fixed `pi-messages` response bodies being decoded byte-wise. Chunk splits inside a multi-byte character, a leading byte order mark, invalid UTF-8, `\r\n` record separators, and falsy JSON records now behave as in Pi's `TextDecoder` and `JSON.parse` path, and the 64 MiB SSE record limit that Pi does not have is gone.
- Fixed the assistant message a consumer observes at each `pi-messages` delivery differing from Pi's. The provider loop now spends the same number of microtasks per await, yield and rejection as Pi's `readPiMessagesEvents` and its `for await`, so `message_start` and every later event describe the message at the same point of the stream.
- Stop setting `responseModel` from Gemini `modelVersion`; Pi reads only `responseId` from Google Generative AI and Vertex chunks.
- Keep a `context` handler's in-place edits to `event.messages` when a Go handler returns a nil `messages` list, and when a Rust handler returns no result or a null `messages` value, as Pi does.
- Apply a Go extension's `context` handler result when its `messages` value is a typed list such as `[]map[string]any`, as Pi applies any returned array. A `messages` value that is not a list is now that handler's error in every SDK instead of a silently unchanged context.
- Bind `ctx.getContextUsage()` to the Session for extensions in print, JSON, and RPC mode, as Pi does in every mode. It previously returned `undefined` outside interactive mode.
- Shut RPC mode down in Pi's order: publish already-queued Session events, detach the stdout subscription, emit `session_shutdown`, then disconnect and abort the run. The aborted run still delivers its `turn_end` boundary and `agent_settled` to extensions. A boundary without a persisted assistant entry reports Pi's `extension_error` on stdout.
- Restore signal termination during an RPC shutdown, and exit 0 at once when stdin ends during a signal-triggered shutdown. Stop owned extension processes without awaiting suspended handlers.
- Leave unanswered RPC dialogs and unsettled shutdown Promises pending when stdin ends. A Node event-loop drain ends the process without fabricating a handler result.
- Preserve the deterministic shutdown-notification-before-command-response order when an RPC extension command requests shutdown.
- Start a new Session at a `--session` path that does not exist yet and persist it there, as Pi does, instead of failing with "session not found".
- Send `tool_execution_update` events to extensions, RPC and JSON consumers with Pi's `partialResult` shape: the `content` member is an array of blocks (`[]` for a startup update, `[{"type":"text","text":"42\n"}]` for output) instead of a string, and images and several blocks survive.
- Keep the member order of a JSON object an extension process supplies, as a JavaScript object does, and hold it as JSON.stringify writes it whichever SDK encoded it: integer-like keys first, `1.0` as `1`, `\u00e9` as `é`. A tool's `details` and partial results, a `tool_result` handler's replacement `details`, `appendEntry` data, `sendMessage` and `before_agent_start` message `details`, compaction and branch-summary `details`, and the custom messages a `context` handler receives are no longer rewritten with sorted keys on their way to the session file, the JSON and RPC wire and other extensions.
- Write a tool's result members in the order the tool wrote them on the JSON and RPC streams and to extension `tool_execution_update` and `tool_execution_end` handlers, as Pi hands the tool's own object on. A Node tool that returns `{details, isError, content}` is no longer rewritten as `{content, details, isError}`; Python dicts and Rust JSON objects keep their order too.
- Write a tool result's `isError: false`, `terminate: false`, `details: null` and `usage: null` when the tool wrote them, as Pi's object holds them.
- Return a `ctx.executeTool` outcome result and each partial result with the called tool's member order, as Pi hands on the called tool's own object.
- Answer a Go, Rust or Python extension's session reads (`getEntries`, `getTree`, `buildSessionContext`, `buildSessionProjection` and the rest) with Pi's member order and number forms: entries as `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify` hold them, messages built in the order of Pi's object literals, and `model` as `{provider, modelId}`. Go maps no longer sort them.
- Return an MCP server an extension registered from `getMcpServers` exactly as registered: its members in the order written, and members the host does not know kept, instead of the host's fixed member order.
- Match upstream 0.99.1 in the extension API: a Node factory's `registerMcpServer` validates and checks ownership when it is called and throws to the factory, `getMcpServers()` lists the live registry while the factory runs, and a pre-bind `unregisterVirtualModel` also removes a virtual model another extension queued (every SDK). A router that returns its request's state leaves the stored state unchanged whatever the JSON encoder's escaping. `ctx.tools` reflects `setActiveTools` inside the same handler. A throwing `executeTool` `onUpdate` rejects the nested call with its first error, without `afterToolCall` or `tool_execution_end` for that call. A host call that a Go, Rust or Python handler starts without awaiting keeps running after the handler returns.
- Refresh the footer name after `/fork` from the destination Session instead of retaining the source name. Forks and clones keep only names in their copied branch; `/new` clears the name.
- Model Runtime passes cancellation to injected credential stores during Radius catalog restoration and request authentication. Cancelled refreshes return without waiting for an uncooperative store, while runtime shutdown drains retained reads, including native provider reads. Storage failures reach the caller instead of falling through to ambient Radius credentials. A concurrent OAuth refresh leaves an already refreshed credential unchanged and uses the store's post-modify result.
- Send extension model headers, models.json `modelOverrides` headers and `$VAR` or `!command` header values with requests to providers registered through `RegisterProvider` or `RegisterNativeProvider`, and keep a header that `TransformHeaders` deletes out of the request. Native providers now resolve model auth through the same registry path as other providers.
- Ordinary npm libraries install and remain listed even when they contain no Pi resources. Node entrypoint discovery no longer misclassifies them as extensions.
- Build legacy Go standalone extensions against the running binary's complete SDK module, including subpackages such as `sdk/json`, without changing authored files.
- Preserve registration identity for duplicate extension folders in native cells. Separate Go copies with the same module path so each runs its own code. Report capability conflicts after loading instead of rejecting the second copy's registration.
- Restrict native extension build notices to interactive mode. Print, JSON, and RPC remain silent during compilation even when stderr is a terminal.
- Match JavaScript trimming for grammar definitions, request authentication headers, Anthropic beta features, OAuth pasted input and polling intervals, auth environment presence checks, and provider error details. Preserve nonblank payloads where Pi uses trimming only as a presence check.
- Preserve NEL and remove BOM consistently when naming sessions and listing their saved names.
- Node extensions in one process share Pi's single registered configuration root for a provider: `getRegisteredProviderConfig` returns the same object to every member, a partial re-registration merges over it, an invalid re-registration is rejected before it changes anything, and `streamSimple` runs with that root as `this`. The latest registrant owns the provider's cleanup, so a superseded extension's exit no longer removes it.
- A Node extension in another process now receives the same registered Provider configuration object as the author, through a cross-process reference: repeated reads are identical, writes to the root are shared, the author's later changes to its models and functions are visible, and a partial re-registration there merges over the author's configuration instead of dropping its fields. Registrations authored or read by Go, Rust or Python extensions remain snapshots (D78).
- Keep OAuth credentials as Pi's complete token object. An `auth.json` entry with a fractional, absent or non-number `expires` no longer makes the whole credential store unreadable, and expiry decisions follow Pi's JavaScript comparisons. Extension refresh and API-key callbacks receive the stored credential, including `type: "oauth"` and provider-owned keys, and their results keep every key and the exact expiry in the Go, Rust, Python and Node SDKs.
- Builtin Anthropic, OpenAI Codex, Kimi, Radius, xAI and GitHub Copilot logins compute expiry with Pi's arithmetic, so a fractional or string `expires_in` is accepted and not truncated. Interactive and CLI logins now store the complete credential, including OpenAI Codex `accountId`.
- Node extension OAuth callbacks now receive Pi's AbortSignals: `refreshToken(credentials, signal)` gets its caller's signal, which a cancelled host request aborts, composed with Pi's 15 second timeout only when a stored credential is refreshed during auth resolution (a model refresh passes the caller's signal alone), and `login` callbacks carry the interaction `signal`. A signal an extension retains after the callback returns stays live and still follows its caller's later abort.
- Run the Anthropic Messages stream as an executor turn chain that follows Pi's `iterateSseMessages`, `iterateAnthropicEvents` and `for await` ticks, so the partial that a consumer receives describes the stream at its own tick: open blocks carry Pi's `index` (and a tool call its `partialJson`) until `content_block_stop`, and `message_start`'s response ID and usage are visible without a push. `Stream` now returns before the request settles, as Pi's does.
- Deliver each stream partial with its exported fields set to the stream state at delivery, so listeners read `event.Partial` and `event.Message` directly without a special call. `ai.RefreshEvent` and `agent.RefreshEvent` re-read a retained event.
- Name generated Google and Vertex tool-call IDs `name_<epoch ms>_<counter>` with one counter per API, and regenerate an ID that repeats an earlier call in the same message, as Pi does.
- Match Pi's partial-message ordering across extension handlers, stdout backpressure and lazy stream setup: an awaited extension handler or stdout write no longer freezes the provider, RPC and JSON print mode register Pi's backpressure listener, and a synchronous provider setup runs before its stream is returned. Aborts report Pi's texts: `Request aborted` before response headers, `This operation was aborted` for an interrupted read, and `Request was aborted` for the provider's own signal check (OpenAI Completions and Responses, Anthropic).
- `OverlayHandle.unfocus({ target })` in a Node extension focuses exactly its target when the target is `null`, the extension's installed editor component, or another of its mounted overlays, as in Pi. It previously threw for every target (D73).
- Fold later system messages into the leading Bedrock system prompt, as Pi does, even when a model's compat settings claim mid-conversation system message support. Bedrock requests no longer carry system updates as user turns or drop them by Unicode whitespace rules.
- Match Pi's ECMAScript whitespace rules when OpenAI Completions, Mistral and Google requests drop blank assistant text and thinking: BOM-only blocks are dropped and NEL-only blocks are sent. Mistral tool results trim with the same rules, and Mistral sends a later whitespace-only system update as Pi does.
- Keep lone UTF-16 surrogates in session names, labels, custom entries, custom messages, compaction and branch summaries, and model changes when PiG writes or reads a session file, as Pi's `JSON.stringify` and `JSON.parse` do. RPC `get_state`, `get_tree` and `get_messages` no longer report them as U+FFFD.
- Non-interactive startup reserves stdout for explicit JSON/RPC/text and print-mode metadata, including `--list-models`; plain help and model listings retain stdout. Empty model-list filters and repeated `--list-models` flags preserve the last requested value.
- Session selection now precedes `--name` validation. Missing-session and missing-fork errors are no longer masked by invalid names, and exact-ID creation warnings remain visible before a name error.
- Remove deleted Sessions from the picker before asynchronous refresh so Enter cannot resume a deleted path while loading.
- Match Pi's Mermaid fallback: preserve unsupported and naturally over-wide source without extra hints or label narrowing. Retain final partial-parse warnings and streaming diagrams.
- Normalize CLI Session names with JavaScript whitespace rules and validate them after Session selection, preserving missing-session diagnostics and BOM/NEL behavior.
- Preserve explicitly empty system-prompt and append-prompt selections and empty file-backed append entries.
- Resolve automatic extension exclusions relative to the config root, as Pi does.
- Load physical extension aliases once while retaining the first selected path and distinct native factories in one module.
- Transform only top-level Mermaid code tokens. Preserve examples nested in other code blocks, lists, blockquotes, HTML, and multiline reference definitions, while allowing subsequent top-level diagrams to render.
- Match Pi's mixed closing-fence suffixes, space-only fence closers, whitespace-token boundaries, and HTML tag-prefix exclusions.
- Resolve Mistral request URLs as Pi does: `v1/chat/completions` is resolved beneath the normalized base path with WHATWG host, port and path rules, so a custom base URL ending in `/v1` is no longer rewritten. Invalid base URLs fail before any request, empty error bodies report the HTTP status text, and whitespace handling follows JavaScript `trim`.
- Compare models by ID and declared provider, as Pi's `modelsAreEqual` does, even when a model has no attached backend.
- Preserve custom cancellation reasons when the runtime credential overlay rejects an aborted operation.
- Allow queued file-backed credential mutations to cancel without waiting for another active callback on the same store.
- Expose raw automatic theme settings through `SettingsManager.GetThemeSetting` and keep fixed-theme lookup separate.
- Preserve credential insertion order while sharing file reloads without coupling reader cancellation.
- Preserve distinct UTF-16 provider IDs through credential decoding and persistence.
- Retain provider-owned metadata when reading validated read-only credentials.
- Preserve Kitty printable-key deduplication across an empty stdin-buffer flush.
- Match Bedrock's ECMAScript blank-text rules for user, assistant, thinking, signature, and tool-result content. Sanitize assistant text before checking whether it is empty.
